## 1. Part 1

*kāvalil pulanai vaittuk kalitannaik kadakkap pāyndu nāvalittu uḻi taruginṟōm namantamar talaigaḷ mīdē mūvulagu uṇḍu umiḻnda mudalva nin nāmam kaṟṟa āvalippu udaimai kaṇḍāy arngamā nagaruḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mū ulagu – all the worlds; uṇḍu – (during the time of deluge or annihilation) keeping in the stomach (and protecting); umiḻnda – (later) brought them out; mudalva – the entity responsible for the creation of universe; nin nāmam kaṟṟa – by learning (through ācāryan) your divine names; āvalippu udaimai – due to the sense of pride (of learning the divine names); pulanai – the five sensory perceptions (seeing, hearing, feeling, smelling and eating); kāval il vaittu – letting the senses wander about without securing them firmly; kali tannai – all the masses of sins; kadakkap pāyndu – get rid off, with all traces; nāvalittu – with a victorious war-cry; naman tamar talaigaḷ mīdē – both atop yama (dēvatā or demi-god for justice and righteousness) and his followers; uḻi taruginṟōm kaṇḍāy – we keep walking, see for yourself

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) We (the jīvātmās) have been under yamadarmarāja’s control because of the sins that we have committed and are committing. Reciting emperumān’s  divine names will deliver us from these sins and thus from yamadarmarāja. Hence these divine names purify us. Also, reciting his divine names gives the person who recites it, a special status, such as belonging to a regal heritage (in other words, the person who recites it will be held in high esteem). pāsuram காவலில் புலனை வைத்துக் கழிதன்னைக் கடக்கப் பாய்ந்து நாவலிட்டு உழி தருகின்றோம் நமன்தமர் தலைகள் மீதே மூவுலகு உண்டு உமிழ்ந்த முதல்வ நின் நாமம் கற்ற ஆவலிப்பு உடைமை கண்டாய் அரங்கமா நகருளானே*

**kāvalil pulanai vaittu** – keeping the sensory perceptions free from any control (allowing them to roam around freely without any inhibitions). There are certain disadvantages to this. It will not help in any way in reaping the advantage stated later in the pāsuram (of walking atop *yamadarmararājā*’s and his followers’ heads). It will also make the person (who has no control over his senses) to behave like an animal (*nrupaśu* – an animal in the form of human being). Animals do not have any control over their senses; human beings are expected to have control. But if a person lets his senses loose, he will not be different from an animal. Such a person will not follow what the śāstras have ordained us to do – do what is permitted and refrain from doing, what is prohibited. How then did such a cētanan (a [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)) get the power to walk atop *yamadarmarāja*? It is the prowess of emperumān’s tirunāmam (divine names) alone which can elevate the jīvātmā by removing his *dōśas* (faults) and providing him the required resource to walk atop *yamadarmarāja*.

When a person is stung (or bitten) by a poisonous *jantu* (creature like a scorpion or snake), there are several restrictions imposed on him to negate the effect of poison. He cannot have oil bath or he cannot eat certain types of foodstuff or he cannot sleep. But if an expert in providing antidote to such poisons tells the person bitten that he need not observe any of these restrictions and that he can lead a normal life, but that his medicine will still be effective, will it not be wondrous? In the same way, reciting the divine names of emperumān will provide succour to a samsāri bitten by the poison of samsāram.

“**vaittu**” (kāvalil pulanai vaittu) – it is not that the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) had allowed the senses to wander  without his knowledge; here it means that knowingly he had allowed his senses to do so and indulge in wrongful acts.

**kāvalil** – “kāval illādapadi” – without any control: will this meaning be appropriate when it should really be taken as “keeping the senses under control”? Experts in language will confirm that the meaning – without any control – is superior to the meaning – with control. This is so, because it is like the expert in antidote to poison saying that the person bitten need not follow any restrictions. If restrictions are imposed, it is a normal case. Only when restrictions are withdrawn, will it translate into a superior treatment. It is the same way here.

Now we can consider the meaning of the same phrase (*kāvalil pulanai vaittu*) with the second meaning for “kāvalil” – i.e. keeping the senses under control. In this case, this act (of keeping the senses under control) becomes the fruit (end result) of reciting emperumān’s divine names. This is like the situation in a kingdom when there is no king and everyone breaks the law. When a new king takes over, all the wrong doers are brought under control. Here (in the case of the pāsuram), the divine names are like the new king, the senses are the wrong doers and keeping under control is taking the wrong doers inside the prison cell and correcting their behaviour. A third meaning to this term is that the senses which were wandering in all the improper entities were made to focus on the proper (ordained) entity – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s tirumēni (physical form of [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)). This was made possible by reciting his divine names. The vyākhyātā (commentator) raises a doubt here – is this possible? And, he himself gives the explanation that this had indeed happened in śrī rāmāyaṇam, wherein it is stated that śrī rāma had the ability to conquer the sight and mind of those who laid their eyes on him (*druśta cittāya apahāriṇam*).

**kali tannaik kadakkap pāyndu** – Getting rid of all the sins (kali) which are the basis for inability to keep senses under control, with all traces. The term “tannai” would appear to give a special emphasis to kali. The reason for this is – it (kali) is the cause for [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) getting engaged with worldly matters of enjoyment. After death, it is the cause for suffering in hell. It is also the cause for jīvātmā for not only not getting engaged with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), but also showing hatred towards him and it is also the cause for believing that (physical) body is ātmā (dēhābhimānam). It is also the cause for various other wrongful acts of a jīvātmā. Another explanation given is that the term “*tannai*” denotes an enemy who was controlling me and ordaining me all along and now I have won over him and to emphasise that, this word has been used.

**kadakka** – to cross over. To what extent was it crossed over? vyākhyātā says that it was to such an extent that all contact with samsāram (materialistic realm) was cut off (making the person like a nityasūri, as only nityasūris are the ātmās who do not have any contact with samsāram). Earlier two meanings were given for kali – one as a collection of sins and another, as an enemy. Now kali is considered as time (period). For this, the meaning of crossing over is that the person is now in kruta yugam (the first yugam) and does not have any connection with kali yugam \[he is far removed from kali and hence will not be affected by it\].

**pāyndu** – How was it (kali) crossed over? Just like *tiruvadi* (hanuman) crossed the ocean to reach lankā by flying over it, without touching it any way, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) crossed the ocean of sins without touching any part of it.

“nin nāmam kaṟṟa” – the fact remains that he had got the power to oppose *yama* and his followers only because he had learned the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Why then does he state here that he had crossed over the ocean of sins on his own effort? This is because of the nature of the divine names. Despite doing so much, the divine names will stand aside and not take any credit for carrying out the tasks. This is similar to what is usually referred to by our pūrvācāryars as “*irāmadam ūttuvāraippōlē*” \[a son fought with his mother and told her that he will not stay with her anymore and went to a free shelter in the area. At nightfall, the mother knows that her son will be hungry and that he will not get any food in the shelter. She takes food for him, but instead of giving it to him directly, for fear of rejection, she hands the food to the owner of the shelter to feed her son. Hiding herself from the sight of her son, she sees her son eating the food and feels satisfied\]. Another explanation is that since the benefit of learning the divine names is his, it is not inappropriate to say that he had crossed over the ocean of sins. Moreover, we should remember that he is still in this realm (*samsāram*) only, with his body. But he feels as if he has already left this realm and gone to śrīvaikuṇṭam. That is so because of the special affection that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has for him.

**nāvalittu uḻi taruginṟōm** – “nāvalō nāval” and “aṟaiyō aṟai” are slogans raised by the victorious warriors in a war. Here āḻvār, who was earlier scared of the name *yama*, with the backing of having learned the divine names, now has the courage to call *yama* and his consort, and tell them that he is going to walk on top of their heads. *yama* is the dēvatā (demigod) who gives punishment for those who have indulged in sins in samsāram. But he is powerless in front of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names. This is similar to the situation in kiśkintā where sugrīva, earlier afraid of his elder brother vāli, now goes to his palace and calls him for a fight after befriending śrī rāma. There it was the strength of the bearer of the name (emperumān). Here it is the strength of the name itself.

**uḻitaruginṟōm** – he walks regally in front of *yama* and his dear ones, without any fear. He tells *yama* to bring the account book which he maintains (written by *yama*’s assistant, *citragupta*) for jotting the sins of samsāris so that he can tear them off, with the strength bestowed on him by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names. Instead of saying in the singular number (*taruginṟēn)*, why is [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) saying it in plural? (*taruginṟōm*). One explanation is that he grows in size as he learns the divine names and since he has become huge now, he feels that he is equivalent to many persons. Another explanation is that he teaches the importance of learning the divine names of emperumān to others and these people now leave behind their hatred for emperumān and join him. It was only one hanuman who crossed the ocean in search of [sītāp pirātti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) and waged a mini-war in lankā. But all the monkeys in his group felt that they had themselves engaged in the mini-war and hence destroyed the wonderful garden (*maduvanam*) of sugrīva out of great joy when they returned to kiśkintā. It is similar to that, here, with only [toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) walking atop *yama* and each of his followers feeling that he himself accomplished this task. It is similar to one person getting to know the king of the land and that person’s relatives enjoying that benefit.

**naman tamar talaigaḷ mīdē** – Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) has got many others to come with him, he feels that one head alone will not be enough for so many people to walk on and hence calls *yama*’s supporters too. When people in this world commit sins, they will name their children after *yama* and his assistant *citragupta* and offer salutations to them so that the real *yama* will be pleased. But now, having learned the divine names, āḻvār feels that he can very well keep his foot on their heads. However, *yama* would feel that the purpose of being given a head is to keep it at the feet of one who has learned the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). This may sound strange. Is there any pramāṇam (proof) for this?

We shall see the remaining portion of this pāsuram in the next part.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 2. Part 1

*paccaimā malaipōl mēnip pavaḷavāik kamalac cengaṇ accutā amararēṟē āyartam koḻundē ennum iccuvai tavira yānpōy indiralōgam āḷum accuvai peṟineum vēṇḍēn arangamā nagaruḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

arangamā nagaruḷānē – Oh emperumān! who is residing permanently in tiruvarangam for the sake of his servitors; paccai mā malai pōl mēni – having tirumēni (divine physical form) similar to a huge emerald mountain; pavaḷa vāi – having coral like bright, divine, lips; sem kamala kaṇ – having divine eyes similar to lotus; accutā – one who does not let go of his followers \[Oh acyuta!\]; amarar ēṟē – the controller of nityasūris; āyar tam koḻundē – the leader of cow-herds; ennum – like these \[as a figure of speech\]; iccuvai tavira – leaving aside this wonderful taste; yān – I (who takes pleasure in reciting your divine names); pōy – go far off; indira lōgam āḷum – if I have to rule over śrīvaikuṇṭam; accuvai – that enjoyment; peṟinum – even if I were to get that; vēṇḍēn – I will not like (that)

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the first pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about the purity that reciting emperumān’s divine names gave him and how it made him great (to the extent of keeping his feet on the heads of yama and his followers). In this pāsuram (the 2nd) he speaks about how great the divine names are, as a source of enjoyment. vyākhyātā (commentator) asks here as to why āḻvār had mentioned about the purity that the divine names give in the first pāsuram and how sweet they are and fit for enjoyment in the 2nd pāsuram, and not vice versa? i.e. why did he not say first that they are sweet and fit for enjoyment in the first and how they purified him in the second pāsuram? He himself gives the explanation – if a person were sick, the sickness has to be cured first and only then he can enjoy milk. In the same way, when the fear of yama is there in the mind, one cannot enjoy the sweetness of reciting the divine names. First the fear of yama has to be removed. This is the reason that āḻvār mentioned about the purity that he obtained by reciting emperumān’s divine names first and then the joy that he found, in the second. Reciting the divine names will bestow whatever benefit that a prayōjanāntaparar (one who is engaged in worldly pleasures and pursuits) desires; it will remove the hurdles in the path of a upāyāṇṭharaniśtar (one who is attempting to reach emperumān through means other than prapatti or surrendering) and purify him; it will provide the means for spending remaining days (like a pastime) for a prapannan (one who has surrendered to emperumān). This is similar to a pearl diver exchanging his pearl for rice or fruits or such lowly materials; or selling it to a merchant for a price and the merchant who knows its value offering it to the king for a higher price and the king wearing it as on ornament. Thus the same pearl can be sold for a very low value, for a high value or be an ornament adorning the king. This is the case with divine names at the hands of a prayōjanāntaparar, an upāyāntaraniśtar and a prapannan. The vyākhyātā gives another explanation for connecting the first and second pāsurams: emperumān tells āḻvār that he has recited his divine names. He would like to present a gift (śrīvaikuṇṭam) to the āḻvār. emperumān says that it is not possible for him not to give that gift to one who has recited his names and it is also not possible for the person to whom he has given the gift, to reject it. So, please accept this gift, says emperumān. āḻvār says that the joy of reciting his divine names itself is enough for him and he doesn’t want any other gift to enjoy, as a gift for this. பச்சைமா மலை போல் மேனி பவளவாய் கமலச் செங்கண் அச்சுதா அமரரேறே ஆயர்தம் கொழுந்தே என்னும் இச்சுவை தவிர யான் போய் இந்திரலோகம் ஆளும் அச்சுவை பெறினும் வேண்டேன் அரங்கமா நகருளானே.*

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) describes the physical features of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)‘s tirumēni (physical form) and says that he cannot take his eyes off even one *angam* (part of his tirumēni) and asks emperumān as to how he expects āḻvār to leave all these things and go to śrivaikuṇṭam.

**paccai mā malai pōl mēni** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s tirumēni is green in colour like a green top mountain and is also special (*mā*). It is pleasing to the eyes as well as to the heart. Since it is green in colour, it is cool (pleasing) to the eyes and heart. [cētanas (sentient entities)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) suffer from three types of bodily discomforts, collectively called as *tāPatrayam* – *ādyātmikam, ādi daivikam* and *ādi bhoudikam* – discomfort brought on by the cētan himself, brought on by God and brought on by other living beings respectively \[Example for the first type is fever, stomach ailments etc; for the second, it is cyclone, hot summer etc; for the third trouble from animals, reptiles etc\]. emperumān’s tirumēni would be an antidote to these tāpatrayam. Just as ghee solidifies on top of food when the food cools, the auspicious qualities of emperumān also come out from deep inside and settle on top (of his tirumēni) and exhibit as a green mountain.

When we attempt to reach [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) (through *bhakti yōgam*, path of meditation), we keep our five senses on his tirumēni so that these senses remain under control and do not wander as they please, allowing us to meditate in bhakti. His tirumēni is called as *subhāśrayam* (auspicious resting place) for our senses. Once we attain emperumān and the time comes to reap the fruit of our labour, it is the same tirumēni which is the object of enjoyment \[puruśārtam or fruit of enjoyment\]. This is the reason for qualifying the mountain as special (*mā malai*).

While mountain was taken as a simile for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s tirumēni, it can at best be taken as an equivalent for firmness and growth. There is nothing to enjoy in a mountain unlike emperumān’s tirumēni or the wondrous nature of his tirumēni with all auspicious qualities.

**mēni** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is not concerned with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s svarūpam (basic nature) or auspicious qualities. He sees only the tirumēni of emperumān, which is full of joy. Just as śrīviṣṇu purāṇam says “*iccāgruhītābhimatōrudēha:*” emperumān, who is full of auspicious qualities and who bears the universe through an infinitesimal part of his physical form, exhibits many forms of his physical body as he desires. Similarly, [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) says (in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-8-9) that emperumān lies like heavily laden clouds that cannot take a step further because of their heaviness and rest atop a mountain; he displays himself like a flower in a pond which we can see and admire; at the same time he is like the ocean which we cannot enjoy completely as it is so huge and expansive; and he is like the colour of the peacock’s neck. Peacock is taken as a simile here because we will never tire of seeing a peacock and in the same way we will never tire of having emperumān’s tirumēni darśan (vision).

**pavaḷavāi kamalaccengaṇ** – If [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s tirumēni is like an ocean, different divine parts of his tirumēni are like the swirling waters in that ocean. Just as we get trapped in the swirling waters, we can be trapped by different divine parts of his tirumēni. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) first describes his divine lips. The lips are red in colour like coral. Just as a coral-spread would add beauty to an emerald green mountain, his reddish lips add beauty to his green coloured physical form (*paccai mā malai pōl mēni*). Another explanation given is that sea, on a full moon day, would be very excited and in its excitement would throw up the waves repeatedly. In the same way, emperumān, like the sea, looks at his moon-like āśritars (his followers) who come to have his darśan and the waves thrown are his lips and his eyes.

**kamalaccengaṇ** – For those [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) who escaped the trap of the first swirl in the ocean (lips, as seen above) due to their long life, this swirl (the divine eyes) would certainly trap them. What the lips try to speak to, is completed by the eyes. When śrī rāma received vibhīśaṇa at the sea-side camp when he came to surrender, he felt sad that he had made him wait for a while till he was able to convince sugrīva and others to allow vibhīśaṇa inside. After hearing vibhīśaṇa, śrī rāma comforted him with his words and further looked at him softly with his eyes. Thus when words are not sufficient, it is the eyes that come to the rescue to complete the task. The divine eyes of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) bring out the auspicious qualities such as vātsalyam (motherly love), souśeelyam (simplicity) etc from his heart. While it would have been sufficient to say “kamalakkaṇ” (lotus-like eyes) why did [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) further qualify it with the word “cengaṇ”? While lotus would have been a (somewhat inadequate) simile for softness, coolness and sweet smell, how could it bring out the meanings of the auspicious qualities of emperumān such as his vātsalyam etc? This is the reason for calling it as *sem kaṇ* (cengaṇ when the two words are joined into one) \[“sem” means beautiful\].

**accutā** – unchanging. The similes taken earlier (mountain for his tirumēni, coral for his lips and lotus for his eyes) may over a period of time change their form. However when it comes to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), his divine tirumēni and its components will not change. vyākhyātā quotes from śrīviṣṇu purānam “…sadaika rūpa rūpāya” (he is always with the same form which is unchanging at all times). Another explanation given is that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is singing mangaḷāśāsanam (singing praises of emperumān) by saying that he is always unchanging. A third meaning given is that he does not let go of his followers who get trapped by his divine lips or eyes or tirumēni.

We shall enjoy the remaining portion of this pāsuram in the next part.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

## 3. Veda nul pirayam

*vēdanūl pirāyam nūṟu manisartām puguvarēlum pādiyum uṟangippōgum ninṟa ippatinaiyāṇḍu pēdai pālagan adāgum piṇi pasi mūpput tunbam ādalāl piṟavi vēṇḍēn arangaMa nagaruḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aranga mā nagar uḷāne – Oh, one who is dwelling in the town of tiruvarangam; manisartām – samsāris (those who live in this materialistic realm); vēda nūl – as per vēda śāstram (as laid out in the holy scriptures); nūṟu pirāyam puguvarēlum – though they may live for hundred years; pādiyum – half of that, i.e. 50 years; uṟangippōgum – will be spent sleeping; ninṟa ippadinaiyāṇḍu – the balance 50 years; pēdai – in the ignorant state of infancy; pālagan – in childhood state; adu āgum – (later) going after worldly pleasures in the state of youth; piṇi – being trapped by diseases \[in each of the states mentioned above\]; mūppu – being in old age; tunbam – time spent in various other sorrowful ways; ādalāl – since the entire life is being spent in such activities; piṟavi – (such lowly) birth; vēṇḍēn – I will never desire

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(introduction) After hearing from āḻvār in the previous pāsuram that he does not want paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam), emperumān tells him “since you do not want paramapadam, stay in samsāram, which is rubbing shoulders with kōyil \[in our sampradāyam, kōyil refers to śrīrangam\] and keep enjoying my names”. āḻvār tells him “ having gone deep into this aspect, I can tell you that this samsāram has so many faults that there is no time to recite your divine names. Hence I do not want this samsāram”. When emperumān asks him as to what the shortfalls are in samsāram which prevent him from reciting his names, āḻvār explains him these faults in this pāsuram. Due to such faults, āḻvār says that reciting emperumān’s divine names generates a sweetness in him. This makes him hate the samsāram despite being habituated with samsāram due to innumerable births. Let us look at the pāsuram: வேத நூல் பிராயம் நூறு மனிசர்தாம் புகுவரேலும் பாதியும் உறங்கிப்போகும் நின்றதில் பதினையாண்டு பேதை பாலகன் அதாகும் பிணி பசி மூப்புத் துன்பம் ஆதலால் பிறவி வேண்டேன் அரங்கமா நகருளானே.*

**vēda nūl pirāyam nūṟu** – As manu smriti says “*vēda śāstrā virōdi nā*”  – one who delves into vēdas (which have been approved by riśis), smritis which discuss dharma (righteousness) and delves into these using logic,  without being inimical to vēda śāstras, understands dharma; others do not.  [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) has replaced the word śāstras with “nūl” and has called it as “vēda nūl”. Another meaning for vēda nūl is it is a combination of vēdas and texts. Texts are śrīsūktis (great works) composed by learned persons  such as riśis, āḻvārs and ācāryas, who had full knowledge of vēdas.

**pirāyam nūṟu** – vēdas say “*satāyurvai puruśa:*” – the life of jīvātmās is 100 years. This is the boundary as determined by vēdas. This is the upper limit and does not mean that every jīvātmā will live upto the age of 100 years.

**manisar** – this limit of 100 years is as measured by yardstick applied to *baddātmās* (human beings bound in *leelā vibhūti* or materialistic realm) and not by dēva mānam or brahma mānam (yardstick applied to celestial persons or brahmā). Instead of 12,000 years for dēvas or kalpam (thousands of lakhs of years) for brahmā, it is 100 years for “manisar” (bhaddātmās).

**tām** – The bhaddaātmā can die any day from the day that he enters the womb, but still believes that he has a long life and makes many plans in his life for holding onto several things. Taking pity on such bhaddātmās, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says “manisar tām” \[such people\], both with a sense of wonder and sorrow.

**puguvarēlum** – if at all they live this life \[for 100 years\]. \[This is because, as mentioned earlier, there is no definitive period that has been granted to bhaddātmās ,unlike dēvas or brahmā\]. The bhaddātmā may die (i) in the womb itself, (ii) at the time of coming out of the womb, (iii) just after being born, (iv) within a few days of being born, (v) in infancy, (vi) in youth when the person goes about with arrogance, (vii) in middle or old age. Hence it is difficult to specify the longevity for bhaddātmās. This is quoted from viṣṇu purānam (*garbhē vileeyatē bhūyō jāyamānōstamēti ca*…..). People, not knowing the uncertainty of their life-span, get excited with sunrise and sunset – at sunrise they feel happy that they are going to earn by working during the day and at sunset they feel happy that they can spend their time with their families, using the money earned during the day. The same meaning was mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇa (*nandantyudita ādityē  nandantyastamitē ravov*…… – they feel happy when sun rises and they feel happy when the sun sets, but they do not understand that a day has passed \[when they haven’t thought of [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)\] in their lives). Thus, despite so many uncertainties, if they still live for 100 years by some means or the other…..

**pādiyum uṟangippōgum** – half the life is wasted in sleep. This is similar to half a farmer’s produce getting wasted through various means \[eaten by insects, rodents, swept away by breeze etc\]. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) feels saddened that half the time, which should have been spent in reciting the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), has been wasted in such sleep. lakśmaṇa had kept himself awake to carry out kainkaryam to perumāḷ (śrī rāma) and sītā. We should have been like that, says āḻvār. “*satatam kīrttayanta*….” ([śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/) 9.14) – they keep reciting my names always, says kriṣṇa about his devotees.

For the next phrase, there are two versions: one is ninṟadil padinaiyāṇḍu and the other is ninṟa ippadinaiyāṇḍu. Let us consider both.

**ninṟadil padinaiuāṇḍu** – of the remaining part of life, fifteen years are spent as infant and child (when the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is ignorant and is totally dependent on others) and another fifteen years are spent as youth, indulging in worldly pleasures \[as youth, the jīvātmā is expected to know about emperumān but chooses not to know\].

**ninṟa ippadinaiyāṇḍu** –  here it says that the remaining part of life is ten times 5 years, viz. 50 years. Instead of saying “appadinaiyāṇḍu” (with some amount of superiority), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) refers it to as “ippadinaiyāṇḍu” as he considers it being lowly, as all the planning of the jīvātmā does not appeal to him.

**pēdai** – infant days. This is a stage when the jīvātmā is not even qualified to recite the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). For its needs, good and bad, it is dependent on its mother. This stage could include a part of childhood also.

**pālagan adāgum** – *pālagan* is childhood stage when it engages in playful acts for which one cannot even get angry with the child. It is not the age when one would get angry with the child for not reciting emperumān’s divine names.

**adāgum** – it is the stage after childhood, when he becomes a youth and starts indulging in worldly pleasures. Now, he accrues knowledge but wastes it totally. It would appear that he was better off as an ignorant child, dependent on his mother, than this stage of youth when his knowledge is wasted on pleasurable ways.

**piṇi** **pasi** – disease and hunger. Due to his indulgence in wrongful activities, before his youthfulness ends, he is attacked by diseases. He tries to cure these by suitable medication. By then, he gets hungry – the hunger here is of his five senses, each of which demands its needs for satisfying.  Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said in tiruvāimoḻi 7.1.10 “*aivar tisai tisai valitteṟṟuginṟanar*” (the five senses pull me in different directions) and as [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) said in periya tirumoḻi 7.7.7 “*aivar aṟuttut tinṟida anji ninnadaindēn*” (i surrendered to you, in fear, as the five senses are prying and eating me), these five senses keep demanding appeasement. As the disease gets cured, the demands of the five sense increase. In olden days, when a person is in the throes of death, he would gift a golden idol of yama (the dēvatā for righteousness) so that he can escape death. The person who receives it would know fully well that he would come under yama’s grip, but he still takes it so that he can make money out of it and satisfy his senses. Thus, people would do anything (including hurting others to extract money, working fervently to earn money) to satisfy their senses. But this ultimately leads to illness.

**mūppu** – old age.  By now, old age settles in. This old age is a terrible thing for all. Even when he is not fit to enjoy the worldly pleasures, he still gets tempted. As [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) said in periya tirumoḻi 1.3.5, “*paṇḍu kāmarānavāṟum*” (he was a pleasure-seeker in younger days), he wants to keep talking about his deeds in satisfying the senses in younger days, and satisfy himself in his old age. Around this time, death approaches him like a bolt from the blue (*tunbam* that we are going to see next, is referred to as death).

**tunbam** – grief (death). Thus, as he keeps suffering in his old age, death sneaks in on him suddenly. This is most grievous. He loses connection with his friends, relatives, cows (wealth), properties etc. As said in viṣṇu purāṇam “*bālye krīdanasaktā:  youvanē viśyānmukhā:* I *ajñā nayantyaśaktāśca vārdakam samupastitam* īī” (he indulges in playful acts in childhood, in worldly pleasures as youth, and as he goes about his life without proper knowledge and getting to be an invalid, he is seized by grief and death). Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says

**ādalāl piṟavi vēṇḍēn** – hence I do not want birth. Just as a farmer, who goes to buy a piece of land for cultivation, tells the existing owner of the land that “this much of area is too deep; this much of area is too high; this has lot of sand; there is a stream going in between” and tries to reduce the price of the land drastically, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) tells [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) “of the 100 years that you have given me, I spend 50 in sleep, balance 50 in infancy, childhood, youthful state, disease, hunger, old age etc; hence how can I recite your divine names? Hence I do not want birth”. emperumān tells āḻvār, “you said in the first pāsuram that you do not want śrīvaikuṇṭam and in the second pāsuram, you do not want samsāram. I have only two vibhūtis (properties- nitya vibhūti or śrīvaikuṇṭam and leelā vibhūti or samsāram). Where do I keep you?” āḻvār responds

**aranga mā nagar uḷānē** – outside the two vibhūtis, is it so difficult to find a place for me in the third vibhūti , kōyil? (in our sampradāyam, kōyil refers to śrīrangam). [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/), “would not the issues that you had mentioned earlier with infancy, childhood, youthful state etc not trouble you in śrīrangam?” āḻvār says “even in infancy, for *annaprāsanam* (first time that solid food is given to the infant), elders will take the infant to kōyil; as child, he will join with other children and follow the procession of selvar (processional idol of emperumān); as youth, he will go to the temple looking for his lady-love; thus, the child is always connected with the beauty of emperumān without any break. This will purify the person and remove his attachment to worldly pleasures”. How about old age? He cannot walk to the temple for having darśan of emperumān. When this happens, emperumān (utsavar) comes out of the temple and gives darśan to the old person and keeps the connection strong. Even in dreams, it will be emperumān’s form which will be seen. As mentioned in gāruda purāṇam about śrīrangam “*pavitram paramam puṇyam dēśōyam sarva kāMa duk*” – this divyadēśam (holy place) is sacred, will lead the dwellers to a high status \[giving them śrīvaikuṇṭam\] and will grant all their noble wishes.

Hence this divyadēśam (śrīrangam) possesses all the special features that the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) desires is the summary for the third pāsuram.

We shall next move on to the 4<sup>th</sup> pāsuram “*moytta valvinaiyuḷ*”.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

pramēyam (goal) – [https://koyil.org](https://koyil.org/)  
pramāṇam (scriptures) – [http://grantams.koyil.org](http://granthams.koyil.org/)  
pramātā (preceptors) – [http://acaryas.koyil.org](http://acharyas.koyil.org/)  
śrīvaiṣṇava education/kids portal – [http://pillai.koyil.org](https://pillai.koyil.org/)

## 4. Moytta val vinaiyul

*moytta valvinaiyuḷ ninṟu mūnṟeḻuttu udaiya pērāl kattirabandum anṟē parāngati kaṇḍu koṇḍān īttanai adiyar ānārkku irangum nam aranganāya pittanaip peṟṟum andō! piṟaviyuḷ piṇangumāṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

moytta – surrounding fully; valvinaiyuḷ ninṟu – standing in the ocean of grave sins; mūnṟeḻuttu udaiya pērāl – due to the divine name of “gōvinda” with three syllables; kattirabandum anṟē – even kśatrabandu; parāngati – high status of paramapadam; kaṇḍu koṇḍān – had the experience of enjoying; ittanai adiyar ānārkku – for such agreeable people; irangum – having pity and showering grace; nam arangan āya pittanai – our aḻagiya maṇavāḷan (śrirangam utsavap perumāḷ) who has deep affection for his followers; peṟṟum – even after having him as svāmy (master); piṟaviyuḷ – getting caught in repeated births; piṇangum āṟē – the way we despair; andō – Oh! \[how sad it is!\]

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the first three pāsurams (hymns) āḻvār expressed the experience that he had in reciting emperumān’s divine names (how they purify and how they are enjoyable). Starting from this (4th) pāsuram till the 14th pāsuram, he preaches the benefits of reciting his divine names to samsāris (those bound souls who live in leelā vibhūti or materialistic realm). What is the reason for preaching instead of enjoying the divine names himself? Three reasons are cited by the vyākhyātā (commentator): (1) This is because it is very simple to recite and attainable by samsāris; yet they are not doing so and as a result are losing a great deal; āḻvār is unable to bear this sorrow. (2) In this significant matter (of reciting divine names), it is not possible to enjoy alone and it is pleasing to emperumān only when many people join in this. (3) “ēka: svādu na bhunjīta” is a saying, which means do not eat alone, quoted from bhāratam (the full slōka says, do not eat alone, do not contemplate on a noble issue alone, do not walk through the forest in the night alone and when everyone else is sleeping do not be awake, alone). Hence āḻvār, not satisfied with his own experience, wants all samsāris to have similar experience as that is his svabhāvam (basic nature). Hence he is preaching from this 4th pāsuram. Just when he started talking about the benefits of reciting emperumān’s divine names, samsāris asked him, “are sinners like ourselves qualified to recite his divine names?” āḻvār responded saying “even a person like kśatrabhandu, who had no means for atoning for all his sins, got through to paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) because he recited the divine name. Hence do not try to slip away saying that you are not qualified” But despite hearing this assurance, the samsāris did not recite emperumān’s divine names. āḻvar is surprised that even after hearing him, these samsāris continue to indulge in activities which sink them deeper into the quagmire of repeated births, instead of getting out of it by reciting the divine names. Let us enjoy the pāsuram now: மொய்த்த வல்வினையுள் நின்று மூன்றெழுத்து உடைய பேரால் கத்திரபந்தும் அன்றே பராங்கதி கண்டு கொண்டான் இத்தனை அடியார் அனார்க்கு இறங்கும் நம் அரங்கனாய பித்தனைப் பெற்றும் அந்தோ! பிறவியில் பிணங்குமாறே*

**moytta** – sins keep falling over him shouting “I am the first” “I am the first” just like bees would surround a bee-hive or ants would surround a pot containing ghee ([periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/)’s pāsuram (5-2-1) is quoted here “*neykkudattaip paṟṟiyēṟum eṟumbugaḷ*”) or when the army of monkeys landed on the shores of lankā cried out “it is for me” out of sheer expectation of winning. In the same way, sins are surrounding the jīvātmās completely. It would appear as if the jīvātmās have the same power to create sins as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has to create the worlds.

**valvinai** – How grave are these sins? To atone for each of these sins, a jīvātmā has to spend the entire duration of kāla (time, the third among acit’s three components). As kūrattāḻvān said in śrivaikuṇṭa stavam “*yat kalpaniyutānubhavēpyanāśyam tat kilbiśam srujati janturiha kśaṇārtē*” – the jīvātma commits in a moment a sin which cannot be rid of even in thousand kalpas (one kalpa is the longevity of brahmā, composed of many lakhs of years). The gravity of these sins is such that only emperumān can remove these out of his mercy.

**uḷ ninṟu** – standing inside. Just as a silkworm spins a web and gets caught within its web and just as a fly gets tossed about in the middle of an ocean, this jīvātmā, who is of a size less than an atom, gets caught right in the middle of ocean of sins. As [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi (4-7-1) “*siṟiyanēlum seivinaiyō peridāl*” – even if I am small, the sins that I commit are huge.

**ninṟu** – standing amidst the sins. Taking root in a bed of burning wood/charcoal, the jīvātmā is right amidst his sins.

**mūnṟeḻuttudaiya pērāl** – with a divine name having three syllables. Will the antidote for sins depend on the time over which the sins were committed or the dimensions (spread) of the sins? No, just a word with three syllables will do for this, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). Even one syllable in the word is sufficient to remove all the sins. Unlike praṇavam (Om) which when split into its individual letters (a, u and ma) gives meanings for each of those letters, the name “gōvinda” which is referred to here as the three syllable word, will not convey any meaning when the three syllables are split. Still, each of these three syllables “gō”, “vi” and “da” will elevate the person to mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam). There are words which are 10,000 letters and 1000 letters long. When one starts reciting those words, one will start wondering as to when they will end. Unlike those names, this three syllable name “gōvinda” is easy to recite.

**pērāl** – instead of saying “mantram” (this is a word which is recited during vēdic rituals) why is [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) saying “pēr” (name)? Reciting mantram” requires certain qualifications such as “riśi, candas, dēvatā, śakti, bījam, varṇam, viniyōgam, dyānam” (these are specific to each mantram and are to be recited first before the mantram is recited) whereas reciting a name does not require any such qualification. Only certain sections of the samsāris are autorised to recite mantrams. Anyone, from any of the four varṇams, including women and children, can recite [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine names. When a person trips while walking (s)he will cry out “ammā” irrespective of varṇam. Reciting emperumān’s divine name is also like that only, when faced with any hurdle.  Instead of revealing which three  syllable name it is, why is he hiding it? āḻvār says that only if it is hidden, the person who hears it will start wondering what word it is and once he has the curiosity and comes asking for it, he will tell the person all about it and get him interested in it.

**kattirabandum** – a lowly brāhmaṇa is referred to as *brahmabandu (*a mere relative of a brāhmanṇa*)*. In the same way, a lowly kśatriya person is referred to as *kśatriyabandu*. The purpose of adding ‘um’ (kattiraband*um*) at the end is to indicate that he is the limit for lowliness.

**anṟē** – even. In a gathering of our pūrvācāryars (preceptors) when someone mentioned the name of “nālūrān” everyone will close his ears \[nālūrān was the person responsible for [periya nambi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periya-nambi/), the ācāryar of [bhagavat śrī rāmānujar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/), and [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kurathazhwan/) losing their vision, as well as for rāmānujar to leave śrīrangam for 12 years\]. In the same way in a gathering of riśis, if someone mentions the name of khsatrabandu, everyone will close his ears. Such was his reputation. \[He was a robber, ostracised by his own relatives and had a bad conduct and mind. One day, an aged riśi was going through a forest and he was waylaid by kśatrabandu. But the riśi was in poor health and swooned. Due to the causeless mercy of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), kśatrabandu revived the riśi. On seeing kśatrabandu’s condition, the riśi took pity on him and taught him the divine name “gōvinda”. That became a turning point in the life of kśatrabandu and he set right his life and reached śrīvaikuṇṭam. This is mentioned in viṣṇu darmam\].

**parāngati kaṇḍukoṇḍān** – reached exalted position. While getting rid of sin itself would have been a reward for reciting [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine name, kśatrabandu went beyond svarga (heaven) experience, beyond the greatest treasures and kaivalyam (ātmā getting detached from all other connections including emperumān and enjoying self) and getting the status of those who do not have any connection with samsāram. In other words, the lowliest of samsāris, kśatrabandu, got the status of nityasūris who are permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam, enjoying emperumān constantly.

**kaṇḍukoṇḍān** – it is as if he had got back what he had lost earlier. It is not as if he got something for reciting the divine names, but that he got what was always his. Just as viṣṇu darmam says “*prakāśyantē na janyantē*…….” – just as a gem will shine brightly when the dirt covering it is removed, the ātmā will gain its true stature of being *jñānamayan* (full of knowledge) when its *dōśas* (faults) are removed. When a well is dug, and water springs out at a particular depth, it is the water which was always there that came out and not a new source of water that was created. Thus when bad qualities are removed, ātmā gets back its svarūpam (its basic nature and good qualities). This is the case with kśatrabandu too when he got elevated to śrīvaikuṇṭam on reciting divine names.

**ittanai adiyar ānārkku** – be dedicated to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) at least to the extent of kśatrabandu. It is enough if one is as favourably disposed as kśatrabandu. Another explanation is – accept that one is a servitor and emperumān is the master (to be served) and not show hatred towards him. When someone says as in taittiriya upaniśat that “*patim viśvasya* ….” (it is only śrīman nārāyaṇan who is the lord of the worlds, controlled only by himself and by no one else, always permanent, auspicious and does not fail his servitors) or says as in yajur brāhmaṇam  “*yasyāsmi* …..” (I will not go away from someone to whom I am a servitor), if someone does not get shocked, it is enough to qualify for emperumān’s grace. Is this enough to qualify for being a follower? Yes, it is, as per emperumān’s opinion, says the vyākhyātā, quoting from sātvatam samhitā “*durācārō api sarvāśī…..nirdōśam viddim tam jantum prabhāvāt paramātmana:*” which means: even if a person who had lowly conduct for a long time, had been eating whatever he wanted, was  being ungrateful and was atheist, surrenders to emperumān with dedication, know that emperumān forgives him of his faults due to his (emperumān’s) greatness.

*“ittanai* “ – even this small effort (of reciting [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine name) appears as a mēru parvatam (a huge mountain) for emperumān to bear. vyākyātā further quotes from īśāvāsya upaniśat  “…..*bhūyiśtām tē nama uktim vidēma*” – we shall keep reciting “nama:” a word which emperumān is pleased to hear and which he thinks is a great deed.

**irangum** – having pity and showering grace.  Due to the natural connection with [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) expects him not to show hatred towards him and to show involvement with him. All that the jīvātmā shows is not shunning him (not showing hatred). But what he gets is emperumān himself. How will such a small hold give rise to such a huge benefit? Just as a cow shows tremendous love and affection towards its calf on the day that it gave birth to, emperumān also shows his affection and hence has pity, because of the relationship that he has with the jīvātmā and not because of any benefit that the jīvātmā has with him. Despite giving him śrīvaikuṇṭam itself, emperumān feels that he has not done anything for the jīvātmā. “*un adiyārkku en seyvan enṟē  irutti nī*” said [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in periya tiruvandādi  (you keep thinking about “what else can I do for my follower”).

**nam aranganāya** – our arangan; [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is dwelling in the śrīrangam temple, for the sake of [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s like us who have nothing in our hands. paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) is for nityātmās (nityasūris) and muktātmās (those who were earlier in samsāram but had since got liberated from it). tiruppāṟkadal (kśīrābdi) is for people who are almost like muktātmās (sanaka, sanatkumāra et al as well as brahmā and other celestials). Incarnations are for those who were fortunate to live at that time. Only in temples does he show himself to people who were not fortunate to be around during the time of incarnation. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) uses the term ‘nam” in nam aranganāya to confirm that we are of the same clan as kśatrabandu and there is very little to choose between him and us.

**pittanai** – He ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) does not know as to how the others were earlier. Also, he is one who does not know himself. After reaching śrīrangam, emperumān had forgotten about how great he was earlier (paratvam) and has become simplicity personified. He does not know about his magnanimity when he gives, the lowliness of the people who receive from him and what he gives them (śrīvaikuṇṭam itself). Hence [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) refers to him as “pittan” (mad, ignorant).

**peṟṟum** – Normally if a person is wanting in something, he will go to someone who has it and ask him for it. But here, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who has everything to give, has come to śrīrangam (from śrīvaikuṇṭam) and is asking [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s “is there anyone here who wants me?”.

**piṟaviyuḷ piṇangumāṟē** – even if people want to move away from one object to another, the second should be at least as good, if not better than the first. But in this case, what they are moving away from is a treasure and what they are going after is lowly matter to satisfy their sensual desires. In sheer disgust, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says, “*andō*”. Just like two vultures fight over a carcass, the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s are fighting over lowly materials. While jīvātmās are eligible to adorn the tirumēni (physical form) of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as “*śrī koustuba hāram*” (the garland of gems adorning emperumān) they are going after sensual objects and keep getting in the quagmire of repeated births. Just as righteous people will cry out “murder” when they see terrible things happening in front of their eyes, āḻvār is also crying out loud “*andō*” on seeing jīvātmās getting into samsāram repeatedly.

Let us move on to the 5th pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 5. Pendiral sugangal uyppan

*peṇḍirāl – trough vomen sugangaḷ – all types of comforts / pleasures uyppān – tinking tat he is enjoying periyadu ōr idumbai – very huge problems pūṇḍu – taking on oneself irā uṇḍu – eating in te night kidakkumbōdu – vhen lying on te bed udalukkē karaindu – vorrying only about protecting te body naindu – getting troubled in te mind taṇ tuḻāy mārban – sarvēśvaran (emperumān) vho is adorning te cool, tuḷasi (basil) garland tamargaḷ āy – as his follovers pādi – singing (about his auspicious qualities and divine names) ādi – (hence not remaining in te same place) dancing about toṇḍu pūṇḍu – becoming a servitor (to emperumān) amudam uṇṇā – not eating te nectar (of enjoying emperumān’s qualities) toḻumbar – lovly persons sōṟu ugakkumāṟē – hov do tey reliś food?!*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār  was disgusted with the samsāris for being caught in repeated births by focussing on lowly pleasures and not bothering about the supreme emperumān even though he is waiting to help samsāris. vyākhyātā (commentator) creates an antagonist in the form of lōkāyatan (one who thinks that life is there to be lived only in pleasurable ways and not for bothering about matters which are not seen (ātmā, emperumān et al)). The purpose of creating this lōkāyatan is to describe the background for āḻvār composing this pāsuram. On hearing the āḻvār lamenting about the samsāris  getting deeper into the quagmire of repeated births by engaging in lowly activities, lōkāyatan asks the āḻvār “what is the issue with worldly matters? Why do you talk ill of samsāram which is full of sixteen year old damsels who give so much pleasure? That there is something which is different from and superior to the body is false. That there are both darmam (righteousness) and adarmam (wrongful deeds) is false. That there is another world above is also false. What we see here with our eyes (the body) and what we enjoy with this body alone are true. It is not true that there is something to be experienced after this body falls”. āḻvār replies “whatever you enjoy with this body is too lowly and very transient. Even this is very difficult to attain. On the other hand, emperumān is readily available to us and we can attain him without difficulty”. Let us look at the pāsuram: பெண்டிரால் சுகங்களுயப்பன் பெரியதோர் இடும்பை பூண்டு உண்டிராக் கிடக்கும்போது உடலுக்கே கரைந்து நைந்து தண்டுழாய் மாலை மார்பன் தமர்களாய்ப் பாடி யாடி தொண்டு பூண்டமுதம் உண்ணாத் தொழும்பர் சோறு உகக்குமாறே. peṇḍirāl sugangaḷuyppān periyadōr idumbai pūṇḍu uṇḍirāk kidakkumpōdu udalukkē karaindu naindu taṇḍuḻāi mālai mārban tamargaḷāyp pādiyādit toṇḍu pūṇḍamudam uṇṇāt toḻumbar sōṟu ugaggumāṟē Meanings of Words*

**peṇḍirāl sugangaḷ uyppān** – one can enjoy all comforts with the help of women \[if the reader is a woman, the sentence will change to “one can enjoy all comforts with the help of men”\]. This seems to be the deep understanding of samsāris, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). This is similar to a person hugging fire in order to cool himself or taking rest under the expanded hood of a poisonous snake or a person eating poison in order to live forever. Without realising that it is the reason for all sorrows, samsāris think that it is the reason for all pleasures. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 9.1.1 states  “*koṇḍa peṇḍir suṟṟamellām*” meaning that wife will ask of her husband to get this and that and make him run around to earn money and obtain things for her which are very difficult to get. Her love and affection for him will last only until his health and his wealth last. This is samsāram. This is the case when the word peṇḍir refers to one’s wife. But if it is taken to mean a woman who sells herself for money, his problems become more acute. Since she sells her body for money, she will be willing to have him with her only until he has money. He cannot enjoy her forever. Moreover if someone sees him with her, it will be a matter of shame for him. Even if he escapes that, he will surely rot in hell. This is the kind of comfort that he can enjoy with the help of women. It is as if in his estimate, she is the ultimate source of happiness. The reason for using the plural form for sukam (sugangaḷ) is because the man thinks that the wife will deliver all comforts to him – the day she enters his house, she will bring him wealth; once she attains the state of bearing him children, she will give him physical pleasures and later on bring forth his children etc.

**uyppān** – samsāri keeps dreaming about comforts but in reality there is nothing comfortable in any of what she has to offer. This is so, because these so called comforts are so transient in nature that he will not be able to recall the comfort that he had enjoyed in such lowly pleasures. He (the samsāri) goes behind the damsels out of lust but they would not even bother about him and even put him to shame when they find that he does not have wealth any more. Their putting him to shame makes him long for them more and increases his lust towards them.

**periyadōr idumbai pūṇḍu** – pūṇḍu means to wear (like adorning oneself with jewels or garland etc). But here, the samsāri wears a huge sorrow (idumbai is sorrow). Just as a person wears the neck of a pot (which cannot be removed) around his neck, he wears sorrow around his neck. Thinking that he is going after unbounded pleasure, he ends up getting unbounded sorrow. While [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is ready to give even śrīvaikuṇṭam, expecting only an anjali (folded hands) from us \[meaning  “I don’t have anything in me to get to your divine feet; only you have to take care of me and give me the opportunity to carry out kainkaryam to your divine feet”\]. But the samsāri does anjali to those who cannot grant him anything. kaṇṇan says in [śrī bhagavad gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/) 9.2 “*susukham karttum avyayam*” (*bhakti mārga*, the path of devotion, is the one which will enable you to reach me and this path is comfortable to do; even after giving you the benefit, it will not be destroyed). In our regular day-to-day routine, when we work (to earn money) it is hard work. It is not comfortable. The comfort comes at the end of the work when we get paid for it and enjoy with it \[in his opinion\]. But when it comes to emperumān, whether it is doing bhakti (devotion) or śaraṇāgati (surrendering), the act of doing it is comfortable and the result obtained is also comfortable. Instead of this, if a person goes after worldly pleasures, it is only sorrow both during the time of putting in the effort and during the time of enjoyment.

**uṇḍirā** – \[irā uṇḍu\] eating at night. During daytime he works tirelessly earning money, without even having the time to eat, and eats only after sunset.  First he eats to satisfy the hunger in his stomach and then only he can even think of going after worldly pleasures.

**kidakkumpōdu** – After that, he looks for a place to rest. Having worked during the day, his body begs him to give it some rest. At this point of time, he does not have the energy even to think of worldly pleasures, let alone engaging in such activities.

**udalukkē karaindu naindu** – Even when he goes to sleep, he is worried about how he is going to earn money the next day for meeting his family’s and his own needs. The reason for using the term ”*udalukkē*” is that he does not even think that there is another entity ([ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)) which is superior to the body  and that he has to worry about satisfying its needs too. He does not even bother about how to reach the *puruśārtam* (ultimate benefit) of śrīvaikuṇṭam. Nor does he worry about avoiding *naraka* (hell) and returning to samsāram with another physical form. His only focus is on satisfying the body. As [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 9-6-2 “*ninaidoṟum sollundoṟum nenju idindugum*” (whenever I think of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) or speak about him, my heart melts), for the samsāri it is always looking after his bodily needs.

**taṇ tuḻāy mālai mārban tarmagalāyp pādi ādi** – The samsāri is fit to go to śrīvaikuṇṭam as muktātmā and recite sāma vēda hymn “*aham annam aham annam*” – the muktātmā tells emperumān that he is the food to be enjoyed by emperumān and once emperumān eats him and feels happy, the muktātmā eats the happiness of emperumān. He feels proud of being the food for emperumān and in this exalted state sings and dances with other nityasūris. When he is qualified to enjoy this, it is indeed sad that he is still in samsāram, suffering all the ignominies.

**taṇ tuḻāy mālai mārban** – he wears the beautiful tuḷasi garland to hint that he is sarvēśvaran \[Lord of all\]. He is waiting to protect us; all we need to do is to tell him that “I have nothing in my hands to protect myself and I have nowhere else to go” \[in our sampradāyam, ākincanyam (I have nothing) and ananya gatitvam (I have nowhere else to go) are prerequisites to surrender to emerpumān\]. Unlike those lowly people \[who give worldly pleasures\] demanding money and other things, emperumān asks for nothing. But still we are not ready to go to him. Even if he is not going to protect us, the very image of emperumān with the tuḷasi garland will give happiness to those who reach him.

**tamargaḷāy** – as servitors. There is nothing great that the samsāri needs to do to be his servitor. It is enough if he accepts his svarūpam (his basic nature) and the svābhāvika (natural) relationship with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) – that he is the servitor and emperumān is the svāmy (master). When emperumān wants to do something good, all that is required of the samsāri is not to object to it. vyākhyātā quotes three faultless pramāṇams (proofs) to establish the relationship between emperumān and samsāri –(a) taittiriya upaniśat “*patim viśvasya*” (he is the svāmy or master of all), (b) yajur brāhmaṇam “*yasyāsmi*” (I will not go away from the one to whom I am the servitor) and (c) śrī rāmāyaṇam“*dāsōham*” (I am the servitor of kōsala rāja, śrī rāma). “tamar” (being a servitor of [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)) is the natural state for samsāri whereas being servitor to women is an assumed state.

**pādiyādi** – making merry by singing and dancing due to the experience of enjoying [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). In sundara kāṇḍam of śrī rāmāyaṇam, when the troop of monkeys (returning after sighting śitāp pirātti in lankā) went about destroying the *maduvanam* of sugrīva, they were in such a merry-making state of mind. Some monkeys were singing, some were prostrating, some were dancing, some were jumping from one tree to another, some were falling down – the samsāri can engage in such activities as he is the servitor of emperumān. When he goes to śrīvaikuṇṭam, he can sing sāma gānam (*hā vuhā vuhā vu*….) in ecstasy.

**toṇḍu pūṇḍu** – donning the role of servitor – what is specified for the samsāri. It should be taken up with joy and not as a duty. As we had seen earlier, the word “pūṇḍu” means adorning oneself with. śri ranganātan wears cēra sundara pāṇḍiyan (a pendant) as an ornament on his chest. In the same way, the samsāri dons the role of being a servitor as an ornament on his chest. Unlike “idumbai pūṇḍu” (we had seen the meaning of this term a few paras back), toṇḍu pūṇḍu is not something enforced. It is taken up willingly. This is more like lakśmaṇa saying “*aham sarvam kariśyāmi*”  (I will carry out all services) and nammāḻvār saying in [tiruvāimoḻi 3.3.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/08/thiruvaimozhi-3-3-1-ozhivil-kalam/), “*vaḻuvilā adimai seyya vēṇḍum nām*” (we should carry out all service without missing anything).

**amudam uṇṇā** – The food referred to here is the service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the happiness that emperumān has out of eating this food (service) and the resultant happiness that muktātmā gets after seeing the brightened, happy face of emperumān. This is what is ordained for us and what is permanent for us, instead of the lowly pleasure that we get out of worldly pursuits. [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) (one of our important preceptors) said in his stōtra ratnam that he would rather be born as a worm in the hut of emperumān’s servitor and eat whatever is available there than being born as brahmā in the palace of a non-servitor. [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kurathazhwan/) ([śrī rāmānuja](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/emperumanar/)’s disciple) said in his varadarāja stavam that he would rather enjoy the elixir of being emperumān’s servitor than the pleasures of samsāram or the positions of brahmā, rudra et al (which would be like sea-water (salty) to him).

**toḻumbar** – lowly person. Instead of enjoying the service rendered to emperumān, the samsāri runs after lowly, not enjoyable, not ordained worldly pleasures. It is not that the samsāri has lost out on being [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s servitor because he is not qualified for it; or because it is very difficult to reach emperumān; or because he does not know the difference between enjoyment of emperumān and that of worldly pleasures. Knowing everything, he desires for the lowly pleasures. Hence the term “toḻumbar”

**sōṟu ugakkumāṟē** – he enjoys cooked food knowing that it is superior to sand. But why does he not know that for the ātmā the food is carrying out service to emperumān? Is it because he has seen his parents eating food and hence he too is eating it? If he has knowledge in one (difference between sand and food) why does he not have knowledge in the other (between food for body and food for ātmā)? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) wonders as to how the samsāri enjoys this food.

The summary of this pāsuram is that were the samsāri capable of delving deeper into his intelligence, would he not have found out what is beneficial for the ātmā? If he had the knowledge in one aspect, would he not have the same knowledge in another aspect too?

We shall move on to the 6<sup>th</sup> pāsuram in the next article.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 6. Maram suvar

*maṟam suvar – vall of cruelty as nature \[speaking or acting in a cruel vay\] madiḷ eduttu – raise as protective vall maṟumaikkē – for benefits in te oter vorld veṟyumai pūṇḍu – take on poverty puṟam suvar – as outside vall ōttai – to be destroyed mādam – tis \[physical\] body puraḷumbōdu – te time vhen te body falls on to te ground aṟiya māttīr – you vill not knov aṟam suvar āgi ninṟa – one vho is standing vit darmam (righteousness) as vall aranganārkku – to śrī ranganātan āḷ seyyādē – instead of being a servitor puṟam suvar – tis body vhic is like te outer vall kōlam seydu – decorate tis body puḷ kavva – being pecked by vultures kidakkinṟīṟē – lying dovn, vasted*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**maṟam suvar  madiḷeduttu –  samsāris construct a protective wall with their cruel thoughts, cruel words and cruel action. Cruelty is the only “auspicious” quality that they have, it appears to āḻvār.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction samsāris told āḻvār that even though sensual pleasures have faults such as being lowly and being transient, we will not give them up. Even if we cannot enjoy total satisfaction in any one sensual pleasure, since there are so many of them we can get satisfaction in one or other of these pleasures and what we missed in one, we shall make up in the other. āḻvar responded saying that for enjoying these pleasures, the person who enjoys (samsāri) should be permanent. But even he has limited life and he is as short-lived as these sensual pleasures are. He goes on to paint a picture of the samsāri’s transient state in this pāsuram. First let us enjoy the pāsuram: மரம் சுவர் மதிள் எடுத்து மறுமைக்கே வெறுமை பூண்டு புறம் சுவர் ஓட்டை மாடம் புரளும்போது அறிய மாட்டீர் அறம் சுவராகி நின்ற அரங்கனார்க்கு ஆட்செய்யாதே புறம் சுவர் கோலம் செய்து புள் கவ்வக் கிடக்கின்றீரே maṟam suvar madiḷ eduttu maṟumaikkē veṟumai pūṇḍu puṟam suvar ōttai mādam puraḷumbōdu aṟiya māttīr aṟam suvarāgi ninṟa aranganārkku ātseyyādē puṟam suvar kōlam seydu puḷ kavvak kidakkinṟīṟē*

**maṟam** – without mercy; being angry; being proud are the meanings of the word *maṟam*. If good people look at a cruel person and think, “Oh! Why he is always so angry? He is inviting problems” and want to correct him, they cannot even approach him because of his deep anger which he has spread out as a protective wall around himself. When mārīcan, mālyavān, kumbhakarṇan and śrī vibhīśaṇāḻvān tried to counsel rāvaṇan he was not willing to listen to them. As a result, some of them, like mārīcan and kumbhakarṇan died with him while others, such as śrī vibhīśaṇāḻvān were driven out of the country. This is what happens when someone tries to advise the lowly person.

**madiḷ eduttu** – construct a wall. This means that it is not there as a natural behaviour. Since it has been constructed by the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) around himself, it also means that one day, when [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) decides to take the samsāri under his wings, the wall will come down in pieces. The next word describes the result of his building the wall…..

**maṟumaikkē veṟumai pūṇḍu** – The samsāri’s action \[borne out of cruel thoughts, speech and actions\] in this birth leads to an abject state as far as śrīvaikuṇṭam is concerned. Along with providing whatever the body wanted, had he thought about providing food for ātmā also, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) would have given him all the protection, thinking of the samsāri’s commitment to improve himself. Just as varāha perumān said, “…*dātu sāmyē stitē smartā*…” (when the [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is having his senses in balance and thinks of me for protecting him) emperumān would have provided him with all benefits including *mōkśam* (liberation from samsāram) as varāha perumān said further in the same ślōka, “…*aham smarāmi*…” (I shall think of him when he is in his final stage like a stone or a log of wood and take him to śrīvaikuṇṭam). On the other hand, if he remains as a cruel person, when he dies, there will not be a single person who would look at him with comforting eyes. If a person lives physically in poverty in this world, the poverty will end on the day the body ends. But if his ātmā lives in poverty, there is no end to that poverty and it will become permanent.

**puṟam suvar ōttai mādam puraḷumbōdu aṟiyamāttīr** – *puṟam suvar* refers to the physical body. It should have been used for good deeds, as desired by emperumān, in going towards him. Instead the samsāri uses it for indulging in worldly pleasures. Hence it is referred to us *puṟam* (outside) *suvar* (wall).  But the same body would very much become likeable if it were used for doing something good for [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (doing *kainkaryam* for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and becoming his servitor).

**ōttai mādam** – “It fell on this day” “It fell on that day” is what people say about this body. It is that transient, so fleeting it is in its existence. Another meaning for ōttai mādam – having a roof and lock; hence called as house. “mādam”  is a big house. It is thought to be big, in samsāri’s opinion. samsāris told [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) that he is telling them only now about the problems with this body and what they should do. We shall follow your advice in due course of time, they said. His response was……

**puraḷumbōdu aṟiyamāttīr** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says “you do not know as to how long you will live”. He says “the messenger of death is close at hand. Can you afford to delay doing good for the ātmā under these circumstances?  All along, you were engaged in worldly pleasures and forgot to do any good for the ātmā. Now on the throes of death, you do not have the strength to search for the good deeds. By saying that you would do good things in due course of time, you are only going to destroy everything for yourself”

**aṟam suvarāgi ninṟa** – the one who is standing with righteousness as wall ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)). vyākhyātā quotes from several parmāṇams (authentic proofs) to state that emperumān is darmam (righteousness). He says that it is natural for emperumān to be darmam. First he quotes from śrī rāmāyaṇam “*lōkānām tvam parō dharma:*” (you are the superior darmam for all the people in the world). Then he quotes again from śri rāmāyaṇam “*rāmo vigrahavān dharma:*” (rāma is darmam personified). He quotes from bhāratam āraṇya kāṇḍam “*kriṣṇam darmam sanātanam*” (kriṣṇa is darmam from time immemorial) and finally from śrīrangarāja stavam “*upāyōpēyatvē tadiha tattvam na tu guṇou*” (providing both the means of attaining you and enjoying your kainkaryam are natural to you)

**āgi ninṟa** – unlike other upāyams (paths or means for attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) where the samsāri has to take efforts \[referred to as sādyōpāyam in our sampradāyam\], emperumān is ready to be attained through himself \[this is called as siddōpāyam in our sampradāyam\]. Unlike the other upāyams which will look up to him for providing benefits, this siddōpāyam does not look at anything or anybody else for providing benefit \[granting śrīvaikuṇṭam\]. Is there anything that the samsāri has to do for this, asks the vyākyātā and says that taking shelter under this wall and not rejecting him is the only expectation from him.  As it says in mahābhāratam “*prakārassarva vruśīnām*…” [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) provides shelter to all those who accept him as their protector.

**ninṟa** – sumantran said in śrī rāmāyaṇam “*āśayā yadi vā rāma:….*” (expecting that śrī rāmā will call me I was waiting along with guhan for several days, said sumantran). In the same way, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took position at the banks of kāvēri waiting for samsāris to take refuge under him.

**aranganārkku** – There is no shortcoming that the object to be attained is too far away. Asking “who will utilise my presence and attain me” lies [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in śrīrangam, saying that he will stay here till he grants mōkśam to all samsāris.

**āḷ seyyādē** – the samsāri need not even carry out surrendering. It is enough if he involves in carrying out kainkaryam (service). His basic nature is being a servitor to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/); this is the benefit that has been mentioned in śāstras. This is precisely the wealth that he had lost from time immemorial. When he is a samsāri he  is a servitor to his karma (deeds). Once he becomes the recipient of emperumān’s causeless mercy he becomes a servitor to emperumān. He is a servitor to karmas due to past deeds in innumerable births. He did not become a servitor to emperumān due to these sins. Another meaning given for *āḷ seyyādē*  is that it is like staying without food.

**puṟam suvar kōlam seydu** – decorating only the physical body and not the ātmā. Can ātmā be decorated? vyākyātā quotes from taittiriya upaniśat “*asannēva sa bhavati*….” (a person exists only if he knows that there is brahmam (superior power)  and does not exist if he does not know of brahman). Hence realising this ātmasvarūpam (knowledge of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) and carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān is the decoration for ātmā. Instead of doing that, the samsāri keeps decorating the body which is incidental and not a permanent feature. In the olden days, palm leaf manuscripts were protected on both sides with wooden planks and tied with a string. The vyākhyātā says that decorating the body and leaving the ātmā to rot is like the manuscripts being eaten away by termites and rodents and the wooden planks and string being decorated with flower, sandalwood paste etc.

**puḷ kavva** – being bitten by bird. Even birds (like vulture) which eat carcasses take a bite of the samsāri’s body and refrain from eating it further because the birds know that this is the carcass of a person who was treacherous. \[the act of treachery here is that the samsāri, who was granted the body and the senses, instead of submitting them to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) through kainkaryam, indulged in worldly pleasures\]

**kidakkinṟīṟē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) mocks at samsāris saying that while emperumān was waiting nearby (at śrīrangam) to provide them sustenance, the samsāris went their own ways so much that even vultures are not interested in their carcasses. He is saddened because of the difficulties that the samsāri encounters in the samsāram by not following the correct path. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), in the decad “kōvai vāyāḷ” (tiruvāimoḻi 4.3) talks about his body being the sandalwood paste, garland and vastram (divine dress) for emperumān. A similar body of the samsāri is being pecked by birds instead of being offered to emperumān through kainkaryam, feels the āḻvār. Is he saying these words after the samsāri has encountered death? No, even when the samsāri is alive and spending his life in worldly matters, āḻvār is able to conclude for sure, the events to come (including pecking by the birds) and feels sorry for such samsāris and tells them what they should instead do. Even when rāvaṇan was alive and sitting on his throne in his court, tiruvadi \[hanuman\] told the courtiers “*nēyamasti purī lankā na yūyam na ca rāvaṇa:* ….” (since you have invited the enmity of the great warrior and descendent of ikśvāku kulam, śrī rāma, this lankāpuri, you and rāvaṇa will not be there soon). This was said even when rāvaṇa was alive. When duryōdana and his clan were lying dead in the battlefield, vēda vyāsar says with contempt that they did not pay heed to his advice and have paid the price. In a similar vein, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) feels contemptuous of the wayward lives of samsāris and warns them of what would happen if they do not mend their ways.

We shall move on to the 7<sup>th</sup> pāsuram in the next article.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan
