## 7. Pulaiyaramagi ninra

*pulaiyaṟamāgi ninṟa puttodu samaṇamellām kalaiyaṟak kaṟṟa māndar kāṇbarō kētparō tām talaiyaṟuppuṇḍum sāvēn sattiyam kāṇmin aiyā silaiyināl ilangai seṟṟa dēvanē dēvan avān*

**Пословный перевод:**

kalai – śastrams (vēdas, itihāsams etc)\[all sacred texts\]; aṟak kaṟṟa māndar – men (and women) who had learnt well the deeper, real meanings; pulai aṟam āgi ninṟa – those other lowly sects such as; puttodu samaṇamellām – bouddam, jainam etc; kāṇbarō – will they investigate with their hearts?; kētparō tām – will they listen with their ears?; talaiyaṟuppuṇḍum – even if I were beheaded; sāvēn – I will not die; aiyā – Oh, the great people; kāṇmin – Please see (I will show you); sattiyam – this is a fact; silaiyināl – with his bow; ilangai seṟṟa – one who destroyed lankā; dēvanē – and became famous; dēvan āvān – the one emperumān who is fit to be attained

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*samsāris told the āḻvār after he had sung the 6th pāsuram “you are despising us since we are lowly persons who are not carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān and not eating his happiness (when he receives our kainkaryam) as our food; you are also despising us that we are lying dead, being pecked by birds. Why do you find fault with us? It is the nature of the samsāram (materialistic world). You asked us to attain “taṇ tuḻāy mārban” (one who is adorning the comfortable tuḷasi garland). He is so far away that we cannot even reach him. There are several other hurdles for us in samsāram in reaching him. Please tell us how to overcome these hurdles and show us the person who we have to reach”. āḻvār tells them “attain the emperumān who came here as the divine son of cakravarti (reference is to śrī rāma, who incarnated here as the son of daśarata cakravati) and who has as his nature, removal of all hurdles “ This is the basis for the 7th pāsuram. Let us see the pāsuram: புலையறமாகி நின்ற புத்தொடு சமணமெல்லாம் கலையறக் கற்ற மாந்தர் காண்பரோ கேட்பரோ தாம் தலையறுப்புண்டும் சாவேன் சத்தியம் காண்மின் ஐயா சிலையினால் இலங்கை செற்ற தேவனே அவான்.*

**pulaiyaṟamāgi ninṟa puttodu samaṇamellām** – the four types of baudda matam (philosophy or sect) , jaina matam etc.

**pulaiyaṟa** – lowly sects. Those who do not accept vēdas as basis. The four types of bauddam were headed by four disciples of budda namely vaibhāśikan, soutrāntikan, yōgācāran and mādyamikan. Of these, vaibhāśikan’s hypothesis is that there are only two entities – knowledge and materials dependent on knowledge. There is no one called jñātā (one who has knolvedge). Knowledge is gained through *pratyakśam* (what is seen by the eyes) and *anumānam* (deductive logic). There is nothing called śāstras which pass on knowledge. The second disciple, soutrāntikan’s view is that there is only knowledge. There is no jñātā. Since knowledge is of many types, there are many kinds of materials that we know through deductive logic. yōgācāran, the third disciple said that that there is nothing that can be gained through *pratyakśam* or *anumānam*. There is only knowledge and no material having knowledge. These three disciples accept that whatever material they accept is of transient (lasting for fleeting moments) nature only. The fourth disciple, mādyamikan refused even the knowledge accepted by yōgācāran. He said that everything is in vacuum (this is called as *sarva sūnyavādam* in their parlance). The jaina matam which does not accept baudda matam asks “how can there be vacuum? This world which is based on cause and effect is composed of permanent and transient components. It is one and it is many. It is true and it is false” Thus the world is of many different types, says jainan. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) calls such persons and philosophies as lowly type (*pulaiyaṟam*).

**āgi ninṟa** – just as weeds grow opposing the growth of paddy, these lowly sects have been opposing the vaidika darmam (philosophy based on vēdas) for a long time. Usage of the word *ellām* refers to other philosophies such as sānkhyam, vaiśēśikam, pāsupatam etc. The reason for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) terming these as lowly secs is because in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s leelā vibhūti (material realm), if a person harms another  (by thought or by words or by action) he accrues sin and these sects harm both emperumān and his cētanars / acētanams (sentient and insentient entities) using the sword of ajñānam (ignorance). They are the types who try to destroy the indestructible.

**kalaiyaṟak kaṟṟa māndar** – those who firmly believe in śruti (vēdas), smruti (the works of manu et al which came to explain vēdas), itihāsa (epics such as śrī rāmāyaṇam and mahābhāratam) and purāṇas (the 18 primary purāṇas such as śrīviṣṇu purāṇam etc) and know the deep meanings of vēdas. They are people such as [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kurathazhwan/) et al.

**kāṇbarō kētparō tām** – will they delve into such sects with their hearts or hear them with their ears? Once kūrattāḻvān came home late after listening to the works of a lowly sect. His father, kūrattāḻvār, gave him śrīpāda tīrtam (the divine water of a śrīvaiṣṇava’s lotus feet) to purify him and told him “should you listen to the works of a lowly sect? You should remember [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)’s pāsuram – *pulaiyaṟa kaṟṟa māndar kāṇbarō kētparō tām..*.  ”. It is not necessary for the bāhyars (those who do not accept vēdas) and kudruśtis (those who give wrong interpretation to vēdas) to do any harmful act for us to get worried. Their mere existence is enough worry for us. This is similar to bhagavat bhāgavatas’ (devotees of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) mere existence being good for us even if they do not do anything good to us.

**talaiyaṟappuṇḍum sāvēn sattiyam kāṇmin** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says ”for those who do not have belief in the words of faultless vēdam (śāstram or śruti), I shall prove, without any doubt, through pratyakśam (direct evidence seen by the eye). You can see that”.  In olden days, to prove a statement that someone makes, that person will hold a red hot weapon (called *maḻu* or axe) and say that the red hot iron will not scald him if what he says is true. In the same way āḻvār takes an oath saying that even if he were beheaded, he will not die and the onlookers can get convinced that the supreme entity to be attained is the divine son of daśaratan, [śrī rāman](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). vyākhyātā [periyavāccān piḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyavachchan-pillai/) quotes the words of lakśmaṇa from śrī rāmāyaṇam “*darmātmā satyasantaśca rāmō dāśaratir yati* I *pauruśē cāpratidvantva: śarainam jahi rāvaṇim* īī” (If it is true that daśarata’s son śrī rāma is a righteous person, speaks only truth, has no equal in his valour, let this arrow destroy rāvaṇa’s son indrajit). It is a fact that the arrow killed indrajit, who could not be killed by anybody. In the same way, āḻvār wishes to establish the supremacy of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) by taking an oath that even if he were beheaded, he will not die. Could such a thing happen? wonder samsāris…

**sattiyam kāṇmin** – why do you doubt an event that you are going to witness right in front of your eyes? See what I am going to do now and learn the truth, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).

**aiyā** – when the samsāris hesitated to see what he was going to do, he pleads with them calling them ”*aiyā*” (elderly person). samsāris said “please do not carry out such difficult acts. We believe your oath. Please let us know who the person is that we have to attain”……..

**silaiyināl ilangai seṟṟa dēvanē dēvan āvān** – To ensure that those who granted boons to rāvaṇan (brahmā et al) do not lose face, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) came down to earth as the son of daśaratan. With the help of the bow in his hand, he destroyed lankā. Such emperumān, śrī rāman is the object \[goal\] for you to reach, avers the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). What would have happened if he had destroyed rāvaṇan with his will power (*sankalpam*) sitting in śrīvaikuṇṭam? In that case, the boons which were acting as a protective cover for lankā would have gone waste. Since the boons were granted by his followers (brahmā, rudra et al) he had to keep their words. Hence he took an incarnation as śrī rāma and with the help of the bow in his hand, he destroyed lankā.

**dēvanē dēvan āvān** – there are two roots for the word dēvan – in the first it means “divu – kānti“ (radiance) and in the second it means “divu – gati” (refuge). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that śrī rāma, who was resplendent with radiance, holding the bow in his hand near the fallen body of rāvaṇa is the supreme entity in whom all have to take refuge.

We shall move on to the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 8. Veruppodu samanar

*veṟuppodu samaṇar muṇḍar vidiyil sākkiyargaḷ ninpāl poṟuppariyanagaḷ pēsiṟ pōvadē nōya dākik kuṟippenaku adaiyumāgil kūdumēl talaiyay āngē aṟuppadē karumam kandāy arngamānagar uḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

arangamānagar uḷānē – Oh, tiruvarangā! The dweller of śrīrangam!; veṟuppodu – (unable to listen to anything good about emperumān) full of hatred; samaṇar – the jainas; muṇḍar – the śaivas; vidi il – the unfortunate (for they cannot attain emperumān); sākkiyargaḷ – bhauddas; nin pāl – in matters relating to you (who is the sarvēśvaran, the Lord of all); poṟuppu ariyanagaḷ – the intolerable matters; pēsil – had they spoken; aduvē nōyāgi – such abuses would become disease; pōvadu – ending in demise (which would have been the best); enakku – to me (the one who cannot take such abuses about emperumān); kuṟippu adaiyum āgil – should I get an opportunity; kūdumēl – if I have (the strength too); āngē – at the same place (where they had abused emperumān); talaiyai aṟuppadē – beheading such persons; karumam kandāy – is the just deed

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār wonders that despite clearly stating that only śrī rāma is to be taken refuge under, the samsāris are going about their ways, eating and making merry as well as talking ill of emperumān, instead of seeking him. He concludes that this must be due to the deep involvement that they have with puṟaccamayīs (other sects) and tells emperumān that these puraccamayīs should be eliminated. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meaning: வெறுப்பொடு சமணர் முண்டர் விதியில் சாக்கியர்கள் நின்பால் பொறுப்பரியனகள் பேசிற் போவதே நோய தாகிக் குறிப்பெனக்கு அடையுமாகில் கூடுமேல் தலையை ஆங்கே அறுப்பதே கருமம் கண்டாய் அரங்கமாநகர் உளானே.*

**veṟuppodu  samaṇar muṇḍar** – with hatred, jainas and śaivas. These people, displaying their hatred,  become red in their faces without any reason, on hearing auspicious details about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). The glories that vēdas speak about and the torture that animals are subjected to during conduct of jyōtiśtōma yāga (a ritual conducted for a person to reach indra lōkam (svargam or heaven)) may be intolerable for the jainas and śaivas. śaivas would hang themselves when emperumān comes out of temples in a procession or bang their heads when they hear the tiruccinnam  (bugle) blowing.

**muṇḍar** – this refers to śaivas. As said in tiruccanda viruttam (71) “*mundan nīṟan*…” Siva’s disciples are like him with tonsured heads. ōr it could be taken as samaṇar muṇḍar together, which would refer to the tonsured jainas.

**vidiyil sākkiyargaḷ** – the bhauddas who are not fortunate enough to know about the greatness of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and to attain and enjoy him.

**ninpāl poṟuppariyanagaḷ pēsil** – if they utter anything that will be unbearable, about you – who is greater than everyone else and who is the leader among those with auspicious qualities. The jainas deny the existence of īśvaran and his paramapadam. śaivas elevate a jīvātmā, Siva, as paramātmā and have a habit of talking ill of paramātmā (śrīman nārāyaṇan). These acts are naturally unbearable for vaidikas. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/), unable to repeat the crude words that such people utter, mentions these as “*poṟuppariyanagaḷ*” (those which are unbearable). These [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) feel shy even to bring themselves to repeating the cruel thoughts and words of such people about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) – example, “*tān tīngu ninanta*” in tiruppāvai, “*anṟinnādana sey sisupālanum…*” in nācciyār tirumoḻi. An incident is quoted here by vyākhyātā – mahābali, the grandson of prahlādāḻvān, approached prahlādāḻWan and told him that his empire was shrinking and wanted to know the reason for this. prahlādāḻvān told him  “you are troubling the dēvas (celestial people) to please asuras (demons) and dēvas have complained to śrīman nārāyaṇan in tiruppāṟkdal (the milky ocean – where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) resides to hear the issues of celestial people). śrīman nārāyaṇan has promised to redeem their lost possessions and has taken incarnation as son of aditi. You can take it that your possession is as good as lost” Upon hearing this, mahābali started abusing śrīman nārāyaṇan. śrī prahlādan plugged his ears and told mahābali “You are lost. You abused śrīman nārāyaṇan in my presence. Instead of this you could have done me a favour by beheading me. As a result of your abusing emperumān, may you lose all your possession” and cursed him, says purāṇas. [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/), one of our preceptors, asked his ācāryan [śrī parāsara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) “prahlādan is an ascetic. Is it correct on his part to consider an empire as desirable and curse mahābali that he would lose his kingdom?” bhattar responded saying that denying food that it craves for is the way of punishing a dog. In the same way, denying the kingdom that he values most is the punishment that can be meted out to mahābali. Denying sandalwood paste and betel to a prince is the just punishment, says the vyāKhyātā.

**pōvadē nōyadāgi** – if the person who hears the abuses about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) were like piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūraiyar (who gave up his life on seeing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s vigraham set afire by miscreants at tottiyam tirunārāyaṇapuram near śrīrangam), he would give up his life. ōr else, instead of remaining at the place where emperumān is being abused and looking at the abusers, he should leave the place immediately. “*gurōr yatra parivādō nindāvāpi pravarttatē* I *karṇau tatra* *pidātavyau gantavyam vā tatōnyata:* īī” This is from manu smriti which says where one’s ācāryan is being abused or spoken ill of, one should plug his ears or leave the place.

**kuṟippenakku adaiyumāgil** – if instead of ending one’s life or leaving that place as mentioned in the previous paragraph, opportunity is available to destroy the enemy. **kuṟittal** – thinking about carrying out a task and opportunity being available to execute it.

**kūdumēl** – if possible. Instead of getting caught by the enemy and getting killed, if it is possible to catch hold of the enemy and destroy him. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) uses the condition “*ēl*” (if) to emphasise that it is not easy to carry out this task (of eliminating the enemy).

**talaiyai āngē aṟuppadē karumam** – it is proper and justified to behead the person who had indulged in abusing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) at the same place where the abuse took place. It is one’s duty. It is a good deed for the one who carries out beheading because he doesn’t have to hear the abuse anymore.  It is also a good deed for the abuser because it prevents him from further abusing emperumān and earning emperumān’s wrath more and more.  In *agniśōmīya yāgam* (a ritual) in which an animal is sacrificed, śāstram says that both the animal which is killed and the yajamāna (one who carries out the ritual) who kills the animal reach *svargam* (heaven). In the same way, it benefits both the person who is beheaded and the person who beheads in *maṟumai* (after death).

**kaṇḍāy** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that he is appealing to emperumān who, as śrī rāma, removed the enemies (rāvaṇa et al) of *ruśis* (sages) in daṇḍakāraṇyam (forest near madya pradēś) as only he could carry out such a task.

**aranga mānagar uḷānē** – The purpose of adopting a lying posture at śrīrangam is to exterminate the enemies of followers who came after his rāmāvatāram, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). It is your duty to put an end to the jainas, bhauddas and śaivas who speak ill of you is the message conveyed here.

An [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) such as toṇḍardippodi, who is full of mercy, who always goes in the path of righteousness and who is always at peace – how is it that he is talking of killing someone? More so, when vēdas (sacred texts) forbid cruelty to others. (yajur vēda says “*na himsyāt sarvā bhūtāni*” – no living creature should be harmed). Just as the same vēda says elsewhere that it is alright to sacrifice an animal in *yāgam* (ritual) such as *agniśōmīyam*, this āḻvār also asks for killing the abuser to save him from committing further sins. There is no friendship between the person who is deeply involved with the auspicious qualities of emperumān and the person who abuses emperumān (vyākhyātā quotes from tirumangai āḻvār’s periya tirumoḻi 11.5.7 “*eṇṇāda mānidattai eṇṇāda pōdellām iniyavāṟē*” – it is always pleasant not to think of those who do not think of emperumān).

We shall go on to the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

pramēyam (goal) – [https://koyil.org](https://koyil.org/)

pramātā (preceptors) – [http://acaryas.koyil.org](http://acharyas.koyil.org/)

## 9. Marrum or deyvam

*maṟṟūmōr deyvam uṇḍē madiyilā mānidangāḷ uṟṟapōdanṟi nīngaḷ oruvan enṟu uṇara māttīr aṟṟamēl onṟu aṟiyīr avanallāl deyvamillai kaṟṟinam mēytta endai kaḻaliṇai paṇimin nīṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

madiyilā – without vēdānta (upaniśat) knowledge; mānidangāḷ – Oh men!; maṟṟum – (other than the entity mentioned by me) another; ōr deyvam – (fit to take refuge) a God; uṇḍē – is there anyone? (No, there is none); nīngaḷ – you people; uṟṟapōdu anṟi – (only at the time when the dēvatā \[other than śrīman nārāyaṇan\] that you had surrendered to is in) difficult times; oruvan enṟu – he is (the supreme) one entity; uṇara māttīr – you will not know; mēl – more than (the meanings given in śāstram (sacred texts)); aṟṟam – the hidden entity; onṟu aṟiyīr – you will not know at all; avan allāl – other than him; deyvam – Lord (fit to take refuge under); illai – (there is) no one; kaṟṟu inam mēytta – the one who herded cattle; endai – my svāmy (master) \[kriṣṇa’s\]; kazahliṇai – the two exalted feet; nīr paṇimin – you hold on to, as in surrendering; nīr – you

**Перевод:**

**maṟṟūmōr deyvam uṇḍē – other than the entity that I am talking about, will any other entity be supreme enough to attain? While there may be those who claim “I am the Controller” there is no one who is divine enough and of natural affinity towards us, for us to attain. As said in śrī bhagavat gītā (9.23) “yē tvanya dēvatā bhakta:…” (when the devotees of other dēvatās (celestial beings) pray to those dēvatās with faith, they pray to me only; but in an improper way) kaṇṇan makes a distinction between himself and the other dēvatās. āḻvār also points out to a similar disparity.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) After listening to his previous pāsuram, the samsāris, afraid of being despised by the learned āḻvār, told him “we have not attempted to reach emperumān, not because of any affection for the other sects such as bauddam etc. dēvarīr (your highness) had asked us to reach \[daśarata\] cakravarty’s son śrī rāma. But he is dwelling inside a huge palace with many security guards guarding him throughout, not allowing common people like us to approach him. Even if we were to go past these security guards, there are sages such as vaśiśta who would not allow ignorant persons such as ourselves to approach him. Hence to us, śrī rāma is very similar to paravāsudēvan \[who is in śrīvaikuṇṭam\] in that we cannot approach either of them. We can approach only that entity who will not mind our lowly nature and our lack of qualification. Show us such a person” āḻvār says “in that case you have to approach only kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who, unmindful of any qualification or lack of it, falls head over heels on his followers and gives them refuge” in this pāsuram. Let us first look at the pāsuram: மற்றுமோர் தெய்வம் உண்டே மதியிலா மானிடங்காள் உற்றபோதன்றி நீங்கள் ஒருவன் என்று உணர மாட்டீர் அற்றமேல் ஒன்று அறியீர் அவனல்லால் தெய்வமில்லை கற்றினம் மேய்த்த எந்தை கழலிணை பணிமின் நீரே.*

**ōr deyvam** – just as people living in abject poverty are counted among the citizenship, these dēvatās are also considered as “Gods” even though they will be lacking in the auspicious qualities associated with a God, if one delves deeper.

**uṇḍē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is not asking if there is any other dēvatā who is equal to him. He is emphatically denying that there is anyone else who qualifies to be called as a God. Another interpretation is that he is asking if he is not the only famous protector. As said in [tiruvāimoḻi 2.2.2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/01/thiruvaimozhi-2-2-2-epavam-parame/), if someone were to ask “*ēḻulagum īpāvam seydu aruḷāl aḷippārār*?” – in all the worlds, who else is there who will protect the samsāris and remove their sins? Even his enemies will respond saying that it is only he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\]. He is that famous. That he is the protector of all cannot be denied by anyone.

**madiyilā mānidangāḷ** – rig vēda 8.1-12-6 says “*trīṇi śatā sahasrāṇyagnīm trimśacca dēvā nava cāsaparyan*” (agni claims that three thousand three hundred and thirty nine celestial people did service to me). samsāris, quoting this, ask the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/), when vēdas say that there are thousands of such dēvas  (celestial beings) who are being vorśipped, how can he say that no one else has divine qualities? To which āḻvar responds, derisively, “*madiyilā mānidangāḷ*” – Oh men, without the wealth of vēdānta knowledge. The vyākhyātā quotes from several pramāṇams here to establish the supremacy of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/):

1. From yajur vēdam 3-11-21 “*caturhōtārō yatra sampadam gaccanti dēvai:*” – various karmas starting with agnihōtra darśapūrṇamāsa karma, derive their pride from various dēvatās only because these dēvatās form the body of emperumān who remains their inner soul (antaryāmi) for all.
2. From śrī bhagavat gītai 9-24 “*aham hi sarvayajñānām bhōktā ca prabhurēva ca*” – It is true that for all *yajñams* (rituals), I remain the *bhōktā* (one who enjoys) and one who gives the benefits. Those who attempt to reach other dēvatās do not realise this and they slip away from me.
3. From dakśa smruti 355-4  “*yē yajanti pitrūn dēvān* ….” – those who worship *pitrūn* (forefathers), celestial people, brāhmaṇas and agni, worship viṣṇu who remains their inner soul.

Thus, those without knowledge of the sacred texts (*śruti* and *smruti*) are born as cētanas (sentient entity) who, by definition, are supposed to be with knowledge and [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) laments on their misfortune. The vyākhyātā quotes from [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/)’s periya tirumoḻi 11.7.9  “*ānvidayēanṟadāṟku āḷānārallādār mānidarallavenṟu en manattē vaittēnē*” – I did not consider as cētanas those who were not blessed by kriṣṇa and from bhūtattāḻvār’s  iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 44 “*cengaṇmāl nāmam maṟandārai mānidamā vaiyēn*” – I will not consider as cētanas those who forget the glory of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). taittiriya upniśat also says “*asannēva sa bhavati* I *asat brahmēti vēda cēt* I” – one who does not have the credit for knowing brahmam is considered to be non-existent. śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 6.5.87 says “*tat jñānamajñānam anōnyaduktam*” – that is jñānam which knows the one who is faultless, pure and shining. The rest is ajñānam. [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) says in the mudal tiruvandādi -67 “*oṇ tāmaraiyāḷ kēḷvan oruvanaiyē nōkkum uṇarvu*” – jñānam will consider only the lord of [mahālakśmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). Finally the vyākhyātā quotes from śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1.19.41 “*vidyānyā śilpanaipuṇam*”  – that which is not reason for bondage (to samsāram) is karmam; that which is reason for liberation (mōkśam) is knowledge. The rest of the karmas is for looking after the physical body and the rest of knowledge is suited for repairing one’s sandals.

Thus āḻvār says that samsāris are without knowledge since they have not gained knowledge of the one entity about which they should have gained. Hearing this, the samsāris told him “since we do not have knowledge, you, the knowledgeable, please enlighten us” [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) responds….

**uṟṟapōdanṟi nīngaḷ oruvan enṟu uṇaramāttīr** – āḻvar says “however much I tell you, until the day of praḷayam (deluge) comes and both you and the Gods that you took refuge under go together into the mouth of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), you will not realise that the supreme being is *puruśōttaman* (emperumān).

**oruvan** – non pareil; peerless; without any equal.

**uṟṟapōdu** – when deluge strikes

Another interpretation given is that  “only on that day when the Gods that you took refuge under are troubled by demons such as rāvāṇa, hiraṇya, basmāsura et al and they cry out to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will you realise that emperumān is the supreme entity”

**oruvan enṟu uṇaramāttīr** – “samsāris like you who are vorśipping other dēvatās will not even know until the troubled times (as mentioned earlier) come that emperumān is also a God like the other dēvatās” is another interpretation to these words. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) feels dejected that the samsāris are not having affection towards emperumān, who can grant mōkśam itself, that they are having towards the other dēvatās who can fulfil only their insignificant wishes.

**aṟṟamēl onṟu aṟiyīr** – **aṟṟam** – meanings hidden. The reason for samsāris’ ignorance is that in the vēdas, they know the meanings only superficially but not the inner, deep meanings. In jitantē slōkam 1.7, it says “*vacasām vācyamuttamam*” – great people would know that you \[emperumān\] are the cause behind all the effects, the real, superior meanings of words and the greatest goal to be reached. kriṣṇa, in bhagavat gītā 15.15 says “*vēdaiśca sarvairahamēva vēdya:*” – it is I who is known by all the vēdas (sacred texts). śrī bhāgavatam 2.15.15 says “*nārāyaṇaparā vēdā:*” – vēdas talk about nārāyaṇa, the supreme being. Even when śāstras praise other deities, it is only their antaryāmi (inner dwelling soul) [śrīman nārāyaṇan](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is praised.

**avan(aiy)allāl deyvamillai**  – no one else has total divine quality. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is not saying that there are no other deities. As said in taittiriya upaniśat śīkśāvalli 5.1 “*sa ātmā angānyanya dēvatā:*” – the other dēvatās exist as his forms and physical body; he is their soul. When emperumān with complete, natural divinity is there, it is not proper to take refuge under other dēvatās who have only a fraction of his divine qualities, which were also granted only by him. āḻvār refers to kriṣṇa, who is mentioned next, as “*avan*”

Please identify the person who you are referring to as “avan”, say the samsāris. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) responds….

**kaṟṟinam mēytta endai kaḻaliṇai paṇimin nīrē** – Instead of calling him as kriṣṇa, āḻvār is using the term “*kaṟṟinam mēytta*” – one who herded the cows and calves. This is to emphasise the fact that unlike other deities who are difficult to worship, he is very simple and makes himself available even to cows and calves. “kaṟṟu” is a modified form of “kanṟu” which means calves. Instead of saying “*āvinam mēytta*” (herding cows) āḻvār is using the term “*kaṟṟinam mēytta*” because cows, being grown up, would know where to go for grazing grass and take care of themselves while calves do not know even this. Cows are like nityasūris to emperumān who do not need constant care from him while he is more considerate towards calves, who are like samsāris, unable to protect themselves. As [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said in his tiruvāimoḻi 10.3.10 “*tivattilum pasunirai mēyppuvatti*” – he takes more immense pleasure in herding calves than in being in śrīvaikuṇṭam. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) in his tiruneduntāṇḍakam 10 conveyed similar meaning “*kanṟu mēyttu iniduganda kāḷāy*” – you were pleased about herding calves.

**endai** – father. You can also get attracted to this kriṣṇāvatāram (incarnation of Lord kriṣṇa) just as I got attracted to, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).

**kaḻaliṇai paṇimin nīrē** – hold on to his two exalted feet. Just as iḷaiyaperumāḷ (lakśmaṇan) said in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkintā kāṇḍam 4-12 “*ahamasyāvarō bhrātā guṇair dāsyamupāgata:*” – by his (śrī rāma’s) account, I am his brother. But I am a servitor to his qualities. I fell at the exalted feet of emperumān seeing how fond he was of calves and cows. You could also hold on to his exalted feet. It is only emperumān who, even when he takes incarnation in a lowly birth, allows his āśritars (followers) to enjoy to the same extent whatever he himself experiences. Other deities cannot share this pride as is clear from events that happened in kriṣṇāvatāram (*kaṟṟinam mēytta*) and as seen in itihāsas and purāṇas. In mahābhāratam, there is a story on yayāti. He was a king who attained the position of īndra by his sheer puṇya (good deeds) and sat on the same throne that īndra sat on, along with īndra. īndra was very jealous of him and wanted to push him out of his place somehow. Very casually, he asked yayāti as to who, in his time on earth,  was the one who had done greatest puṇya? Afraid of telling a lie, yayāti very truthfully said that people used to say that it was he who had done the greatest puṇya. īndra thundered that he had indulged in vanity and said that such a person was unfit to sit on his throne and toppled him from his seat. nanjīyar, one of our [pūrvācāryas](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/full-list), told his ācārya, [śrī parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) that the purpose of this incident in mahābhārat was not clear to him. bhattar responded saying that this was quoted to explain that other deities (such as indra) cannot bear others coming anywhere near them and partaking in their enjoyment and that only emperumān allows that and hence only he is fit to be surrendered to and not the other deities.

We shall move on to the next (10<sup>th</sup>) pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 10. Nattinan deyvamengum

*nāttinān deyvam engum nalladōr aruḷ tannālē kāttinān tiruvarangam uybavarkku uyyum vaṇṇam kēttīrē nambimīrgāḷ geruda vāhananum niṟkac cēttai tan madiyagattuc celvam pārttirukkinṟīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

engum – at all places; deyvam – different types of rājasa (those who are passionate and short tempered) and tāmasa (those who are ignorant and lazy) deities; nāttinān – established; uybavarkku – for those interested in living an exalted life; uyyumvaṇṇam – to find the means; nalladu ōr aruḷ tannālē – with his incomparable quality of mercy; tiruvarangam – śrīrangam; kāttinān – pointed out; nambimīrgāḷ – those having total dedication (on matters other than those relating to emperumān); kēttīrē – did you hear this meaning?; gerudavāhananum niṟka – even when emperumān, who uses garudan as his vehicle, is around; cēttai than madiyagattu – at the door of mūdēvi \[deity for penury\]; selvam pārttu irukkinṟirē – waiting, begging for wealth

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) samsāris told āḻvār “you had asked us to take refuge under taṇ tuḻāy mālai mārban (paramapadanātan or śrīvaikuṇṭanātan) in the 5th pāsuram. Since we cannot reach his place, you had told us in the 7th pāsuram that silayināl ilangai seṟṟa dēvanē dēvan āvān (the one who destroyed lankā, śrī rāma, is the supreme lord). In the 9th, previous, pāsuram, you had told us kaṟṟinam mēytta endai is kriṣṇa himself, a simple person and asked us to fall at the exalted feet of kriṣṇa. For us, born much after the two incarnations, it is equally difficult to attain śrī rāma and kriṣṇa”. āḻvār responds to them through this pāsuram, saying “due to differences in place and time, it is not possible to attain paravāsudēvan (who is in śrīvaikuṇṭam) and śrī rāma or kriṣṇa. But it is very easy for you all to attain periya perumāḷ (the mūlavar) who is lying in tiruvarangam. Come and surrender to him. Attaining some other deity to reach mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) is similar to a person, who is longing for wealth, to wait at the door of mūdēvi (the Goddess of penury)” First let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meaning. நாட்டினான் தெய்வம் எங்கும் நல்லதோர் அருள் தன்னாலே காட்டினான் திருவரங்கம் உய்பவர்க்கு உய்யும் வண்ணம் கேட்டிரே நம்பிமீர்காள். கெருட வாகனனும் நிற்கச் சேட்டை தன் மடியகத்துச் செல்வம் பார்த்திருக்கின்றீரே.*

**nāttinān** – empeumān’s devotees, being endowed with more of *satva guṇam* (quality of being purely good) would take refuge under him due to the knowledge born out of such *satva guṇam*. But those who are endowed more with *rajō guṇam* (quality of being passionate/ill tempered etc) and *tamō guṇam* (quality of being ignorant, lazy etc), lacking in such knowledge that is given by *satva guṇam* will not come directly to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Instead of letting them to get engrossed in worldly pleasures fully due to their independence, emperumān decides to make them approach deities with rājasa and tāmasa qualities initially. Over a period of time, with awakening of their sātvika knowledge, these samsāris would know about the auspicious qualities of emperumān and take refuge under him. It is for this reason that he deployed many deities. *nāttinān* means deployed. Another meaning is that being the inner soul of these deities, he established these deities. The subtle meaning here is that without emperumān, these deities will be like creeper plants without the supporting pole. Just as bean plant needs a short stick initially when it is growing and later on a full-fledged network of sticks, samsāris with rājasa and tāmasa qualities need the support of other deities initially and once they develop themselves they can latch on to emperumān who acts like an arbour (*pandal*). [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 5.2.8 “*niṟuttinān deyvangaḷāga*” – deployed as deities.

**deyvam engum** – emperumān created various deities such as *ayyan, eḻumūrtty, saptakannigaigaḷ, pidāri* et al so that samsāris with various qualities, and births based on such qualities, right down to *caṇḍāḷan* (a birth below the 4<sup>th</sup> varṇa) could approach these deities. In mahābhātam it says “*viṣṇurātmā bhagavatōbhavasyāmitatējasa:* I *tasmāt danurjyāsamsargam sa viśēhē mahēśvara:* īī” – viṣṇu was the indvelling soul of Siva, due to which only Siva was able to tolerate the difficulty in shooting the arrow after drawing the bow. As [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi 1.1.5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-5-avaravar-thamathamathu/) “*iṟaiyavar avaravar vidi vaḻiyadaiya ninṟanarē*” – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), by remaining as the indvelling soul of other deities, enabled the followers of these deities to reach their goals as per their deeds. Thus emperumān, by his quality of dwelling inside the souls of these deities, enabled them to get name and fame. emperumān wishes that samsāris should reach him and get liberated. It is due to this superior thought that he deploys the other deities and gives vision (*vēdas*) to the samsāris. His expectation is that these samsāris, with the help of vēdas, would know about the inferiority of the other deities and the supremacy of emperumān and ultimately come to him. Most of the samsāris, because of the excess of pāpa (sins) that they have committed, remain dedicated to these deities, showing indifference to emperumān, just as a beans creeper, instead of going onto the arbour, remains with the short stick, planted initially to support it, and withers over a period of time.

**nalladōr aruḷ tannālē** – he shows mercy on samsāris so that they will know what is beneficial to them and reach [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). **nalladōr aruḷ** – this is to be seen in three parts: (a) *aruḷ* – enabling the samsāris to approach other deities based on their deeds; (b) *ōr aruḷ* – enabling samsāris to approach him through other deities; (c) *nalladōr aruḷ* – through his causeless mercy, showing himself at *tiruvarangam* as the path and the goal to be achieved by these samsāris, after seeing their lowly state. Our supreme preceptor, āḷavandār says in his stōtra ratnam 58 “*aparāda sahasra bhājanam patitam bhīma mahārṇavōdarē* I *agatim śaraṇāgatam harē krupayā kēvalam ātma sātkuru* īī” – with your causeless mercy, make me as your property – me, who is the embodiment of thousands of evil deeds, who has fallen into the deep ocean called samsāram, who has no other place to go, and who has surrendered unto you. It is this causeless mercy which is showered on samsāris even when they do not realise their lowly state and feel sorry for themselves, that is termed as *nalladōr aruḷ*. Just as he is going to refer to tiruvarangam next because of this mercy, even deploying other deities is due to this mercy, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).

**aruḷ tannālē kāttinān tiruvarangam** – showed tiruvarangam out of that mercy. Nobody appealed to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to show them tiruvarangam. Just as a nursing mother will express milk onto the ground due to excessive pain in her breast, emperumān shows himself up at tiruvarangam as he would otherwise not be able to thrive. Similar to a person presenting a gem with the wrapper, emperumān presents himself at tiruvarangam temple. To know the difference between the samsāris (and the deities that they take shelter under) and himself, and for them to realise one day that they are protected by emperumān, he dwells in tiruvarangam permanently. Who did he show himself up to?…..

**uybavarkku** –  [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) showed himself at tiruvarangam to all those who wanted to live an exalted life. It is enough if samsāris do not think that they will not take shelter under him. Since this is the way to live properly, taking shelter under other deities will only bind the samsāris to the samsāram is the meaning emphasised here.

**uyyum vaṇṇam** – To worship [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is antaryāmi (inner dwelling soul) of other deities through karma (deeds) and upāsana (bhakti or devotion) is one way of attaining mōkśa (liberation) for samsāris. But this path is defined only for samsāris born in the first three varṇas (brāhmaṇa, kśatriya and vaiśya). Going to tiruvarangam and taking refuge under emperumān as both the path and the benefit is common to all samsāris irrespective of varṇa.

**kēttīrē nambīmīrgāḷ** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is asking those samsāris who, forgetting emperumān, are feeling fully satisfied after their involvement in worldly matters “will you listen to what I am saying now?”. Another meaning to this term is that āḻvār is asking these samsāris “when emperumān is around, forgetting him, you people are going after other deities and feeling fully satisfied. Will you now listen to what I am saying?” When people are playing *caturangam* (a variant of modern day chess) they will be so involved in the game that they will not look at other things. In the same way, the samsāris, forgetting emperumān, are busy in worldly matters and other deities. āḻvār is awakening them by tapping them on their thighs.

**gerudavāgananum niṟka** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is having garuda, considered as vēdasvarūpan (epitome of vēdas), as his vehicle is the supreme entity to be reached. Sitting majestically astride garuda, tirelessly holding the discus (*sudarśana cakram*), having [lakshmi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/), the Goddess of all auspicious things – possessing even one of these three features would identify emperumān. *gerudavāgananum niṟka* – he urges garuda to take him immediately to anyone who shows even a little bit of affection. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says “*niṟka*” it shows that he is eagerly waiting for samsāris to get involved with him, similar to how sumantra, the minister in daśarata cakravarty’s court was waiting for rāma at the banks of gangā “*āśaya yadi vā rāma..*.”. Another interpretation is that this indicates that when samsāris reach out to other deities, they have to go past him even as he is waiting close by for them.

**cēttaitan madiyagattuc celvam pārttu irukkinṟīṟē** – you samsāris are waiting to get money from mūdēvi (deity for penury).  Expecting mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) from other deities is similar to expecting money from mūdēvi. Is it possible to obtain mōkśam (from which there is no returning to samsāram) from deities who can offer only lowly benefits, and which bind the samsāris further to samsāram? Begging for dress from a person who himself is not wearing anything is equivalent to killing him. In the same way, asking for mōkśam from other deities will lower their esteem. How can one get mōkśam, which is reachable only by a person with satva (purely  good) qualities, from deities who are immersed in rajō and tamō qualities (passion and ignorance respectively)? Is it proper to call deities which have knowledge and strength as mūdēvi? The answer is that a cakravarty’s (emperor’s) wife and children will not bother about his servants. In the same way, those who have taken refuge under [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will not value other deities. It is pertinent here to remember that [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) continued to stitch his vēśti (dōti, lower body cloth) even after rudra and pārvati were waiting to bless him and grant him boon.

We shall move on to pāsuram 11.

## 11. Oruvillal ongu

*ōru villāl ōngu munnīr adaittu ulagangaḷ uyyac ceruvilē arakkar kōnaic ceṟṟa nam sēvaganār maruviya periya kōil matiḷ tiruvarangam ennā karuvilē tiruvilādir! kālattaik kazikkinṟīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

oru villāl – with a bow that he could lay his hands on; ōngu munnīr adaittu – constructing a dam on the turbulent ocean; ulagangaḷ uyya – so that all worlds could get uplifted; ceruvilē – in war; arakkar kōnai – rāvaṇa, the head of demons; ceṟṟa nam sēvaganār – our aḻagiya maṇavāḷan who destroyed that rāvaṇa; maruviya – dwelling permanently; periya kōil – the temple which is famous; madil tiruvarangam – at śrīrangam, with several protective walls; ennā – not saying so; karuvilē tiru ilādīr – not having emperumān’s mercy when you were inside your mother’s womb; kālattai – time (when you should be carrying out service to him after surrendering); kaḻikkinṟīrē – wasting

**Перевод:**

**oru villāl – with the help of a bow that he could lay his hands on, he blocked the ocean. But vasn’t the ocean blocked with stones? How is it that āḻvār says that śrī rāma blocked it with a bow? When it was decided to build a dam across the ocean, śrī rāma surrendered to samudrarājan (king of ocean). But when the king of ocean did not appear even after 3 days, śrī rāma told lakśmaṇa “cāpamānaya soumitrē śarāmścāṣī viśōbamān I sāgaram śōśayiśyāmi padbhyām yāntu plavangamā: īī” – hēy lakśmaṇa! Bring the bow and arrows which are like poisonous snakes. I shall dry up the ocean. The army of monkeys can walk through the ocean bed. After lakśmaṇa brought the bow and arrows, śrī rāma drew the bow and was about to shoot the arrow when the king of ocean appeared. Thus āḻvār says that with the help of the bow he created the dam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār had shown the extreme limit of simplicity – emperumān lying at śrīrangam temple, as periya perumāḷ. Despite that, samsāris were not showing any involvement with periya peumāḷ. He feels sad that despite telling them that the same śrī rāma who he had earlier (in pāsuram 7) pointed out to them for taking refuge under, is now lying in śrīrangam to help them, the samsāris, instead of reciting his names, are wasting their time. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings, first: ஒரு வில்லால் ஓங்கு முந்நீர் அடைத்து உலகங்கள் உய்யச் செருவிலே அரக்கர் கோனைச் செற்ற நம் சேவகனார் மருவிய பெரிய கோயில் மதிள் திருவரங்கம் என்னா கருவிலே திருவிலாதீர்! காலத்தைக் கழிக்கின்றீரே.*

**ongu munnīr** – turbulent ocean. It is not the natural turbulence in the ocean that is referred to here.  On seeing śri rāma with the bow and the way he stood with a courageous stance, the ocean felt shaken and thus became turbulent. The king of ocean did not appear when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) surrendered to him. Only when he took the arrow did he appear. It is similar to children in school bowing to the class teacher when he takes the stick. vālmīki bhagavān describes the anger of śrī rāma “*samudrasya tata: kruddō rāmō raktāntalōcana:* I” – rāma, because of his anger towards the king of ocean, was red in his eyes.

**adaittu** – only after śrī rāma let go off a few arrows did varuṇa appear and begged śrī rāma to build the dam on his back.. Hence [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says, he blocked the ocean with bow and not with stones. Without the backing, the mountains which were thrown into the ocean by the army of monkeys would have sunk and the dam would not have been built.

**ulagangaḷ uyya** – For all the worlds, including earth, nether world, *svargam* (heaven) and *antarikśam* (world between earth and heaven) to live in comfort. When rāvaṇa was alive, these worlds were in disarray due to the strength of the boons that he had obtained. It was only after his death that these worlds became liveable. śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1.13.14 says “*na yaśtavyam na dātavyam na bhōgtavyam katancana:* I” – at no point of time should there be *yajñam* (a form of worship) or donations or *hōmam* (ritual with agni, or fire) was the order during rāvaṇa’s time. He conquered all the worlds with his strength and power and made the worlds almost unliveable. Another way of interpreting is “*ulagangaḷuyya munnīr adaittu*” – for the worlds to live in comfort, he blocked the ocean. “*sētum druśtvā samudrasya gangā sāgara sangamam I brahmahatyā vimucyatē mitradrōhi na mucyatē I*I” – by looking at the sētu built in the ocean \[by śrī rāma\] and the place where gangā joins with ocean, a person gets rid of killing a brāhmaṇa, but not betrayal of a friend, goes a saying. samsāris, at all times, can get rid of their dōśas (impurities or faults by merely looking at the sētu (bridge)).

**ceruvilē** – in war. Unlike the coward rāvaṇa who, using a bait (the illusory deer) removed rāma and lakśmaṇa from their dwelling and entering an open house like a dog and took away pirātti when she was alone, śrī rāma engaged in war with rāvaṇa and killed him.

**arakkar kōnai** – king of demons. As [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says in periya tirumoḻi 4-4-6 “*arakkar tangaḷ kōn pōlum*” – rāvaṇa was arrogant that he was the king of demons, looking at the strength of demons with him. Apart from indulging in evil acts, he gave room for other bad people too to indulge in such acts.

**ceṟṟa –** destroyed. Right in front of rāvaṇa’s eyes, śrī rāma destroyed his supporters who were the cause for his arrogance; he destroyed his sons; he cut off his heads one by one so that he would be able to see his heads falling on the ground, a sight which no one else would have seen, thus shaming him. This is how rāvaṇa was destroyed. In śrī rāmāyaṇa, yudda kāndam ślōkam 59.144, śrī rāma tells rāvaṇa “*gaccānujānāmi  raṇārtitas tvam praviśya rātrincararāja! lankām* I *āśvāsya niryāhi ratīca danvī tadā bhalam drakśyasi mē ratastata:* īī” – Oh, king of demons! You have become tired in the battlefield. Go back! I allow you to go. Go to your place, recoup your strength and come back with your chariot and your bow. Sitting on your chariot, you will see my strength. śrī rāma disarmed rāvaṇa completely and told him to leave the battlefield, demonstrating the extreme limit of his valour.

**nam sēvaganār** – our servant. He is not a *sēvagan*  by his being [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). He is a servant because he is dependent on us (*paratantran* to us). If a [*cētanan*](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (a sentient entity) folds his palms together in anjali fashion to emperumān, he forever becomes a servant to such cētanar. **nam** – as said in nācciyār tirumoḻi 5.3 “*tāy talaiyaṟṟaṟṟu vīḻattodutta talaivan*” – by removing the heads of rāvaṇa one by one and making them fall, śrī rāmā removed the enemy for uniting with pirātti. In the same way he will remove our enemies too; here, the term “*nam*” refers to us who don’t have any other refuge. pirātti is the recommending authority for all of us (*puruśakāra bhūtai*). Hence if he helps her (by removing her enemy) it can be construed as helping us all. The following incidents which took place with our [pūrvācāryas](http://acharyas.koyil.org/) is quoted to explain this term – (1) [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) was listening to his ācāryar [bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) explaining this pāsuram. After the session was over, nanjīyar was thinking about this pāsuram when he saw a soldier and a lady having an argument. The soldier was demonstrating his authority to her and trying to frighten her. The lady calmly told the soldier “what can you do to me? Only for protecting poor people and ladies like me, our king is ruling this country adorning all the weapons” nanjīyar was amazed that the meaning for this pāsurtam came from a commoner. (2) [bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) was recounting to cōḻa sikāmaṇi pallavarāyar the śrī rāmāyaṇa ślōkam  yudda kāndam 25.33 “*rāmalakśmaṇaguptā sā sugrīvēṇa ca vāhini* I *babhūva durtdarśadarā sarvairapi surāsurai:* īī” – rāma and lakśmaṇa were protecting the army of monkeys that had sugrīva in it and it could not be disturbed by dēvas or asuras (celestial or demonic people). After reciting this, bhattar told them to keep remembering this incident which happened on the seashore. The audience, which included nanjīyar, pillāī viḻupparaiyar and nambi śrī gōvardana dāsar among others, thought of another pāsuram, in nānmugan tiruvantādi 8 “*ilai tuṇai maṟṟu en nenjē – ilangayai aḻitta kūrambanallāl*” – there is no  protector other than the one who destroyed lankā with his sharp arrows. Thus, for samsāris like us who have no other refuge, cakravarti tirumagan (son of daśarata, śrī rāma) is the only refuge.

**sēvaganār maruviya periya kōil** – the temple where [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) came willingly. After he destroyed rāvaṇa in the battlefield and fulfilled his word to dēvas and ruśis, emperumān came and lay down in this temple in order to help those who came after his period of incarnation. Instead of saying sēvagan (servant) the purpose of adding the honorific sound *Ar* at the end (sēvaganār) is not only to highlight his glory; it is to emphasise his dedication in serving his followers here.

**maruviya** – emperumān’s permanent place of dwelling, with total commitment. Going beyond *para* (śrīvaikuṇṭam), *vyūha* (tiruppāṟkadal) and *vibhava* (places where he took various incarnations), he chose tiruvarangam which is the root for all [*arcāvatārams*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/p/archavathara-anubhavam.html) ([divya dēśams](https://koyil.org/)).

**periya kōil** – big temple. The adjective big does not refer to the expanse of the temple, but considering the greatness of the place (divya dēśam). If the King of a country issues an order from his capital he would not think of rescinding it. In the same way, the *sankalpams* (solemn vows) that he takes from here makes it a big temple. What are the vows that he takes from here? We have already seen in the explanatory notes of pāsuram 3 that this place is “*pavitram paramam puṇyam dēśōyam sarva kāma duk*” (gāruda purāṇam 11) – this place is sacred, takes the people to liberation, and fulfils their desires. linga purāṇam says “*vaiṣṇavānām viśēśōsti viṣṇōr āyatanam mahat*” – there is glory among vaiṣṇavas that this temple is a big temple for viṣṇu.

**madiḷ tiruvarangam** – In order to allay the fears of what will happen to the soft tirumēni (physical body) of periya perumāḷ, who is lying inside the temple, from his enemies, this temple has huge protective walls. Knowing the greatness of the [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), veren’t protective walls raised by persons such as [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/)?

**ennā** – there is no need to trouble the body and carry out superhuman deeds. It is enough just to say a few words. There is no need to even recite his names with auspicious qualities and deeds such as *nārāyaṇa* et al. It is enough if one says the name of the place that he resides in, namely *tiruvarangam*.

**karuvilē tiruviḷdīr** – not blessed in the womb. When it is so simple to recite tiruvarangam, why did you not recite it? asks the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). Is it because you were not blessed by emperumān when you were in your mother’s womb? As said in mahābhāratam, śānti parvam (358.73) “*jāyamānam hi puruśam yampaśyēn madusūdana:* I  *sātvikas sa tu vijñēya sa vai mōkśārta cintaka:* īī” – that person who is blessed by *madusūdanan* ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) as he comes out into this world from his mother’s womb, is the person who is known to possess sātvika (purely good) qualities and he is the one who thinks of the greatness of mōkśa. It is this blessing from emperumān when he is about to be born that attracts him towards emperumān. āḻvār tells the samsāris that they have lost that blessing. [poigai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) and [bhūtattāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/bhuthaththazhwar/) (the first two āḻvārs) conveyed the same meaning in their respective tiruvandādi (of being blessed by emperumān when they were inside the womb).

**kālattaik kaḻikkinṟīrē** – wasting your time. As viṣṇu tatvam says “*vicitrā dēha sambandīśvarāya nivēditum* I *pūrvamēva krutā brahman hasta pādādi samyutā* īī”- this splendorous body with arms and legs was structured yugas (ages) ago to be offered to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is asking, instead of attaining emperumān and carrying out the appropriate kainkaryam, why are you wasting your time? emperumān, who has left timeless śrivaikuṇṭam and taken position in this time-controlled world, is waiting for you all to attain him. But you people are wasting your time. While nityasūris (viśvaksēnar, ādisēśan, garuda et al, who are permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) are willing to carry out appropriate kainkaryam by knowing his desires, he has, forfeiting them, come here so that you people could take refuge under him. But you people are running after food and dress for the body and wasting your time. While he is waiting here so that he can slowly get you interested in him and he can train you to enjoy him, you people are holding onto everything except him. How sad this is, feels the āḻvār.

We shall proceed to pāsuram 12.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 12. Namanum murkalanum

*namanum muṟkalanum pēsa naragil ninṟārgaḷ kētka naragamē suvargamāgum nāmangaḷ udaiya nimbi avanadu ūr arangamennā(du) ayarttu vīḻndaḷiya māndar kavalaiyuḷ paduginṟarenṟu adanukkē kavar(l)ginṟēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

namanum – yamadarmarāja (yama, the deity for justice); muṟkalanum – and mudgala bhagavān; pēsa – when they were conversing; naragil ninṟargaḷ kētka – as soon as those in narakam (hell) heard those words; naragamē – that narakam itself; suvargam āgum – would become svargam (heaven); nāmangaḷ udaiya – with divine names; nambi avanadu – the perfect emperumān’s; Ur – dwelling place; arangam ennādu – not saying “tiruvarangam”; aḷiya māndar – great samsāris; ayarttu – forgetting (emperumān’s divine names); vīḻndu – falling down (into the pit of worldly issues); kavalaiyuḷ paduginṟār enṟu – being plagued by sorrows; adanukkē – only for that; kavar(l)ginṟēnē – I am worrying

**Перевод:**

**namanum muṟkalanum pēsa – In ancient times, there was a person by name mudgalan. He was constantly indulging in vices. Once, to atone for his sins, he gifted a cow made of sesame (til or eḷḷu). At the time of giving the gift, he said “kriṣṇārpaṇam” and gifted it. After he died, he was dragged by the servitors of yama to hell. yama welcomed him which surprised mudgalan. He told yama “your followers are dragging me here and you are welcoming me and treating me well. What is the reason for this?” yama replied “neither my followers nor you know your greatness. Once you said kriṣṇārpaṇam and it is because of that, I am welcoming you and treating you nicely” explaining the greatness of reciting emperumān’s divine name. As these two people were discussing the greatness of divine names, those who were suffering in hell heard this. As they were hearing, the troubles in hell disappeared for them and they felt as if they were enjoying in heaven. This anectode is given in viṣṇu darmam. āḻvār is quoting this incident in this pāsuram.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The samsāris told āḻvār “you had told us to recite his divine names. Due to our sins, we are only able to talk about various worldly matters and are unable to recite emperumān’s divine names” āḻvar responded saying “in that case, hearing emperumān’s divine names would uplift you” and told them about mudgalōpākhyānam (the story of a person called mudgan). He further added “I am unable to bear the samsāris’ sorrows because they continue to suffer in samsāram instead of being uplifted by saying ‘tiruvarangam is the dwelling place of emperumān whose divine names are so great’” நமனும் முற்கலனும் பேச நரகில் நின்றார்கள் கேட்க நரகமே சுவர்க்கமாகும் நாமங்கள் உடைய நம்பி அவனது ஊர் அரங்கமென்னாது அயர்த்து வீழ்ந்தளிய மாந்தர் கவலையுள் படுகின்றாரென்று அதனுக்கே கவல்கின்றேனே.*

**namanum muṟkalanum pēsa** – mudgala bhagavān asked yama for the reason for his good treatment and yama responded saying that it was because he had mentioned [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine name. This is all that happened in narakam (hell). yama did not mention emperumān’s divine name for his own uplifting neither did mudgalan ask yama and yama preached him. He did not mention it so that people coming to hell to get beaten by him, causing pain to his hands, would get out of narakam. yama once asked a jīvan (an ātmā) “*kim tvayā nārcitō dēva: kēsava: klēśanāśana:*” – was kēśavan, with beautifully braided hair, who removes all the sorrows, not vorśipped by you? It was not that the jīvātmā was despised by yama when he reached hell to suffer the punishment for all the wrong doings in samsāram. It was out of sheer fear that he would lose his position since a person who had recited emperumān’s divine name has come to narakam that yama welcomed mudgalan and spoke about the greatness of divine names, says the vyākhyātā.

**naragil ninṟārgaḷ kētka** – they \[the jīvātmās who had gone to hell to suffer for the sins that they had committed\] did not listen to the divine names at the time of carrying out penance for indulging in sinful activities, after feeling sad and vowing not to indulge in such activities. It was at the time of suffering in hell as a result of being punished for their sins that they heard the divine names. Even then it was not as a disciple \[listening to his ācāryan\] with samit (stick used in yajñams or rituals) in hand. During a conversation between two persons, they happened to hear the divine name. This is about all. If someone asks “When you were indulging in sinful activities, did not anyone mention these divine names and did not you not hear them?” they will say that since they were vain while indulging in such activities these words vouldn’t have entered  their ears. It is only now when they are experiencing sorrow that they think “will not someone say a good word?” and listen to the divine names in this status.

**naragamē suvargamāgum** – the hell itself became heaven which gives pleasure. How did the hell become heaven? It could change if [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) changes his *sankalpam* (solemn vow). śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 116.13 says “*vibhīśaṇa vidēyam hi lankaiśvaryam idam krutam*” the same lankā which was the repository of all evils during rāvaṇa’s time became the repository of good persons due to śrī rāma’s katākśam (glance). Like this, did the hell become heaven due to the possessor of the name (emperumān)?…….

**nāmangaḷ** – This change was the result of the greatness of the names. Even though it is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who grants the fruits of effort, since he gives that only *after* divine names are recited, it is not out of place to state that it is the divine names that brought about the change \[from hell to heaven\].

**nāmangaḷ udaiya nambi** – emperumān becomes perfect because he has these divine names. While everyone attains him and gets benefitted, these divine names add to his greatness. mahābhāratam says “*dēvō nāma sahasravān*” – the one with innumerable faces, eyes, feet, hands; the one who pervades everything; the one who knows everything – such a dēvan nārāyaṇan has thousands of names. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi  9.3.1 “*pērāyiram koṇḍadōr pīdudaiyan*” – he is great because he has thousand names. [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4.6.1 says “nāraṇan tamannai naragam pugāḷ” – if you name your child as nāraṇan, the child’s mother will not enter hell. sahasranāma adyāya says *“nārayaṇa śabdamātram vimukta dukkā: śukhinō bhavanti”* – by merely hearing the sound nārāyaṇa, one gets rid of his sorrows and attains comfort.

**avanadu Ur arangam ennādu** – all  that the samsāris have to say is that the permanent dwelling place of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who attained perfection due to his names is tiruvarangam (śrīrangam). But what to say of their pāpa (sins) that prevent them from saying such an easy to pronounce word, but containing great benefits! This samsāram is full of people who decide not to live near others who recite divine names or drive them out of their places (such as krimi kaṇḍa cōḻan) or torture their sons who recite divine names (such as hiraṇya kaṣyap). An incident is quoted by our pūrvācāryas – a person was in his death bed. He was taught [*tirumantram*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html) [(aśtākśari)](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html) and was asked to recite it. He said “*āmāgil sollippārkkiṟēn*” (if possible I shall say). Both the tirumantaram and what he said contain 8 syllables; instead of reciting tirumantram, he said “*āmāgil sollippārkkiṟēn*” and breathed his last. Such was the strength of his sins that they vouldn’t allow him to recite divine name and attain emperumān.

**ayarttu vīḻndu** – forgetting the divine names of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is the natural relative for all, these samsāris are indulging in worldly matters and losing their balance. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) in periya tirumoḻi 6.2.2. says “*maṟandēn unnai munnam …… iṟandēn*” – I forgot you earlier….and I died. gāruda purāṇam pūrva 222.22 says “*yanmuhūrtam kśaṇam vāpi vāsudēvō na cintyatē* I *sā hānistanmahaccidram sā bhrāntis sā ca vikriyā* īī” – if in a muhūrta \[48 minutes of our time\] or in a moment vāsudēva is not thought of, that is a great loss; that is great sorrow; that becomes illusory; that is deterioration. Thus [āḻvars](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) and riśis also said the same meaning. The great samsāris who are qualified to recite the divine names should recite āḻvārs’ pāsurams such as “*tāmuḷarē*” (iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 21) – the [cētanar](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s are there (to praise emperumān with the physical body and the sensory organs given by emperumān) and “*nāvāyil uṇḍē*” (mudal tiruvandādi  95) – tongue is there inside (to recite tirumantram).

**kavalaiyuḷ paduginṟārenṟu** – being caught in sorrow

**adanukkē kaval(r)ginṟēnē** – I suffer because of that itself. While being qualified to recite the divine names and immerse themselves in the ocean of happiness, these samsāris are immersing in the ocean of sorrow and [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that he is suffering because of their action. The reason for adding “E” to adanukku to make it adanukkē (only for that) is because instead of suffering for self or suffering for self and others, he is suffering only for others (samsāris). The identity of a śrivaiṣṇava is one who cannot bear the suffering of others and āḻvār shows that quality here. *kavalai* means sorrow and kaval(r)ginṟēnē means suffering.

We shall move on to the 13<sup>th</sup> pāsuram in the next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 13. Eriyu nir veri kol velai

*eṟiyum nīr veṟikoḷ vēlai mānilattu uyirgaḷ ellām veṟi koḷ pūntuḷaba mālai viṇṇavar kōnai ētta aṟivilā manisar ellām arangam enṟu aḻaippar āgil poṟiyil vāḻ naragam ellām pul eḻundu oḻiyum anṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

eṟiyum nīr – water with lapping waves; veṟikoḷ – (from the meat) having bad odour; vēlai – surrounded by ocean; mānilattu uyirgaḷ ellam – all the cētanars (sentient entities) on this huge mass of land called earth; veṟi koḷ – having sweet fragrance; pūm – beautiful; viṇṇavar kōnai – the lord of nityasūris \[śrivaikuṇṭanātan\]; ētta – (are meant to) only worship; aṟivu ilā manisar ellām – these men without any knowledge; arangam enṟu aḻaippar āgil – if they say “tiruvarangam” \[śrīrangam\]; poṟiyil vāḻ – living, controlled by the senses; naragam ellām – this entire world, which is like narakam (hell); pul eḻundu – sprouting grass; oḻiyum anṟē – will it not go waste?

**Перевод:**

**eṟiyum nīr veṟi koḷ vēlai mānilattu uyirgaḷ ellam – all the creatures living on this huge earth, surrounded by oceans with lapping waves and smell of fish. āḻvār has used the term “eṟiyum nīr” which means that these cētanars (sentient beings) who are qualified to live comfortably in the expansive paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) are living like crowded jackal caught in flood (periya tirumoḻi 11.8.5 “veḷḷattidaippatta nariyinam pōlē” – like a pack of jackals caught in flood).  “veṟi koḷ vēlai” – he says that instead of being in śrīvaikuṇṭam enjoying the sweet fragrance of tuḷasi \[which he brings in the next line\] these cētanars are on earth, being the target of foul smelling ocean. “mānilam” – earth with an expanse of 50 crore yōjana \[one yōjana is 10 miles or 16 kilometers\]\[ a word of caution here – the term earth (bhūmi) as our pūrvācāryas say should not be mistaken for this earth that we live. It encompasses all the 14 worlds (atala, sutala, pātāla etc comprising 7 worlds below the earth that we are familiar with and bhū:, bhuva:, suva: etc comprising 7 worlds from earth upwards. The entire distance between these 14 worlds is 50 crore yōjana\]. “uyirgaḷ” – if the connection with these dēham (body) is removed, the ātmā is qualified to be like śrī koustubam (the radiant garland adorning emperumān’s chest); instead he is remaining here like a wastrel, feels the āḻvār. “ellām” – includes the independent-minded celestial people (dēvas) and their citizens, the human race on earth.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Despite āḻvār telling the samsāris many beneficial words, they were not in a position to receive such advice. Not considering their lowliness (since they were not listening to good words), he thinks of their sorrowful state and tells himself “even though they do not realise that their svarūpam (basic nature) is to serve only emperumān, if they say once, without any restraint, ‘tiruvarangam’, this hell-like samsāram will disappear and grass will grow where it once was”. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings now எறியும் நீர் வெறிகொள் வேலை மாநிலத்து உயிர்கள் எல்லாம் வெறி கொள் பூந்துளவ மாலை விண்ணவர் கோனை யேத்த அறிவிலா மனிசர் எல்லாம் அரங்கம் என்று அழைப்பராகில் பொறியில் வாழ் நரகம் எல்லாம் புல் எழுந்து ஒழியும் அன்றே.*

**veṟikoḷ puṇṭhuḷabamālai viṇnavar kōnai ētta** –  It is not only nityasūris who, looking at emperumān adorned with sweet smelling and beautiful tuḷasi garland, worship him. Even samsāris are meant to worship him. sumitrā tells lakśmaṇa prior to his departure for forest with śri rāma and sītāp pirātti “*sruśtastvam vanavāsāya svanuraktas suhrujjanē* I *rāmē pramādam mā kārśī: putra! bhrātari gaccat*i īī” – lakśmaṇa! You were created for dwelling in the forest. You are having deep affection for śrī rāma who loves you dearly. My son, when śrī rāma walks ahead, do not make the mistake (of admiring and engaging with his gait and losing sight of those who would harm him).  Just as lakśmāṇa was created only to serve śrī rāma, the samsāris were created only to serve and worship emperumān.

“People with knowledge \[such as nityasūris\] were meant to get this sort of fortune in vorśipping emperumān and caring for him. Can samsāris, with low level of knowledge get this?” doubts āḻvār and responds to this in the next words…

**aṟivilā manisar ellām arangam enṟu aḻaipparāgil** – despite being born in the human race where they are supposed to be with the knowledge of discriminating \[between good and bad, high and low, dos’ and don’ts’ etc\], and even if they do not have any knowledge, and any involvement with *tiruvarangam* because of lack of that knowledge, it is enough if they merely say “*tiruvarangam*”. Usage of the word “*āgil*” (if) denotes that many of the samsāris with high degree of pāpa (sins) could not say even this.

**poṟiyil vāḻ naragam** – those living in leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm), enslaved by their senses. “*poṟi*” – in the meaning of trap, this indicates that the senses, in all situations, would trap samsāris so that they cannot escape in any way. “*naragam*” – to those without knowledge, yama lōkam (the dwelling place of yama) is narakam (hell). But for those with knowledge, samsāram itsel f is hell. samsāram makes samsāris believe that living a life on it is the greatest thing and does not allow them to think of paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam).  nammāḻvār says in tiruvāimoḻi  8.1.9 “*nanṟum anjuvan naragam nān adaidal*” – I am scared that I will lose my *śēśatva jñānam* (knowledge that I am your servitor) if I stay in this samsāram called narakam.

**ellām pul undu oḻiyum anṟē** – if all the samsāris leave samsāram and reach paramapadam, the leelā vibhūti will lose its fields of samsāris and become arid.

We shall now move to the 14<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan
