## 14. Vandinam muralum solai

*vaṇḍinam muralum sōlai mayilinam ālum sōlai koṇḍal mīdaṇavum sōlai kuyilinam kūvum sōlai aṇḍar kōn amarum sōlai aṇi tiruvarangam ennā miṇḍar pāynduṇṇum sōṟṟai vilakki nāykku iduminīrē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaṇḍinam – a swarm of bees; muralum sōlai – gardens where the bees keep humming; mayil inam – a muster of peacocks; Alum sōlai – gardens where the peacocks are dancing; koṇḍal mīdu aṇavum – clouds overhanging and hugging; sōlai – gardens; kuyil inam – a bevy of quails; kūvum sōlai – gardens where the quails keep calling out to each other; aṇḍar kōn – sarvēśvaran (emperumān) who is the lord of nityasūris; amarum sōlai – gardens where emperumān has taken permanent residence; aṇi – like an ornament (to samsāram); tiru arangam ennā – those who do not pronounce the word “śrīrangam”; miṇḍar – ungrateful fool; pāyndu uṇṇum sōṟṟai – falling over \[others\] to eat food; vilakki – prevent (them from eating); nīr nāykku idumin – you give that \[food\] to a dog

**Перевод:**

**vaṇḍinam muralum śolai – just like people crowd together near (flowing) holy rivers and have a bath, the bees come in swarms to these gardens as they contain lot of honey. “muralum” – since they have consumed honey up to their necks, they are not able to stay in one place and keep humming around. This is similar to nityātmās and muktātmās in śrivaikuṇṭam, enjoying emperumān and as a consequence, singing sāma gānam (the musical notes of sāma vēda).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār counselled the samsāris about the divine names of emperumān and his divyadēśam but the samsāris did not pay heed. He then recited the previous pāsuram, as if to himself, but in reality again to the samsāris only. Despite taking so much efforts to say good things to them, seeing their indifference and not being involved with bhagavat viśayam (knowing and enjoying about emperumān) he becomes averse to them. Just as a person chews a block of camphor to rid of foul taste in the mouth, he immerses himself in the sweetness of tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) and laments that while the samsāris too are born to enjoy this sweetness they are not showing any inclination towards this. Thus he brings to an end the parōpadēśam (counselling others) that he started with the 4th pāsuram. Even though this pāsuram appears as if āḻvār is talking to himself, it is considered as part of parōpadēsam. The good words coming out of a sātvic (purely good) person should be followed by others too. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings: வண்டினம் முரலும் சோலை மயிலினம் ஆலும் சோலை கொண்டல் மீதணவும் சோலை குயிலினம் கூவும் சோலை அண்டர் கோன் அமரும் சோலை அணி திருவரங்க மென்னா மிண்டர் பாய்ந்துண்ணும் சோற்றை விலக்கி நாய்க்கு இடுமினீரே.*

**mayil inam Alum sōlai** – looking at the verdant gardens and hearing the humming of the bees, the peacocks think that monsoon has arrived \[the swarm of bees appear like cloud to these peacocks\] and start dancing in ecstasy.

**koṇḍal mīdu aṇavum sōlai** – hearing the humming of the bees, looking at the vastness and greenery of the garden and mistaking tiruvarangam for ocean, the clouds come over this place and hang around in a hugging fashion. Another interpretation is that these gardens have risen up to the level of cloud. As [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says about *tirumālinruncōlai* in [tiruvāimoḻi 2.10.8](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/06/thiruvaimozhi-2-10-8-valam-seydhu/) “*mayal migu poḻil sūḻ māliruncōlai*” similar to tirumāliruncōlai deluding the clouds, the gardens in śrīrangam also delude clouds into believing that it is an ocean. In tirumāliuncōlai, because of the darkness of the gardens, the starting point and the end-point were not clear, creating a delusion. Here, the gardens make the clouds and peacocks to mistake something for something else \[as explained above\] and create delusion in them.

**kuyil inam kūvum sōlai** – unlike the bees, clouds and peacocks, which came out of their places, the quails remain wherever they are, and call out one to another, due to the coolness of the place.

**aṇḍar kōn amarum sōlai** – Just as bees, clouds, peacocks and quails, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) also looks at the expanse of the gardens here and their sweetness, forgets that he is the lord of *nityasūris* (permanent dwellers) in *paramapadam* (śrīvaikuṇṭam) and wants to be here permanently. Just as emperumān is the refuge for all the samsāris who he protects, this place is the refuge for sarvēśvaran himself. “*aṇḍar*” refers to *nityasūris* and “*aṇḍarkōn”* refers to their lord, *parapapadanātan*.  “*amarum*” –  forgetting *parapamadam*, he takes permanent residence here. The term *paramapadam* also implies *vyūha* (his *kśīrasamudra* or *tiruppāṟkadal or milk ocean*), *vibhava* (various incarnations such as rāma, kriṣṇa et al).

**sōlai aṇi tiruvarangam** – one meaning is that the gardens are like an ornament to the temple here. Another meaning is that the temple is an ornament to samsāram (materialistic realm).

**tiruvangam ennā miṇḍar** – While he has taken up permanent residence in this place for their sake, the samsāris, like fools, are not mentioning the name of the place that he is residing in. *miṇḍar* – they do not realise their present position nor do they know what is coming up next. In other words, hell and womb are catching the scruff of their necks to push them and they keep desiring for their food and clothing to stay in samsāram forever.

**pāyndu uṇṇum sōṟṟai vilakki nāykku idumin nīrē** – those who do not say the word *tiruvarangam* are ungrateful people. They eat by stealing the materials from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Remove the food that they are eating and give it to dogs, ordains the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) of good people.  sarvēśvaran, who created the samsāris, out of consideration for their well-being, has left śrīvaikuṇṭam and is dwelling in the temple. Forgetting such a great help, these samsāris are enjoying their food. Remove that food and give to a dog, which is grateful to someone who gives it food once and remains at his doorstep to protect him. *nīrē* – this refers to good people who remove materials from bad people and give them to good people (such as [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/)).

We shall move on to the 15<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 15. Meyyarkke meyyanagum

*puḷ kodi udaiya kōman – te lord vho has garuda as his flag vidiyilā ennaip pōla – an unfortunate person suc as ī am (vho for a long time did not get involved vit matters related to emperumān) meyyarkku – tose vho do not have hatred (tovards emperumān) meyyan āgum – displays his svarūpam (his basic nature) poyyarkku – for tose vho are interested in matters (oter tan emperumān) poyyan āgum – vill display falseness (vitout displaying his true self) uyyappōm uṇarvinārgatku – tose vho have te knovledge tat tey śould knov hov to uplift temselves oruvan enṟu uṇarnda pinnai – after tey knov tat tere is “īśvaran” aiyappādu aṟuttu – removing te (remaining) doubts tōnṟum – displaying himself aḻagan – emperumān vho enslaves te entire vorld by his beauty ūr – dvelling place arangam anrē – vould be tiruvarangam*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**meyyarkkē meyyanāgum – the “mey” (truth) that is referred to here is the advēśam (non-hatred) without any admixture which lies on the part of cētanan (sentient entity). For a very long time, the cētanan had been living life only for himself. Since it had not yielded lasting benefit, it is called as false. Avoiding that life (of living for himself), to live for emperumān from now onvards is termed as “mey” (truth). If it is only the non-hatred feeling that is now with the cētanan, how is paramacētanan (emperumān) disposed towards him? meyyanāgum – he behaves towards the cētanan who has only non-hatred feeling towards him, in the same way that he behaves with his mind, body and speech towards the great ātmās who live their lives totally for him; i.e. he displays his svarūpam (basic nature), rūpam (physical form) and vibhūti (wealth) to the cētanan. The same word “mey” has been used with two totally varied meanings in the two words seen above – in the first, it means showing non-hatred towards emperumān and in the second, it means emperumān behaving with equanimity with his mind, body and speech. How can this fit, asks the vyākhyātā and responds to the query himself saying that this particular section \[the 3rd section as we have seen in the avatārikai\] deals with emperumān’s qualities and hence it is apt to consider that with his mind, body and speech, he is truthful. emperumān is full of auspicious qualities and hence the meaning of non-hatred cannot be taken as a quality for him when defining the second “mey” Since emperumān and cētanan are strongly bound by the “father-son” relationship, emperumān has  to protect the cētanan considering his non-hatred feeling towards him as a quality. Since emperumān is avātptasamastakāman (with no desire unfulfilled) there is no need for emperumān to expect more than this from cētanan. For someone who has been all through living his life only for himself, to show non-hatred towards emperumān to get out of this vortex is acceptable. It is not easy for the cētanan to suddenly change himself and live only for emperumān. To protect the cētanan based on this advēśam alone is a glorious aspect for emperumān and not  a shortcoming. For these reasons we take different meanings for the two versions of “mey”. Moreover, only nityasūris can display unadulterated truthfulness to emperumān and it is not possible for samsāris to display this quality and samsāris can attain mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) with whatever quality they have. In śrī rāmāyaṇa yudda kāṇḍam, śrī rāma tells his army chieftains on vibhīśaṇa’s surrender “mitrabhāvēna samprāptam na tyajēyam katancana I dōśō yatyapi tasya syāt satām ētadagarhitam īī” – I shall never let down this vibhīśaṇa who has approached us in the mask of a friend; even if he has any dōśam (fault), let it be; good people will not treat him with irreverance. Thus we have to take only the meaning that emperumān displays truthfulness to the samsāri with his mind, body and speech in harmony. The non-hatred quality displayed by the samsāri, leaving aside his goal of living only for himself all along, cannot be a path for mōkśam. It is only for the knowledgeable that it will be part of their svarūpam (basic nature) to live life only for emperumān. This desire will be there during the time of enjoying emperumān in śrīvaikuṇṭam too. Hence it will not be a path to mōkśam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the first three pāsurams (hymns), āḻvār spoke about the good fortune that he had in reciting emperumān’s divine names. In the next eleven pāsurams he spoke to the samsāris and counselled them to extol the divine names of emperumān. Since they did not pay heed, he stepped back a little in the previous pāsuram. From this pāsuram \[15th\] until the 24th pāsuram, for 10 pāsurams, he speaks about how he benefitted from the causeless mercy of emperumān. From being one among samsāris, emperumān made him to counsel the other samsāris, in the bargain removing his doubts at the beginning and showering him with paramabhakti (final stage in devotion in which the jīvātmā cannot sustain himself even for a moment if separated from emperumān) at the end. All these were done by emperumān himself, says the āḻvār. nammāḻvār, in his tiruvāimoḻi 2.7.8 says “ennait tīmanam keduttāy unakku en seygēn’ – you diverted me from vices; can I ever repay you? In this pāsuram āḻvār says that once we remove the feeling of hatred towards emperumān, he shows all his greatness and that once a person realises that emperumān is there, he removes all the doubts from that person’s mind. He affirms in his case that once he got rid of his hatred towards emperumān, he showed his greatness which the āḻvār enjoyed. Let us look at the pāsuram. மெய்யர்க்கே மெய்யனாகும் விதியிலா என்னைப் போலப் பொய்யர்க்கே பொய்யனாகும் புட்கொடி யுடைய கோமான் உய்யப்போம் உணர்வினார்கட்கு ஒருவன் என்று உணர்ந்த பின்னை ஐயப்பாடு அறுத்துத் தோன்றும் அழகனுர் அரங்கமன்றே meyyarkkē meyyanāgum vidiyilā ennaippōlap poyyarkkē poyyanāgum putkodi udaiya kōman uyyappōm uṇarvinārgatku oruvan enṟu uṇarnda pinnai aiyappādu aṟuttut tōnṟum aḻaganūr arangamanrē*

**poyyarkke poyyanāgum** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) gives up on the samsāri and punishes him only when  he finds that there is no way that the samsāri will even put on an act of showing no hatred towards him. emperumān can do something good to the samsāri, taking this falsified non-hatred as truth. This is when emepurmān becomes “*poyyan*” (being false). In olden days when a person is bitten by a snake, they will scratch the person’s forehead and consider him ad dead and give him up when they find that there is no wetness \[no blood flow\]. In the same way, emperumān gives up on the samsāri and retires when he finds that there is no way he can uplift the samsāri. The long syllable in *meyyarkkē* denotes non-hatred feeling. The long syllable in *poyyarkkē* denotes that the samsāri does not even put on an act of showing non-hatred feeling towards emperumān. “mey” refers to truth, leading to beneficiary knowledge about emperumān. “poy” refers to worldly matters which are different from emperumān, very lowly and uncertain.

Where have we seen that emperumān is true to those who do not show hatred towards him and punishes those who are unfaithful to him? …………

**ennaippōla** – like me. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) quotes himself as the example for the above. āḻvār says “for a long time I was indifferent towards him and he did not come anywhere near me. From the day that I showed no hatred towards him, he fell head over heels on me and took me under his wings”

**vidiyilā ennaippōla** – unfortunate like me. When he is so simple, I was engaging myself in worldly pursuits, forgetting about him. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) feels sorry that he had lost [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s grace all along and hates himself. poygai āḻvār, in the first tiruvandādi 16 says “*paḻudē pala pagalum pōyina enṟu anji aḻudēn…*” – I cried bitterly for all that I had lost all along by not engaging with emperumān. In śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 17.14, vālmīki says “*yaśca rāmam na paśyēttu yaśca rāmō na paśyati* I *nidita: sa vasēllōkē svātmāpyēnam vigarhatē* īī” – the one who has not seen rāma or the one who has not been seen by rāma, such a person lives in the world reproached even by his [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/).

Is it that emperumān doesn’t have anyone else whom he can take under his wings other than this cētanan who puts on an act of not showing hatred towards him? ……

**putkodi udiya kōman** – the Lord who has garuda as his flag. Mentioning garuda is equivalent to mentioning all the nityasūris. [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/), in  his stōtra ratnam 41 says “*dāsas sakhā vāhanam āsanam dvaja: yastē vidānam  vyajanam trayīmaya:* I *upastitam tēna purō garutmatā tvadanghrisammarda kiṇangasōyinā* īī” – the garudāḻvān who is having vēdas as his body is your servitor, friend, vehicle, throne, flag, roof, flag. He has as his identification the scar formed by your divine feet constantly touching his body, due to which he gains brilliance. In his presence, I have vorśipped you, says āḷavandār. This garudan is taken as an example by [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) for referring to all nityasūris who are there for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). What surprises āḻvār is that such an emperumān, who has nityasūris , each of whom is capable of carrying out service which can be done only by many of his true servitors, has taken him \[āḻvār\] under his wings, based on his non-hatred quality. vyākhyātā quotes another nityasūri’s servitorśip from stōtratnam 40 “*nivāsa śayyāsana pādukāmśukōpadāna varśādapavāraṇadibhi:*I *śarīrabhēdais tava śēśatām gatair yatōcitam śēśa itīritē janai:* īī” – ādiśēśan serves emperumān as his divine palace, mattress, throne, sandals, divine dress, divine cushion, umbrella and so many other services; serving him at various points of time in various forms, appropriately. Thus each of emperumān’s nityasūris is capable of serving him in varied ways which are difficult to match even by an assembly of servitors. *kōmān* – [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi 1.1.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-1-uyarvara-uyarnalam/) “*ayarvaṟum amarargaḷ atipati*” – he is the Lord of tireless nityasūris. Why is such a great person, who has an army of servitors, longing for a samsāri to take him under his wings? Just as even a crorepati would still look for a coin that had fallen from his hand because that is his, emperumān also looks for every samsāri, since every samsāri is his property.

**uyyappōm uṇarvinārgatku** – for all those who have been living a falsified life and have extricated themselves from that life  and thinking what is the way to redeem themselves. For those who do not have this feeling it means an end to their lives. [bhagavat gītā 2.63](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-63/) says “*smruti bhramsāt buddi nāśō buddi nāśāt praṇaśyati*” – intelligence gets ruined because his memory fades. Since intelligence is ruined, he gets immersed further in samsāram and gets ruined. Thus kriṣṇa also shows what happens when the samsāri  gets the feeling of things getting ruined. What is the feeling of *uyyappōm uṇarvu*? It is a feeling  when, by accident, one does  a puṇya (virtuous deed) due to which he realises the position that he was in, thinks about his svarūpam (basic nature) and  finds out how he can get out of this position and get to a stage where he can redeem himself.

**oruvan enṟu uṇarndapinnai** – after getting the knowledge that there is someone called [īśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/). For those who know that the body is not the only entity existing, that there is another entity called [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) , different from and superior to the body, which exists and that there is an īśvaran who is the Lord of the ātmā. There are many samsāris who accept that there are 24 [acit (insentient)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) entities and that there is a 25<sup>th</sup> entity called ātmā but many among them do not accept that there is a 26<sup>th</sup> entity called īśvaran.

**aiyappādu aṟuttuttōnṟum** – only when the samsāri does not accept the entity of sarvēśvaran,  he has the doubt. Once he accepts sarvēśvaran’s existence, īśvaran himself tells him “I am the object to be attained; I remove your hurdles and enemies” and removes his doubts. How does he remove these doubts? ……

**aḻagan** – arjuna tells kriṣṇa ([bhagavat gītā 2.7](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-7/)) “*kārppaṇya dōśōpahatasvbhāva: pruccāmi tvā darmasammūda cētā:* I *yaccrēya: syānniścitambrūhi tanmē śiśyas tē’ham śādi mām tvām prapannam* īī” – not knowing what is to be done, confused on seeing different types of darmams (virtuous ways), I ask you; whatever you decide as to what is good and what is bad for me, please tell me that. I am your disciple. Direct me, who has surrendered to you, correctly. kriṣṇa removed the doubt of arjuna through discourse. For those who do not have such knowledge, he removes their doubts through his physical beauty. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his triuvāimoḻi 9.7.3 says “*sengamalattalar pōlum kaṇ kai kāl senganivāi angamalattilai pōlum tirumēniadigaḷukkē*” \[his divine eyes, divine arms, divine legs and divine mouth are like just blossomed reddish lotus; and the divine feet are like the leaves of the beautiful lotus\] In tiruvāimoḻi 4.5.10, he says “*kaṇḍavṟṟāl tanadē ulagena ninṟān”* \[by looking at the way he is standing, it is evident that the entire world is his i.e. it is under his control\]. His beauty is such that, that he is *paramporuḷ* (highest entity) will be known to everyone who looks at him.

A doubt arises that this beauty can be enjoyed only at *paramapadam.* [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) responds saying

**Ur arangamanrē** – There is  no need to go to *paramapadam* to enjoy his beauty. This can be enjoyed in this samsāram, in tiruvarangam itself, he says. If paramapadam is his court hall, fit for an emperor, once the court sitting is over, the palace where he takes rest is tiruvarangam. This is why he rests permanently in śrīrangam.

We shall move on to the 16<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 16. Sudanayk kalvanagi

*sūdanāyk kaḷvanāgit tūrttarōdu isainda kālam mādarār kayal kaṇ ennum valaiyuḷ pattu aḻunduvēnaip pōdarē enṟu sollip pundiyuḷ pugundu tanpāl ādaram peruga vaitta aḻagan uṟ arangam anṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sūdan āy – saying that there is no īśvaran (emperumān), darmam (virtuous ways) and adarmam (evil ways); kaḷvan āgi – claiming that ātmā (soul) is mine and not īśvaran’s; dūrttarōdu isainda kālam – during the time of being together with those who are engaged in worldly pursuits; mādarār – women’s; kayal kaṇ ennum – in the beautiful fish-like eyes; valaiyuḷ pattu – caught in the net; azunduvēnai – I, who am sinking; pōdu arē enṟu solli – calling out “come here”; pundiyuḷ pugundu – entering my heart; tanpāl ādaram peruga vaitta aḻagan – emperumān, who created a flood of affection towards him; Ur – dwelling place; arangam anṟē – is it not tiruvarangam?

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār divined that his doubts regarding the path to attaining emperumān and the puruśārtam (goal) were clarified by emperumān. Having got rid of the doubt, in this pāsuram, āḻvār says that emperumān created a flood of affection in him, towards emperumān. சூதனாய்க் கள்வனாகித் தூர்த்தரோடு இசைந்த காலம் மாதரார் கயல் கண் என்னும் வலையில் பட்டு அழுந்துவேனைப் போதரே என்று சொல்லிப் புந்தியில் புகுந்து தன்பால் ஆதரம் பெருக வைத்த அழகன் ஊர் அரங்கம் அன்றே.*

**sūdanāyk kaḷvanāgi** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) describes his earlier status. *sūdu* (deception) is stealing someone else’s belongings even as the other person is watching. *kaḷavu* (theft) is taking someone else’s belonging, thinking it’s one’s own. In the context of this pāsuram, these two words indicate the negation of meanings as propounded in *pramāṇams* (evidence). For example, the deadly act of considering the [ātmā (soul)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) which is the property of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as one’s own is *kaḷavu*. People with good behaviour would think that ātmā is a servitor to emperumān, that emperumān is the master of ātmā, that any act which makes emperumān happy is puṇya (virtuous deed) and that which makes emperumān unhappy is pāpa (sin). But people with fallacious reasoning will object to all these and call these as false. Such an act on the part of the illogical persons to cause delusion in the minds of the others and convert them as atheists \[here atheism is that which does not believe in vēdas\] similar to themselves is *sūdu*. The *sūdu* and *kaḷavu* mentioned in this pāsuram are more dangerous than the definitions that we had seen at the beginning of this paragraph with regard to worldly issues. vyākhyātā explains this through examples. The crime committed by a person stealing a gem is far higher than that committed by a person stealing a stick. Similarly stealing an article from a vaidika (one practising vēdas) brāhmaṇa is more serious than stealing the same article from a caṇḍāḷan (one who is below the 4<sup>th</sup> varṇa). Here (in this pāsuram), the stolen entity [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is very dear to emperumān, like his koustubha maṇi \[an ornament adorning his chest\]. Thus the sin committed in stealing this invaluable entity ātmā from the lord of all, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), is unbounded.

If what the samsāri indulges in is deception and theft, he would be moving with other samsāris with similar bent of mind. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that in the next phrase…..

**dūrttarōdu isainda kālam** – the time spent, instead of with emperumān who is our ordained lord, with those who are fully engaged in worldly pursuits, and who are not congruous with our basic nature.  *isainda kālam* – since āḻvār is unable to bring himself to even think about that period, he feels saddened that he spent his life this way. He is unable to put a time frame for the number of years that he had spent in the company of such people. While the svarūpam of the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is to be with other śrīvaiṣṇavas, he had gone astray by being with those who cause incalculable harm, āḻvār laments. While being with bhāgavatas (followers of [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)) will enable one to get involved with matters relating to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), being with people going after worldly pursuits will only push us into the quagmire of worldly matters.

**mādarār kayaṟkaṇ ennum valaiyuḷ pattu aḻunduvēnai** – I, who gets caught in the net of fish-like eyes of women, and unable to extricate from it.  *mādarār* – the samsāri immersed in worldly pursuits will look at a woman merely as a woman and will not bother about her lineage or practices. kayaṟkaṇ – the women, though sharp like a knife in their hearts, will enslave the samsāri with their mere glance, by making him believe that they cannot sustain themselves if he leaves. *valaiyuḷ pattu* – while it goes by the name of eye, it is actually a net which is used for catching samsāri, just as nets are used to catch animals. *pattu aḻunduvēnai* – just like an animal which gets caught in a net will not be able to escape, the samsāri also gets trapped by the net in the form of eye, unable to extricate himself. Just as the person who laid the net to catch the animal will not come near the net, these women, who had laid a trap for him, will not make themselves easily available to him. They know the lowliness of the so called enjoyment since at the end of the act, they have to go in search of water to purify themselves and hence keep themselves at a distance.  *aḻunduvēnai* – since the samsāri does not know their inner thoughts, he gets trapped by the outer net like eyes and suffers.  *valaiyutpattu aḻunduvēnai* – the term *pattu* denotes that it is impossible to extricate; *valaiyuḷ* indicates that there is nothing to enjoy and only sorrow will remain; *aḻunduvēnai*  indicates that this sorrow is made to appear as if it were a pleasure.

Now [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) explains how [periya perumāḷ (śrī ranganātan)](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) redeemed him from the sorrowful state that he had got sunk in…

**pōdarē enṟu** – periya perumāḷ asks the āḻvār “āḻvīr! (Oh, āḻvār) desiring what, have you sunk yourself in?” āḻvār responds “due to my poor taste, despite knowing that it is not proper, I have sunk myself in the eyes which engaged me. If a virtuous person were to show his beautiful eyes befitting his nature, I would engage with those eyes and enjoy that virtuous person” periya perumāḷ decides that he would redeem āḻvār from the lowly eyes of women by showing his eyes. Removing the screen between them, he shows āḻvār his divine eyes. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) in periya tirumoḻi 7.1.9 says “uyirkkellām tāyāyaḷikkinṟa taṇḍāmaraikkaṇ” – the comforting eyes which shower motherly affection on all creatures. periya perumāḷ tells āḻvār “āḻvīr! Look at the person who has the eyes that you like so much! See here!” āḻvār wonders at the beauty of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine eyes and compares with the eyes that he had seen earlier (women’s) and decides that these eyes \[of emperumān\] are befitting his svarūpam and is able to redeem his heart from the fishnet-like eyes of women. hanuman tells sītāpirātti “*rāma: kamalapatrākśa: sarvasatva manōhara:* I *rūpa dākśiṇyasampanna: prasūtō janakātmajē!* īī” – Oh sītapirātti! rāma has lotus-petal like divine eyes. He attracts all creatures (including a monkey like me) with those beautiful eyes. He is full of qualities such as beauty of the physical form and kindness of his ātmā. Thus even a sworn bachelor such as hanuman, was attracted to and got engaged with those divine eyes.

**pundiyuḷ pugundu** – the word pundi is normally used with thoughts \[memory\]. Here it refers to the heart from where thoughts emanate. The meaning conveyed here is “he entered the heart as antaryāmi (indvelling soul) of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). The reason for āḻvar moving away from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) from time immemorial is his heart. When emperumān wanted to enter his heart, āḻvār did not oppose it and hence he entered his heart.

What did he do after entering his heart? ………

**tanpāl ādaram peruga vaitta** – he made [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) to show his affection, which he had been showing all along towards worldly matters, now towards him. Just as water is diverted from fallow land to the field, emperumān made āḻvār’s affection beneficial. p*eruga* – he made the affection grow very big, bigger than [cit (sentient)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) entity, bigger than [īśvara entity (himself)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 10.10.10 “*adanil periya*” – bigger than everything. *vaittu* – āḻvār had nothing in him for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to do this. It was due to emperumān’s causeless mercy, similar to those keeping water pots in summer for all people.

By displaying what, did emperumān make [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) to show affection?  ……

**aḻagan** – he showed his unparalleled divine beauty, just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 5.3.4 “*pēramar kādal kadal pruaiya viḷaivitta kāramar mēni nam kaṇṇan*” – the physical beauty of kriṣṇa was such that the love that it created in the eyes of the beholder was much bigger than the ocean. For upāsakas (those who are immersed in bhakti yōga), karma yōga and jñāna yōga would complement bhakti yōga. In the same way, for [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s beauty took the place of karma jñāna yōga and complemented his bhakti. emperumān’s beauty removed his doubt “*aiyyappādu aṟuttut tōnṟum aḻagan”* \[[previous pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-15/)\] and developed his affection “*ādaram perugavaitta aḻagan*” – this pāsuram. a*ḻagan* also means one who has beauty as his identity.

**Ur arangamanṟē** – the place of dwelling of emperumān, who was waiting for the right moment to enter his heart, is tiruvarangam, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). By saying *arangamanṟe* (it is only tiruvarangam) āḻvār implies that *paramapadam* (śrīvaikuṇṭam) is there only for convening his court, thus making it as a place to stay from time to time, whereas tiruvarangam is his permanent dwelling place. Unlike *paramapadam* which is unapproachable for samsāris, this place (tiruvarangam) is one which they can enter and enjoy whenever they acquire the taste in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) related matters.

We shall next see the 17<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 17. Virumbi ninretta

*(for suc a person) irumbu pōl valiya nenjam – a mind \[heart\] as hardened as iron iṟai iṟai urugum vaṇṇam – softening gradually surumbu amar – occupied by bees sōlai sūḻnda – surrounded by gardens mā arangam – great tiruvarangam \[śrīrangam\] kōyil koṇḍa – taken as a dvelling place karumbinai – periya perumāḷ vho is an object of enjoyment, like sugarcane en kaṇ iṇai – my tvo eyes kaṇḍu koṇḍu – seeing and enjoying kaḷikkum āṟē – hov tey enjoy!*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsurm, āḻvār said that there was a flood of affection in his heart. In this pāsuram he says “unlike samsāris who, despite having affection in their hearts, will not display it, emperumān made even insentient entities such as my eyes to rejoice exceedingly upon seeing him, like sentient entities”. Let us look at the pāsuram விரும்பி நின்றேத்த மாட்டேன் விதியிலேன் மதியொன்றில்லை இரும்பு போல் வலிய நெஞ்சம் இறை இறை உருகும் வண்ணம் சுரும்பு அமர் சோலை சூழ்ந்த அரங்கமா கோயில் கொண்ட கரும்பினைக் கண்டுகொண்டு என் கண்ணினை களிக்குமாறே! virumbi ninṟētta māttēn vidiyilēn madiyonṟillai irumbu pōl valiya nenjam iṟai iṟai urugum vaṇṇam surumbu amar sōlai sūḻnda arangamā kōyil koṇḍa karumbinaik kaṇḍukoṇḍu en kaṇṇiṇai kaḷikkumāṟē!*

**virumbi ninṟu ētta mātten** – in the first line, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) gives the previous states of the three parts – his mind, speech and body. If a great object is seen, should it not be praised with the mouth, with total desire and force? śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 5.7.70 says “*sō’ham tē dēva dēvēśa nārcanādou stutau na ca* I *sāmartyavān krupāmātra manōvrutti praśīda mē* īī” – Oh, the Lord of nityasūris and celestial persons! A person such as I, am not capable of praising or adoring you. Please show mercy on me with your benevolent mind.

In the same way [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)’s tongue was without any strength when it came to speaking on matters related to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). But it had all the enthusiasm and strength to speak about matters other than emepeumān.  emperumān asks āḻvār “the tongue may have been going around on matters other than myself. But, has the body done anything for me?” āḻvār says …

**vidiyilēn** – as per amarakōśam 2534; 3.3.100 “*vidir vidānē*” – the word *vidi* is associated with bodily function.  Since time immemorial, I have not done anything with my hands which is beneficial towards you. viṣṇu darmam 1.13 says “*sā jihvā yā harim stauti taccittam yattadarpitam* I *tāvēva ca karau ślāghyau yau tatpūjākarau karau* īī” – that which praises hari ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) is tongue; that which is proffered to him is heart; only those which carry out pūjā (worship or veneration) to him are great. Thus, even if hands and tongue were involved with worldly matters, did you at least think of me with your heart, asks emperumān….

**madi onṟu illai** – it is not even that it (heart) did not know of your greatness. It did not even know of your existence, responds the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). While I knew the nature and purpose of everything other than you, in matter related to you, I did not even have the knowledge that “you are there”  As per this statement, even though āḻvār had useless knowledge about all the other things, he considers such a knowledge as “not knowledge” at all,

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) says that even though he had allowed āḻvār’s mind, speech and bodily functions to engage with worldly matters, he would rectify āḻvār’s mind and take it under his wings…

**irumbu pōl valiya nenjam** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that his mind is unfit to be rectified. One can bring even iron under one’s control by melting it, but his mind is hard and cannot be rectified at all. Up to this, āḻvār states the status that he was in, earlier. He now states the greatness of emperumān who changed that state.

**iṟai iṟai urugum vaṇṇam** – Though hard like iron, instead of destroying his mind, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) softened it gradually. Just as rainfall softens a hardened land, emperumān was able to soften his mind which had been grazing on worldly matters, through his beauty.

[āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) next describes the entity that changed his mind….

**surumbu amar sōlai sūḻnda aranga mā kōyil koṇḍa karumbinai** – *surumbu amar sōlai* – garden  occupied by bees. Just as sweet smell emanates from a garden and stays with it, the bees were also born in these gardens and stayed put there. Wouldn’t the bees go out? Why should they, when the gardens are always fresh and the honey is always available. Since honey does not dry up, bees do not have a reason for going out. It is as if it is always spring season. *sōlai sūḻnda arangamā kōyil koṇḍa* – the sweetness of the temple is so great that the sweetness of the gardens described above appears lowly in comparison. To affirm this, āḻvār uses the adjective “mā” – great. Another meaning given is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) considered this temple as *paramapadam* (śrīvaikuṇṭam).

**karumbinai** – sugarcane. Instead of saying sugarcane juice which would feel sticky on the hands, he says sugarcane itself, which is enjoyable. Unlike sugar which is obtained by boiling the juice and made in an unnatural way, this \[sugarcane\] is natural and sweet. taittirīya upaniśat says “*rasō vai sa: rasa hyēvāyam labhdvā nandī bhavati*” – rasa (greatly joyous) personality is emperumān. After attaining him, (the jīvātmā) becomes joyous. Thus upaniśat also claims that emerpumān is naturally sweet.

**kaṇḍukoṇḍu** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is like sugarcane drunk by the eyes. śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19.7 says “*lōcanābhyam pibanniva* “ – śrī rāma looked at śrī vibhīśanāḻvān and drank him with his eyes. In the same way, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) drinks in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**ēn kaṇṇiṇai kaḷikkumāṟē** – my eyes, which were all along engaged with worldly pursuits, ultimately leading to narakam (hell), are now enjoying this great emperumān. *iṇai* – two. One eye is comparable to the other eye. There is no other comparison. muktars (those who have got liberated from samsāram and have subsequently reached śrīvaikuṇṭam) also enjoy him. But the joy of āḻvār is much higher than that of muktars. There is no comparison at all. *kaṇṇiṇai kaḷikkumāṟē* – the eyes are not waiting for the owner, but are enjoying on their own. Instead of being conduits for sensory perceptions, these eyes are themselves enjoying like a cētanan (sentient entity). daśarata cakravarty tells kausalyā (śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 42.24) “*rāmam mē’nugatā druśtiradyāpi na nivartatē* I *na tvā paśyāmi kausalyē! sādu mā pāṇinā spruśa* īī”  – my eyes went behind rāma and have not returned till now. Oh kausalyē! Touch me with your hands (so that I can see if at least the sensory perception of touch is active). Thus due to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s sweetness, the sensory organs get elated when they see him and get sad when they are separated from him.

We shall go on to the 18<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 18. Inidiraittivalai moda

*inidu – being sveet tirai tivalai mōda – droplets from te vaves, beating eṟiyum taṇ paravai mīdu – (vaves) agitating atop kāvēri vhic is like a cold ocean tani kidandu – sleeping alone arasu seyyum – ruling over (destroying te ego of cētanars (sentient entities)) tāmaraik kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa vit red-lotus like eyes emmān – my svāmy (lord) kani irundu anaiya sevvāy  kaṇṇanai – śri kriṣṇa vit reddiś lips like a fruit kaṇḍa kaṇgaḷ – te eyes vhic sav him pani arumbu – cool, tears of joy udirum – vill flov copiously pāviyēn – (one vho could not properly vorśip) sinner like me en seygēn – vhat vill ī do?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**inidiraittivalai mōda – delightful droplets emanating from waves touched \[periya perumāḷ\]. Similar to “oṇsangadai vāḷāḻiyān” in tiruvāimoḻi 8.8.1 where oṇ sangu gadai morphed into oṇsangadai with the last letter of sangu getting shortened, here the words iniya and tirai morphed into inidirai with the last word in iniya getting shortened. Initially huge droplets came from the waves; these mountain-like droplets broke into smaller droplets. These smaller droplets touched the tirumēni (the divine body) of emperumān, lulling him to sleep (similar to sleep induced by massaging the thighs). Even though the droplets only touched him gently, keeping in mind the soft nature of emperumān’s divine body, āḻvār is using the term mōda – to hit hard against.  In pāsuram 11, āḻvār divined “arakkar kōnaic ceṟṟa nam sēvaganār” speaking about his strength when he took refuge under him. Now, at the time of enjoying emperumān, he speaks about the softness of emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about his eyes enjoying on their own. Due to this, there were tears of joy in his eyes which became a flood.  āḻvār feels sad that “this is acting like a wall and is preventing me from enjoying emperumān” Let us see the pāsuram and its meaning. இனிது திரைத் திவலை மோத எறியும் தண் பரவை மீதே தனிகிடந்து அரசு செய்யும் தாமரைக் கண்ணன் எம்மான் கனியிருந்தனைய செவ்வாய்க் கண்ணணைக் கண்ட கண்கள் பனி அரும்பு உதிருமலோ என் செய்கேன் பாவியேனே. iniditu tirait tivalai mōda eṟiyum taṇ paravai mīdē tanikidantu arasu seyyum tāmaraik kaṇṇan emmān kaniyirundanaiya sevvāyk kaṇṇanaiak kaṇḍa kaṇgaḷ pani arumbu udirumālō en seygēn pāviyēnē*

**eṟiyum taṇ paravai mīdē** – waves breaking out from the ocean. Refers to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who, to get rid of his tiredness, is lying on kāvēri in which waves are breaking out like in an ocean. Alternatively, it also means that due to its expanse and water flow, kāvēri is like an ocean.

**tani kidandu** – lying all alone. The entity who has a retinue of servitors in *paramapadam* to serve him by merely following his thoughts, is lying here all alone. While he is lying here, instead of engaging in deeds which will uplift themselves, the samsāris are engaging in pursuits which deface the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), while they think that they are enjoying unparalleled ecstasy. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is lying here so that samsāris will have an opportunity to get him when he is alone, without any disturbance just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 10.10.1 “*taniyēn vāḻmudalē*”

What has been done to get him here like this? …..

**arasu seyyum** – to rule means avoiding dual rule. When emperumān says “*tvam mē*” – you are mine, the samsāri says “*aham mē*” – I am mine. This is called dual rule. Avoiding this is ruling. Making the samsāris to say “*nama:*” – I am not mine, is taken as ruling. In mahābhāratam śāntiparvam 344.45 it says “*muktānām lakśaṇam hyētat yaccvētadvīpavāśeenām* I *nityānjaliputā hruśtā nama ityēva vādina:* īī – whatever is the identity of svētadvīpavāsis is also the identity of muktars (the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)s which liberated from samsāram and reached śrīvaikuṇṭam). Hence they will always be applying *anjali* (folding the palms together) to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). They will be happy. They will be capable of reciting “*nama:*”. Thus emperumān is lying here, waiting for samsāris to be like nityasūris, without *ahankāram* and *mamakāram* \[egoistic and possessiveness respectively\].

What is the implement that he has with him, to rid the samsāris of their ego? ….

**tāmaraik kaṇṇan** – when one has brahmāstram \[a highly potent weapon\] there is no enemy who cannot be defeated. It is the same beautiful eyes with which he won over nityasūris that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is employing here to win over samsāris too. As [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in [tiruvāimoḻi 2.6.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/04/thiruvaimozhi-2-6-4-vallale-madhusudhana/) “*tāmaraikkaṇṇanai viṇṇōr paravum talaimaganai” –* even nityasūris lost out to his lotus-like eyes. It is with the same eyes that he destroys the ego and possessiveness of samsāris.

Who became the target of this brahmāstram? ….

**emmān** – I lost out to these eyes and gave in writing my being a servitor to him, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).   ”Is there any other samsāri who cannot be won over by these eyes which have won over me, the lowest among all samsāris?” says the āḻvār.

**kani irundanaiya sevvāy** – Reddish lips similar to a fruit. For those whose possessiveness was cut off by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), this is the object to be enjoyed. The lips are so sweet that they create an urge in the person who sees them to immediately eat them up like a fruit. Unlike the fruits in the world which will dry up over a period of time, these lips remain fresh always, as said in śrīviṣṇu purāṇam “*sadaika rūpa rūpāya*” – always with the same form.

**kaṇṇanai** – If one worships [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), he would think that it is kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who is lying there to help those who had missed out during the time of his incarnation. It is pertinent to note here that [parāsara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) \[son of [kūrattāḻvāvn](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kurathazhwan/)\] would say “when one looks at periya perumāḷ, the eagerness with which yaśōdā pirātti brought him up without faulting him for all the mischief that he engaged in, would appear” and that “when one looks at namperumāḷ (utsasvar), one would think of cakravartit tirumagan (śrī rāma, son of daśarata ckaravarti), well brought up by vaśiśta and other riśis and showing the well mannered behaviour”

**kaṇḍa kaṇgaḷ** – the eyes \[of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)\], which had lost a great deal since time immemorial, and are seeing now

**pani arumbu udirumālō** – because of the enjoyment in the eyes that resulted in happiness, there was a flood of tears of joy. paniyarumbu – since the tears were the result of happiness borne out of enjoyment, they were cold such that even fried lentil would germinate if immersed in the tears, says the vyākhyātā. The tears would be warm only if they had been shed due to separation from [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). śrī viṣṇu tatvam says “*āhlāda śītanētrām bū: puḷakīkruta gātravān* I *sadā paraguṇāviśtō draśtavya: sarva dēhibhī:* īī” – the bhagavat bhakta (devotee of [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)) who has cold tears of joy due to elation, who has goose pimples all over his body and who has been enslaved by the qualities of bhagavān, is fit to be seen by all [jīvātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)” Here too it says that tears of joy are cold.

**en seygēn pāviyēnē** – since the tears of joy appear like a wall, he is unable to worship periya perumāḷ and [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) laments “what will I do?” He feels saddened that while he should be enjoying the rare darśan (sight) of [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the tears are preventing him from seeing, by drawing a curtain over his eyes and he feels like a sinner. The sound “*ālō*”\[in the previous word\] denotes the extremity of sadness. Since there is no limit to the greatness of emperumān, there is no limit to the elation that one would get out of enjoying that greatness and it is impossible for one not to shed tears of joy. āḻvār feels sad that he has sinned so much that he cannot even atone for it and wonders what he can do. If there is a gap in the formation of tears, he could see periya perumāḷ in that gap. But the tears are coming copiously without any gap. All along, due to his ignorance, he could not enjoy periya perumāḷ. But now when he knows about periya perumāḷ, he is unable to enjoy because of the tears. The implied meaning is that his experience has become one of flood and drought. So far the meanings were given considering the world *pāviyēn* (sinner) to denote sadness. This word is also used in conjunction with musical concert when audience will exclaim “*pāviyēn*” and “*aiyyō*” which are uttered due to excessive joy. Some people will say that āḻvār is using the term “*pāviyēnē*” out of joy \[in line with the second meaning given above\].

We shall now move on to the 19<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 19. Kudadisai mudiyai vaittu

*ulagattīrē – tose vho are in tis vorld kadal niṟam kadavuḷ – sarvēśvaran vho is of te colour of ocean endai – my svāmy (my ḷord) kudadisai – in te vestern direction mudiyai vaittu – keeping te divine head (as an indication of his being te ḷord) kuṇadisai – in te eastern direction pādam nītti – stretcing (to reac me) his divine feet (vhic are te refuge for all sentient entities) vadadisai – for te people in te nortern direction pinbu kātti – śoving te beautiful form of his back ten disai – in te soutern side ilangai nōkki – looking (affectionately) at lankā (vhere vibhīśaṇa dvells) aravu aṇai – on te bed of tiruvanantāḻvān \[te serpent ādiśēśa\] tuyilum ā kaṇḍu – after looking at te beauty of his sleeping enakku udal urugum – my body vill melt ālō – ōh! en seygēn – vhat vill ī do?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsauram āḻvār mentioned about the issue caused by the sensory organ eyes. In this pāsuram he mentions about how all the organs in his body are melting. குடதிசை முடியை வைத்துக் குணதிசை பாதம் நீட்டி வடதிசை பின்பு காட்டித் தென்திசை இலங்கை நோக்கி கடல் நிறக் கடவுள் எந்தை அரவணைத் துயிலுமா கண்டு உடல் எனக்கு உருகுமாலோ எஞ்செய்கேன் உலகத்தீரே. kudadisai mudiyai vaittuk kuṇadisai pādam nītti vadadisai pinbukāttit tendisai ilangai nōkki kadal niṟak kadavuḷ endai aravaṇai tuyilumā kaṇḍu udal enakku urugumālō enseygēn ulaattīrē*

**kudadisai  etc** – in two lines [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) shows what is the entity that melted him. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had created bhūmi (earth) for enabling cētanas who have done a mixture of puṇya (virtues) and pāpa (sins), tiryak (animals) and stāvara (plants) who have done more pāpa than puṇya, to live. He created heaven for enabling dēvas (celestial entities) who have done more puṇya than pāpa to live. āḻvār thought all along that there was no purpose for the four directions east, west, north and south. Now he has realised the reason for creating these four directions. He has realised that the purpose of creating cētanas is only to make them long for mōkśam (liberation), the purpose of creating the worlds is to enable jīvātmās to live and the purpose of creating the directions is for keeping his divine head and divine feet so that the cētanas get a taste for mōkśam.

**kudadisai mudiyai vaittu** – in order to benefit the western direction, he kept his divine head adorned with the crown that indicates that he is the Lord of both nityavibhūti (spiritual realm) and leelāvibhūti (materialistic realm). Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) had called out in tiruvāimoḻi 3.8.1 “*mudiyānē*” – one with the divine crown, keeping his quality of being the Lord, he kept his divine head in the west direction so that those in the west direction would prosper.

**kuṇadisai pādam nītti** – nammāḻvār had called his divine feet in tiruvāimoḻi 3.8.1 as “*mūvulagum toḻudēttum sīradiyān*” – one with the beautiful divine feet which are vorśipped by people in all the worlds. He stretched such divine feet, which are the refuge for all worlds to prosper, in the eastern direction. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) points out that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) stretched his divine feet all the way to tirumaṇḍangudi (āḻvār’s place of birth) in order to attract him. When the western direction, which was the direction in which duryōdana sat \[in kriṣṇavatāram, when duryōdana went to seek help of kriṣṇa, he sat near his head\] itself prospers, is it required to say that the eastern direction in which he stretched his divine feet, where arjuna sat, will also prosper? It is pertinent to note here that [parāsara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) \[[śrī kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kurathazhwan/)’s son\] used to tell his disciples “whenever [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) \[one of our important preceptors\]went to worship periya perumāḷ, he would never stand near his divine head which he used to call as duryōdana’s position. There was not a spot on the eastern side, towards emperumān’s divine feet, where āḷavandār had not stood while vorśipping him”

**vadadisai pinbu kātti** – the northern direction is inhabited by people who are proficient in tough samskritam (Sanskrit language) and where āḻvārs’ bhakti (devotion) laden words are not much heard of. To correct the people in this direction, he wanted to show his back side rather than the front as his back is considered to be more beautiful than the front. As said in these words ”*pūrvāngādadikaparānkhakalaham*” – the back side of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is more beautiful than his front side…

**tendisai ilangai nōkki** – Until now, in each of the directions he kept one part of his divya tirumēni (divine physical body) in order to remove the purposelessness in those directions. He looked at the southern direction to remove the shortcoming in him. The others were the help that he rendered out of his mercy. This (looking in the southern direction) is the help that he rendered himself to overcome his powerless state. [kulaśēkara āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kulasekarazhwar/) in his perumāḷ tirumoḻi 1.10 said “anbodu tendisai nōkkippaḷḷikoḷḷum” – lying down, looking at the southern direction with affection. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) looks at the southern direction which is the dwelling place of his protector vibhīśaṇa. [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4.9.2 says “*mannudaiya vibīdaṇarkā madiḷilangait tisai nōkki malar kaṇ vaitta ennudaiya tiruvarangaṟku*” – after removing the hurdles for vibhīśaṇa, emperumān gave the kingdom to him and lying at śrīrangam, he is looking at the way in which vibhīśaṇa is ably ruling lankā. This is similar to how a mother who is nursing her infant, looks at the infant’s cheerful face. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) said in periya tirumoḻi 6.8.5 “*selva vibīdaṇaṟku vēṟāga nallānai*” – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) has special affection towards vibhīśaṇa who was blessed by [pirātiti](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/). When sugrīva threatened to walk out with his army of monkeys should śrī rāma accept vibhīśaṇa, śrī rāma said “even if I have to forego you and your army, I will not let down vibhīśaṇa”. This is similar to a cow, in order to protect its calf that it gave birth to that day, pushes aside the calf which is older, with its horns and hoofs. When that sugrīva did something which could have resulted in harm to him, śrī rāma said “*tvayi kincit samāpannē kim kāryam sītāyā mama* I *bharatēna mahābhāhō! lakśmaṇēna yavīyasā*  *śatrugnēna ca śatrugna! svaśarīrēna vā puna:* īī” – Oh, sugrīva with long arms! If something shameful had befallen you, what benefit would I get through sītā or bharata or lakśmaṇa or śatrugna? Oh warrior who kills enemies, what benefit would I get out of my own body? Such was rāma’s affection towards sugrīva. But his affection for vibhiśaṇa was greater than that which he had towards sugrīva and hence he is lying in śrīrangam, looking in the direction of vibhīśaṇa.

**kadal niṟak kadavuḷ** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is constantly desired by nityasūris, is lying in wait for a *samsāri* [*cētanan*](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) (a sentient entity in samsāram). Since his physical form removes the ennui of beholders like an ocean, āḻvār refers to him as “*kadal niṟakkadavuḷ*” In a way the ocean may match his physical form, but his *aprākruta tirumēni* (his divine physical form composed of upaniśats and not the 5 elements that our bodies are composed of) goes beyond the ocean. Hence he says “*kadavuḷ*” \[in tamiḻ language, *kada* means beyond\]

**endai** – he displayed such a form and enslaved me. When a new well is ready, the old well is cleaned up for maintenance. When I have been enslaved by this form, there is no need to talk about his taking under control the nityasūris, displaying this form. His form has enslaved someone like me who is totally under the control of body and senses and who is the epitome of all that is low.

**aravaṇait tuyilumā kaṇḍu** – Just as a gemstone’s value goes many times higher after being fixed on a gold ornament, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s form became incomparable after lying on ādiśēśa.

**udal enakku urugumālō** – after seeing emperumān’s tirumēni (divine physical form) lying on ādiśēśa, instead of enjoying that, āḻvār’s body is melting. rāmānujar in [śaraṇāgati gadyam 12](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/01/saranagathi-gadhyam-12/) says “*bhagavat svarūpa tirōdāṇakarīm*” – the body hides the basic nature of bhagavān. Such a body, which hides emperumān’s svarūpam is now melting after seeing his divine form. brahma sūtram 3.2.5 says “*dēhayōgādvā sō’pi*” – the reason for [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)’s svarūpam being hidden is because of its connection with the( gross and subtle forms of) the body. Thus, the body which is supposed to hide the knowledge of ātmā, is not only enjoying the divine form of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) but is melting as a result of that joy. When samsāris who came with āḻvār to worship [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) said that their bodies are not melting, āḻvār responds *enakku urugumālō* – see, it is melting for me.

**enseygēn ulagattīrē** – looking at the samsāris who, without showing any difference, are leaving in a firm manner after looking at the beauty of periya perumāḷ, āḻvār asks them “will you not tell me the way to be steady like yourselves?” He cannot ask the other āḻvārs for a remedy as they too are like him when it comes to having darśan of periya perumāḷ (they are also like a leaking boat which sinks along with another boat which came to its rescue since the second boat had a bigger hole). He cannot ask this of periya perumāḷ as it will expose his weakness to the person who is the reason for his discomposure. It may also be the reason for periya perumāḷ to feel proud that “I have been able to conquer him” Hence he is asking the samsāris who are so steady even after seeing the wonderful form of [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) “you people are keeping your body so taut as if affected by rheumatism. Why don’t you give me a little bit of the medicine that you are taking \[so that I will also be steady like you all\]?”

We shall now move on to the 20<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 20. Payu nir arangam tannul

*pāyu nīr – surrounded by \[te river\] kāviri in vhic vater is floving arangam tannuḷ – in  tiruvarangam \[śrī rangam\] pāmbu aṇai – in te bed of tiruvanantāḻvān \[ādiśēśa\] paḷḷi koṇḍa – lying, asleep māyanār – emperumān’s, vit vondrous activities tiru nal mārvum – te supremely great cest vhere pirātti \[śrī mahālakśmi\] resides maradagam uruvum – colour of tirumēni \[divine form\] like emerald stone tōḷum – divine śoulders tuvar idaḻ – red-coloured divine lips pavaḷam vāyum – coral like divine mout āya sīr mudiyum – crovn vit unparalleled greatness, for a very long time tēsum – te radiance (as a result of all te aforementioned aspects) adiyarōrkku – for his follovers (vho knov teir svarūpam, basic nature) agalalāmē – can tey be lost?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram when the samsāris heard āḻvār’s involvement with periya perumāḷ and his resultant suffering \[his tears preventing him from seeing the wonderful form of periya perumāḷ\], they told āḻvār “who don’t you keep aside this involvement with emperumān and enjoy like us, taking care of food and clothing so that you will not have to bother about the body melting and you can be steady and firm like us?” āḻvār responds saying “we realise after seeing periya perumāḷ in this sleeping posture that he is doing this for our sake. We know our svarūpam (basic nature); how can we be like you, not being involved with emperumān related matter, and exhibit our ahankāram and mamakāram (ego and possessiveness) like you?” Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings. பாயு நீர் அரங்கம் தன்னுள் பாம்பணைப் பள்ளி கொண்ட மாயனார் திரு நன் மார்வும் மரகத உருவும் தோளும் தூய தாமரைக் கண்களும் துவரிதழ்ப் பவள வாயும் ஆயசீர் முடியும் தேசும் அடியரோர்க்கு அகலலாமே. pāyu nīr arangam tannuḷ pāmbaṇaip paḷḷi koṇḍa māyanār tiru nan mārvum maradaga uruvum tōḷum tūya tāmaraik kaṇgaḷum tuvaridaḻp pavaḷa vāyum āyasīr mudiyum tēsum adiyarōrkku agalalāmē*

**pāyu nīr arangam** – instead of being at an elevation, requiring construction of a dam and forcing water to go up, tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) is at a lower level so that water will flow naturally. Water is used as a metaphor to denote all substances that purify and which are clear. This temple is the repository for such substances. As mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 2.5 “*agartdamidam tīrtam bharatvāja! niśāmaya* I *ramaṇīyam prasannāmbu sanmanuśyamanō yatā* īī´- hēy bharatvāja! Look at this river which is without mud, beautiful, and clear like the minds of good people. Thus water is taken as a simile for good people.

**tannuḷ pāmbaṇai** – in order to prevent any irritation to his divine body, which is softer than a flower, by the water, he has spread ādiśēśan as a mattress on top of the water. [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) said the same in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5.1.7 “*veḷḷai veḷḷattin mēl oru pāmbai mettiyāga virittu*” – spread ādiśēśa on top of the white water, as a mattress.

**paḷḷikoṇḍa māyanār** – his way of sleeping is wondrous. sītā pirātti tells hanuman in śrī rāmānyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 28.25 “*vāyasēna tatastēna balavatkliśyamānayā* I *sa mayā bōdita: śrīmān sukhasupta: parantapa:* īī – śrī rāmapirān, who was sleeping comfortably, (due to which) who was so handsome, and who burns his enemies, was woken up since that kākāsuran kept troubling me.  Thus pirātti enjoyed his sleeping posture. The word *māyā* denotes something wonderful. The word *māyanār* \[used here by [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)\] denotes [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is wondrously beautiful.  *paḷḷikoṇḍa māyanār …… agalalāmē* – if emperumān were in standing posture or sitting posture, it would have been possible for one to leave that place. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 5.8.1  “*ērār kōlam tigaḻakkidantāy*” displaying such a beautiful form, you were lying, and [kulaśēkarap perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kulasekarazhwar/) says in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 7.2 “*ānaiyiṟkidanta kidakkai kaṇḍidappeṟṟilēn*”  – I have not been fortunate to see you lying as an elephant;  when he is in lying posture is it possible for anyone to leave him and go?

**tirunal mārbum** – his chest, made greater by the presence of pirātti. What was the greatness due to pirātti, to his chest? The marks left by her feet with *sempanju* \[ a paste prepared from red cotton, used to dye women’s feet\] and the garlands discarded by her were always there on his chest and this was essential in deciding emphatically that he is the *paramporuḷ* \[supreme being\]. [parāśara bhatta](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/)r says in śrī guṇa ratna kōśam 4 “*vēdāntās tatvacintām murabhi durasi yatpāda cihnais taranti*” – by looking at which pirātti’s marks of divine feet on the chest of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the vēdāntams bring to an end the discussion on who is the supreme being … Another explanation is: as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said in 6.10.10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu*” – I \[pirātti\] will not leave his chest even for a moment, his chest becomes great because pirātti will not leave it even for a moment.

**maradaga uruvum** – his form which has the radiance of emerald green, bathing the eyes of the beholder with a coolness.

**tōḷum** – unlike his chest and form which needed adjectives to bring out their greatness, his shoulders are great, by nature. His shoulders provide a float to those who were sinking after seeing the beauty of his chest and form.

**tūya tāmaraikkaṇgaḷum** – the divine eyes which cast a gracious look on both pirātti and demons such as  hiraṇyan without any difference. The reason for this is the causeless mercy in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s eyes. In [śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/) 5.29, kriṣṇa says “*bhōktāram yajñatapasām sarvalōkamahēśvaram* I *suhrutam sarvabhūtānām jñātvā mām śāntimruccati* īī” – the one who accepts rituals and penances, the one who is the lord of all the worlds, the one who is the friend of all living creatures – knowing such a person \[emperumān\] everyone attains peace. Thus irrespective of whether the person is a friend or a foe, emperumān remains friendly with everyone. In jitantē ślōkam 1.2 it says “*dēvānām dānavānāmca sāmānyamadidaivatam* I *sarvadā caraṇadvantvam vrajāmi śaraṇam tava* īī” – I take refuge under your divine feet which are available for holding on to , by both devotees of śrī viṣṇu and those who do not have any devotion towards him. Thus for both celestial and demoniac persons, the relationship with him is the same. Despite this, the reason for the troubles faced by demons is entirely due to their faults. The wind, which is beneficial to everyone, dries up lotus. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) has used the adjective tūya tāmarai (pure lotus) for his divine eyes, the lotus would equal emperumān’s divine eyes only in terms of softness, coolness and fragrance. But when one looks at the flood of grace which is there in emperumān’s divine eyes, the lotus can never equal that.

**tuvaridaḻp pavaḷa vāyum** – with reddliś lips and mouth with coral-like row of teeth. The colour of the teeth is due to the reflection from the reddish lips. cāndōjña upaniśat 3.14.2 says “*avākya nādara:*” (due to profundity) not talking to anyone and not regarding anyone. It appears that from such a state of supreme being, [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) losing his profound state, gives words of comfort to those who attain him. Another interpretation is that *tuvaridaḻ* refers to the petals of drum-stick flower and hence his lips are like the petals of drumstick flower and coral. His lips smile at the cētanas (sentient entities) who, thinking about their faults, are afraid to approach him and tell them “do not fear”.

**āya sīr mudiyum** – crown, being ready and with greatness for a very long time. *āya* refers to being ready for a long time. *sīr* refers to great wealth. Starting from *tirunan mārbum* up to *āya sīr mudiyum* the gracefulness of [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s different divine parts of the body was highlighted.

**tēsum** – with this word, *samudāya śōbhā,* the grace of his total divine form, is highlighted.

**adiyarōrkku agalalāmē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) asks, “when we look at the greatness of the physical form of [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), is it possible for people like us with svarūpa jñānam (knowledge about the true nature of [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)) to leave? “Only those who have carried out such terrible sins that they cannot enjoy this, and those who are enslaved by ego and possessiveness can leave. By using the term *āmē* in the end, he is emphasising that even samsāris will say “yes, it is not possible for you people to leave”, highlighting the greatness of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s physical beauty and the involvement of āḻvār in enjoying that beauty.

In the previous word āya sīr mudiyum, the vyākhyātā says that “*āyam*” means gold and with a shortened phoneme, it becomes *āya* and *āya sīr mudiyum* thus refers to emperumān’s golden locks which are great.

We shall now go on to the 21<sup>st</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 21. Panivinal manamadonri

*pavaḷa vāy – having divine mout like coral aranganārkku – in te matter of tiruvarangan (śrī ranganātan) paṇivināl – being humble manam adu onṟi – (in matter relating to emperumān) keeping te mind in harmony tuṇivināl – vit determination, boldness vāḻamāttā – unable to live tollai nenjē – since time immemorial, having lost out in bhagavat viśayam (matter relating to emperumān), ōh my heart! aṇiyin ār – perfectly beautiful sem pon āya – made of reddiś gold aru varai anaiya – like te great mēru parvata (a mountain in te higher vorlds) kōyil – in te temple maṇiyinār – emperumān śining like a blue diamond kidanda āṟṟai – te beauty of lying dovn and sleeping manattināl – trough te mind (or heart) ninaikkal āmē – is it possible to measure? nī sollāy – you please tell*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Explanatory Notes)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār’s heart told him “if you cannot leave periya perumāḷ, you can attempt to measure his beauty and enjoy that experience” to which āḻvār responds “oh my dear heart! your idea of measuring his beauty and knowing about him is like your ignorance and the resultant loss since time immemorial when you did not have the firmness to enjoy his beauty”. Let us first enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings. பணிவினால் மனம தொன்றிப்  பவளவாய் அரங்கனார்க்கு துணிவினால் வாழமாட்டாத் தொல்லை நெஞ்சே! நீ சொல்லாய் அணியனார் செம்பொன் ஆயா அருவரை அனைய கோயில் மணியனார் கிடந்தவாற்றை மனத்தினால் நினைக்கலாமே paṇivināḷ manama donṟip pavaḷavāy aranganārkku tuṇivināl vāḻamāttat tollai nenjē! nī sollāy aṇiyinār sempon āya aruvarai anaiya kōyil maṇiyinār kidandavāṟṟai manattināl ninaikkalāmē*

**paṇivināl manamadonṟi** – resolving that “shall bow down to periya perumāḷ” redeeming the mind from worldly matters and involving deeply in this \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s\] matter. *onṟugai* – being in harmony. All that is expected of us is to indicate the resolve to remove the mind from all other matters and involve, as per the *svarūpam* (true nature) in *bhagavat viśayam* (matter relating to [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)). Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) addresses *nenjē* \[heart\] and says *manamadu onṟi* \[with mind in harmony\], he indicates to thought here, which is the activity of heart. Even if one were to bow down to anyone other than emperumān, one cannot say for sure that they will show compassion. They may or they may not. Even if they take pity, it may not lead to good results. In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36.11, rāvaṇa says “*dvidā bhajyēyamapyēvam na namēyam tu kasyacit* I *ēśa mē sahajō dōśa: svabhāvō duratikrama:* īī” – even if I were to be split into two parts, I will not bow down to anyone like this. This is my fault which has been there since birth. Is it possible to overcome one’s basic nature?

In the case of  [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), it is enough if one were to change the vow that “I will not bow” It is enough if we indicate to emperumān who, for a very long time, is engaging in creation, incarnation etc, desiring “will the cētana (sentient entity) not involve with me” that I have changed. Even if we bow down completely to emperumān that act is not going to yield the result. It is emperumān’s mercy which is going to. If what the samsāri does (bowing) is not the cause for any benefit, then how did this become a cause for emperumān’s mercy? Just as, when a person raises his arm to hit another person, even if he does not hit him, the other person will remember for his life this act of the first mentioned person which had disgraced him. In the same way, when we bow to emperumān, he remembers this act of the samsāri. āḷavandār says in stōtra ratnam 28 “*tvadanghri mutdisya kadāpi kēnacit yatātatā vā’pi sakrut krutōnjali:* I *tadaiva muṣṇātyaśubhānya śēśata: śubhāni puṣṇāti na jātu hīyatē* īī” – if someone had at some point of time, in some way carried out *anjali* \[folding the palms together as a mark of obeisance\] towards you, his sins are removed at that time itself. The benefits grow multifold; they never reduce. In mahābhāratam śānti parvam 46.163, the same meaning is conveyed “*ēkō’pi kriṣṇē sukruta: praṇāmō daśāśvamēda avabhrutēna tulya:?* I *daśāśvamēdī punarēti janma kriṣṇapraṇāmō na punarpbhavāya* īī” – can the benefit of carrying out a salutation to kriṣṇa be compared to the benefit of carrying out ten *aśvamēda*? \[an aśvamēda was a practice in olden days when a king, to prove his superiority would let his horse wander all over the place and if anyone holds the horse, the king will defeat that person and establish his rule in that place\] (It should not be done, because) the one who carries out ten aśvamēda is born again in this samsāram while there is no *janma* (birth) for the one who has vorśipped kriṣṇa. gāruda purāṇam 11.26 says “*anjali: paramā mudrā kśipram dēvaprasādinī*” – anjali, which makes [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) very happy , is a superior identification \[to show one’s humility\]. Thus emperumān is pleased with the cētana and shows mercy on him.

**pavaḷavāy aranganārkku** – if [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) gets someone who shows the initial signs of involvement with him, he comforts that person for all the loss that he had suffered all these days with his coral-like mouth. *aranganārkku* – “when he comes near me with involvement, I should not be too far away from him” and hence he stays permanently close-by at tiruvarangam.

**tuṇivināl vāḻamātta** – it is his supreme form that creates humility in the cētana as well as determination and stimulates him to enjoy the experience

**pavaḷavāy aranganārkku manamadonṟi tuṇivināl vāḻamātta** – the cētana did not have the determination to enjoy [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) all along. It is only determination that is required now to enjoy. mahābhāratam śānti parvam 324.47 says “*vyavasāyādrutē brahman! nāsādayāti tat param*” – Oh brāmaṇa! He will not attain that paravastu ([bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)) if he does not have mental strength – janaka, who was clear in the path to attain [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) told his ācāryan vēdavyāsa that it is only determination which is the unblemiśed means to attain brahmam \[supreme entity\].

**tollai nenjē** – looking at his heart, the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says “it is only due to a lack of determination that you have lost out all along to enjoy in this matter”

**nī sollāy** – you can now very well say that it is due to ignorance that you had lost enjoying this, all along and now you can enjoy *bhagavat viśayam* (emperumān related matters). He is hinting that this is also due to ignorance.

**aṇiyinār semponāya aruvaraiyanaiya kōyil** – *mahā mēru parvatam* (the great mēru mountain) is referred to as one that is beautiful and made of reddish gold. The temple in tiruvarangam is very much like that mēru. Since this temple is like an ornament to samsāram, the term *aṇiyinār* will suit well. Since it looks very much like reddish golden colour, *semponāya* will also suit it. Since it is stable like a mountain and is the basis for the entire world, the term *aruvaraiyanaiya* will also suit.

**maṇiyanār kidandavāṟṟai** – if that mēru mountain were to be pierced in the centre, and it puffs up all around, and a blue gem were to be found in the centre, it would be like periya perumāḷ lying in a sleeping posture under the turret of śrirangam kōyil.

**kidandavāṟṟai manattināḷ ninaikkalāmē** – looking at the beauty of his sleeping posture, is it possible to esimate his beauty with one’s heart? It may be possible to even estimate his svarūpam (basic nature) and his auspicious qualities but it is not possible to put an estimate to his physical beauty, is [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)’s opinion. If it is possible to think of something other than through the mind, it may be possible to measure his beauty. \[in other words, it is impossible to measure his beauty\].

We shall now take up the 22<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan
