## 22. Pesirre pesalallal

*(Hence) māsu aṟṟār manattu uḷānai – residing permanently in te minds of tose blemiśless persons (vho have left aside oter benefits) nām vaṇangi iruppadu allāl – oter tan vhatever has been enjoyed by us (vho have surrendered totally to him) pēsa tan āvadu uṇḍō – is it possible to speak trough hymns (his greatness)? nī sollāy – you please tell*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**āren’t prākruta matters (worldly issues) spoken by the mouth? Why then, can’t this (relating to bhagavat issues) be spoken? Worldly matters are limited to certain boundaries. But that is not the case with bhagavat viśayam which is unbounded and hence it is not possible. viṣṇu darmam 1.13 says “sā jihvā yā harim stauti” – whatever praises hari is tongue. If the tongue is not going to speak about bhagavān, should we leave it at that? …**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*“If it is not possible to think through the mind (heart), can you not speak through the mouth?” asked the heart. taittiriya samhitai 7.5.3. states “mana: pūrvō vāguttara:” – initially mind and then the mouth engage. Thus, the mouth can speak only what the mind thinks. If there is a matter which is outside the scope of mind, can the mouth speak about it? Oh my heart, who thinks that it is possible to speak, please tell, says the āḻvār. Let us first go through the pāsuram and its meanings. பேசிற்றே பேசலல்லால் பெருமை ஒன்று உணரலாகாது ஆசற்றார் தங்கட்கல்லால் அறியல் அவனுமல்லன் மாசற்றார் மனத்துளானை வணங்கி நாம் இருப்பதல்லால் பேசத்தான் ஆவதுண்டோ பேதை நெஞ்சே! நீ சொல்லாய் pēsiṟṟē pēsalallāl perumai onṟu uṇaralāgādu āsaṟṟār tangatkallāl aṟiyal āvānumallan māsaṟṟār manattuḷānai vaṇangi nām iruppadallāl pēsattān āvaduṇḍō pēdai nenjē! nī sollāy*

**pēsiṟṟē pēsal allāl** – We can repeat only whatever the *vēdas* (scriptures) and *vaidikars* (those who follow vēdas) had spoken about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s greatness earlier. It is not possible to recite new hymns appropriate to this supreme entity. What is it that the vēdas and those who followed these vēdas spoke earlier?

- nārāyaṇavalli 11 says “*tasya madyē vahniśikā aṇiyōrdvā vyavastita:* I *neelatōyada madyastā vidyullēkēva bhāsvarā* īī” – paramātmā’s (emperumān’s) divine, auspicious form, agni śika, is located in the middle of the lotus shaped heart, like a miniscule, turned upwards. It is luminous like a lightning amidst blue coloured clouds.
- tiruviruttam 29 says “*neelamuṇḍa minnanna mēni*” – his divine physical body is like lightning which has swallowed blue colour \[for the flash of blue\].
- mahābhātam śānti parvam 46.118 says “*atasīpuśpasankāśam pītavāsasamucyatam* I *yē namasyanti gōvindam na tēśām sidyatē bhayam* īī” –  there is no fear for those who worship gōvinda, who does not ever let go of his followers, who is of the colour of kāyāmpū (purple coloured flower)  and one wears yellow coloured silken dress.
- cāndōjña upaniśat 1.6.6 says “*ya ēśō’ntarādityē hiraṇmaya: puruśō druśyatē* I *hiraṇyaśmaśrur hiraṇyakēśa āpraṇakhāt sarva Eva suvarṇa:* īī *tasya yatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkamēvamakśiṇī*” – the *parama puruśan* (supreme entity) who is seen in the middile of the sun, has a divine form which appears like gold, has golden moustace, has golden locks and upto his nails has golden hue. He has two divine eyes looking like lotus, just blossomed by sun.
- manu smruhti 12.122 says “*prāśītāram sarvēśāmaṇīyām samaṇīyasām* I *rukmābham svapnadīgamyam vidyāttu puruśam param* īī´- it should be understood that *paramapuruśan* controls all entities, has the radiance of melted gold, can be perceived by wondrous knowledge such as svapna buddi \[intelligence gathered through dreams\]
- [tiruvāimoḻi 3.1.2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thiruvaimozhi-3-1-2-katturaikkil/) says “*sutturaitta nanpon un tirumēni oḷi ovvādu*” – even purified, melted gold will not come anywhere near the radiance of your divine form.

In all the cases mentioned above, they had to use prākruta vastus (materials in this world) as similes for comparing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s physical form. But aren’t these worldly materials too lowly for such a comparison? Why should these be taken for comparison? For defining entities which are not visible to the sensory organ of eye, one has to consider only those entities which the eye has seen. This does not mean that they were able to speak fully about emperumān.

**pēsiṟṟe pēsal allāl** – other than quoting from vēdas, it is well nigh impossible for us to recite even one hymn about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). How did they speak about him? taittiriya upaniśat ānandavalli 9.1 says “*yatō vācō nivartantē aprāpya manaśa saha:* I *ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān na bibhēti kutaścanēti* īī´- emperumān’s *ānandam* (joy) is such that words and mind cannot fathom it and return empty handed. One who knows this does not fear anyone. Thus the vēdas confirmed that it is not possible to speak fully about emperumān. We can also say that it is not possible to speak about him and say that he cannot be spoken about in any other way. This is another interpretation for these words. What is the reason for this?

**perumai onṟu uṇaralāgādu** – not even one of his greatness can be perceived. It is not possible to point out an equivalent for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) related matter. It is not possible to measure its greatness either. For other worldly matters, one may show a simile – for example, one can say that the face is like the moon. In such cases, the subject of comparison \[face\] will not come anywhere near the object that it is compared with \[moon\]. In emperumān related issues, the object that is compared with will not come anywhere near the subject of comparison. For example, if one says that emperumān’s eyes are like lotus, the lotus can never equal the eyes in any of the parameters of assessment. By saying *onṟu uṇaralāgādu,* [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) emphasises that emperumān’s *svarūpam* (his nature), *guṇas* (his qualities), his *vigraham* (physical beauty), his *vibhūti* (his wealth) can not be perceived or measured. In that case, can no one know about this entity?

**āsaṟṟār tangatkallāl aṟiyalāvānumallan** – he cannot be known by anyone other than those who are without blemish. Even though the word *āsu* mentioned here and the word *māsu* appearing in the next line have the same meaning, blemish, we should consider that they refer to different types of blemish in the two places. Here, the word *āsu* refers to the blemish relating to *upāyam* (the means of attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)).  The cētana (sentient entity) instead of being completely dependent on emperumān (*atyanta paratantran*), thinks that he could indulge in some other means (path), to attain emperumān out of his ego, in keeping with his strengths. When the cētana tries to attain emperumān through other means, these other means will have to look upto emperumān’s grace for granting the desired result as they don’t have the power within themselves to grant it. When emperumān is himself the means to attain him, he does not have to look upto anyone else or anything else. Thus it is not possible to know about \[perceive\] emperumān except for those who approach emperumān only through him.

**māsaṟṟār** – those without blemish. *māsu* is also a blemish of the mind. Due to the effect of sins committed, going after other goals such as *aiśvarya* (wealth), *kaivalya* (ātmā enjoying itself) is called as blemish of the mind. Those who go after only [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) as fruit \[or target or goal\] of attaining him are referred to as *māsaṟṟār.*

**manattuḷānai** – being in the minds of. Living in the minds of those who, leaving aside all other means \[of attaining emperumān\] and all other goals to enjoy \[except himself\], leaving aside the materialistic realm, reaching paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) and thinking of enjoying emperumān, leaving the responsibility of their protection with him, admitting that they have nothing in them for this. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says *manattu uḷānai* (being inside the mind) he indicates that emperumān considers areas outside the minds of his followers as unfit to stay, as if a forest fire is raging. Just like fish cannot sustain itself if removed from water, emperumān also cannot sustain himself if he is not inside the minds of such people. The purpose of emperumān staying in all the [divya dēśams](https://koyil.org/) (divine temples which have been sung by [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/)) is only in expectation of such a person who does not have *āsu* and *māsu* \[blemish of reaching emperumān through other means and blemish of reaching out to other goals respectively\]. Just as [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/) sang in tiruccanda viruttam 65 “*ennilāda munnelām*”, emperumān has taken positions \[such as standing, sitting, lying down\] in various divya dēśams to wait for such a person.

**vaṇangi nām iruppadu allāl** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that there is nothing else for us, who have left the responsibility of our lives to him, to do, except to enjoy him and pay our obeisance to him, once he has decided to stay put in the mind. Paying obeisance here is indicative of enjoying him through all three channels of mind, speech and action.

**pēsattānāvaduṇḍō** – when one thinks of emperumān’s greatness, was it such that one could recite a hymn about it?

**pēdai nenjē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) asks his mind \[heart\] whether it can talk about emperumān the same way that it has been used to talking about worthless worldly matters  all these ages due to its ignorance.

**nī sollāy** – āḻvār as imploring his mind to talk about this as it seemed to be too anxious and desirous to speak about emperumān.

We shall next enjoy the 23<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram.

## 23. Gangaiyil punidamaya

*gangaiyil punidamāya kāviri naduvu pāttup pongu nīr parandu pāyum pūmboḻil arangam tannuḷ engaḷmāl iṟaivan īsan kidandadōr kidakkai kaṇḍum enganam maṟandu vāḻgēn ēḻaiyēn ēḻaiyēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēḻaiyēn – fickle minded person like I am; gangaiyil – more than gangai \[gangā\]; punidam āya – with the quality of sanctity; kāviri naduvu pāttu – in the middle of kāviri; pongu nīr – frothing flood; parandu pāyum – flowing in all the places uniformly; pūmpoḻil – having beautiful groves; arangam tannuḷ – in the temple; engal māl – having affection towards his followers; iṟaivan – the Lord of all; īsan – the controller of all, periya perumāḷ’s; kidandadu ōr kidakkai – unparalleled lying posture; kaṇḍum – after seeing and enjoying; maṟandu – forgetting (that divine posture); enganam vāḻgēn – how can I sustain myself?; ēḻaiyēnē – (caught in emperumān’s matter) I am standing, stunned, unable to do anything

**Перевод:**

**gangaiyil punidamāya – kāviri, which is greater than gangai in sanctifying others. While it is greater than gangai in terms of beauty and sweetness of the waters, it is greater than gangai in the purifying quality of sanctifying others. śrīranga mahātmyam states “gangāsāmyam purā prāptā dēvadēvaprasādanāt I rangāvadaraṇādūrdvam ādikyancāpyavāpa sā īī” – kāviri, who became equal to gangai with dēvadēvan’s \[emperumān’s\] grace, became superior to gangai after the installation of śrīranga vimānam (turret). purāṇas spoke high of kāviri and said that she was superior to gangai after periya perumāḷ took his position there. The sanctity for gangai is because she had the link to emperumān’s divine feet once, when brahmā cleansed his divine feet. In īśvara samhitai, brahmā says “dravībhūtas tadā darmō haribhaktyā mahāmunē! I gruhītvā darmapāṇīyam padam natasya tuśtayē īī kśālitam parayā bhaktyā pādyārghyādibhir arcitam I tadambu patitam druśtva dadāra śirasā hara: īī pāvanārtam jatāmadyē yōjñō’smīdyavadāra- varśāyutānyata bahūn na  mumōca tatā hara: īī” – Oh, the great one among the riśis! At that time (when bhagavān measured the worlds), darmam (virtue), due to its devotion towards hari, became water. jagannātan’s (Lord of the universe) divine feet were cleansed (by me) with superior devotion, with that virtue turned water, for his pleasure. He was also vorśipped with pādyam (water used for cleaning divine feet) and arjñam (water used for cleaning divine hands). Looking at the water which was falling \[from emperumān’s divine feet\], Sivan affirmed that “I am qualified (to purify myself)” with that water and bore the water in the centre of his head to sanctify himself. Then for tens of thousands of years, rudran did not let go of that water. Thus, the water, which was borne by Sivan to sanctify himself, got a fault also that it had connection with Sivan. śaivas, the followers of Siva, do not consider it appropriate to touch the garland or prasāda (offering) of Siva since they are nirmālyam (unfit or useless). Hence, as tirumangai āḻvār said in tiveḻukūṟṟirukkai “āṟupodisadaiyōn” – matted hair with the weight of river, since gangai stayed on in his matted hair, she contacted the rancid smell and connectivity with the matted hair. Since kāviri did not have such flaws and since periya perumāḷ lies amidst her tributaries, does she not have a superior sanctity over gangai? Even though gangai has that flaw since she stayed in sankaran’s matted hair, since she became the “śrīpāda tīrtam” (water from divine feet) of emperumān, wherever she went, her sanctity overshadowed her fault. poygai  āḻvār, in his mudal tiruvandādi 97 said “undan adi sērndu aruḷ peṟṟāḷanṟē podi sēr anarkangaiēṟṟān avirsadai mēṟpāynda punaṟgangai ennum pērppon” – gangai, who flowed into the matted hair of rudran to purify him, attained your divine feet and received your grace so that she could purify all those who had committed sins. kāviri’s greatness is that such an emperumān came on his own and lay down amidst her tributaries.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvar’s heart told him “if it is not possible to think and speak about this matter \[emperumān\], you could forget about it and live” to which he responds “how can I, who am deeply involved in the periya perumāḷ’s (mūlavar at śrīrangam\] lying posture, forget him and live?” Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings. கங்கயில் புனிதமாய காவிரி நடுவு பாட்டு பொங்கு நீர் பரந்து பாயும் பூம்பொழில் அரங்கம் தன்னுள் எங்கள்மால் இறைவன் ஈசன் கிடந்ததோர் கிடக்கை கண்டும் எங்ஙனம் மறந்து வாழ்கேன் ஏழையேன் ஏழையேனே.*

**naduvupāttu** – in the middle. Just as he lay down in the lap of yaśōdāppirātti, periya perymāḷ lay down in the middle of kāviri to rest awhile.

**pongu nīr parandu pāyum pūmpoḻil arangam tannuḷ** – due to excess flow of water and with the excessive joy of impending darśan of periya perumāḷ, kāviri is coming in with lot of enthusiasm, throwing waves all over. *parandu pāyum –* instead of coming in as a narrow stream, she is coming with lot of desire, touching both shores. *pūm poḻil arangandannuḷ* – due to the copious flow of water, the gardens are always full of flowers. With water flow suiting the softness of periya perumāḷ , with the gardens looking as if it is always spring season, the [divyadēsam (divine place)](https://koyil.org/) removes the ennui of all persons. By saying *gangaiyil punidamāya* the sancity of tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) and by saying *pongunīr parandu pāyum pūmpoḻil* the sweetness have been indicated.

**engaḷ māl** – emperumān appears as the epitome of affection towards his followers. By saying *engaḷ* \[our\] he takes in other [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) and others who have surrendered to emperumān and who have left their protection fully with him. By initially talking about the sanctity of the place and then its sweetness, it would appear superficially that emperumān is lying there because of these two reasons. But if one were to delve deeper, it would be clear that he is lying there only to show his *tirumēni* (divine form) to his followers out of the profound affection that he has towards them. [āḻvar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) hints that it is not only affection, but also the result of natural relationship with followers.

**iṟaivan** – the reason for the affection is because he is their svāmy (lord).

**īsan** – not only is he their lord, he also has the power to control them and guide them towards virtuous ways. Thus with these three words \[*engaḷ māl*, *iṟaivan* and *īsan*\] he shows that emperumān shows affection towards his followers, is their lord and controls them. Thus, preventing his followers from getting involved in worldly matters which are controlled by the three guṇas \[*sattva*, *rajas* and *tamas*\], he wishes to take them to nitya vibhūti (śrīvaikuṇṭam) where they can enjoy permanently and is lying here, at śrīrangam, to get ownership of his possessions \[the [ātmās](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) who are willing to come to śrīvaikuṇṭam) with his quality of *vātsalya* (motherly affection)

**kidandadōr kidakkai** – leaving aside the three auspicious qualities just mentioned previously, [āḻvar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is now showing how he has been trapped by the beauty of emperumān’s divine lying posture. Had he been in the divine standing or divine sitting posture, it might have been possible to forget that. To emphasise that it is not possible to forget his divine lying posture, āḻvār is using the word *ōr* (as in *kidandadōr*) which emphatically says that he is *non pareil*. For the beautiful people of the world, their beauty would be enhanced when they are in standing or sitting but would be totally erased when they are lying. In the case of periya perumāḷ, his beauty surpasses everything when he is in the lying posture. Did not pirātti admire and enjoy this, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 38.25 “*śrīmān sukha supta:*” – emperumān who was lying down and looked beautiful? *ōr kidakkai* – He is using the word *ōr* to emphasise that he is not sure whether emperumān is lying on one side or lying supine. It is clear that it is āḻvārs’ natue to be immersed in emperumān’s divine postures. If he is standing, they would say as in periya tirumoḻi “*nilaiyāra ninṟān*” – he was standing so beautifully; if he is sitting, they would say as in tiruvāimoḻi “*pirān irundamai kāttinīr*” – you showed us your beauty in sitting posture, and as in this pāsuram if he is lying down.

**kaṇḍum enganam maṟandu vāhzgēn** – If this had not been enjoyed before, it might have been forgotten. But, having enjoyed this posture, how can it be forgotten? *ēnganam maṟandu vāḻgēn* – āḻvār says, “when this matter is like food for me, how can I forget this and subsist on worldly matters which are like sand? If I forget this, there is no way that I can sustain with other matters”

**ēḻaiyēn ēahziyēnē** – even If I approach [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with greed, to enjoy him, since he cannot be enjoyed fully, I am unable to enjoy. If I separate from him, I am unable to sustain myself. Thus in both ways, I have become impoverished. *ēḻaiyēn ēḻaiyēnē* – if ēḻmai were to refer to weakness, since time immemorial, I had become weakened due to involvement in worldly pursuits. Now, getting involved in the immeasurable beauty of *bhagavat viśayam* (related to emperumān), I have become weakened as I cannot go for other worldly pursuits too. Earlier, due to karma for a very long time, I had become weakened. Now, due to involvement with emperumān, I have become weakened, is another meaning for this term.

We shall proceed to the 24<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 24. Vella nir parandu payum

*veḷḷa nīr parandu pāyum viripoḻil arangam tannuḷ kaḷvanār kidandavāṟum kamala nanmugamum kaṇḍum uḷḷamē valiyai pōlum oruvan enṟu uṇara māttāy kaḷḷamē kādal seydu un kaḷḷattē kaḻikkinṟāyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

veḷḷam nīr – kāvēri with huge floods; parandu pāyum – flowing on all sides; viri poḻil – having expansive gardens; arangam tannuḷ – inside the temple; kaḷvanār – aḻagiya maṇavāḷan \[śrī ranganātan\] who steals (the hearts of his followers); kidanda āṟum – the way that he is sleeping; kamalam nal mugamum – divine, beautiful face like a lotus; kaṇḍum – even after vorśipping; uḷḷamē – Oh, heart!; valiyai pōlum – you are too hardened, it appears; oruvan enṟu – that he is incomparable; uṇara māttāy – you do not realise; kaḷḷamē kādal seydu – faking your love (in emperumān related matter); un kaḷḷattē – in your falsified actions; kālattaik kaḻikkinṟāyē – you are wasting your time!

**Перевод:**

**veḷḷanīr parandu pāyum – From the source point of sahyādri hill where kāvēri begins her journey, her intention is only to come to śrīrangam. Displaying her intention and her desire, she comes with such a forceful flood flowing in all directions that no one can stop her by building a dam across her. Since there is no end to her desire \[to come to śrīrangam and worship the Lord there\] there can be no end to her flow also. Hence no one can say that she flowed \[using the past tense\]. Thus āḻvār uses the word pāyum which is in the present tense. In other words, she keeps flowing continuously.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the 21st pāsuram, āḻvār said “manattināl ninaikkalāmē” (is it possible to measure emperumān’s beauty through the mind?). In the 22nd, he said “pēsattān āvaduṇḍō” (is it possible to talk about emperumān?). In the 23rd, he said “enganam maṟandu vāḻgēn?” (how will I live forgetting him?). Once periya perumāḷ started showing his overflowing love for him after seeing that he was trying to find ways of forgetting him, āḻvār thinks that his love towards emperumān was nothing compared to that which emperumān showed towards him. He feels that his love is not congruent with the greatness of emperumān and that the love born out of this insentient body is like the clarity that a mad person gets on occasions \[in other words his love is not permanent, but only occasional\] and cannot be believed. Taking pity on himself he disparagingly tells his heart “Oh heart! are you trying to play truant with your love in this matter too, like you do with worldly matters?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings, first. வெள்ள நீர் பரந்து பாயும் விரிபொழில் அரங்கம் தன்னுள் கள்ளனார் கிடந்தவாறும் கமல நன் முகமும் கண்டு உள்ளமே வலியைப் போலும் ஒருவன் என்று உணர மாட்டாய் கள்ளமே காதல் செய்து உன் கள்ளத்தே கழிக்கின்றாயே.*

**viri poḻil arangam** – wherever the water \[kāvēri\] flows, the gardens also spread out expansively.

**arangam tannuḷ kaḷvanār** – when he enjoys with the innocent nityasūris, he does so honestly. But when he wants to attract the crafty samsāris, he decides that he needs to be crafty himself and hence lies down as if he is sleeping inside the temple. An alternative explanation is: while it may appear superficially that he is lying down after seeing the richness of the flowing water and the gardens, in reality he is lying there only to entice the samsāris. Hence āḻvār is using the term *kaḷvanār*. When confidants such as [tiruppāṇāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thiruppanazhwar/) go near him and worship him they will say “*seyya vāy aiyyō! ennaic cindai kavarndaduvē*”- implying that there is a crafty look on his face. In other words it would appear that if someone approaches him, he would steal their [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and pocket it smoothly. In other words, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) would make that person as his confidant. The person had all along been thinking that the ātmā, which actually belongs to emperumān, is his own since time immemorial. emperumān decides that he will make it his own without that person being aware of it. He displays his physical beauty and as said in jitantē ślōkam “*jitantē puṇḍarīkākśa!  namastē viśva bhāvana!* I *namastēstu hruśīkēśa! mahāpuruśa! pūrvaja!*” (Oh kaṇṇa with red coloured lotus eyes! This (ātma) vastu (entity) has been won over by you. (this) is not mine; only yours; Oh the one who created all the worlds! Oh the one who controls the sensory organs! Oh, the magnanimous and great person! Oh the one who came ahead of everyone else! (this entity ātmā) instead of being mine, should only be yours). This *ātmāpahāram* (coveting of ātmā) has been going on for a very long time. But when emperumān takes this ātmā, which is always his, it appears that he is stealing it. Even submitting this ātmā to him is equivalent to stealing the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/). In *tiruvāyppādi* (in gōkulam) along with butter, he also steals the jīvātmās. Our submitting this ātmā to him is equivalent to king mahābali returning his lands to him as a gift.

**kidandavāṟum** – what enticed me is neither his auspicious ātmaguṇa (qualities of his ātmā) such as simplicity nor the lāvaṇya rūpa guṇa (qualities of various parts of his physical form) such as his divine eyes, but the beauty of his lying posture – *kidandadōr kidakkai* as [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) mentioned in the previous pāsuram which is samudāya śōbhā (spendour of his entire physical form).

**kamala nanmugamum** – for the qualities of well blossomed nature, freshness, coolness and fragrance, lotus can be taken as a similie for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine face to an extent. But since it cannot equal emperumān’s divine face, āḻvār qualifies the divine face as *nanmugam.* Can the prākruta (belonging to materialistic realm) lotus come anywhere near the *sudda satva* (purely good) and radiantly divine face of emperumān? Would the lotus which blossoms on seeing sun equal the divine face which blossoms on seeing *āśritars* (followers)? By qualifying as *nanmugam* āḻvār says that only [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s face is a cool \[comforting\] face in samsāram and all the other faces are hot \[repelling\] ones.

**kaṇḍum uḷḷamē valiyai pōlum** – even after having darśan of the beautiful lying posture of emperumān, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)’s heart did not show any change in its appearance. āḻvār wonders “Oh heart! are you this hardened that you are not showing any change even after looking at emperumān?” Compared to the greatness of emperumān, whatever changes he had shown earlier are nothing, as far as āḻvār is concerned.

āḻvār’s heart asked him, “what is the hardness that I have now shown?”

**oruvan enṟu uṇaramāttāy** – you are not realising that he is beyond any comparison. If you realise, you should realise his greatness and auspicious qualities such as *āśrita vātsalyam* (motherly affection towards his followers) as they are. If you realise these qualities as they are, you should have matching involvement towards him.  Only because you don’t have this involvement, your heart is hardened, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).

If such an involvement had not taken place, in the earlier pāsurams. what is the meaning of saying “*kaṇṇinai kaḷikkumāṟē”* ([pāsuram 17](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-17/)), “*paniyarumbu tīrumālō*” ([pāsuram 18](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-18/)), “*udal enakku urugumālō*” ([pāsuram 19)](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-19/)?

**kaḷḷamē kādal seydu** – āḻvār tells his heart “just as you had exhibited falsified love towards worldly matters, you had shown similar false love towards [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) also as a matter of habit. You did not have true love towards emperumān”.

**un kaḷḷattē kaḻikkinṟāyē** – This deceitful nature has been with you since time immemorial. Just as you had shown false love for a long time in other matters, you had shown similar false love in emperumān’s matter also. You thought that this matter is similar to the other worldly matters. Your love does not match the greatness of emperumān’s matter, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).

We shall now take up the 25<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

archived in [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/)

pramēyam (goal) – [https://koyil.org](https://koyil.org/)  
pramāṇam (scriptures) – [http://grantams.koyil.org](http://granthams.koyil.org/)  
pramātā (preceptors) – [http://acaryas.koyil.org](http://acharyas.koyil.org/)

## 25. Kulittu munru

*kuḷittu mūnṟu analai ōmbum kuṟikoḷ andaṇamai tannai oḷittittēn enkaṇ illai ninkaṇum pattan allēn kaḷippaden koṇḍu nambi kadalvaṇṇā kadaṟuginṟēn aḷittenakku aruḷ sey kaṇḍāy arangamā nagaruḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

arangamānaruḷānē – Oh, tiruvarangā!; kuḷittu – after having a bath; mūnṟu analai – the three types of agni (the element, fire); ōmbum – to have the qualification for carrying out karma with agni; kuṟikoḷ – that which is difficult to ward off any shortcoming due to wrong-doing with mantram (reciting ślokas); andaṇamai tannai – being a brāhmaṇa; oḷittittēn – I had driven off; en kaṇ illai – I do not have (the knowledge of ātmā related matters); nin kaṇ pattanum allēn – I do not have love towards you; kaḷippadu enkoṇḍu – (the one without repentence) how can I be glad; nambī – the one who is full (with auspicious qualities such as simplicity); kadalvaṇṇā – the one has form like an ocean; kadaṟuginrēn – I am calling out to you; enakku – in my matter; aḷittu aruḷ sey kaṇḍāy – you must bless me by bestowing me with everything, beginning with being qualified

**Перевод:**

**kuḷittu mūnṟu …. – āḻvār tells emperumān “a person born as a brāhmaṇa has to take bath thrice a day, worship three types of agnis \[agni is dēvatā for the element fire; agni also means fire\], carry out sandyāvandanam” is what you have written in śāstras. But I have not carried out any of these from the day that I was born, thus acting against your commands. kuḷittu – there are different types of baths. nityasnānam is what is done three times a day, morning, noon and evening. naimittika snānam is what is done for a specific purpose, for example on days of eclipse (both solar and lunar). prāyaścitta snānam is bath taken as an angam (part) after committing a mistake and prior to carrying out atonement for it. Thus there are different types of snānams. One cannot carry out only one among these and avoid out the rest. These are all independent. Since taking a bath is a qualification for carrying out karma (deed or action) such as sandyāvandana, kuḷittu as mentioned here is to be considered as being a qualification for carrying out all karmas. While it is a rarity to carry out all the karmas as mentioned in śāstras, āḻvār says that it is rare for him even to observe this act \[of taking a bath\] which is a qualification for carrying out karmas. mūnṟu analai – three types of anal (fire) are mentioned: gārhapatyam, āvahanīyam and dakśiṇāgni. anal is the tamiḻ equivalent of the samskrita word analam. “na vidyatē alam yasya sa: analam” is the definition for the word analam (that which does not have a state called “enough” is analam) \[agni will never say “enough”. As one puts things into it, it will go on burning and consuming them\]. The reason for āḻvār using the word anal instead of agni is because it will never be satisfied with any vorśipping and will keep expecting more and more vorśipping.  mūnṟu anal – while observing even one anal is a rarity, śāstram has specified that a brāhmaṇa should observe three anals. ōmbum – just as a mother runs with food and water behind an emaciated child, a brāhmaṇa has to run behind these anals with umi (husk) and samittu (sticks) for vorśipping them. kuṟikoḷ – while observing any religious rite, there are a few faults which will entail. Some of these are: mantralōpam – mistake in pronouncing the mantra; kriyālōpam – mistake in observing the rite; dravyalōpam – faults in the materials used in the rite; kālabhēdam – difference in the time when the rite is observed; dēvatāvyatyayam – reciting the name of the wrong dēvatā. Thus even if one of these mistakes is committed, the benefit of carrying out the rite will not be obtained. It is like taking a wrong step and falling down to one’s death and hence one has to be very careful while following these rites. Unlike emperumān who is pleased by a mere anjali and forgets all the faults in his āśritar (follower) this is not like that. What is this? andaṇamai – the essence of being a brāhmaṇa. What happened to it? oḷittittēn – my activities were such that it is not only that I lost the essence of being a brāhmaṇa, but I brought indignity to the entire community of brāhmaṇas. When some people see true brāhmaṇas, I had made them say “these people belong to the same community as this person who has lost the essence of brāhmaṇa!”,  thus bringing disrepute to them also. The meaning hinted by āḻvār is that when he did not even  have the quality of being a brāhmaṇa, there is no possibility of his carrying out karma yōgam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the last 10 pāsurams, āḻvār recounted the great assistance that periya perumāḷ  provided him. In the previous pāsuram the love that he showed towards emperumān was in line with emperumān’s greatness as it was the love born out of the acit (insentient) body with which he is associated. That love was something similar to the clarity that a mentally ill person gets on occasions, not being credible. He wonders “What should I do?” about his heart and stands in pity. He says “I should give up my connection with this body so that I would not have to suffer like this, reach paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) and have permanent experience. But I do not have any means for that” and feels sad. Seeing this, periya perumāḷ says “Why do you feel sad? To attain me, after getting rid of this body, there are several means available in the śāstrams (sacred texts). One can observe karma yōgam, jñāna yōgam or bhakti yōgam (path of carrying out deeds, knowledge and devotion respectively) suited to menfolk in brāhmaṇa, kśatriya and vaiśya varṇas (classes). ōr, carry out śaraṇāgati (surrendering) which is common for these three varṇas as well as the fourth varṇa (śūdra), women, children, others born in lower castes and animals without any difference. ōr recite the divine names of emperumān. Thus you can follow any one of these means and attain me”. āḻvar responds saying that “I do not have any qualification for following any of these means” and states that he does not have anything in his hand (ākincanyam), starting from this pāsuram, until the 34th pāsuram. But, one can ask whether this is not in contradiction with the jñānam and bhakti that he had shown in the earlier pāsurams. Those were granted by emperumān’s grace to enjoy him and they will not be the means for attaining him, says āḻvār. Whatever experience he had obtained through emperumān’s grace should last not only in this birth, but also in paramapadam and for that he does not have any means, he says in this pāsuram. In this first pāsuram, among these 10 pāsurams, he says that he does not have involvement in karma, jñāna, bhakti mentioned very clearly in śāstrams for traivarṇikas (brāhmaṇas, kśatriyas and vysyas). Let us see the pāsuram and its meanings. குளித்து மூன்று அனலை ஓம்பும் குறிக்கோள் அந்தண்மை தன்னை ஒழித்திட்டேன் என்கண் இல்லை நின்கணும் பத்தன் அல்லேன் களிப்பதென் கொண்டு நம்பீ கடல்வண்ணா. கதறுகின்றேன் அளித்தெனக்கு அருள் செய் கண்டாய் அரங்கமா நகருளானே.*

“Alright, you don’t have karma yōgam, but there is a possibility for jñāna yōgam, is it there?” asks emperumān …

**enkaṇ illai** – there is nothing in me regarding this. The opinion here is that I do not have any knowledge about myself. Since I do not have any knowledge about ātmā, which is the basis for jñāna yōgam, there is no possibility for jñāna yōgam being in me. When it is known that there is no karma yōgam, which is a prerequisite for jñāna yōgam, why should one say separately that he has no jñāna yōgam? The reason for this is that even if he did not have karma yōgam in this birth, it is possible that he had attained completeness in karma yōgam in the previous birth, and had died at that time. Due to the benefit of this karma yōgam in the previous birth, he might have been bestowed with jñāna yōgam at the beginning of this birth. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) denies this too.

In that case, is at the least bhakti yōgam present in you? asks [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) …

**ninkaṇum pattanallēn** – I do not have even an iota of affection towards you, when those who see you cannot but have a deep desire for you. Since he does not have the affection which is the prerequisite for bhakti yōgam, he denies having bhakti yōgam in him. When he denied having karma yōgam and jñāna yōgam, which are the prerequisites for bhaktiyōgam, is it required to say that he does not have bhakti yōgam? laghvatri smruti says “*janmāntara sahasrēśu tapōjñānasamādibi:* I *narāṇām kśīṇapāpānām kriśnē bhakti prajāyatē* īī” (after observing karma jñāna yōgams in tens of thousands of births, for those who get rid of sins (which are the hurdles for the commencement of bhakti yōgam), bhakti towards kriṣṇa begins).  Thus, karma and jñāna yōgams could have been completed in the previous births and bhakti yōgam could have begun at the start of this birth. āḻvār says that even this is not the case. Also, “*jñānabhaktiyanvitam karma janakādiśu druśyatē* I *karmabhaktyanvitam jñānam prāyēṇa b haratādiśu* īī *karmajñānānvitā bhakti: prahlādapramukhāśrayā* īī” (karma yōgam, with jñāna yōgam and bhakti yōgam as its parts, is seen in people like janaka \[father of sītāp pirātti\] as a means of attaining mōkśa (liberation); jñāna yōgam, with karma yōgam and bhakti yōgam as its parts, is seen in people like jatabharata as a means of attaining mōkśa; bhakti yōgam, with karma yōgam and jñāna yōgam as its part, is seen in people like prahlāda as a means of attaining mōkśa). Thus it used to be said in purāṇas that each of karma, jñāna and bhakti yōgams, with the other two as its parts, is a means of attaining mōkśa. Hence in line with this, it could be said that āḻvār admitted that he did not have any of the three yōgams in him. “*tryaṇāmapi yōgānām tribhiranyōnyasangama:*” (in each of karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams, the other two are mingled well); “*ātmārtdi cēttrayō’pyatē tatkaivalyasya sādakā:*” (if one likes *ātmānubhavam* (experiencing ātmā, the self), each of karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams will be a means of attaining *atmamātrānubhavam*, also called as *kaivalya mōkśam*).  [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) in his [gītārta sangraham](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/githartha-sangraham/) divined each of these three yōgams as means for attaining kaivalya mōkśam. Thus, one can surmise from āḷavandār’s śrīsūkti  (divine composing) that for a person immersed in kaivalya mōkśam, if he were to remember [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in his last moments for enjoying him, each of these three yōgams will be the means for attaining paramamōkśam (utmost liberation). Hence, our pūrvācāryas also agreed with this purāṇa characteristics. [śrī bhagavat gītā 3.20](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/3-20/) says “*karmaṇaiva samsiddimāstitā janakādaya:*” (people like janaka attained mōkśa only through karma). Again, gītā 4.32 says “*śrēyān dravyamayāt yajñāt jñānayajña: parantapa!* I *sarvam karmākilam pārtta! jñānē parisamāpyatē* īī” (Hey arjuna! In karma yōgam, supplemented with jñānam, the part played by jñāna (knowledge) is greater than that played by *dravyam* (material). All the karma, with all its means, end in jñānam).  Again, gītā 11.54 says “*bhaktyā tvananyayā śakya ahamēvam vidōrjana!* I *jñātum draśtum ca tatvēna pravēśtunca parantapa* īī” (Oh arjuna! One who roasts the enemies! Only with the assistance of bhakti, I, like this, can be truly known, seen and attained). Thus, we can consider through gītā that, all the three yōgams could be taken as means, individually, aligning with purāṇa characteristics. Thus, starting with *kuḷittu mūnṟu,* [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) showed that he has no connection with karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in “*nōṟṟa nōnbilēn*”, [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/) in “[*kaṟavaigaḷ pinsenṟu*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-29-chirram-chirukale/)” and [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) in stōtra ratnam 22 “*na drma niśtō’smi nā cātmavēdī na bhaktimāmtvaccaraṇāravindē* I *akincanō’nyagati: śaraṇya! tvatpāda mūlam śaraṇam prabadyē* īī”  (Oh emperumān, fit to be surrendered unto! I am not steeped in karma yōgam; I do not have ātma jñānam too; I do not possess bhakti in your divine feet; having no other means and no other place to go, I affirm that your divine feet are the means for reaching you), advanced their emptiness \[possessing nothing\] as a supplement to their means \[for attaining emperumān\].

periya perumāḷ , as his considered opinion, divined “āḻvīr! Just as knowledgeable people do, your advancing your emptiness is a rarity. Since you are thinking of advancing your emptiness, which is a means for my grace, is there any hurdle to get the benefit?”…..

**kaḷippaden koṇḍu** – with what can I rejoice that I will attain the means? Even though I do not have karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams, I do not even have the repentence that I do not have them. Even if I have that repentence, as you said in gītā 18.66 “*sarvadarmān parityajya mām ēkam śaraṇam vraja* I *aham tvā sarva pāpebhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā suca:* īī” (leaving aside all karmas with their traces, as a means of attaining me, take refuge only under me as the means. I shall liberate you from all the sins. Do not feel sad) you could  grant me protection. When I do not have even that, with what can I console myself, asks the āśvār. Thus he has convinced himself that he does not have even repentence that he has nothing in him. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tells him “since you do not even have repentence, I do not have any alternative other than giving you up”. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) responds “though I do not have anything with me as means for attaining you, you are full of auspicious qualities, with which you can offer me the means through your mercy”

**nambī** – āḻvār asks emperumān “Are you not complete in all the auspicious qualities just as I am complete in being without anything as a supplement for means?”  Just as [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/) said “[*kuṟai onṟum illāda gōvindā*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-29-chirram-chirukale/)” (one without any fault) soon after saying “[*aṟivonṟumillāda āykkulam*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-29-chirram-chirukale/)” (we are from the covherd clan without any jñānam), āḻvār also calls him “*nambi*” being full of auspicious qualities, soon after admitting his emptiness.

**kadal vaṇṇā** – Are dēvarīr’s (your highness) auspicious qualities such as simplicity etc required for completeness? dēvarīr’s mere beauty of physical form is sufficient for me as a means, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). kamba rāmāyaṇam says “*karuṇaiyangadal kidandanan karungadal nōkki”* (the beautiful, merciful ocean \[emperumān\] laydovn, looking at the black ocean). In the same way, periya perumāḷ is lying at śrīrangam. As said earlier “*aiyappādu aṟuttuttōnṟum aḻagan”* ([pāsuram 15](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-15/)) “*ādaram perugavaitta aḻagan*” ([pāsuram 16](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-16/)) āḻvār had mentioned this physical form only as his means. Thus, the meaning indicated \[by āḻvār\] here is that even though he does not have anything with him, the śaranyān (one who offers refuge) emperumān has auspicious qualities and physical beauty and hence he does not have to lose.

periya perumāḷ  divines “since you have known that you have nothing with you and also that I am complete in auspicious qualities, there is nothing beyond this for you to get the means”…

**kadaṟuginṟēn** – was I patient with firm belief in the means? Just as those who follow karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams cry out that there has been no benefit for their efforts, I am also wailing till the ears of those who listen to this, get ruptured. After I had rid myself of protecting self, it should have been my pastime to sing praises on you, looking at the beauty of your lying posture. Instead of that, I had brought bad name to you by making people say “when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) blesses even those who show a little bit of affection, why is he not coming to the aid of āḻvār who is wailing like this?” emperumān asks of the āḻvār “If you do not have anything beneficial with you, what do you want me to do?”

**aḷittenakkū aruḷ sey kaṇḍāy** – you should create repentence in me after making me an object of your causeless mercy. emperumān asks, “what is the compulsion that I should do good to you, starting with creating repentence?”

**arangamā nagar uḷānē** – the purpose of your coming to this place and lying down like this is only to protect people like me who do not have anything in them. If you wanted to protect only those who do not have any shortcoming, there was no need for you to come here. You could have stayed back in paramapadam and protected nityasūris and muktas. īsn’t it your purpose of showing the beauty of your lying posture and showing your simplicity by being under the control of arcakars (priests who carryout kainkaryam to emperumān), only to create everything for samsāris starting with taste \[in your matters\] and to protect them?

We shall proceed to the 26<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 26. Podellam podu kondu

*pōdellām pōdu koṇḍu un ponnadi punaiya māttēn tīdila moḻigaḷ koṇḍu un tirukkuṇam seppa māttēn kādalāl nenjam anbu kalandilēn adu tannālē ēdilēn arangaṟku ellē enseyvān tōnṟinēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pōdu ellām – at all times; pōdu koṇḍu – with flowers; un ponnadi – at your beautiful divine feet; punaiya māttēn – am without strength to offer; tīdu ilā – without faults; moḻigaḷ koṇḍu – with words; un tirukkuṇam – your auspicious qualities; seppa māttēn – am unable to recite; kadālāl anbu – the affection which comes out of love; nenjam – in my heart; kalandilēn – have not made; adu tannālē – due to that; arangaṟku – you, śrī ranganātan; ēdilēn – did not enjoy through any part of the body; ellē – Oh!; en seyvān – for what; tōnṟinēnē – was I born?

**Перевод:**

**pōdellām pōdu koṇḍu un ponnadi punaiya māttēn – It is proper to decorate your gold-like divine feet, with beautiful, fresh flowers, at all times. It is not that I was not eager in doing that. I did not have even the strength for doing that, says āḻvār. While engaging in matters other than you, I had both the eagerness and the strength, but not in matters relating to you, is the opinion expressed here. viṣṇu darmam 1.13 says “yau tat pūja karau karau” (only those are hands which worship hari). With the hands which were meant to worship you, I was vorśipping inappropriate things which would cause harm to me both in this samsāram and in śrivaikuṇṭam. Among the two instances of usage of the word pōdu, the first one refers to time and the second, to flower. pōdellām punaiya māttēn – the opinion here is not that he is not able to worship all the time and could worship only at some of the times. He is not able to worship at any time at all, is the meaning here. viṣṇu darmam, haryaśtakam 70.84 says “sakrutuccaritam yēna harirityakśaradvayam I badda: parikarastēna mōkśāya gamanam prati īī” (through whomsoever the two syllable word hari has been pronounced, all the deeds which are to be done for going to mōkśam have been done by him). Again, “sakrut smrutō’pi  gōvindō nruṇām janma śatai: krutam I pāparāśim dahatyāśu tūlarāśimivānala: īī” (gōvindan, even if thought of once, will remove the swarm of sins committed in hundreds of births, just as fire destroys a huge heap of cotton). śrī rāma says in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18.33 “sakrudēva prapannāya tavāsmīti  ca yācatē I abhayam sarvabhūtēbhyō dadāmyētat vratam mama  īī“ (I offer protection from all other creations to one who surrenders to me and one who prays to me after saying that he is my servitor. This is my oath). Thus in all the above-mentioned quotes, emperumān’s greatness is such that if a person were connected to him even once in his lifetime, emperumān ensures that the person attains the path \[to reach him\]. āḻvār laments that he was unable to worship him even once. emperumān tells him “even if you have not been able to worship me with your body, but indulged instead in worldly matters, you could have praised me with your faculty of speech, at least”. . . . .**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*periya perumāḷ divines to āḻvār “even if you did not have karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams which are prescribed for brāhmaṇa, kśatriya and vaiśya men, you could still carry out vorśipping and praising of me, as prescribed for all cētanas”. āḻvār responds “I did not have these also”. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings. போதெல்லாம் போது கொண்டு  உன் பொன்னடி புனைய மாட்டேன் தீதிலா மொழிகள் கொண்டு  உன் திருக்குணம் செப்ப மாட்டேன் காதலால் நெஞ்சம் அன்பு  கலந்திலேன் அது தன்னாலே ஏதிலேன் அரங்கர்க்கு எல்லே எஞ்செய்வான் தோன்றினேனே.*

**tīdilā moḻigaḷ koṇḍu un tirukkuṇam seppa māttēn** – even if my words are faultless and beautiful, I am not able to talk about your auspicious qualities. viṣṇu darmam 1.13 says “*sā jihvā yā harīm stauti …*” (that is tongue which praises hari); unlike this, my tongue, which was meant to praise you,  does not roll when it comes to talking about you. However, when it comes to talking about lowly matters with lowly qualities, it is able to talk like an implement “vācci” which cuts across a palm leaf. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) then says “even if your body and tongue are involved in worldly matters, at least with your heart \[mind\], you could show affection towards me” . . . .

**kādalāl nenjam anbu kalandilēn** – in one particular matter, my heart has not exhibited affection borne out of love. As [bhagavat gītā 2.62](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/2-62/) says “*sangāt sanjāyatē kāma: kāmāt krōdō’pi jāyatē*” (desire results out of involvement in worldly matters. Anger results out of desire), if desire results out of involvement with worldly pursuits, it is possible to divert it towards your matter. Earlier, when I had shown affection towards women, it was with the ulterior motive of stealing their possessions. I did not have affection to truly love them. It is apt to think of the following authentic sources as proof of the above: 1) [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/) says in stōtra ratnam 62 “*smaraparavaśō vancanapara:* “ (I am lecherous and cheat the women who  respond to my lust); 2) viṣṇu purāṇam 3.7.30 says “*paramasuhrudi bāndavē kaḷatrē suta tanayā pitrumātru bhrutrutyavarkkē*  I *śadamatrupyāti yō’rttatruṣṇām tamadamacēśtamavēhi nāsya bhaktam*  īī“ (the person with depraved intelligence who runs after the possessions of close friend, relative, wife, children, parents, servants –know that such a mean person is not a devotee of emperumān).

**adu tannālē ēdilēn** – while I should be involved with at least one among the three channels of manam, vākku, mey (mind, speech and body) to attain the benefit that great people attain by geting involved with, I do not have even that. Hence I have nothing. *arangarku ēdilēn* – as said in bhagavat gītā 5.29 “*suhrutam sarvabhūtānām*” (exhibiting good thoughts towards all beings) and as said in stōtra ratnam 18 “*vaśi vadānyō guṇavān ruju: śuci: mrudur dayāḷur madura: stiras sama:* I *kruti krutajñas tvamaśi svabhāvatas samasta kalyāṇaguṇāmrutōdadi* īī “ (one who is under the charge of his followers; gifting himself to his followers; being harmonious\[with his mind, speech and action\]; having purity \[to shower on followers without any reason\]; unable to bear separation from followers; unable to tolerate (their) sorrow; being sweet; being steadfast (in his followers’ goals); being equal (to all those who take refuge under him); carrying out followers’ deeds as his own; magnifying even the smallest good deed of his followers; you are by nature, the repository, as an ocean of nectar, of all auspicious qualities), you have the honesty of being in harmony through your mind, speech and action in matters concerning your followers. I am without anything towards such an entity as you, who has come to śrīrangam so that people can see you for themselves instead of merely hearing about you through śāstras.

**ellē** – oh, how terrible! I have not lost you because you are not near me; I have not lost you because you are not appropriate; I have not lost you because you are having a habit of finding faults with me; I lost you only because of my sin. This entity called [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) became wasted instead of being a part of the *kaustubamaṇi* (a pendant worn by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) on his chest) that you like so much. I have gone too far away from you, who is referred to by [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) in his periya tirumoḻi 3.7.6 “*arangattuṟaiyum intuṇaivan*” (sweet companion who dwells in śrīrangam). Have I lost out because you are like a medicine, which is good for the body but bitter to taste? No, just as milk itself doubles up as medicine, you are the sweet person who also grants mōkśam; yet I lost you.

**en seyvān tōnṟinēnē** – viṣṇu tatvam says “*vicitrā dēha sampatti*” (this wondrous body has been gifted to worship emperumān) – instead of putting to use the limbs that emperumān gifted in vorśipping him, I had wasted them; why was I born? My birth, instead of benefitting anyone, has become the cause for many bad deeds for others as well as for myself, says the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).

Let us now move on to the 27th pāsuram.

## 27. Kurangugal malaiyai nukka

*kurangugaḷ malaiyai nūkkak kuḷittut tām puraṇḍittōdit taranga nīradaikkaluṟṟa salamilā aṇilum pōlēn marangaḷ poḷ valiya nenjam vanjanēn nenju tannāl aranganārkkātceyyādē aḷiyattēn ayarkkinṟēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kurangugaḷ – monkey warriors (to carry out a little bit of kainkaryam to prove their basic nature of servitorśip); malaiyai – mountains; nūkka – pushing them; tām – they; kuḷittu – immersing in water; puraṇḍittu – (after that) rolling in the sand on the shore; ōdi – running; tarangam nīr – ocean frothing with waves; adaikkal uṟṟa – engaged in blocking; salam ilā – without deceit; aṇilum pōlēn – (I am) not like the squirrels; marangaḷ pōl – like the trees; valiya nenjam – having hardened mind; vanjanēn – engaged in deceit; aḷiyattēn – (qualified for all services) me, having eminence; aranganārkku – to tiruvarangan (śrī ranganātan); nenju tannāl – wholeheartedly; āḷ seyyādē – not carrying out service; ayarkkinṟēnē – standing foolishly, forgetting

**Перевод:**

**kurangugaḷ malaiyai nūkka – āḻvār has used the plural number for monkeys and singular number for mountain to indicate that monkeys were thousands of times more than mountains and each mountain was touched and lifted by many monkeys to build a path to cross the ocean. In a school, if the teacher asks the students to fetch his cane, many students, anxious to serve the teacher, would touch the cane and bring it to the teacher. In the same way, there were fewer mountains and many monkeys. Each of the monkey warriors is capable of lifting a mountain as if it were a small stone. However, all the monkey warriors wanted to take part in the rāma kainkaryam to prove their svarūpam (carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān is the basic nature of ātmā) and hence many monkey warriors moved one mountain. Is it possible for animals to get such a desire (to carry out kainkaryam)? Since the person who is getting the service done is the ordained Lord, and looking at the way he is standing with the bow and arrows in his hand, without any distinction between those with and without svarūpa jñānam, even a small piece of twig would carryout kainkaryam and hence it is possible for animals to get such a desire.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*periya perumāḷ opined divinely, “as mentioned in the previous two pāsurams, even if you had not done anything good as a cētana (sentient entity), I had taken an incarnation as śrī rāma in order to get kainkaryam (services) from animals, similar to the services carried out by anantāḻvān (ādiśēśan), garudan, viśvaksēnar et al in śrīvaikuṇṭam. At that time, when monkeys had rendered service befitting their strength, squirrels too, with good hearts, carried out services appropriate to their strengths. It is enough if you had carried out a service similar to those”. āḻvār responds “not even that” Let us read the pāsauram and the meanings of words. குரங்குகள் மலையை நூக்கக் குளித்துத் தாம் புரண்டிட்டோடித் தரங்க நீரடைக்கலுற்ற சலமிலா அணிலம் போலேன் மரங்கள் போல் வலிய நெஞ்சம் வஞ்சனேன் நெஞ்சு தன்னால் அரங்கனார்க்காட் செய்யாதே அளியத்தேன் அயர்க்கின்றேனே.*

**malaiyai nūkka** – the term *nūkka* indicates being pushed. Instead of saying the mountains were put in the ocean, why is āḻvār using the term *nūkka* \[pushed\]? Each group of monkey warriors would push the mountain for some distance. Then the next group will take over the mountain from the first group and push it for some distance. Thus, the mountain was being pushed from one group to the next till it reached the ocean. In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam, 5.9, śrī rāma tells lakśmaṇa “*avagāhyārṇavam svapsyē saumitrē! bhavatā vinā* I *kadancit prajvalan kāma: sa mām suptam jalē dahēt* īī” (lakśmaṇa! (since I am reminded of sītā when I look at you) I am going into the waters to sleep, leaving you. The burning *kāmāgni* (warmth of lust) will warm me inside the ocean, only with difficulty). The monkey warriors who have been seeing the difficulty of perumāḷ, due to his separation from pirātti, want to participate in this kainkaryam of blocking the ocean and this shows in the eagerness with which they were involved in the service. They wanted to reach the shore on the other side of the ocean, after blocking the ocean and that anxiety is shown in their speed.

**kuḷittu . . . . . salamilā aṇilum** –  the opinion of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) regarding this incident is as follows: Some squirrels were witnessing the work done by monkey warriors in blocking the ocean. It appears to the squirrels that the speed of the monkeys is not sufficient in blocking the ocean. The squirrels thought “when perumāḷ is very anxious to retrieve pirātti, these monkeys are blocking the ocean very slowly. Moreover, they do not have the strength to block the ocean. It is clear that they do not have sufficient manpower to fill the gaps between the mountains with sand. Only because of this, they are not carrying sand to fill the gap between the mountains”. In order to overcome this deficit, the squirrels entered the waters to wet their bodies. Then they ran to the shore and rolled in the sand to enable the sand to adhere to their bodies. Then they went over the mountains and reached the gaps between the mountains to shake their bodies so that sand would fall off their bodies to fill the gaps between mountains. By repeating these steps they helped in building the bridge across the ocean. The squirrels, keen on involving themselves in rāma kainkaryam, thought that by repeatedly wetting their bodies with water, the ocean will also start drying up soon and by bringing in sand from shore, the gap between the mountains would also get filled up this way.

**tām** – Due to the anxiety to carry out kainkaryam and the resultant agitation in their minds, the squirrels did not know that by immersing in the ocean, only a few drops of water would come out and that the ocean will not dry up due to this. They also did not know that the few grains of sand carried by them would not help in filling up the gaps between the mountains.  [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) uses the word “*tām*” here to indicate the smallness of their bodies. Alternatively, it could be thought that the squirrels felt proud that they were able to carry out tasks which were beyond the capability of the monkeys and the word “*tām*” indicates that pride.

**puraṇḍittōdi** – rolling themselves in the sand near the mountains, they thought that the sand was  not sufficient to fill the gaps between the mountains. Hence they ran some distance to enable more sand to stick to them. They did not know that by running that fast, whatever sand had originally stuck to them, would wither away. Looking at the speed with which they were running here and there, the monkey warriors stopped them to find out why they were running. The squirrels responded “we are running because we feel that perumāḷ should reach the northern gate of lankā by noon today for having his lunch there. You do not seem to have this speed”. Their involvement in emperumān’s kainkaryam did not allow them to think whether they were upto the difficult task that they had undertaken.

**taranga nīr adaikkal uṟṟa** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is amazed that these little squirrels are trying to block the ocean  which is full of lashing waves. *uṟṟa* – the squirrels think that only they are working in blocking the ocean and that the monkeys are only carrying out a supporting role by feeding them the stones \[mountains\] like a female worker would carry stones at a construction site so that they \[squirrels\] could complete the construction like a mason.

**salam ilā aṇilum pōlēn –** the word *salam* denotes the benefit obtained out of a cause. *salam ilā*  denotes that the squirrels are doing this as a service and do not expect any benefit out of it. While it is a fact that whatever these squirrels do cannot help even an iota in blocking the ocean, how can this be a kainkaryam? For [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is taking the service, it is enough if the person who carries out the service has purity of thought that he is not expecting anything in return (*salamilā*) for the service rendered. perumāḷ sees the anxiety in these squirrels that “the ocean should be blocked and emperumān should reach the shore on the other side soon”. He sees the *suhrut* (the good thoughts) in these squirrels, without any shortcoming in them. He does not have any necessity to see if they have achieved the impossible task. The word *salam* could be construed to mean *salanam* (disturbance) and thus the word *salam ilā* could be taken to mean remaining steadfast in the work undertaken. These squirrels are unlike human beings who are under the control of śāstram. They do not have any compulsion for varṇāśrama duties (carrying out tasks ordained by śāstras for each varṇa (class of people) and each āśrama (state such as brahmacāri – bachelor, gruhasta – married, sanyāsi – one who has renounced everything and vānaprasta – one who has entered forest towards the end of his life). They do not even have limbs to perform kainkaryams like human beings. Yet, by immersing in water, running, rolling in the sand and carrying out kainkaryam, they show their good thoughts which I, who should be carrying out the daily ordained rituals as kainkaryam to [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/), do not have, laments the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/), citing the [previous pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thirumalai-25/) (*kuḷittu mūnṟanalai ōmbum*) and this pāsuram. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who has no unfulfilled desire, does not expect anything other than these good thoughts from [cētanas (sentient entities)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/). Those who think that “for the completely fulfilled emperumān, what is it that these unfulfilled entities can do” and refrain from doing any kainkaryam, are unfortunate people. Those who think that since emperumān is completely fulfilled, he will be satisfied even if we do an iota of kainkaryam, and thus carry out kainkaryam, without expecting any benefit are the fortunate ones. This is why āḻvār feels that the kainkaryam carried out by squirrels is *salam ilā* kainkaryam and laments that he does not have even this.

**marangaḷ poḷ valiya nenja vanjanēn** – āḻvār is citing trees as a simile for being without involvement in [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s matters. In [pāsuram 17](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-17/), he had said *irumbu pōl valiya nenjam* (mind as hard as iron). Why is he now talking about trees when they are weaker than iron? While iron can be beaten, drawn or shaped up to any desired object when it is put in fire, trees will get burnt and be of no use to anyone when put in fire. Hence utilising this property of trees he says that his heart is as hard as trees.

**vanjanēn** – while my heart \[mind\] is as hard as trees, I put on an act of deceit and make even you \[emperumān\] to wonder whether this person is so soft like a liquid. While I do not have any involvement in your matters and am more interested in worldly pursuits, people would say “there is none like him when it comes to showing love towards emperumān” by believing my act of deceit, hiding my real intentions. He emphasises here that his earlier statement *iṟai iṟai urugum vaṇṇam* (pāsuram 17, softening gradually) was also false. That was also an act of deceit.

**nenju tannāl arangarkku āḷ seyyādē** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that he did not carry out kainkaryam to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) with the heart given by him, unlike the squirrels who carried out kainkaryam with good thoughts. *āḷ seydal* is carrying out kainkaryam like a slave. While it refers to total kainkaryam with utmost devotion, can it denote a small involvement? When it is seen that emperumān values this highly when done with good thought, this does not matter.

**aḷiyattēn** – just as lakśmaṇa said “*aham sarvam kariśyāmi*” (I will carry out all kainkaryam), this [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) is expected to carry out all kainkaryam. But instead of doing continuous service without any break, the ātma vastu (the entity called ātmā) has come to this pass…

**ayarkkinṟēnē** – I have forgotten [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) and am standing here, being of no use. I have not become useless because I am far removed from emperumān or because I am not qualified to carryout servitorśip to emperumān. Just as [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says in periya tirumoḻi 6.2.2. “*maṟandēn unnai munnam*” (I have forgotten about you), I have become useless because I have forgotten all about emperumān.

There is a variant in the vyākhyānam, “*salamilā marangaḷ pōl*” which means that a tree can be shaken with the help of an implement while my mind cannot be shaken by anything.

We shall move on to the 28<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.
