## 28. Umbaral ariyalaga

*umbarāl aṟiyalāgā oḷiyuḷār ānaikkāgic cempulāl uṇḍu vāḻum mudalai mēl sīṟi vandār nam paramāyaduṇḍē nāygaḷōm siṟumai ōrā empirāṟku ātceyyādē en seyvān tōnṟinēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

umbarāl – (starting with brahmā) celestial entities; aṟiyal āgā – unable to know (that it is this much, as per a measure); oḷi uḷār – emperumān who is in the radiant paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam); ānaikkāgi – for gajēndrāḻvān; sem pulāl – red meat; uṇḍu vāḻum – eating for sustenance; mudalai mēl sīṟi – getting angry with crocodile; vandār – came (to the bank of the pond); nam param āyadu uṇḍē – (when he is biased towards his followers as a protector) is there any responsibility for us in our protection?; nāygaḷōm – being lowly creatures like dogs; siṟumai ōrā – not considering our faults; em pirāṟku – for my emperumān; āḷ seyyādē – instead of being a servitor; en seyvān – for what; tōnṟinēn – was I born?

**Перевод:**

**umbarāl aṟiyalāgā oḷiyuḷār – the  word umbar refers to celestial people such as brahmā in the upper worlds upto satyalōkam. oḷi  refers to śrīvaikuṇṭam which cannot be understood by those such as brahmā and oḷiyuḷār refers to paramapadanātan (emperumān) who dwells in śrīvaikuṇṭam. cāndōkya upaniśat 3.13.7 says “ata yatata: parō divō jyōtir dīptyatē viśvata: pruśtēśu sarvata: pruśtēśu anuttamēśūttamēśu lōkēśu” (in paramapadam, that which is superior to individual and collective entities, that which is without anything superior to it, that which emits radiance in higher reaches). mahābhāratam āraṇya kāṇḍam 136.18,23 says “atyarkānaladīptam tat stānam viṣṇōr mahātmana: I svayaiva prabhayā rājan! duśprēkśam dēvatānavai:īī tahtra gatvā punarnemam lōkamāyānti bhārata! īī “ (Oh king! That place belonging to mahātmā viṣṇu is more radiant than sun and agni \[god of fire\]; because of its radiance, it cannot be seen by celestial and demoniac entities; Oh, one who was born in the clan of bharata! No one, who has ever gone there, has returned). Thus, mahābhāratam also mentions that śrīvaikuṇṭam cannot be seen by celestial people.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*periya perumāḷ told āḻvār “even though squirrels are animals, they took efforts to carry out kainkaryam for me. Even though such a good thought was not with you, did you at least have a thought to look up to me as the protector, just as śrī gajēndrāḻvān called out to me to protect him when he was caught by the crocodile when he stepped into the pond?”. āḻvār responded “No, even that was not there with me” Let us see the pāsuram and its meanings. உம்பரால் அறியலாகா ஒளியுளார் ஆனைக்காகிச் செம்புலால் உண்டு வாழும் முதலை மேல் சீறி வந்தார் நம் பரமாயதுண்டே நாய்களோம் சிறுமை யோரா எம்பிராற்கு ஆட் செய்யாதே என் செய்வான் தோன்றினேனே.*

**aṟiyalāgā** – even people who have high levels of jñāna (knowledge), as said in bhagavat gītā 16.14 “*īśvarō’hamaham bhōgī siddō’ham bhalavān sukhī* “ (I am īśvaran (one who controls everything and everyone), only I attain all enjoyment, I am endowed with supernatural faculties (by nature); only I am endowed with strength and I enjoy all comforts (by nature)), such as brahmā and Sivan, who think that they are īśvaran cannot attain paramapadam. Not only can they not attain it, they cannot know about it. Such is paramapadam. śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1.9.55 says “*yanna dēvā na munayō na cāham na ca śamkara:* I *jānanti paramēśasya tat viṣṇō: paramam padam* īī “ (the one place which celestial entities, sages, I (brahmā) and Sankara do not know of, that is viṣṇu’s paramapadam). Thus, brahmā also spoke about this supreme place.

oḷiyuḷār – mūnṟam tiruvandādi 51 says “*kalangāp perunagaram*” (the calm, supreme place). One who is enjoying nityasūris (permanent dwellers of paramapadam) constantly, came to samsāram to protect a suffering person such as I.

**ānaikkāgi** – such an [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), with unbounded eminence, came with dress astray and hair diśevelled in great hurry. Did he come for pirātti or tiruvadi (garudan) or tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) or such great entity? No, he took so much of effort for an animal. If one looks at the difference between the suffering elephant and the helping emperumān, it is something between a pond and a mountain. If he has to protect a follower, he does not look at his greatness or the lowliness of the follower. He only looks at the danger that his follower is in and the inseparable relationship between him and his follower, is the considered opinion here.

Let him not see the lowliness of the follower. Was the enemy befitting the stature of emperumān? Who was it, who created such an anger in emperumān?

**sem pulāl uṇḍu vāḻum mudalai mēl śiri vandār** – the one which gobbles up different types of fish which are much lower than itself, as a result of which it thinks that it has got a fullness equivalent to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), as mentioned by [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in tiruvāimoḻi 4.5.1 ”*vīṟṟirundu ēḻulagum tanikkōl seyya* “ (ruling all the worlds, sitting in paramapadam) – it was a worm in the water \[that is how vyākhyātā describes the crocodile\] on which he came with a rage. śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1.9.25 says “*yadāvat katitō dēvair brahmā prāha tata: surān* I *parāvarēśam śaraṇam vrajad vamasurārdanam*  īī” (brahmā, who was informed of the sufferings of the celestial entities, told them that the one who is īśvaran to all without any difference between the great and lowly people, the one who is the opponent of demons, take refuge under him, the emperumān). If emperumān had destroyed demons in order to protect brahmā et al who came to surrender to him to protect them from their enemies, it would not have been considered as anything wondrous. What is wondrous is that he came with a rage to protect an elephant which called out to him, to kill its enemy with his dress and hair in diśevelled condition considering it as a great enemy. śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 24.34 says “*krōda māhārayat tīvram vatārtam sarvarakśasām* I *duśprēkśa: sō’bhavatkruddō yugāntāgniriva jvalan*  īī “ ((śrī rāma) brought on himself deep anger to kill all the demons. He appeared like a huge ball of fire, a rare sight to behold). When someone commits an irreverent act on emperumān,  emperumān remains calm, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 1.10 “*sa ca nityam praśāntātmā* “ (perumāḷ is always peaceful). But he changes his nature and brings on anger when he sees that his follower is put to difficulty by an irreverent act, as seen in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 59.136 “*ata rāmō mahā tējā rāvaṇēna krutvraṇam* I *druśtvā pravaga śārdūlam kōpasya vaśamēyivāt* īī “ (after that, śrī rāma, the splendrous person, became angry on seeing hanumān, the greatest among monkey warriors, who was injured by rāvaṇa). When he is this angry, he will not care whether the enemy is a lowly person or not and hence there is nothing wondrous in this. Just as [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) says in periya tirumoḻi 5.8.3 “*kodiyavāi vilangin uyir malangakkoṇḍa sīṟṟam onṟu uṇḍu*” (there is a rage which killed the animal with cruel mouth), it is this great quality that people consider as their refuge.

**vandār** – while he could have destroyed the crocodile from his place through his sankalpa (will), how wondrous that he came to the pond, with diśevelled dress and hair! If he wanted to annihilate the world, he could have done that with his will. To protect his follower, if he does not come in person and destroy the enemy with his own hands his anger will not subside, is the opinion here. It appears to [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), by coming that day to help the elephant, helped āḻvār himself. ḍidn’t [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) describe the crocodile as “*kodiya vāy vilangu*” in periya tirumoḻi 5.8.3, as if he himself had been caught in its jaws? The surmise here is that if we don’t think that emperumān coming to help a follower is like helping ourselves, then we do not have *vaiṇavatvam* (the fundamental nature of being a śrī vaiṣṇavan).

**nam param āyaduṇḍē** – when emperumān comes to protect his follower due to his bias towards his follower, is there any responsibility for us in our protection? If emperumān looks at his greatness or at our lowliness or gets angry with the excessive sins that we have committed and decides “let this type of person suffer for some more time”, we can attempt to protect ourselves. When he is not like this, we have no basis for protecting ourselves.

**nāygaḷōm** – our lowliness is such that the elephant gajēndran is at the level of riśis such as sanakar (mānasa putra of brahmā). Even if we enter houses where the doors are open, we are being driven out like unwelcome dogs; we are without a recourse. Even if the person who brings up a dog touches us, he has to take a bath \[to get rid of dirt etc\], such is our lowliness.

**siṟumai ōrā empirāṟku** –  however lowly we are, those who are unable to see our lowliness. Just as śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam says “*avijñāta sahasrāmśu*” (even with thousands of eyes, he is ignorant \[of  his followers’ faults\]) he is ignorant of and blind to our faults and multiplies our \[good\] qualities with his thousands of eyes. muṇḍakōpaniśat 1.1.10 says “*yassarvajñas sarvavit yasya jñānamayam tapa:*” (which *paramapuruśan* \[supreme being\] knows the true character and inherent nature of all entities,  whose will is *jñanamayam* (full of knowledge)) – when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is praised as omniscient, what is the meaning of saying that he does not see our faults? He does not judge the extent to which he should help after seeing the faults in his followers. He ignores the faults and even considers the faults as good qualities. *empirāṟku* – thus, not looking at our faults and protecting us considering the danger that we are in, being our ordained Lord, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) refers to him as *empirāṟku.* Alternatively, *em*  would refer to being his svāmy and *pirāṟku* would refer to emperumān helping him.

**āḷ seyyādē** – It is apt to serve such a Lord. Once emperumān takes up our work as his responsibility, it is apt to submit our physical body and five senses to his service, as per our svarūpam (basic characteristics). Instead, āḻvār laments that he is not doing that.

**ēn seyvān tōnṟinēnē** – my birth has become wasted, says the āḻvār. Just as sumitrā tells her son lakśmaṇa that the purpose of giving birth to him was to help śrī rāma in forest, while the purpose of one’s birth is to carry out kainkaryam to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that since he did not do this, his birth is wasted. Instead of saying “*piṟandēn*” (was born), āḻvār is using the term “*tōnṟinēn*” to indicate that he appeared, like an acētana (insentient) entity. While lakśmaṇa was born to carry out all kainkaryams to perumāḷ, āḻvār says that he “appeared” since he was not useful in carrying out any kainkaryam.

We shall take up pāsuram 29, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dasan

## 29. Urilen kani illai

*ūrilēn kāṇi illai uṟavu maṟṟoruvar illai pāril nin pāda mūlam paṟṟilēn parama mūrty kāroḷi vaṇṇanē (en)kaṇṇanē kadaṟuginṟēn āruḷar kaḷaikaṇ ammā arangamā nagar uḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

Ur ilēn – I was not born in a divya dēśam where you \[emperumān\] are dwelling; kāṇi illai – I do not have hereditary rights over land (given for carrying out kainkaryam such as reciting tiruppallāṇḍu); uṟavu illai – do not have relatives too; maṟṟoruvar illai – I do not have anyone else; pāril – on this earth; nin pāda mūlam – your divine feet (the refuge for anyone who does not have any other refuge); paṟṟilēn – (as refuge) I have not embraced; parama mūrtti – the lord for all; kāroḷi vaṇṇanē – of a hue like dark clouds; (en) kaṇṇanē – my kriṣṇa (who has qualities such as simplicity); kadaṟuginṟēn – I am crying out to you; ammā – my Lord; arangamā nagar uḷānē – one who is dwelling in śrīrangam; kaḷai kaṇ Ar uḷar – who else is my protector (apart from you)?

**Перевод:**

**Ur ilēn – the “Ur” (town) that āḻvār refers to here is not any ordinary town. He refers to divya dēśams  where emperumān constantly dwells such as śrī sālagrāmam, śrī rāma’s birth place tiru ayōddi, the root of all arcāvatāra stalams tiruvarangam \[śrīrangam\] periya kōyil etc. How will the term “Ur” refer to divya dēśam? It will, because of the strength of this particular section of tirumālai. This particular section deals with his ākincanyam – that he has nothing in his hands to attain emperumān. Since he has said “ūrilēn”, the term “Ur” should be a path \[means\] to mōkśam. Since there is no cētana who is not born in a town, if the term “Ur” were to refer to any town, then all towns will become a means to attaining mōkśam. There is no authentic source to consider this meaning. Hence considering several authentic sources, one has to conclude that “Ur” refers only to divya dēśam. Had I been born in a divya dēśam, you would have tried to rescue me since I was born in a place which gives you pleasure. I do not have even that in me, says āḻvār. Is there any proof to say that living in a divya dēśam will rescue a person? There are many. gāruda purāṇam 11 says “dēśō’yam sarvakāma duk” (this place confers everything); śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 1.20 says “tē vayam bhavatā rakśyā bhavat viśaya vāsina:” (we, who are living and suffering in your place, are to be protected by you) \[the riśis living in daṇḍakāraṇyam inform śrī rāma when he goes there}; śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 109.22 says “nōccvasat tadayōdyāyām susūkśmamapi druśyatē I tiryak yōni gatāścānyē sarvē rāmānuvratā: īī ” (Not even a small creature alive is seen in ayōdyā. Animals and all other creatures have followed śrī rāma \[to paramapadam\]). Thus there are several authentic sources confirming this,**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*periya perumāḷ asks the āḻvār, “you do not have the thought which the elephant had when it thought of me as a means when it was in a state of distress. If a cētanan were born or owns land in a divya dēśam where I dwell such as śri sāḷagramam \[muktināt\], tiruvayōddi \[ayōdyā\] or śrīrangam periya kōyil, then I have to necessarily protect him. Do you have anything like this?”. āḻvar responds in this pāsuram that he has nothing like this. Let us see the pāsuram and its meanings. ஊரிலேன் காணி இல்லை உறவு மற்றொருவர் இல்லை பாரில் நின் பாத மூலம் பற்றிலேன் பரம மூர்த்தி காரொளி வண்ணனே (என்) கண்ணனே கதறுகின்றேன் ஆருளர் களைகண் அம்மா அரங்கமா நகருளானே.*

**kāṇi illai** – “even if you were not born in a divya dēśam, it is possible that there is some connection with divya dēśam through landed property. Do you have such a connection?” asked [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that he does not have even that. In olden days, for carrying out kainkaryams to emperumān such as reciting [tiruppallāṇḍu](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu/) in the temple, creating a garden for stringing flower garland to emperumān, either as a gift or as lease, land used to be given. āḻvār says that he does not have this connection too.

emperumān asks, “even if you do not have a connection through birth or through landed property with a divya dēśam, do you have any connection through people in a divya dēśam to whom you may either give or accept anything or through mantram \[either learning or teaching\]? “ . . . .

**uṟavu maṟṟoruvarilllai** – since there are no relatives there, there is no connection with divya dēśam, says the āḻvar. Since he says “no one” even distant relatives whom one may have seen occasionally or friends with whom one may have some connection – there is no one absolutely, says [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).

In that case, where do you live? …..

**pāril** – if one does not have place to stay, this earth creates boundless difficulties; I am living on such an earth says the āḻvār. mūnṟam tiruvandādi 51 says of śrivaikuṇṭam “*kalangāp perunagaram*” (trouble-free great abode). If one were to live in such śrīvaikuṇṭam, one can live without any worry as there will be no troubles due to lust, anger etc. On this earth, instead, 7 people grab the food and clothes that one person needs to eat and wear. I am living in such a horrible place.

**ninpāda mūlam paṟṟilēn** – I do not  have connection with your divine feet which give refuge to those who do not have anywhere else to go. *nin pādamūlam –* śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 38.33 says “*sa pitrā ca parityaktas suraiśca sa maharśibhi:* I *trīn lōkān samparikramya tamēva śaraṇam gata:* īī” (the kākāsuran, given up by his father (and mother) and dēvas (celestial people) who were together with sages, went all over the three worlds and took refuge under śrī rāma himself). The opinion expressed by the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) here is that he has become unfit to seek refuge under [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet which gave refuge even to kākāsuran, who had committed intolerable fault and became the target of his brahmāstra.

periya perumāḷ then tells him “if you do not have any benefit in any way, it appears that there is no alternative except to lose the opportunity of attaining me” . . . .

**parama mūrtti** – I do not have anything to protect myself. But don’t you have anything to protect me? You have a lot. āren’t you the Lord of everyone and much above everyone else? Only if you protect me too, can you become the Lord of everyone. If you do not protect a śēśabhūtan (a follower who serves emperumān), you cannot become a śēśi (the Lord or master). For that to happen, should not a śēśabhūtan realise that he is a servitor? The opinion here is that even when [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/), in a state of stupor, thinks that he is independent, [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/), considering that he is the owner and the cētana is his possession, will correct the cētana and make him realise the relationship between them.

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks the āḻvār, “since it is non-negotiable that you are my servitor, what is the means for you to get that servitorśip?” to which āḻvār responds that his beauty is the means.

**kāroḷi vaṇṇanē** – in [pāsuram 16](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-16/) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) had said “*ādaram perugavaitta aḻagan*” (the beautiful form of emperumān created affection). emperumān initially creates an interest towards him in the mind of cētana. Once the interest is created, his form (beauty) becomes the means for the [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) to attain him. When that beauty is there in emperumān, there is no hurdle for āḻvār to obtain the servitorśip, he says.

**kaṇṇanē** – even if the form is beautiful, instead of remaining in śrīvaikuṇṭam where you could not be reached, you made yourself approachable to us, by taking various incarnations and showing auspicious qualities such as *saulabhyam* (simplicity). Since you are *parama murti*, you are incomparable Lord to us; since you are *kāroḷi vaṇṇan* you create in us as *upāyam* (means), everything  starting with desire; since you are *kaṇṇan* you have auspicious qualities such as simplicity, affability etc; when you have all these, can I lose my goal? You are the apt Lord for us all. Even if you are not the Lord, you have such beauty that we cannot neglect you. Even if you do not have these two great qualities, you have auspicious qualities such as simplicity and affability and move with us closely. When such an entity like you is there, I do not have any shortcoming.

**kadaṟuginṟēn** – When I think of such greatness in you, I cry out to you, just as those who observe other means such as bhakti cry out when there is a delay in attaining you.

**āruḷar kaḷaikan** – Are you delaying protecting me when I cry out to you because you think that someone else will protect me? Can I protect myself with nothing in my hands? Can someone else who does not know as much I do that I do not have anything in me, protect me?

**ammā** – If the possession gets destroyed, is it not a loss to you, the Owner?

**arangamā nagar uḷanē** – even if you are the owner, if you are not near me, I might have remained calm, using that as an excuse. The meaning here is that “when you are lying at the nearby temple \[at śrīrangam\] expecting whether someone will come calling out to you to attain you, how can I remain quiet and not cry out to you?

We shall move on to the 30<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 30. Manattilor tuymai illai

*manattilōr tūymai illai vāyilōr insol illai sinattināl seṟṟam nōkkit teeviḷi viḷivan vāḷā punattuḻāy mālaiyānē ponni sūḻ tiruvarangā enakkinikkadiyēn sollāy ennai āḷudaiya kōvē*

**Пословный перевод:**

punam tuḻāy – tuḷasi which blossoms as if it is in its own land; mālaiyānē – having as a garland; ponni sūḻ – surrounded by kāvēri; tiruvarangā – sleeping in the temple; ennai āḷ udaiya kōvē – svāmy who has made me your servitor; manattil – in my mind; ōr tūymai illai – no purity at all (without lust, anger etc); vāyil – in my mouth; ōr in sol illai – not even one word with affection; vāḷā – without any benefit; sinattināl – due to anger; seṟṟam nōkki – looking inimically; thee viḷi viḷivan – I would speak harsh words full of fire; enakku – for me (with such faults); ini – after surrendering to you; en gadi – what refuge; sollāy – you must divine

**Перевод:**

**manattilōr tūymaiyillai – “na cakśuśa gruhyatē nāpi vācā manasā tu viśuddēna” ((paramaātmā ) can not be realised by eyes or speech; only through pure mind is he realised). śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 1.4.41 says “yē tu jñānavida: śudda cētasastē’kilan jagat I jñānātmakam prapaśyanti tvad rūpam paramēśvara! īī” (hēy paramēśvara! Those who realise that ātmā is jñānasvarūpam (full of jñānam) and are having purified mind  know that world is full of jñānam and it is your śarīram (physical body)). Thus, śāstras say that purity of mind is considered as a means for enjoying paramātmajñānam (knowledge on emperumān). āḻvār says that he does not have that purity of mind. Since he says ōr tūymai, he shows that the little bit of purity that one would get if even one of the faults of mind such as kāmam (lust), kōpam (anger), lōbham (greed), bhayam (fear) dvēśam (hatred), madam (arrogance), mōham (foolish passion), mātsaryam (malice) is lessened, is not there. Since he says tūymai illai he says that it is not that at some point of time the mind is muddled and some point of time it is pure. He says that always he is without purity of mind.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the 25th pāsuram, āḻvār said that he does not have karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams which are well known as means for attaining mōkśam (liberation). Instead of these, which are being observed only by the male members in brāhmaṇa, kśatriya and vaiśya kulam, there are means such as meditation and reciting of divine names which any one including women and śūdras can observe. āḻvār said in the 26th pāsuram that he did not have these also. In the 27th pāsuram, he said that he did not have the good thoughts that even squirrels had. In the 28th pāsuram he said that he did not have the intelligence that an elephant had as upāyam (means) to call out to emperumān. In the previous (29th) pāsuram he said that he did not have the benefit of living in a divya dēśam. Thus in these 5 pāsurams he confirmed that he did not have anything in him (ākincanyam).  emperumān told him “even if you did not have anything good in you, if you do not have anything bad in you which will not pose any difficulty or loss to me, then I can take you under me using that as the reason and grant  you the puruśārtam (benefit) that you desire. Are you without any bad qualities?”  āḻvār says “just as I do not have anything good in me, there is no fault in me which is not present in this world” \[in other words I have all the faults which are there in the world\], starting with this pāsuram for the next 5 pāsurams. In the previous 5 pāsurams he had said, like nammāḻvār said in tiruvāimoḻi 3.3.4 “niṟai onṟumilēn” (I have nothing good). In the next 5 pāsurams, he says, like nammāḻvār said in tiruvāimoḻi 3.3.4 “nīcanēn” (I am a very lowly person) that he is the repository for all lowly things in the world. In this pāsuram he says that he has no ānukūlyam (being good or friendly towards others) and that he has the bad quality of being jealous of others who progress. Let us see the pāsuram and its meanings. மனத்திலோர் தூய்மை இல்லை வாயிலோர் இன்சொல் இல்லை சினத்தினால் செற்றம் நோக்கித் தீவிளி விளிவன் வாளா புனத்துழாய் மாலையானே பொன்னி சூழ் திருவரங்கா எனக்கினிக் கதியென் சொல்லாய் என்னை ஆளுடைய கோவே.*

**vāyilōr insol illai** – There is no purity of mind, accepted. At least, through the mouth, is there the good quality of speaking sweet words?  He says that he does not have even that. By saying *ōr insol illai* he says that he has never enquired about the well being of anyone, which could be pleasing to the ears of the other person. He hints that even if someone else enquires about his well being, he has not responded in a nice way. Just as [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) had said in periya tirumoḻi 1.6.5 “*nedum collāl maṟutta nīcanēn*” (I am a lowly person who denied with harsh words), āḻvār also says the same. By saying *ōr* he hints that even he converses with women who he loves, he uses harsh words only which will singe their ears. Thus with these two sets of words, he has said briefly what he had said expansively in the earlier 5 pāsurams.

From now on, for the remainder of the pāsuram, he talks about his bad qualities.

**sinattināl seṟṟam nōkki** – even if did not have purity of mind and sweetness of words, others could move away from him and sustain themselves. But he will not leave at that. He will voluntarily go to a person who has some greatness in him and create harm for that person without any reason. Initially he will show all the anger in his mind and look at them inimically. *seṟṟam nōkki* – to look inimically. Alternatively, since both *seṟṟam* and *sinam* both indicate anger, and both have been used here, it shows deep anger. The considered opinion in this case is that by looking at the other person with a fiery look, the other person would almost fall down, drooping under the onslaught of his anger.

**thee viḷi viḷivan** – it is not only by look; even the words that he uses would appear like cinders to the other person and he will disappear completely.  Is the purpose of doing all this to gain anything? . . .

**vāḷā** – these are not done anticipating any benefit. Looking at someone with anger, scolding the person with harsh words are done so that these actions become the source of enjoyment. There is no other benefit needed for such actions. The opinion here is that just as good persons think that doing good to others is in itself a benefit, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) thinks that harming others is in itself a benefit to him.

[periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) says that since he is such a bad person, there was no benefit if he compels him. āḻvar responds . . . .

**punam tuḻāy mālaiyānē** – [cētanas](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) cannot leave him and go after seeing their lowliness because he has pledged to protect them if they desire him. He wears a garland of tuḷasi to show this quality openly. The meaning here is that since you are the ordained Lord and after seeing the sweetness in you, how can I let go of you? p*unam* refers to the land in which tuḷasi plant flourishes. The tuḷasi on his chest is in a flourishing state as it does in its own land and he wears such a garland.

**ponni sūḻ tiruvarangā** – even if you are a protector, if you are not near me it will not be beneficial to me. Only to ensure that even lowly persons can attain you, you have come to śrīrangam and taken a lying posture here. This is my fortune, feels [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). *ponnisūḻ* – on top of tuḷasi garland, the holy kāvēri surrounding him is like another garland.

**enakku** –  to me, with all the lowliness that has been spoken about so far.

**ini** – considering my lowliness, I cannot protect myself and after giving the responsibility of protecting me to you,

**gadi en sollāy** – please show me the way for my salvation. Even after accumulating all the harm such that it is not possible to uplift himself, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) could still plead for his case with him for uplifting him because emperumān is the ordained Lord.

What is the compulsion for me to tell you the way for your salvation, asks emperumān  . . .

**ennai āḷudaiya kōvē** – you being the svāmy (Lord) and I being the servitor are states that will last for ever. Hence who else can protect me? If my ātmavastu (the entity called ātmā) were to get degraded, isn’t the shortcoming due to that, yours?

We shall proceed to the 31<sup>st</sup> pāsuram, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 31. Tavattular tammil

*tavattuḷar tammil allēn danam padaittāril allēn uvartta nīr pōla enṟan uṟṟavarkku onṟum illēn tuvartta sevvāyinārkkē tuvakkaṟat turisanānēn avattamē piṟavi tandāy arangamānagar uḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aranga mā nagar uḷānē – one who dwells in śrīrangam; tavattuḷār tammil – among those who observe penance; allēn – I am not with them; danam padaittāril – among those who have earned money (for conducting tadīyārādanam – feeding others); allēn – I am not with them; enṟan uṟṟavarkku – to my relatives; uvartta nīr pōla – like salty water; onṟum allēn – I am not helpful to them for any benefit; tuvartta sevvāyinārkkē – even for women with reddish lips; tuvakku aṟa – in such a way that the connection severs; turisan ānēn – I was like a thief; piṟavi – birth; avattamē tandāy – gave me wastefully

**Перевод:**

**tavattuḷār tammilallēn – bruhadāraṇya upaniśat 6.4.22 says “tamētam vēdānu vacanēna brāhmaṇā vividiśanti I yajñēna dānēna tapasā’nāśakēna īī “ (brahma jñānis (people with divine knowledge)  wish to know about paramātmā through reciting of vēdas, rituals, donations, doing penance without any desire (as benefit)). vēdāntams (second part of vēdas) thus specified penance as a means for attaining mōkśam. Since āḻvār did not specify the benefit for which those who do penance carry it out, he hints here that he did not join those who carried out penance for attaining mōkśam; he did not join those who carried out penance for svargam (heaven) or such benefits; not only that, he did not join those who, after starting a penance find that there is some shortcoming in that, then make atonement for it to overcome the shortcoming, thus doing penance in a half-baked manner.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān tells āḻvār “You have said that you do not have any of the means ordained in the śāstras for attaining mōkśam (liberation) (in pāsuram 25). Even if it is not there, either to obtain svargam (heaven) or to grab money from others by cheating, if you have carried out yāgam (ritual like oblation) as mentioned in vēdas, I can gradually turn you away from those and enable you to attain mōkśam. Have you carried out such yāgams?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram “I have done nothing like that. Instead of attaining you with the birth that you have given me, I had immersed myself in worldly pursuits. I have shown only hatred towards others and my behaviour is such that it appears that I have utilised the birth that you had given only for destroying”. The connection between the earlier pāsuram and this pāsuram is that while he was jealous of the greatness of others in the previous, in this he has become an unwanted person even to his relatives because he was immersed in matters relating śabda etc (giving into the five senses of sound, sight, taste, smell and touch). Let us go torugh the pāsuram and its meanings. தவத்துளார் தம்மில் அல்லேன் தனம் படைத்தாரில் அல்லேன் உவர்த்த நீர் போல என்றான்  உற்றவர்க்கு ஒன்றும் இல்லேன் துவர்த்த செவ்வாயினார்க்கே துவக்கறத் துரிசனானேன் அவத்தமே பிறவி தந்தாய் அரங்கமா நகருளானே*

**danam padaittāril allēn** – earning money is also said to be puruśārtam (benefit) if spent for good purposes. In the example quoted above from bruhadāraṇya upaniśat, earning money is classified as puruśārtam. [śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/) 18.5 says ”*yajñadānatapa:karma na tyājyam kāryamēva tat* I *yajñōdānam tapaścaiva pāvanāni manīśiṇām*  īī” (yajñam (ritual), dānam (donation), tapas (penance) are karmas which should not be given up; they are only to be observed; yajña, dāna and tapas purify those who are immersed in bhakti). Again gītā explains this meaning in 17.20 “*dātavyamiti yaddānam dīyatē’nupakāriṇē* I *dēśē kālē ca pātrē ca taddānam sātvikam smrutam* īī” (that donation which is given to a person with good character, who does not do anything in return, with the thought that “I am bound to donate this” is called as sātvika (pure good) donation). taittirīya upaniśat, bhruguvalli 10.1 says “*na kancana vasatau pratyā cakśīta tat vrtam* I *yayākayāca vidayā bahvantam prāpnuyāt* īī” (we should not drive away anyone who comes to our house as a guest. This is a vow. For this, through means fair or foul, he \[no specific reference\] is ordained to earn food). Thus upaniśat says that money can be earned even in a foul way if it is to be spent for a good purpose. [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) et al followed this way. Thus, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that he was not in the company of those who have money for spending in bhagavat ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) and bhāgavata (followers of emperumān) matters. Even if he did not have penance and sātvika donation, he was not even part of those who donate in a rājasa or tāmasa way. gītā 17.21 says “*yattu pratyupakārārtam palamuddiśya vā puna:*” (that which is given in a grand manner, expecting a deed in return or some benefit is called as rājasa dānam); gītā 17.22 says “*asatkrutamavajñātam*” (that which is given to undeserving people is called as tāmasa dānam). Thus, when asked whether he was part of those who did rājasa donation or tāmasa donation or liberal donation to earn fame, āḻvār says he was not part of any of these groups. Even if he did not have these two (penance and donation), did he help his relatives at least?

**uvartta nīr pōla enṟan uṟṟavarkku onṟum allēn** – just as a person cannot open his eyes when his face is splashed with salty water, I had done so many harmful actions towards my relatives that they did not want to see me at all. If per chance any relative were to come face to face with me, I used to carry out such harmful deeds towards them that they would not live through the day. If a person were related to another, he should do only sweet or good things. But I did only harmful deeds; planned and executed such deeds. Since I was constantly engaged in bringing bad reputation for myself, I was not good even to me. The opinion here is that such a person (as I) became absolutely useless to my relatives, like salty water.

Even people who are not good to his relatives, they will be good to women who are close to their hearts. Do you have this? . . . .

**tuvartta sevvāyinārkkē tuvakkaṟat turisanānēn** – *tuvartta sevvāyinārkku* – these women will hide their evil intentions and, displaying their reddish lips, would enslave him. He will also not know the faults in their minds. He will not even know that they do not have real affection towards him. Not going beyond the beauty of their mouths, he will get entrapped in it. Such women will utilise the beauty of their mouth to hide the faults in their minds and to separate him from his parents and other relatives. *tuvakkaṟat turisanānēn* – I became a thief to even those women by doing harmful deeds even to them such that they will think “ let the relationship with him get severed” *turisan* – thief. In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19.4, vibhīśaṇa says “*tēna cāmsyamānita:*” (I was disrespected by him also). I was discarded even by the lowly rāvaṇa, says vibhīśaṇa. In the same say, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that he was not wanted even by women and was driven away by them.

**avattamē piṟavi tandāy** – after all these events, it appears that the birth that you gave me has been wasted. āḻvār reached lowliness due to his karma. Can he shift the blame to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)? āḻvār certainly knows what is karma (deeds) and what is his svarūpam (basic nature); hence he says this. karma is īśvaran’s reprimand to prevent āḻvār from attaining the benefit appropriate to his svarūpam. His svarūpam is such that his actions and inactions are under the control of īśvaran. If the gracious emperumān says “I have forgiven you” his karma will disappear. As śrī bhagavat gītā 10.10 says “*tēśām satatayuktānām bhajatām prīti pūrvakam* I *dadāmi buddi yōgam tam yēna māmupayānti tē*  īī” (I grant good intelligence with deep affection to those who wish to be with me and worship me always), if emperumān decided to grant good intelligence to this [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and guides him well, there is none to stop him. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) knows all these, he tells emperumān “you have given me a wasted birth” If a child falls into a well and the mother is witnessing it \[without doing anything\], it can be said that the mother pushed the child into the well. āḻvār says in the same manner.

**aranga mā nagar uḷānē** – [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is taking posture in śrīrangam only to correct through his beauty and auspicious qualities those who indulge in worldly pursuits following their karma, and who cannot be corrected by śāstras. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)’s opinion is that this did not fructify in his case.

Let us take up 32<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 32. Arttu vandalambum

*ārttu vaṇḍu alambum sōlai aṇi tiruvarangam tannuḷ kārttiraḷanaiya mēni kaṇṇanē! unnaik kāṇum mārkkam onṟu aṟiyamāttā manisaril turisanāya mūrkkanēn vandu ninṟēn mūrkkanēn mūrkkanēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaṇḍu – beetles; ārttu – making a sound; alambum – moving around; sōlai – of groves; aṇi – (for samsāram, materialistic world) being beautiful like an ornament; tiru arangam tannuḷ – inside the temple (lying down); kār tiraḷ anaiya – like dark clouds; mēni – having divine body; kaṇṇanē – Oh kriṣṇa! (who gives his divine body to his followers); unnai kāṇum mārkkam onṟu – a path to attain you; aṟiyamāttā – not knowing; manisaril – among the people; tirusan āya – like a criminal; mūrkkanēn – a fool who will not let go of what he likes; vandu ninṟēn – (unmindful of my lowliness) came and stood; mūrkkanēn kūrkkanēnē – how foolish am I !

**Перевод:**

**ārttu vaṇḍu alambum sōlai – the beetles, which came to drink honey from the flowers in the beautiful groves, drank much more than what they could hold; as a result, they were not able to remain in one place and were humming and moving around. This is very similar to the muktars (those who had left samsāram and reached paramapadam), who, after enjoying emperumān, start singing sāma gānam (reciting sāma vēdam with musical tone) out of sheer joy. The sweetness of tiruvarangam (śrī rangam) is such that beetles reached the status of muktars. In śrī rāmāyaṇam, sundara kāṇḍam, the army of monkeys, returning after sighting sītāp pirātti in lankā, entered maduvanam, the favourite garden of sugrīva. There, out of joy “gāyanti kēcit, praṇamanti kēcit, nrutyanti kēcit” – some of the monkeys sang, some danced, some pushed those that they were carrying. Thus, the beetles experienced what the monkeys in maduvanam experienced. There is a difference between the calling of āḻvār and that of the beetles, While āḻvār is crying out for his existence, the beetles are crying out of joy of drinking honey.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān tells āḻvār “samsāris do not involve themselves with me. You are a person who is not liked by samsāris. How did you come to me?”. āḻvār says “Is it possible to ascribe reasons to actions that I carry out on impulse, like a fool? I came because I am a fool”. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings. ஆர்த்து வண்டு அலம்பும் சோலை அணி திருவரங்கம் தன்னுள் கார்த்திரளனைய மேனிக் கண்ணனே! உன்னைக் காணும் மார்க்கம் ஒன்று அறியமாட்டா மனிசரில் துரிசனாய மூர்க்கனேன் வந்து நின்றேன் மூர்க்கனேன் மூர்க்கனேனே*

**vaṇḍalambum sōlai** – Just as mārkaṇḍēya maharishi was caught in praḷayam (deluge) and was tossed around, the beetles, entering flowers to drink honey, drank too much of it and were tossed around like the maharishi and like a garden caught in flood.

**aṇi arangam tannuḷ** – tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) is like an ornament to samsāram. In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 16.26 “*bhartā nāma param nāryā bhūśaṇam bhūśaṇādapi* I *ēśā virahitā tēna bhūśaṇārhā na śōbhatē* īī” (husband is a greater ornament than all other ornaments to a wife. This pirātti \[sītā\], who is to be decorated with all ornaments, is not bright as she is separated from her husband). Thus, when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), the consort of bhūmi, himself is an ornament to bhūmi, why mention that śrīrangam is an ornament?  The meaning hinted here is that while emperumān is the lord of leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm), when he is not in śrīrangam, it appears that the world is like those women who are not wearing their māngalya sūtram \[the sacred, yellow thread worn by married women\]. By joining the words, one can get *sōlai aṇi tiruvarangam* which will mean that the large temple has the groves as ornament.

**kārttiraḷ anaiya mēni** – when one looks at the lying posture of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) in śrīrangam it would appear as if the clouds, mistaking the flood of honey in the groves for sea, drank honey to their brim and unable to go out, took shelter inside the turret of śrīrangam temple. emperumān’s form is such that one can equate the clouds to him to an extent, because of the dark coloured form, coolness, helping others causelessly, without expecting anything in return and giving away everything to others, not keeping anything for self. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) also opined that he was reminded of emperumān when he looked at clouds, in his tiruvāimoḻi 8.5.8 “*tokka mēgap palkuḻāngaḷ*” (the mass of dense clouds).

**kaṇṇanē** – this word refers to periya perumāḷ’s greatness, more than the clouds’. Cloud will hover at a great distance, high above us, and help us by giving water. periya perumāḷ is born along with lowly people, remains close by, and helps by giving himself. How can we say that periya perumāḷ is kaṇṇan? Our pūrvācāryas would say that “when one looks at periya perumāḷ, one would see the person who had stealthily eaten all the butter saved by yaśōdaip pirātti and roamed around all over the place like a dark bull”. [tiruppāṇāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thiruppanazhwar/), in his amalanādipirān 10, conveys the same meaning “*koṇḍal vaṇṇanaik kōvalanāy veṇṇey uṇḍa vāyan . . . . aṇi arangan*” (śrī ranganātan is of dark form, with his mouth having gobbled up butter, as a covherd).

**unnaik kāṇum mārkkam onṟu aṟīyamāttā** – as kriṣṇa, in [śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/), [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) had prescribed certain paths to attain him, such as *karma yōgam* (the path of carrying out prescribed deeds), the resultant *jñāna yōgam* (path of knowledge), the resultant *bhakti yōgam* (the path of devotion), *avatāra rahasya jñānam* (knowledge of secret behind emperumān’s incarnations) and *puruśōttama vidyā* (the knowledge that emperumān is supreme). There are other paths too, which have not been mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā, but in other śāstras, such as reciting his divine names, residing in puṇya kśētrams (the sacred places) etc. These have been mentioned so that [cētanas (sentient entities)](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) could follow one of their choice and reach emperumān. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that it was not that he did not ***follow*** any of these, but he did not even ***know*** any of these paths.

**manisaril** –Did I lose the opportunity \[of attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] because I was born as an animal which does not have discriminating knowledge? It was as a human being, with the knowledge of discrimination, that I lost out on the path of attaining *mōkśam* (liberation).

**turisanāya** – when compared to me, other people would appear like nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam), says āḻvār. I am such a thief that even the other people would give up on me. I could have comforted myself had I been born as an animal which is not qualified to know about these paths. But, being born as a human being, I had lost out, laments the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). It is said “*mānuśyam prāpya lōkē’smin na mukō bhadirō’pi vā* I *nāpakrāmati samsārāt sa kalu brahmahā bhavēt* īī” (if a person is born in this samsāram without being deaf or dumb, and does not leave it permanently, he would be considered as having committed the sin of killing a brāhmaṇa). āḻvār says that despite being born as a human being, he did not gain anything. Instead he had lived life like an animal, not belonging to either of the two species. turisu – robbery. turisan – thief, cheat.

“When you are like this, why did you come to me?” asks [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). . . .

**mūrkkanēn vandu ninṟēn** – Since I am ignorant of my lowliness, I came. Had I realised my lowliness and your greatness, just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said in [tiruvāimoḻi 1.5.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/08/thiruvaimozhi-1-5-1-valavezh-ulagin/) “*vaḷavēḻulagu*” I would have realised that it would not  be proper on my part to come near you and stayed away from you. Instead of doing that, being ignorant, I came near you. Just as someone poisons the good water that is used by all the people in a town, I came near [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is enjoyed by wise people, and spoiled him. Another explanation is that just as when one says *kaṇṇan* there is no one who is not inimical to him, I too approached emperumān, not knowing the harm that this would cause him.

**mūrkkanēn mūrkkanēnē** – since he is repeating the word, he says that it is difficult to see such ignorance elsewhere in the world. Alternatively, it could be assumed that he looks at the greatness of emperumān and his own lowliness and says ”what a fool am I” for each of the two aspects.

We shall consider the 33<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 33. Meyyellam pogavittu

*meyyellām pōgavittu viri kuḻalāril pattup poyyellām podindu koṇḍa pōtkanēn vandu ninṟēn aiyanē! aranganē! un aruḷ ennum āsai tannāl poyyanēn vandu ninṟēn poyyanēn poyyanēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aiyanē – Oh Lord!; aranganē – the dweller of tiruvarangam (śrīrangam)!; mey ellām – all the true means or entities (thought, word and deed); pōgavittu – giving them up totally; viri kuḻalāril – in the net of women with well spread locks of hair; pattu – being caught; poy ellām – different types of lies; podindu koṇḍu – holding to the brim; pōtkanēn – I, without any place to go; un aruḷ ennum āsai tannāl – out of the desire kindled by your grace; vandu ninṟēn , poyyanēn, poyyanēn, poyyanēn – through the three means (of thought, word and action), I stood in front of you, as a liar; vandu ninṟēn – I stood in front you, the omniscient

**Перевод:**

**meyyellām – mey (truth) is something which leads to good for all jīvātmās, and describes things as they are \[without adding or removing anything\]. Truth is the basis on which āḻvār had said in the earlier pāsurams the good things that he did not have. In kaiśika mahātmyam 32 nampāduvān says “satyamūlamitam sarvam lōka:satyē pratiśtita: I nāham mityā pravakśyāmi satyamēva vadāmyaham  īī” (it was through satyam (truth) that all these worlds came about; the world sustains on truth; (hence) I will not speak lie; (always) I will speak the truth). Because he had a bit of involvement with emperumān, nampāduvān, despite being born in a lowly caste and carrying out the deeds of that caste, told the brahmarākśas (a demon) that came to eat him “those who are involved with emperumān will not tell lies”. ellām – since truth is of different types, āḻvār says all types. In other words, thinking of something, as it is, speaking it, as it is, and doing it, as it is. The opinion here is that he does not have any of these truths in him. Alternatively, some people will speak truth to their relatives and lies to others; he implies that he does not speak truth even to his relatives.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Until now, in the last eight pāsurams, āḻvār has said that he does not have any virtue in him and that he has all the vices too and that he does not bode well for emperumān. He emphasises the same in this pāsuram too. emperumān, on hearing his statement in the previous pāsuram that he, despite being a fool, had come to emperumān, asks him “if this statement of yours (of being an ignorant, foolish person) came from your heart, I shall take this as accepting your state of being without anything in your hands and try to redeem you. Was the statement that you are a fool said by you sincerely?” to which āḻvār responds, “even that is a lie”. emperumān asks “If there is nothing in you as a means to attain me, how do I redeem you”  and āḻvār says “is there any means other than the connection with your divine feet and your causeless mercy?”, mentioning the ultimate means for attaining emperumān. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings, first. மெய்யெலாம் போகவிட்டு விரி குழலாரில் பட்டு பொய்யெலாம் பொதிந்து கொண்ட போட்கனேன் வந்து நின்றேன் ஐயனே அரங்கனே உன் அருள் என்னும் ஆசை தன்னால் பொய்யனேன் வந்து நின்றேன் பொய்யனேன் பொய்யனேனே*

**pōgavittu** – I got rid of these truths to such an extent that they did not even find a place in the country that I live in. If I speak truth to my relatives, a trace of that would be with me; I did not do even this. Since samsāris are ignorant, it is possible to speak lies to them and manage; but I spoke lies even to you, who is omniscient, laments the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/).

When [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks him as to where he learnt to speak such lies . . . .

**viri kuḻalāril pattu** – I moved with women, says āḻvār. The meaning implied here is that just as it is impossible for someone to speak lies when he is close to emperumān, it is impossible to speak truth when someone is with women. Is it not rare for someone who speaks truth to be in the company of women? *virikuḻalāril* – if the lady had done up her hair, the man would exclaim “how beautiful it is! How well the garland has been strung around the hair!” And, if the lady had let loose her hair, he would exclaim “what can I say about the beauty of the hair let loose, like a peacock spreads its wings!” Just as [nammāḻvār](http://namm/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 5.10.2 “*adu idu udu ennalāvana alla ennai un seygai naivikkum*” (if one thinks of the playful activities of emperumān he would be totally involved and lose himself in emperumān), the person engages with women in the same way that a sātvika (purely good) person engages with emperumān.  p*attu*  – just as a fish trapped by a net cannot extricate itself, a person who gets trapped by women also cannot extricate himself. The extent to which he is trapped can be gauged from the fact that even when ācāryan (teacher) shows him the beauty of emperumān’s tirumēni through his upadēśam (spiritual teaching), the disciple’s heart keeps thinking of the women who are sweet to him.

**poyyellām podindukoṇḍa** – just as he got rid of all the truths, he accumulated gradually, all the lies. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) shows that just as involvement with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) would make liars to speak truth, involvement with women will make those who speak truth, to speak lies. *ellām* – just as truths are of different kinds, lies are also of many types and hence he uses the word *ellām* (all). He has accumulated whatever goes by the name of “lie”*. podindu koṇḍa* – āḻvār says that he made sure that none of the lies that he accumulated, left him. He says that he is the wholesale merchant for lies so that if someone wants to tell a lie to his lady, he would approach āḻvār. He made sure that none of the lies escaped from his clutches.

**pōtkanēn vandu ninṟēn** – if I think of my lowliness, I would step back saying “only those who speak truth can approach emperumān. A liar like me should not approach him”. Since I had no other place to go to, rather shamelessly, I came and stood in front of your divine presence. *ninṟēn* – just as those who carried out certain tasks as means to attain emperumān \[such as karma yōgam, jñāna yōgam, bhakti yōgam\] stand at the doorstep of emperumān to collect their benefit, like a servant stands for his salary, I too stood like them. *pōtkanēn* – one without a refuge.

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks him “I am approached by those good people who are able to control both their external and internal sensory organs. Why are you coming to me? Why don’t you go to those women with spread locks?” . . . .

**aiyanē** – Since I have a natural relationship with you which cannot be severed, I came. Even children, who have caused harms to their parents, approach them through the backdoor. I have such a relationship with you.

**aranganē** – emperumān asks him “was it through śāstras (sacred texts) or through ācāryan’s upadēśam (teaching of a guru) that you came to know of this relationship?”  [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) responds “it was not through these, but because you came to tiruvarangam to take rest, that I came to know” How is this possible?  emperurān, not considering the superiority of śrīvaikuṇṭam, came down to samsāram, knowing very well that it is inhabited by those who hate him, only to ensure that samsāris are redeemed. That the samsāris are people who are not detested by him is clear from the way he is lying at śrīrangam after taking a vow that he will not leave this samsāram until it is fully uprooted \[and shifted to śrīvaikuṇṭam\].

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks āḻvār “what is it that you want from me?” . . . .

**un aruḷ ennum āsai tannāl – vandu ninṟēn** – My desire is that I should get your grace; hence I came. His grace is such that even those who have harmed themselves to such an extent that they will get ruined, can desire for it. In śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 38.35, vālmīki says “*satam nipatitam bhūmau saraṇya: saraṇāgatam* I *vadārhamapi kākutsa: krupayā paryapālayat* īī” (even though kākāsuran, who fell to the ground and surrendered, deserved to be killed, śrī rāma, through his grace, saved him, as he is the refuge).

emperumān says “I am waiting for persons like you who do not have anything good in them and who have all the vices in them and who know the relationship between themselves and me. īsn’t it true that you have come after realising this?” . . . .

**poyyanēn** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) responds “if I look at it deeply, even this appears to be false. Can there be anything that I can claim as truth in front of you, who is omniscient?”.

**poyyanēn** – there is nothing that I do not have which is called as vice. I said this because, ahirbhudnya samhitai 37.30 says “*ahamasmyaparādānāmālayō’kincanō’gati:* I *tvamēvōpāyabhūtō mēbhavēti prārtanāmati:* īī *śaraṇāgatirityuktā sā dēvē’smin prayujyatām* īī” (“I am repository of all vices; I do not have any means (to protect myself); I do not have a refuge (other than you); you have to be my means” – śaraṇāgati is the thought that prays like this. Let this be carried out in the matter of this [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)). śāstras say that if one says like this in your divine presence, it will yield benefit. Hence I said that, says āḻvār. I did not say it because my lowliness hit me.

**poyannēnē** – I said this lie to you, who is omniscient by nature, such that even you were deluded into believing as truth what I said as lie. Thinking of the lies that he had said in the earlier pāsurams such as “*ādaram peruga vaitta aḻagan”* ([16](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-16/)), “*kaṇṇiṇai kaḷikkumāṟē*” ([17](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-17/)), “*paniyarumbu udirumālō*” ([18](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-18/)), “*udal enakku urugumālō*” ([19](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/09/thirumalai-19/)), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says “*poyyanēn poyyanēnē*” . How many lies can I say in your divine presence, where I should be speaking only truth?

Let us move on to pāsuram 34, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 34. Ullatte uraiyum malai

*uḷḷattē uṟaiyum mālai uḷḷuvān uṇarvu onṟu illāk kaḷḷattēn nānum toṇḍāyt toṇḍukkē kōlam pūṇḍu uḷḷuvār uḷḷiṟṟū ellām udan irundu aṟitiyenṟu veḷgippōy ennuḷḷē nān vilavaṟac cirittittēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

uḷḷattē – inside the heart \[mind\]; uṟaiyum – dwelling (constantly); mālai – emperumān, who is omniscient; uḷḷuvān uṇarvu – the knowledge to meditate upon; onṟu illā – without even a little bit; kaḷḷattēn nānum – I, the thief; toṇḍu āy – being subservient to you; toṇḍukkē kōlam pūṇḍu – putting on an act of being subservient; uḷḷuvār uḷḷiṟṟu ellām – the thoughts of those who are thinking; udan irundu – being together with; aṟidi enṟu – (knowing) that you know; ennuḷḷē – within myself; nān – I; veḷgi – being ashamed; pōy – leaving (you); vilavu aṟa – such that the rib will break; sirittittēn – I laughed

**Перевод:**

**uḷḷattē – emperumān dwells in the heart which is the spring-board for thoughts. If he were to dwell outside, he would not know anything other than whatever the eye sees. The opinion here is that since he dwells inside, it is not possible to deceive him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*emperumān lives in the minds of cētanas (sentient entities). Since the mind is the gateway through which our knowledge emerges, emperumān knows all our faults. āḻvār feels ashamed that he, being a liar, had caused blemish to the omniscient emperumān by approaching him. What a terrible deed have I done, exclaims āḻvār. “Instead of bringing blemish to emperumān, I could have stayed with samsāris (dwellers of materialistic realm), who are ignorant of my faults” says the āḻvār, “and it would be better even if I were to die, instead of causing harm to emperumān”. Saying so, āḻvār leaves the divine feet of emperumān in which he had such a strong desire. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings. உள்ளத்தே உறையும் மாலை  உள்ளுவான்  உணர்வு ஒன்று இல்லாக் கள்ளத்தேன் நானும் தொண்டாய்த் தொண்டுக்கே கோலம் பூண்டு உள்ளுவார் உள்ளிற்று எல்லாம் உடன் இருந்து அறிதியென்று வெள்கிப்போய் என்னுள்ளே நான் விலவறச் சிரித்திட்டேனே.*

**uṟaiyum** – dwelling in the heart constantly. If he were to go out for a little while, one can behave in whichever way one wanted. But since he dwells constantly in the heart,  even that is not possible, is the opinion here.

**mālai** – one who is greater than everyone else. Even though [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) dwells inside, he will not know everything which is inside. Even though cosmos pervades everything, it does not control everything that it pervades. The opinion here is that since emperumān knows everything and controls everything, it is well nigh impossible to deceive him.

**uḷḷuvān uṇarvu illā** – without the knowledge to meditate that he resides inside me as antaryāmi (indvelling soul).  *uṇarvu onṟu illā* – it is not that there is no knowledge about [paramātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)); there is  no knowledge about [jīvātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) too \[jīvātmā is the soul that takes up a body and controls it\]. Knowledge about self \[jīvātmā\] is a prerequisite for knowledge about paramātmā who dwells inside the jīvātmā. *onṟillā* – there is no knowledge about jīvātmā. Subsequently there is no knowledge about paramātmā. There is no knowledge about the nature of the body – “this body is very lowly; it is transient; it is different from jīvātmā; jīvātmā is there only to carry out *kainkaryam* (servitude) to paramātmā” etc.

**kaḷḷattēn** – I am such a thief that I put on an act to make other people not think that I do not have any knowledge. In an assembly of fools I will cheat them by saying “there is no fault in me known to myself” and in an assembly of learned people I will cheat them by saying “there is nothing good in me as known to my *antarātmā* (the indvelling soul)” by putting on an act of being lowly. Thus, the learned people will think “he thinks so lowly of himself. Surely he will have very good knowledge about ātmā  as well as of [paramātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)”. Despite being the repository of all vices, I made everyone believe, in this way, that I did not have any vice. I indulged in all these acts of chicanery even while knowing that you \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] are there as a witness.

**nānum  toṇḍāy** – I, without having any of the good qualities, did not carry out the act of servitude that people, with knowledge of themselves, should do. Who should carry out the servitude of whom? śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 17.14 says “*yaśca rāmam na paśyēttu yanca rāmō na paśyati* I *nindita: sa vasēllōkē svātmāpyēnam vigarhatē* īī” (one who has not seen rāma, one who has not been seen by rāma, he lives, despised by the worldly people. His ātmā will also despise such a person). If a caṇḍāḷa (one born in a very low caste) were to learn vēdas, vouldn’t  the people of the world despise him? In the same way, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) despises himself, saying that he was trying to carry out kainkaryam which should be carried out by good people.

**toṇḍukkē kōlam pūṇḍu** – not only did I carry out acts of deceit on you, I also made those who had very good qualities to bow down to me by indulging in falsified acts and earning good name. I wear the 12 *ūrdva puṇḍrams* \[the marks worn by śrīvaiṣṇavas on their bodies to indicate their subservience to emperumān\]; I don the five divine weapons on my body; I recite your divine names such as “*acyudā amararēṟē āyar tam koḻundē*” ([pāsuram 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thirumalai-2-part-1/)) from the lips instead of with full, deep belief; I sing about your sweetness by saying “*iccuvai tavaira accuvai peṟinum vēṇḍēn*” ([pāsuram 2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thirumalai-2-part-1/)); I claim that I placed my feet on the head of yamadarmarāja and his clan by singing “*nāvalittu uḻi taruginṟōm naman tamar talaigaḷ mīdē*” and bewilder people; I counsel others thinking of myself as a learned man. How much have I done like this? How much have I babbled like this?

**uḷḷuvār uḷḷiṟṟellām udan irundu aṟidi enṟu** – I understood that he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] knows every thought that one thinks of, as he dwells constantly inside the heart which is the source for thoughts to emerge. It is pertinent here to think of the words spoken by śakuntalā to duśyanta. śakuntalā vākhyam (bhāratam 38.29) says “*ēkō’hamasmīti ca manyasē tvam na hruccayam vētsi munim purāṇam* I *yō vēditā karmaṇa: pāpakasya tyāntikē tvam vrujinam karōśi* īī” (\[Oh duśyanta maharāja !\] you think “only I am there”. You do not realise that bhagavān, the ādipuruśa \[the first entity\] is dwelling constantly in the heart . You are carrying out sinful deeds at the proximity of one who knows of such sinful deeds). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) suggests here that he was indulging in improper deeds even when emperumān, who is omniscient and who controls everything, dwells in his heart.

**veḷgip pōy** – āḻvār feels ahamed after realising his deceitful nature, emperumān’s omniscience and his dwelling constantly in his heart. *pōy* – Once he felt ashamed, he could not stand in the presence of emperumān, with bowed head, and leaves his presence. If one were ashamed of something, he would leave from that place and go to a place where it is not present. Without the thought that there is not a place where emperumān is not present, āḻvār searches for such a place.

**ennuḷḷē nān** – samsāris cannot join hands with him for laughing off his act of foolishness, as they are totally ignorant. [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) also will not join him in his laughter as [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) wanted to leave the presence of emperumān. Hence āḻvār has to laugh all by himself. Since there was none to join him in this act of laughing and since he realised his foolishness, he himself became his supporter for laughing.

**vilavaṟac cirittittēnē** – He laughed so much that his ribs broke, says āḻvār. Since the deed that he carried out \[of finding a place which did not have emperumān’s presence\] was in the forefront of all foolish acts, there was no limit to his laughter too. His act of trying to leave emperumān’s presence is like a person who, wishing to commit suicide by jumping into fire, eats betel leaves and decorates himself with flowers before he jumps and he laughs at that. When arjuna heard the other paths to attain bhagavān, before kriṣṇa spoke to him about [carama ślōkam](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html), arjuna felt disheartened because the other paths were so very difficult to practice. He waited with the hope that kriṣṇa would offer a solution to this. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/), since he thought that he should not approach emperumān any more, decides to leave him without any desire. He tries to leave the presence of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), laughing to himself, just as a person, deciding to jump into fire, takes betel leaves and decorates himself with flowers.

We shall move on to the 35<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan
