## 35. Tavi anru ulagamellam

*tāviyanṟu ulagam ellām talai viḷāk koṇḍa endāy sēviyēn unnaiyallāl cikkenac cengaṇ mālē āviyē! amudē! endan āruyir anaiya endāy pāviyēn unnai allāl pāviyēn pāviyēnē*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – on that day (when the worlds were seized by mahābali); ulagam ellām – all the worlds; tāvi talaiviḷākkoṇḍa – going across, pervading everyone’s head with divine feet; endāy – my svāmy (lord); unnai allāl sēviyēn – I will not worship anyone other than you; sem kaṇ mālē – Oh the one with reddish eyes, being partial towards his followers!; āviyē – being my vital air; amudē – being the nectar; endan Ar uyir anaiya endāy – my svāmy, being the in-dwelling soul of my life, like nectar; pāviyēn – sinner like I; cikkena – surely (at all times); unnai allāl – other than you; pāviyēn – will not think of (others); pāviyēnē – I have committed lot of sins

**Перевод:**

**tāvi – crossing. If emperumān had given prior information that “I am going to keep my foot on your heads”, the samsāris, with huge egos, would have refused to take his divine foot on their heads and would have ordered him “don’t keep”. Just as people with satva guṇam (pure good qualities) would have ordered “tiruvāṇai ninnāṇai” (tiruvāimoḻi 10.10.2) in order to ensure that emperumān keeps his divine foot on their heads without refusing, these samsāris would have ordered that emperumān’s divine foot should not come near them. It was only because of this that emperumān kept his divine foot on their heads without their knowing about it, and grew rapidly, without either they \[samsāris\] or he, getting enervated.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*periya perumāḷ felt sad that āḻvār had decided to move away from him and may even get ruined. He is worried that he may lose him. He asks āḻvār “āḻvīr? What is the reason for your decision to leave my presence?” to which āḻvār responds “instead of approaching you, by telling lies to an omniscient entity to you and bringing disrepute to you, it is better to move with samsāris, who do not know my intentions and with whom I have been together since time immemorial. I can remain with them from now onvards”.  periya perumāḷ divines “it is good that you approached me. I do not see faults in my followers. samsāris will look at all the faults of others, without exception. I look at all the good qualities of followers, without leaving anything. Hence, irrespective of the number of faults that one has, it is very apt if he leaves samsāris and comes to me”.  āḻvār asks periya perumāḷ “are these mere comforting words or have you shown this in practice?” and periya perumāḷ replies “It is there for a very long time” and shows āḻvār how he measured the worlds as trivikrama. “That I measured all the worlds as trivikrama is mentioned in vēdas for a very long time. Have you not heard of it? By keeping my foot on both brāhmaṇas, who control their sensory organs, and caṇḍāḷas, who are of very low birth, without differentiating between them, I showed that there is none who is ill-disposed towards me” says emperumān, showing āḻvār his simplicity and how it is difficult for him to sustain himself should āḻvār leave him, comforting āḻvār in this pāsuram, who also gets comforted. āḻvār meditates on the meanings meditated by nammāḻvār in his tiruvāimoḻi 1.5.1 “vaḷavēḻ ulagu” in the previous pāsuram and this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings. தாவியன்று உலக எல்லாம் தலை விளாக் கொண்ட எந்தாய் சேவியேன் உன்னையல்லால் சிக்கெனைச் செங்கண் மாலே ஆவியே! அமுதே! என்றன் ஆருயிர் அனைய எந்தாய் பாவியேன் உன்னை அல்லால் பாவியேன் பாவியேனே.*

**anṟu** – on that day when the status of he being the master and samsāris being the servitors was in ruins and mahābali had seized the worlds.

**ulagamellām** – instead of being satisfied with measuring the three worlds seized by mahābali, his divine foot went all the way to brahmalōkam, after it graced the heads of all the people, without any discrimination between the great and lowly ones, and grew in size.

**talai viḷākkoṇḍa** – āḻvār shows that the excitement of the protector \[emperumān\] was much more than the size of the entity that he protected . puruśa sūktam 1 says “*sahasra śīrśā puruśa: sahasrākśa: sahasrapād* I *sa bhūmim viśvatō vrutvā atyatiśtaddaśāngulam* īī” (nārāyaṇa, who is called as puruśa, has countless heads, countless eyes, countless legs; he surrounded the earth on all sides and pervaded for countless yōjanas (one yōjana is 10 miles or 16 kilometers) beyond the earth). Thus puruśa sūktam also mentioned that emperumān pervaded much beyond the earth, all the way upto nitya vibhūti. The term *tāvi talai viḷākkoṇḍa* shows that he measured much more than what he had to measure \[the three worlds seized by mahābali\].

**endāy** – what is the reason for emperumān to keep his divine feet, referred to by tirumangai āḻvār in tirukkuṟundāndagam 6 as “*amarar sennippūvinai*” (the divine feet borne by nityasūris on their heads) on the heads of samsāris, who do not have any involvement with them? āḻvār shows that it is due to the indelible relationship between emperumān and samsāris. āḻvār addresses emperumān as *endāy*  since emperumān had kept his divine foot on āḻvār’s head too, on that day. The opinion here is that it is due to the goodwill of the effort on that day that he has reformed today. Just as a mother hugs her child while it is sleeping, emperumān also graced the samsāris on that day by touching them with his divine foot and protecting them. On that day, āḻvār did not know the relationship that he had with emperumān and today, since he knows it, he calls emperumān as *endāy* (lord or master); even today, the samsāris have not realised this and āḻvār makes himself happier for them too.

**sēviyēn unnai allāl** – emperumān asks āḻvār “why then did you think of moving away from me?” āḻvār responds “I thought of leaving you because I did not know your simplicity. Having known this, I will not leave you hereafter and will not hold on to something else” “If I leave you, I should either be independent like a king or be a servitor to someone else. Such shortcomings will not come to me hereafter” says the āḻvār. In [*tirumantram*](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html) \[aśtākśaram\], the first word, praṇavam negated being servitor to others and the second word, *nama*, negated being independent like a king. āḻvār shows these two aspects in his words *sēviyēn unnai allāl*. Is it correct to say that he will not *worship* others? Since being a servitor to emerpumān is considered to be equivalent to vorśipping him, by force of habit, āḻvār uses the same word (vorśipping) when it comes to talking about others.

emperumān asks him “for how long will you have this determination?” . . . .

**cikkena** – this will last forever. It will last till my ātmā lasts \[i.e. forever\]. Just as nammāḻvār says in [tiruvāimoḻi 2.6.1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/04/thiruvaimozhi-2-6-1-vaikundha-manivannane/) “*pidittēn koḷ cikkenavē*” (I held on to you forever), āḻvār gives refuge to emperumān who is in grief that āḻvār would leave him. It is āḻvār’s turn to tell emperumān “*mā suca:*” (don’t feel saddened), just as emperumān told arjuna in [bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/).

**sengaṇmālē** – this word does not reflect the natural reddiśness in emperumān’s eyes. It denotes the unique reddiśness acquired by emperumān’s eyes on hearing the word “*cikkena*” from āḻvār. After hearing āḻvār say “*sēviyēn unnaiyallāl cikkena*”, the fear that emperumān felt after hearing āḻvār say in the previous pāsuram “*veḷgippōy*” (I am leaving you out of shame) disappeared; the uncontrollable love that emerpumān felt towards āḻvār  manifested as radiance in his eyes. Another interpretation for *sengaṇmālē* is – In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 17.29, sugrīva tells śrī rāma when vibhīśaṇa comes to take refuge under śrī rāma “*vadyatāmēśa daṇḍēna tīvrēṇa sacivais saha* I *rāvaṇasya nruśamsasya bhrātā hyēśa vibhīśaṇa:*  īī” (let this vibhīśaṇa, with his ministers, be tormented, through harsh punishment. Is not this vibhīśaṇa the brother of the cruel rāvaṇa?). On hearing this, the heart of śrī rāma was in tatters. It was similar to that of periya perumāḷ when he heard from āḻvār in the previous pāsuram “*vilavaraccirittittēnē*”. When hanuman responded with this ślōka (yudda kāṇḍam 18.1) “*ata: rāma prasannātmā śrutvā vātu sutasya ha* I *pratyabhāśata durtdarśa: śrutavānātmani stitam* īī” (later, one who cannot be won over (by words), one who had learnt about various matters (from vasiśta and others), śrī rāma, after hearing vāyu’s son (vāyu is the dēvatā for wind) hanuman’s sweet words, became clear in his mind and started speaking from his divine mind). rāma came back to existence after hearing hanuman. In the same way, periya perumāḷ, after hearing āḻvār say *cikkena*  came back to existence. Just as hanuman told śrī rāma in rāmāvatāram “*mā suca:”* (do not feel sad), āḻvār offers refuge to periya perumāḷ in [arcāvatāram](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/p/archavathara-anubhavam.html). *sengaṇmālē — unnaiyallāl sēviyēn* – the opinion here is that āḻvār canot go from perumāḷ when perumāḷ’s face blossoms out of love, on getting āḻvār.

Is that all? . . . . .

**āviyē** – where else can I go from you, who is like my breath \[vital air\]? āḻvār does not consider that air \[which is breathed\] is a state of *vāyu* (wind), as mentioned in śāstras. Just as nammāḻvār says in tiruvāimoḻi 7.2.9 “*en tirumagaḷ sērmārvanē ennum ennudaiya āviyē ennum*” (one who is the repository for pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) and who is the breath of [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)), this āḻvār considers śriman nārāyaṇa as his breath.

**amudē** – apart from being the sustaining force like breath, emperumān is also like nectar, which is infinitely sweet, and which is like a medicine for saving āḻvār’s ātmā from death.

Is that all? . . . .

**en āruyiranaiya** – aren’t you my ātmā? The opinion here is that the body cannot go outside and live, without the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/).

**endāy** – aren’t you the svāmy (master) who took me back when I tried to leave you and ruin myself, thinking that I am not fit to approach you? The view here is that āḻvār has no place to go to, leaving emperumān, who is (1) like sengaṇmāl to āḻvār, showing motherly love through his lotus like eyes; (2) āḻvār’s breath; (3) like a sweet nectar to āḻvār; (4) āḻvār’s ātmā, guiding him properly like his svāmy.

emperumān asks the āḻvār “the words that you speak are beautiful. Are these also lies like you said earlier in the [33](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thirumalai-33/)[<sup>rd</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thirumalai-33/) [pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thirumalai-33/) “*aiyanē aranganē*” and [30](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thirumalai-30/)[<sup>th</sup>](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thirumalai-30/) [pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/11/thirumalai-30/) “*ennai āḷudaiya kōvē*”? . . .

**pāviyēn unnaiyallāl** – āḻvār says “the reason why I decided to leave you (as being unapproachable), who graced the heads of all samsāris with your divine feet, is because of my sins”

**pāviyēn pāviyēnē** – The meaning of first *pāviyēn*  is that he has lot of  sins; the meaning of the second *pāviyēn* is that he will not think of anyone else other than emperumān. Another interpretation for *pāviyēn pāviyēnē* – both the terms mean the word *pāvi*, showing the excessive sin that he has committed. The opinion here is that his sins are the reason for his trying to leave emperumān, who, not only does not mind the faults in followers, but who also does not hate samsāris who hate him. Yet another interpretation is that through the first *pāviyēn* he talks about the sin that he would have committed by leaving emperumān and ruining himself and the second *pāviyēn* would indicate the sin that he would have committed by leaving emperumān, who is the cause for existence of all, by ruining emperumān himself and consequently all the others.

We shall take up the 36<sup>th</sup> pāsuram next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 36. Malaikkanru varaimun endum

*maḻaikkanṟu varai mun ēndum maindanē! maduravāṟē! uḻaikkanṟē pōla nōkkamudaiyavar valaiyuḷ pattu uḻaikkinṟēṟku ennai nōkkādu oḻivadē unnaiyanṟē aḻaikkinṟēn ādimūrtti! aranga mānagaruḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – at that time (when indra created a shower of hailstones); maḻaikku – to stop the shower; varai – a mountain (that could be laid hands on); mun – before (cows and other creatures could get harmed); ēndum – bearing (effortlessly); maindanē – Oh one who has tremendous strength!; madura āṟē – Oh one who is like a most enjoyable river!; uḻai kanṟu pōla nōkkam udaiyavar – women with eyes like a fawn’s; valaiyuḷ pattu – getting trapped in the net (of their eyes); uḻaikkinṟēṟku ennai – quivering person like me; nōkkadu oḻivadē – is it correct not to look at me comfortingly?; ādi mūrtti – the primordial cause; aranga mānagar uḷānē – Oh, one who dwells inside the huge tiruvarangam (śrīrangam)!; unnai anṟē – only you (who is looking for protecting others); aḻaikkinṟēn – I am calling out to

**Перевод:**

**maḻaikku – among āḻvārs, whenever they use the term “maḻai” \[rain\], it always refers to the hailstones that indra \[lord of celestial entities\] showered when kriṣṇa lifted the gōvardan mountain, just as the word “ānai” \[elephant\] would always refer to gajēndrāḻvān \[the elephant which was caught by the crocodile when it entered the pond to pluck a flower and offer it to emperumān\]. Why is āḻvār now referring to preventing hailstones by lifting gōvardana mountain? It is only to attain him and overcome the sorrows that he is having in samsāram (materialistic realm). He is recalling how the covherds overcame the dangers posed by indra by attaining him. Just as people who are hungry call out to wealthy people “Oh Lord! Please remove our hunger”, āḻvār is calling out to emperumān “Oh one, who rids people of dangers! Please remove my danger”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār said in the previous pāsuram that emperumān showed his simplicity and took him under his wings, after āḻvār tried to go away from him thinking that he is not qualified to approach emperumān. After coming out of the “unqualified” tag, his craving for attaining emperumān increased manifold. He desired that emperumān should take him to paramapadam and make him a servitor, as he usually does with followers who crave for him. But emperumān did not do it. This is because emperumān had struggled to keep āḻvār with him after he decided to leave and he felt that only after increasing āḻvār’s craving for him, which is like his hunger to enjoy him, would āḻvār be able to enjoy him without any indigestion. Hence he kept quiet. āḻvar got scared whether he would remain in this samsāram which is ruled by worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc (the five sensory perceptions such as sound, touch etc) and suffer.   Due to his craving desire to attain emperumān and his hatred for the worldly pursuits, he cries out such that periya perumāḷ cannot sleep peacefully and those who hear him would melt. Let us first go through the pāsuram and its meanings. மழைக்கன்று வரை முன் ஏந்தும் மைந்தனே! மதுரவாறே! உழைக்கன்றே போல நோக்கம் உடையவர் வலையுள் பட்டு உழைக்கின்றேற்கு என்னை நோக்காது ஒழிவதே உன்னையன்றே அழைக்கின்றேன் ஆதி மூர்த்தி அரங்கமா நகருளானே.*

**anṟu** – śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 5.11.13 says “*tatastat gōkulam sarvam gōgōpījanasankulam* I *atīvārttam harir druśtvā maitrēyācintayat tadā* īī” (Oh maitrēya! After seeing how the entire gōkulam, full of cows and gopīkās (covherd girls), was sorrowful, \[kriṣṇa\] started thinking immediately). *anṟu* refers to the day when the calves, cows and covherds were quivering on seeing the hailstones showered by indra. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that the danger that he faces today is the same as that faced by calves, cows and covherds. Just as [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) protected them that day from the hailstones, he wants emperumān to protect him from the shower of sorrow. āḻvār asks emperumān “is there any compulsion that one should suffer from hailstone to be protected by you? Will you not protect someone from shower of sorrow? Will you protect only if the entire village suffers? When I am suffering to the same extent as the entire āyppādi (gōkulam) will you not protect me?

**varai** – When indra ordered that all the clouds open up, they showered with such an intensity that it looked as if it was the deluge. On seeing this action of indra because he was denied food by āyppādi, not having the time to think of sudarśana chakra (the divine weapon, disc), kriṣṇa picked up a mountain that he could lay his hand on. It is clear from this that it is not necessary for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to use a particular type of weapon to protect a particular type of people. The opinion of [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) here is that emperumān cannot say that he does not have a suitable weapon to protect him.

**mun** – since he had already used the term “anṟu” to denote a specific time, the word “mun” cannot denote ‘earlier time’. Hence, we have to consider the meaning for this word as “before the cows got harmed”. āḻvār shows here that emperumān did not wait for harm to come the way of cows and covherds, as they had no other refuge, but protected them before they were harmed. [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/), in his stōtra ratnam 49 says “*avivēkaghanāndadingmukhē  bahudā santatadu:kha varśiṇi* I  *bhagavan! bhavadurtdinē pada: skalitam māmavalōkayācuta!*īī” (Oh one who is full of auspicious qualities such as jñāna (omniscience), śakti (power) etc! Oh one who does not let go of his followers! Show mercy on me who has fallen from the good path due to lack of discriminating ability in this samsāram, which is darkened from all sides by clouds and which is continuously buffeted by different types of sorrows by the darkness of monsoon ). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is asking as to why [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is not protecting him when he is calling out to him in pain.

**ēndum** – āḻvār shows how kriṣṇa lifted the mountain effortlessly, when he was only 7 years old, as if it were a ball of flowers. āḻvār is asking whether he is not being protected because emperumān will feel tired if he were to protect āḻvār. Just as nityasūris were holding garlands to adorn him, as said in tiruviruttam 21 “*sūttu nanmālaigaḷ ēndi*”, emperumān held the mountain for protecting his followers.

**maindanē** – Oh one who has huge strength to lift a mountain! The word *maindu* indicates strength. Alternatively, *maindan* also indicates a young person. Thus, we can also consider the meaning as one who becomes young in age since he feels happy on seeing followers who come to him for protection. When a person gets the food that sustains him, he appears younger. The food that sustains a person varies for each person. For [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is the protector of all, protecting his followers is the food that sustains him. Hence once he gets a person to be protected, he becomes younger. āḻvār laments that while protecting him is for the benefit of emperumān, he has to call out to him for protection.

**madura āṟē** – Oh one who is like a river which has taken the form of sweetness! Even if you are not the protector, as mentioned before, but one who is going to beat up and swallow the follower, your beauty is such that no one will leave you. *madura āṟē* – why call him as river instead of sea? Since sea water is brackish, it cannot be used for anything. River water, on the other hand, would be useful for taking a bath on a hot day, for washing the face, for drinking when thirsty. Even when it is not hot, it would be useful for removing dirt. Sea will not come to the spot where he is, while river will. Just as a flowing river, during floods, will break dams and come fiercely, this loving river \[emperumān\] also gushes in, breaking the dam that [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) had built around himself, feeling  “unfit”. Just as the river comes to the place of the person who uses it, this loving river, in its incarnation, comes to his place. āḻvār thinks of how [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took birth in the dwelling of covherds, after leaving his paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam).

emperumān asks āḻvār “that day, I removed the danger when indra showered hailstones. Through whom are you facing danger, today?” āḻvār responds . . . . .

**uḻaikkanṟē pōla nōkkam udaiyavar valaiyuḷ pattu uḻaikkinṟēṟku** – I am suffering, being trapped in the net of eyes of women, which are like the eyes of a fawn. While these women do not have any affection in their hearts, they will feign as if they consider him as everything and they have no other refuge. I am caught in the trap laid by their eyes, which are like the eyes of a beautiful deer. u*ḻai* refers to a deer and *uḻaikkanṟu,* to a fawn. A net is one which will trap an animal, ensure that the animal cannot escape and, by itself, will be of no use to the animal. The vision-like net of these women also traps a person, making it very rare for someone to escape from it; prevents the person from getting involved with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is there as sustaining force and nurturing; and, it is also not having any sweetness within it.

**ennai** – me, who, unlike the cows in gōkulam which were protected, knows that I have fallen in deep sorrow, knows that only you can protect me from this sorrow and who is praying to you to protect me.

**nōkkādu oḻivadē** – when the danger came through stones, you protected with stones \[mountain\]; when the danger is through the glance \[of women\], should you not glance at me (protect me)? There is no trouble for you in protecting me, as you do not have to lift a mountain. It is enough if you just glance at me, to comfort my heart. Instead of praying to him to “look at me”, why is he telling [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), with sadness, “why are not looking at me?” This is because, his closeness to emperumān and emperumān’s firm affability and simplicity make him say these words with sadness. Even after [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) speaks these words, why is emperumān keeping quiet? “If I take him under me, he will again try to leave me, thinking  that he is unfit to be with me”. Hence, just as a mother waits for her child’s indigestion to be cured fully and the child feels hungry, before feeding it, emperumān also decides to wait for āḻvār’s craving to increase further before giving himself to āḻvār for him to enjoy. Just as a sick child’s wailing to his mother “give me food”, after he is cured, makes the mother pleased, emperumān also keeps lying down, listening to āḻvār’s cry.

**unnaiyanṟē aḻaikkinṟēn** – am I calling at the door of any lowly person? When I am calling out “please protect me” to you, who is searching for someone to be protected, why is it not falling in your ears? Are you having some other work? You cannot say that you are involved in the work of controlling the world. Even that work appears to be done only for me.

[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) asks him “what is the compulsion for me to carry out your work at your bidding?” ..

**ādimūrtti** – when these [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) entities were mingled together with [acētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/09/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter/) entities without any difference during deluge, without their asking, you only gave them an opportunity and hence it is your responsibility to protect them. cāndōjña upaniśat 6.2.9 says “*tadaikśata bahu syām prajāyēyēti tat tējō’srujata*” (that brahmam took a vow “l will multiply myself’ for that “I will create myself”. It created radiance). Since you created these, you are responsible for their protection. It is the parents’ responsibility to protect their child. When a child trips and falls, it cries out “ammā” only.

**aranga mānagar uḷānē** – to make you protect me, do I need to go to the origin of creation, just like one tries to clean an old well? Is it not enough that you left paramapadam and came to śrīrangam for sleeping? Is there any benefit for your being in śrīrangam other than protecting me? Based on each person’s deeds (good or bad) you could have protected from paramapadam itself. There was no need for you to come to śrīrangam.

**arangamānagar uḷānē unnaiyanṟē aḻaikkinṟēn** – I cannot call you at paramapadam. After you came to śrīrangam to create a *ruci* (taste for only good things), and after I acquired that taste, should I lose it?

Even after hearing this from [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/), why is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) keeping quiet? emperumān has heard such calls from paramapadam after they have enjoyed him and calling out to him. He has heard samsāris calling out to him for granting them worldly wishes. He knows that āḻvār’s calling out is not one such. The efforts that emperumān had taken since time immemorial are now fructifying and this call of āḻvār is due to that. Since he has not heard this in a long time, he wants to hear this for some more time. Hence he is keeping quiet.

Let us next take up the 37<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 37. Telivilak kalangal nir

*teḷivilāk kalangal nīr sūḻ tiruvarangattuḷ ōngum oḷiyuḷār tāmē anṟē tandaiyum tāyum āvār eḷiyadōr aruḷum anṟē en tiṟattu empirānār aḷiyan nam paiyal ennār ammavō! kodiayvāṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

teḷivu ilā – without being clear; kalangal – being muddled; nīr sūḻ – surrounded by kāvēri; tiruvarangattuḷ – inside the temple; ōngum – being resplendent; oḷi uḷār tāmē anṟē – isn’t he the radiant periya perumāḷ himself; tandaiyum tāyum āvār – is father and mother (to us); en tiṟattu – in my matter (to be carried out); eḷiyadu ōr aruḷum anṟē – to glance at me with comforting eyes; em pirānār – one who does favours to me; nam paiyal aḷiyan ennār – he does not say (a word) “our little fellow is worthy of my grace”; amma O kodiyavāṟē – Oh! (his heart) is so hardened

**Перевод:**

**teḷivilāk kalangal – if the flow of water in kāvēri eases a bit, there would be opportunity for it to become clear. But since there is more and more flood, it remains muddled constantly. This āḻvār is also, like kāvēri, always agitated. Before attaining emperumān he will be worried that “I have not attained him”. Once he attains emperumān, he will be agitated because of his love for him. This river too, when it flows from the west towards the temple, will be agitated because of expectation of seeing periya perumāḷ and when it leaves the temple and flows towards the east it will be agitated because it is going away from him. Hence it is clear that without any distinction between cētana (āḻvār) and acētana (river) entities, even those who walk near the temple would feel agitated.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Despite āḻvār calling out \[as seen in the previous pāsuram\] to periya perumāḷ, since periya perumāḷ is not saying “do not worry” with sympathy, āḻvār feels distressed. “Is he keeping quiet because he thinks that someone else will help me, other than himself? Is he thinking that I am after some other goal in samsāram (materialistic realm)? If he says ‘he is my little fellow’, I will be able to escape from all the troubles. His divine heart has become so hardened that he is not saying even this, and he is sleeping” laments the āḻvār. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings. தெளிவிலாக் கலங்கல் நீர் சூழ் திருவரங்கங்கத்துள் ஓங்கும் ஒளியுளார் தாமே அன்றே தந்தையும் தாயும் ஆவார் எளியதோர் அருளும் அன்றே என் திறத்து எம்பிரானார் அளியன் நம் பையல் என்னார் அம்மவோ! கொடியவாறே.*

**nīr sūḻ** – Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says in tiruvāimoḻi 10.10.10 “*adanil periya en avāvarac cūḻndāy*”, periya perumāḷ envelops the [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/) with his love. In the same way, kāvēri also envelops periya perumāḷ because of her love for him. It is pertinent, here, to think of [parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/)’s śrīrangarāja stavam 1.21 “*dugdābdir janakō jananyahamiyam śrīrēva putri vā śrīrangēśvara ētadarhamiha kim kuryāmitvākulā* I *sanjaccāmara candra candana* *mahāmāṇikya muktōtkarān kāvērī lahareekarairvidadatī paryēti sā sēvyatām* īī” (kāvēri wonders “kśīrsāgara is the father; I (his wife) am the mother; this [śrīranganāyaki](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sri-mahalakshmi/) is the daughter; [śrīranganātan](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the son-in-law. What will I carry as wealth befitting them?”. It appears that she then carries bright *cāmaram* (whisk made out of yak tail, used as fan for emperumān), *paccaikkaṟpūram* (medicated camphor used as a flavouring agent in foods), sandalwood trees, great gems, pearls etc in heaps towards you. You \[emperumān\] should have a bath in that kāvēri). In the world, we see people offering gifts based on the status of the bride, bridegroom and the bride’s parents. At śrīrangam, which is the place where śrīranganāyaki wedded śrīranganātan, the bride is the goddess of wealth, *śrīdēvi* herself. The bridegroom is *aḻagiya maṇavāḷan* (the beautiful groom) who is superior to everyone else and the paramātmā. The bride’s father is the repository of all gems, *tiruppāṟkadalarasan* \[khīrsāgar, the king of all oceans\] and the bride’s mother is his consort, the one who is more sacred than even gangā (ganges), kāvēri. When everything about this is so supreme, how to carry gifts to them, wonders kāvēri and carries all the items mentioned earlier, and comes like an elephant in exultation, exclaims bhattar.

**tiruvarangattuḷ ōṇgum oḷiyuḷār** – just as it is difficult to remove the floods in kāvēri, see the river bed and enter it, it is equally difficult to remove the resplendent radiance of [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), enter the space and admire the beauty of his divine form, which is the repository of his radiance. *tiruvarangattil ōngum oḷi* – after he entered tiruvarangam (śrīrangam), even the brightness in paramapadam got reduced, like light during daytime. The resplendent radiance at śrīrangam helps the lowly samsāris like light during night-time. *ōngum oḷi* – emperumān, standing amidst nityasūris, is like a sun amidst other suns. But standing amidst samsāris, who suffer from the sorrows of samsāram, and protecting them, the radiance also acquires dominance.

**oḷiyuḷār tāmē anṟē tandaiyum tāyum āvār** – subālōpaniśat 16 says “*mātā pitā bhrātā nivāsaś śaraṇam suhrut gatir nārāyaṇa:*” (nārāyaṇa is mother, he is father, he is brother, he is the dwelling place, he is the means for attaining mōkśa (liberation), he is friend, he is the one attained in mōkśa). [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that for him it is not the paramapadanātan, who is common to all; it is not vyūha or vibhava form of perumāḷ who is common to a few people only; it is not other arcāvatāra vigrahas (forms) common for other [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/); it is only [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who is his father and mother. This is the meaning for using the term *tāmē* (he himself) to the exclusivity of everyone else. āḻvār wonders whether periya  perumāḷ, who is everything to him, is thinking that āḻvār has someone else (other than himself) as his relative and hence is keeping quiet. It is pertinent here to think of mahābhāratam vana parvam 192.56 ślōkam “*sarvēśāmēva lōkānām pitā mātā ca mādava:* I *gaccadvamēnam śaraṇam śaraṇyam puruśarśabhā:*īī” (Oh great puruśas! Only lakśminātan is the father and mother for all worlds. Surrender to him, who is fit to be surrendered to). The story behind this ślōka is as follows: During the time that darmaputra (yudiśtra) was carrying out vanavāsa (living in forest) with his family, kriṣṇa and his consort satyabhāma came there. On hearing this, parāśara maharishi and other riśis convened an assembly under the leadership of mārkaṇḍēya maharishi. darmaputra asked the assembly “I always go in the path of righteousness, yet I suffer like this. duryōdana always goes in the path of evil, yet he is always happy. Please tell me, what is the reason for this and what is the remedy for this?”. Each riśi gave a remedy for this. Finally, their head, mārkaṇḍēya maharishi considered all their opinions as *pūrvapakśa* (first objection to an assertion in any discussion) and recited this ślōka as his *siddānta* (demonstrated conclusion of an argument). Either for suffering in sadness or enjoying in happiness, it is not the path chosen by darmaputra or duryōdana which is the cause. It is because of this that the remedies offered by other riśis here, for darmaputra to come out of his troubles, will not stand scrutiny. Surrendering to mādava (bhagavān), who is the natural relative to all and who is the natural means for attaining him, is the reason for real happiness. Instead of holding on to him, if someone holds on to other means, it will only lead to vicious circle in samsāram, and thus to sadness, like following the path of evil. The considered opinion here is that only śrīman nārāyaṇa, who is a mother, father, everything, to all, is the refuge. Once when his brother [śrīrāma bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/vedha-vyasa-bhattar/) fell ill, [parāśara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) recited this ślōka and divined its meaning too. *tandaiyum tāyum āvār* – it is only [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who will do nice things like a mother and good things like a father.

**eḷiyadōr aruḷum anṟē en tiṟattu** – unlike the covherds, there is no need to carry out difficult tasks like lifting a mountain, to protect me. Are you thinking that I am asking for something other than yourself and hence you can grant it at an appropriate time? Are you keeping quiet thinking that to protect me, you have to lift a bow as you did in the case of vibhīśaṇa or a whip as you did in the case of pāṇḍavas \[as a charioteer\]? It is sufficient if you glance at me with your comforting eyes.

**em pirānār** – carrying out favours for me. When emperumān is not even glancing at him, how is he saying that he is doing favours for him? [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is not the type to forget the favours already done by emperumān. Keeping those in mind, he is beseeching emperumān to do this favour. āḻvār cannot deny the favours earlier done by emperumān such as reminding him about the indelible relationship that he has with āḻvār, reminding him that he is the means and the goal, and now, when āḻvār is crying out to him, coming near him and listening to him.

What is the favour to be done now?

**aḷiyan nam paiyal ennār** – the word *aḷi* means grace or mercy. *aḷiyan* means one on whom mercy should be shown. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/)’s desire now is that emperumān should say “he has something  good in him to be shown my mercy. He is my little fellow”. Instead of addressing him as “my little fellow!”, āḻvār feels uncomfortable being addressed as “āḻvīr!” by emperumān. When I am his servitor, is it not enough if I think that I am his little fellow? Should I be called in that manner by him? āḻvār thinks that it is not enough. Only if emperumān, who is the master, calls him that way and confirms that he is the master and āḻvār, his servitor, will āḻvār’s servitorśip last. It is pertinent here to think of incidents in [pūrvācāryās](http://acharyas.koyil.org/)‘ lives which have similar connection. In the first, when [maṇakkāl nambi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/manakkal-nambi/) ([āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/)’s ācāryar) was closeted with his disciples while giving a discourse on secretive meanings of śāstram, one of his disciples, tirumagiḻmārban, knocked on the door. maṇakkāl nambi told his disciples “open the door for my son tirumagiḻmārban”. In the second incident, appiḷḷaiyāḻvān quoted his ācāryan piḷḷaippiḷḷaiyāḻvān saying “little brother appiḷḷaiyāḻvān is my little fellow”.

**ammmavō kodiyavāṟē** – when I am crying out to him in sorrow, should he not get up? How can he sleep even after hearing my voice? Has his divine heart become so hardened? How is his nature! exclaims the [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/). The word *amma* indicates deep anguish.

Let us proceed to the 38<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

## 38. Part 2

*mēmporuḷ pōgavittu meymmaiyai miga uṇarndu āmparisaṟindukoṇḍu aimpulanagattadakki kāmbaṟat talai siraittun kadaittalai irundu vāḻum sōmbarai ugatti pōlum sūḻpunal arangattānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**mēmporuḷ – vyākhyātā (commentator) has given three different meanings for this word: (1) superficial object; (2) pervasive object; (3) object creating eminence. In all the three meanings, this word indicates \[physical\] body which in turn is representative for all materialistic objects. Let us consider each of these three meanings. (1) as a superficial object – having an unnatural relationship on account of karma and once knowledge about emperumān is born, body becomes something that need not be bothered about. Thinking that this is sweet, when jīvātmā (cētana or sentient entity) starts enjoying with the help of this body, it would appear superficially that this is indeed sweet; but when looked a bit deeper, since there is nothing sweet about this, we can consider that this body is a superficial object. (2) as a pervasive object – As śrī bhagavat gītā 7.14 says “daivī hyēśā guṇamayī mama māyā duratyaya I māmēva yē prapadyantē māyāmētām tantitē īī” (ordained by me, this prakruti (primordial matter) also called as māyā, is composed of the three qualities – sātvic (pure good), rājasic (passionate) and tāmasic (ignorance). It is very difficult to cross over. Only those who surrender to me cross this prakruti), this body is intertwined with the ātmā (soul), becoming nearly inseparable. This body makes the person believe that “I am dēva” (celestial entity) or “I am manuśya” (human being) since it is so closely intertwined and hence we can say that this body is pervasive. (3) as an object creating eminence – it makes people to think that they are eminent among all the persons, who know everything. Thus the body can have any of the three connotations as we have seen above, and all the other materialistic objects with which it is connected, can be called as mēmporuḷ.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pōgavittu** – to give up with all the traces (give up completely). As long as one is with this śarīram (body) it cannot be given up. Only when a person gets the knowledge that this śarīram and its connected materialistic objects cannot be the *puruśārtam* (benefit) \[on attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] can it be termed as giving it up will all traces. The connection \[between the body and the soul\] was created by emperumān. As we had seen in the gītā ślokam 7.14 \[in the previous paragraph\] this connection cannot be severed by the cētana. śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 7.11 says “*anātmanyātma buddiryā asvē svamiti yā mati*  I *samsāra tarusambhūti bījamētat dvidā stitam*  īī” (the thought that this body, which is not the true self, is the self, and thinking that those objects which are not one’s, are one’s own, are the seed with dicotyledons which enable the tree called samsāram to grow). Thus, ridding of the thought that this *dēham* is [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), that the objects in this samsāram which  are connected to this dēham are one’s own and thinking of ātmā, which is different from dēham, as svatantran (one who is independent) is called as *pōgavittu* (giving up with trace). This is what great jñānis (highly intelligent persons) such as janaka could do. When two trees, one good and the other, bad, are intertwined so closely that it is not possible to separate them, a person, desirous of separating them, will scratch the surface of the bad tree and apply asafoetida (an organic substance used in cooking) on it so that it will wither away. In the same way, when ātmā and the body are so closely intertwined, if someone wants to rid of the body, he has to remove the thoughts of *ahankāram* (thinking of body as oneself) and *svātantriya buddi* (thinking of oneself as being totally independent). The words which follow later in this pāsuram such as “*meymmaiyai miga uṇarndu*” and “*āmparisaṟindu koṇḍu*” are related to jñāna (knowledge); hence we can consider this word *pōgavittu* also to be related to knowledge.

**meymmaiyai miga uṇarndu** – the word “mey” refers to [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/). Can *mey* denote ātmā? taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6 says *satyancā nrutanca satyamabhavat* (the *paravastu* \[greatest entity\] was *satyam* (without any change) even though it exists as *satyam* (cētana tatvam or sentient entity) and *asatyam* (acētana tatvam or insentient entity). Since upaniśat calls the constantly changing entity of acētana as *anrutam* (false) and the one without any change to its svarūpa, the cētana entity, as *satyam* (*mey* or true), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) also calls ātmā as *mey*. The word *satyam* denotes the permanent nature of ātmā, unlike advaitis (followers of a philosophy which says that there is no separate [cit](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/), [acit](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2013/03/thathva-thrayam-achith-what-is-matter.html) and [īśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/)) who define it as different from *asatya*, without any distinction. āḻvār calls this permanent nature as *meymmai* here.

**miga uṇarndu** – \[vyākkhyātā periyavāccān piḷḷai brings in here, a few philosophies and contrasts them with the philosophy of viśiśtādvaitam\]. Unlike lōkāyatan (also called as cārvākan) who says, “only body is ātmā”; unlike pūrvamīmāmsakan who says “ātmā, who is different from body and who is permanent, is independent”; unlike vaiśēśikan who says “the knowledge of ātmā is acquired \[it is not natural\]; when he remains like a stone, he attains mōkśa (liberation)”; unlike all these philosophies, knowing ātmā as per its true nature – i.e. ātmā is self-radiant, permanent, having knowledge forever, as a quality, having a svarūpam which is like an atom \[śastra defines the size of ātmā: take a grain of paddy, take out the sharp end, divide it into a hundred parts, take one of these parts, divide this into a hundred pieces, the size of the ātmā is less than the size of this finally divided piece\], always belonging to īśvaran, being subservient to his followers also – this is knowing ātmā completely.

**āmparisaṟindu koṇḍu** – After realising that dēham, which is the result of prakruti (primordial matter), and those which are connected to dēham \[relatives, possessions etc\] are fit to be given up, and after knowing ātmā’s true nature \[as explained in the previous paragraph\], the benefit that results is *āmparisu*  which is bhagavat kainkaryam (carrying out service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)). āmparisu (benefit) is the cause for growth. The cause for growth will vary, depending on what the ātmā thinks of itself. If the ātmā is inside the body of animals like cow, a bundle of grass is the cause for its growth. When the ātmā resides in the body of man, food is the cause for its growth. When the ātmā is inside the body of a celestial entity, *amrutam* (nectar) is the cause for its growth. In whichever body the ātmā resides, if it feels that it is an *adiyēn* (servitor), then kainkaryam (carrying out service) is the reason for its growth. Since the person’s intellect gets blunted due to the body and its connected entities \[in this samsāram\], this subject matter is not well known in this world. On reaching paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam), gaining the inner knowledge and realising the true nature, ātmā’s natural servitorśip will be its benefit and cause for its growth.

**aṟindu koṇḍu** – just as one says “carrying wealth”, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) explains its pre-eminence. The wealth for every cētana is the knowledge that dēham and its complements are to be given up, that ātmā is always dependent on emperumān, that servitorśip is its puruśārtam. If this is present, there will be no need for any other means. It is [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s efforts to rid the cētana of his bondage to samsāram and of puṇya and pāpa (virtue and vice) and to make him hold on to himself \[emperumān\] as the means. It is pertinent to remember the following incident: A person asked [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/) ([bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/)’s disciple) as to which is the means – renouncing \[worldly desires\] or holding on \[to emperumān\]? nanjīyar responded “neither; the one who makes him renounce and hold on to \[emperumān himself\] is the means”. While *mēmporuḷ* , *meymmai* and *āmparisu* may not directly mean  dēham \[body\], ātma svarūpam \[true nature of ātmā\] and kainkaryam \[service\] respectively,  since there is a connection for each of these words to the respective meaning, āḻvār brings forth these meanings indirectly, due to the greatness of the meanings.

**aimpulan agattadakki** – keeping the sensory organs under one’s control. At the beginning of this pāsuram, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) had said *mēmporuḷ pōgavittu*  in which he said that one should keep under one’s control everything connected to dēham (body) and its complements. This being the case, would not saying *aimpulan agattadakki* now, be a repetition of the earlier one? In the previous word *āmparisu aṟindu koṇḍu*, when carrying out kainkaryam \[to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\], a happiness could be felt by the jīvātmā itself \[that it is carrying out the kainkaryam\]. Renouncing this happiness for self is the meaning conveyed by the words *aimpulan agattadakki*. Hence there is no repetition. Even while carrying kainkaryam, one thought should be removed – i.e. kainkaryam carried out for one’s happiness or kainkaryam carried out for emperumān’s and one’s happiness will not be termed as puruśārtam (goal), but the kainkaryam carried out exclusively for emperumān’s happiness is puruśārtam. āḻvār conveys this meaning here. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), in his [tiruvāimoḻi 2.9.4](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/06/thiruvaimozhi-2-9-4-enakke-atchey/) “*tanakkēyāga enaikkoḷḷumīdē”* (consider me only for your kainkaryam) and [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/) in her [tiruppāvai 29](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-29-chirram-chirukale/) “*unakkē nāmātceyvōm*” (we will be subservient only to you) conveyed the same meaning. In dvayam, the meaning of the word *nama:* in the end is also the same as in this. In śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāndam 1.31 it says “*ramyamāvasatam krutvā ramamāṇā vanē traya:* I *dēvagandarvasankāśās tatra tē nyavasan sukham* īī” (the three of them (rāma, lakśmaṇa and sītā) who were like dēvagandarva (celestial entities) and who were very happy in the forest, built a beautiful cottage and lived happily in it). While the ślōkam says that perumāḷ (śrī rāma), pirātti (sītā) and iḷaiya perumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) were happy, from the context it will be clear that lakśmaṇa’s happiness is derived from carrying out kainkaryam to the duo of perumāḷ and pirātti.

Thus, in these two lines \[of this pāsuram\], the meaning of prāpyam (the benefit of attaining [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)) has been mentioned. Now we shall see how the meaning of tirumantram \[the first of the [three rahasyams](http://ponnadi.blogspot.in/2015/12/rahasya-thrayam.html) in our sampradāyam\] is being mentioned. In the terms *memporuḷ pōgavittu,* the meaning of *Om nama:* and in the terms *aimpulan agattadakki* the meaning of *namō nārāyanāya* have been mentioned. In the words *meymmaiyai miga uṇarndu* which is the śēśatvam of ātmā (being subservient to emperumān) the meaning of the word nāra in the word nārāyaṇa has been mentioned. Since that śēśatvam succeeds only because of emperumān, his śēśitvam (being the master for all) is also mentioned in this meaning. Thus the meaning of the word *nārāyaṇa* has also been mentioned. In the term *āmparisaṟindu koṇḍu,* the meaning of the fourth case *āya* \[in *nārāyaṇāya*\], which is kainkaryam, has also been mentioned. Since the term *uṇarndu* refers to the jīvātmā who knows \[has knowledge\], the meaning of *praṇavam* (ōmkāram) which includes jīvātmā, has also been mentioned. Thus these two lines establish the meaning of tirumantram.

Now, over the next two lines of this pāsuram, the upāyatvam (being the *means* of attaining emperumān) as mentioned in the first line of dvayam and the first part of carama ślōkam are being mentioned. We shall see this, along with the meanings of the remaining two lines, in the next part.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 38. Part 1

*mēmporuḷ pōgavittu meymmaiyai miga uṇarndu āmparisaṟindu koṇḍu aimpulanagattadakki kāmbaṟat talai siraittun kadaittalai irundu vāḻum sōmbarai ugatti pōlum sūḻ punal arangattānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

punal sūḻ – surrounded by kāvēri; arangattānē – one who is sleeping in the temple; mēm poruḷ – the worldly matters which give an impression of being great; pōgavittu – casting aside, with trace; meymmaiyai – the ātma svarūpam (true nature of ātmā, the soul); miga uṇarndu – knowing, as it is \[completely\]; Am parisu – bhagavat kainkaryam (service to emperumān) which is the puruśārtam (benefit) for ātmā’s true nature; aṟindu koṇḍu – knowing it; aim pulan – the five senses; agattu adakki – controlling (instead of enjoying them); kāmbu aṟa – removing the attachment (in other means); talai siraittu – removing the weight from head; un talaikkadai irundu – standing at your door step (as a guard); vāḻum – those who live; sōmbarai – followers who are lazy (in looking after themselves); ugatti pōlum – do you not enjoy them?; pramēyam (goal) – https://koyil.org; pramāṇam (scriptures) – http://grantams.koyil.org; pramātā (preceptors) – http://acaryas.koyil.org; śrīvaiṣṇava education/kids portal – http://pillai.koyil.org

**Перевод:**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Just as gītā carama ślōkam  (the ultimate ślōka of śrī bhagavat gītā) is the centrepiece of mahābhāratam, this pāsuram is the centrepiece of this divya prabhandam (divine composition of toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār). mahābhāratam is  the essence of vēdas as could be gauged from the ślōka “matimandānamāvidya yēnāsau śrutisāgarāt I jagadditāya janitō mahābhārata candramā: īī” (the moon called as mahābhārata was created by churning the seas of vēdas by vyāsa for the well being of this world). emperumān churned tiruppāṟkadal (the ocean of milk on which he lies) and gave nectar to dēvas (celestial people). The sage vyāsa churned vēdas and gave mahābhārata, which brings forth happiness among those who read it, for the well-being of samsāram. The nectar which emperumān gave to the dēvas was not necessarily for their well-being since it would not even allow them to die when they are in serious trouble; it is more of a millstone to them. On the other hand, the mahābhārata which vyāsa gave the samsāris is capable of ridding them of samsāram. Such mahābhārata can be termed as cakkai (residue such as from sugarcane after the juice has been extracted) when compared to śrī bhagavat gītā. The essence of śrī bhagavat gītā is its carama ślōkam. gītācāryan (kriṣṇa), in order to highlight the lowliness of other upāyams (means for attaining emperumān such as karma yōga, jñāna yōga etc), explained these before reciting carama ślōkam. After reciting carama ślōkam, he recited the following ślōkam highlighting the greatness of carama ślōkam “idam tē nātapaskāya nābhaktāya kadācana I nācāśuśrūśavē vācyam na ca mām yō’bhyasūyati īī” (this śāstram which was recited (by me) to you should not ever be told to one who has not done any penance, to one who does not have devotion (towards you and me), to one who is not interested in it and to one who hates me). In the same way, this entire tirumālai was created only for reciting this 38th pāsuram, mēmporuḷ pōgavittu. The pāsurams coming after this highlight the greatness of those who are deeply involved in the meanings of this 38th pāsuram. Thus it will be clear that just as carama ślōkam is the essence of mahābhārata, this pāsuram is the essence of tirumālai. Apart from giving the meanings of carama ślōkam, this pāsuram gives the meanings of dvayam also \[dvayam is one among the three secretive exponents of our philosophy, the other two being tirumantram and carama ślōkam\]. \[dvayam is composed of two lines, of which the first line expounds on the upāyam (means or path) for attaining emperumān and the second, on the puruśārtam (benefit or goal on attaining him)\]. In this pāsuram, the benefit is mentioned in the first two lines and the means, in the next two lines. What is the reason for this reversal (from the order in which they appear in dvayam)? For any advantage or benefit, there are two different periods – one is vyutpatti vēḷai (time of origin) and the other is anuśtāna vēḷai (time of execution). The time of origin is when one knows about the advantage and the time of execution is when he engages through some means in attaining that advantage. For example, there is a śāstra vākhyam (a sentence from sacred text) svargakāmō jyōtiśtōmēna yajēta (one who is desirous of svargam should carry out jyōtiśtōma yajñam). Here śāstram tells a cētana (sentient entity) about the advantage, svargam. This is in the time of origin. This is why it first informs about the advantage, svargakāma, and later, it tells about the means of attaining that advantage, which is doing the jyōtiśtōma yajñam.  However, at the time of execution, one has to first carry out jyōtiśtōma yajñam (means) and only then attain svargam (goal). It is clear from this that the means and goal will be reversed during time of origin and time of execution. In this pāsuram, āḻvār is talking about goal and means. Hence it is structured on vyutpatti vēḷai (time of origin). Thus, āḻvār is talking about the goal first and then the means. In order to reach the goal, the observance of means, which is reciting the dvaya mantram, is in the execution time. In dvayam, the sādanam (means) is given first and the sādyam (the goal) is given next. nammāḻvār in his tiruvāimoḻi 6.10.10 “agalagillēn”  gave the meaning of the first part of dvayam. āṇḍāḷ, in her tiruppāvai 29th pāsuram “siṟṟam siṟukālē” gave the meaning of 2nd part of dvayam. This (toṇḍaradippodi) āḻvār alone has given the meanings of both halves of dvayam in one pāsuram. What is the link for this pāsuram with the previous (37th) pāsuram? After āḻvār had recited with lot of sorrow, the words “aḷiyan nam paiyal ennār ammavō kodiyavāṟē”, periya perumāḷ felt very happy in his divine mind that “despite staying in samsāram which incites involvement in worldly pursuits, he is praying for kainkaryam (service) to me as his puruśārtam (benefit), in line with his svarūpam (true nature); for attaining that, instead of resorting to his own efforts, he is looking up to me; on top of that, he is feeling saddened that there is a delay in getting that puruśārtam. What a great mahātmā he is!  How great that I have got him!” The happiness in his divine mind manifested into happiness in his divine face, blessing āḻvār. After seeing this happiness, āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Oh one who has done me favours! Is this how your divine mind feels happy when someone attains you, gives the responsibility of protecting him to you and withdraws from protecting himself?” carama ślōkam ordains “hold on to me as means, with deep belief”, dvayam is the mantram that those who are steadfast in this means, will recite. This pāsuram shows how happy emperumān becomes with those who are fully involved in dvayam and its meanings. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings. மேம் பொருள் போகவிட்டு மெய்ம்மையை மிக உணர்ந்து ஆம்பரிசறிந்து கொண்டு ஐம்புலனகத்தடக்கி காம்பறத் தலை சிரைத்துன் கடைத்தலை இருந்து வாழும் சோம்பரை உகத்தி போலும் சூழ் புனல் அரங்கத்தானே.*

## 39. Adimaiyil kudimaiyilla

*adimaiyil kudimaiyillā ayal saduppēdi māril kudimaiyil kadaimai patta kukkaril piṟapparēlum mudiyinil tuḷabam vaittāy moykaḻaṟku anbu seyyum adiyarai ugatti pōlum aranga mānagar uḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mudiyinil – on the divine head/crown; tuḷabam vaittāy – one who has adorned the tuḷasi garland (as a subtle mark of being the lord of all); arangam mānagar uḷānē – dwelling inside the temple at śrīrangam; adimaiyil – carrying out service (to you); kudimai illā – even if they are not involved; ayal – being different (from being servitor); saduppēdimāril – from the vaidikas (those who follow vēdams) who recite the four vēdams; kudimaiyil kadaimai patta – being lowly in terms of their birth; kukkaril – below the level of caṇḍāḷas (of a very low birth); piṟappar ēlum – even if they are born; moy kaḻaṟku – your close, divine feet; anbu seyyum – being affectionate; adiyarai – followers; ugatti pōlum – don’t you enjoy!?

**Перевод:**

**adimaiyil kudimaiyillā – even if they do not have the thought that carrying out kainkaryam is apt for them. It is apt to think that “kainkaryam is natural to us. Just like the food that we eat and the water that we drink, we cannot live if we do not carry out kainkaryam”.  āḻvār is referring to those who do not think this way.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*The greatness of those who have jñānam (knowledge) of the meanings mentioned in the previous pāsuram is described, starting with this pāsuram, until the 44th pāsuram. nammāḻvār spoke about the greatness of emperumān’s followers (servitors) in tiruvāimoḻi 3.7 “payilum sudaroḷi mūrtti” and in 8.10 “nedumāṟkadimai”. This (toṇḍaradippodi) āḻvār does the same, in these 6 pāsurams. āḻvār surmises that even if the followers of emperumān are wanting in birth \[not belonging to the first three varṇams\], practices \[result of the above\] and qualities, if they have knowledge of the meanings mentioned in the pāsuram “mēmporuḷ ” they are fit to be our prāpakam and prāpyam (our means and our goal). In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that even if the followers are born in a very low clan, if they have deep knowledge in the meanings of “mēmporuḷ” pāsuram, they will be adorned on his divine head by emperumān, like tiruttuḻāy (tuḷasi). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings. அடிமையில் குடிமையில்லா அயல் சதுப்பேதி மாரில் குடிமையில் கடைமை பட்ட குக்கரில் பிறப்பரேலும் முடியினில் துளபம் வைத்தாய் மொய்கழற்கு அன்பு செய்யும் அடியரை யுகத்தி போலும் அரங்கமா நகருளானே.*

**ayal saduppēdimāril** – being different from the caturvēdis who are not immersed in kainkaryam. *saduppēdimāril* – those who learn the four vēdas. The benefit of learning vēdas is to realise that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the master of all the worlds,  that we are his possessions, that to carry out kainkaryam to emperumān and his followers is the appropriate goal for us. *ayal* is not knowing that the purpose of learning vēdas is to obtain this knowledge. If the caturvēdis do not have the knowledge that the goal of learning the four vēdas is to know these (above mentioned) meanings, then they are of no use to anyone. *pādmōttaram* says “*viṣṇubhaktivihīnō yas sarvaśāstrārta vēdyapi* I *brāhmaṇyam tasya na bhavēt tasyōtpattir nirūpyatām* īī” (Even if a person knows the meanings of all śāstras (sacred texts), he will not have the qualities of a brāhmaṇa if he does not have devotion towards viṣṇu. His birth is doubtful). brahmāṇḍa purāṇam vihakōsam 24.11 says “*ya: putra: pitaram dvēśti tam vidyādanyarētsam* I *yō viṣṇum satatam dvēśtitam vidyādanyarētasam* īī” (if a son hates his father, it can be surmised that he was born to another person. If a person always hates viṣṇu, it can be surmised that he was born to a candāḷa). Thus śāstras thundered that such people do not have *brāhmaṇyam* (qualities of a brāhmaṇa).

Through the remaining part of the pāsuram, it says about those who are different from these people and who are dear to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/).

**kudimaiyil kadaimaippatta kukkaril piṟapparēlum** – those who spend several births, not carrying out deeds which are ordained in vēdas and carrying out deeds which are forbidden by vēdas, are born in very low births because of the accumulated sins in all these births. *kukkar* (kuśka) refers to a class lower than caṇḍāḷas. Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is using the term *piṟapparēlum* (even if they are born), he indicates that the superiority or inferiority of a person’s birth is not a reason for having knowledge about the meanings mentioned in the *mēmporuḷ* (p*revious)* pāsuram. In śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 68.24, śrī rāma tells lakśmaṇa “*sarvatra kalu druśyantē sādavō darmacāriṇa:* I *śūrā: śaraṇyās saumitrē tiryak yōni gatēśvapi* īī” (hē lakśmaṇa! Those who are like sages, going in the path of righteousness, dēvas et al, fit to be surrendered to, are seen among those born as animals and other species too!). śrī rāma says this when he sees jatāyu (vulture who fought valiantly with rāvaṇa when he flew after abducting sītāppirātti). The opinion here is that knowledge of meanings of the pāsuram *mēmporuḷ* is prevalent across species, even among animals and birds. In śrī rāmāyaṇam, which came about to explain the meanings of vēdas, people who roam around in forest such as guhan (a hunter), śabari (again from the hunters’ tribe), primates such as hanuman, sugrīva, rākśasa such as vibhīśaṇa had this knowledge. In the other epic which came about to describe vēdas, mahābhārata, vidura, born in a lowly caste, darmavyāda, a butcher, ghantākarṇa, the cow herd boys and girls – all had this knowledge. In tiruviruttam 79, [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) says “*pāmbaṇai mēl paḷḷi koṇḍaruḷum sīdanaiyē toḻuvār viṇṇuḷarilum sīriyarē*” (those who worship only the lord who is lying on the serpent bed, are greater than nityasūris). Even though nityasūris are constantly involved with [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), those born in this samsāram (which only breeds ignorance) and reformed due to the efforts of emperumān, are considered to be greater than nityasūris. Just as *syamantakamani* (a stone which will convert even iron into gold when it touches iron) is better than pure gold, since these dwellers of samsāram are capable of converting other samsāris into followers of emperumān due to their connection, which even nityasūris cannot do, such samsāris are considered to be greater than nityasūris.

**mudiyinil tuḷabam vaittāy** – just as emperumān adorns tuḷasi on his head, these followers are also apt to be kept on his head like tuḷasi. Just as he adorns tuḷasi to indicate that he is [sarvēśvaran](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) (lord of all), if he keeps his followers on his head, that would also indicate his quality of being sarvēśvaran.

**moykaḻaṟku anbu seyyum** – Just as in the 38<sup>th</sup> pāsuram [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) had said “*aimpulan agattadakki*” (controlling their sensory organs) to serve emperumān, these people cast aside their happiness \[while carrying out kainkaryam\] and serve only for his happiness, with affection, similar to *sēvadi sevvi tirukkāppu* ([tiruppallāṇḍu 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruppallandu-1-pallandu/)). To them this is the only way to spend time purposefully  and they consider this as their goal. Thus they have emperumān, who is always delightful, as their means.   Next, the means (path) is mentioned.

**adiyarai** – they consider his compassionate divine feet as the means. As mentioned in pāsuram 38 as *sōmbar*, they know that their ātma svarūpam (nature of the [ātmā](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/)) is being subservient to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) for ever and will not have anything to do with their protection themselves. Another interpretation is – *adiyar* – remaining at the feet. The feet are the goal, which are enjoyable, and the feet are also the means, which sustain. This is similar to [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) mentioning in periya tiruvandādi 31 *niḻalum aditāṟum ānōm* (we became the shadow and the lines, of the feet).

**ugatti pōlum** – you enjoy these followers, who have the knowledge, not considering their lowly birth, and not those caturvēdis who do not have the knowledge.

**aranga mānagar uḷānē** – the purpose of coming to śrīrangam and lying here is to hear these people, who have affection towards you, even if they are of lowly birth. If you wanted to hear only great people, you would have remained at śrīvaikuṇṭam, is the meaning here.

We shall move on to the 40<sup>th</sup> pāsuram

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 40. Tirumaru marva

*tiru maṟu mārva! ninnaic cindiiyuḷ tigaḻa vaittu maruviya manattarāgil mānilattu uyirgaḷellām veruvuṟakk konṟu suttittu īttiya vinaiyarēlum aruvinaip payanadu uyyār aranga mānagaruḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiru – periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi); maṟu – mole called as śrīvatsam; mārva – one who is adorning these two (tiru and maṟu) on your chest!; aranga mānagar uḷānē – one who is dwelling in tiruvarangam temple (śrīrangam); mānilattu – of this world; uyirgaḷ ellām – all the creatures; veruvu uṟa – to be frightened; konṟu – killing (the creatures with weapons); suttittu – burning (by fire); īttiya vinaiyar ēlum – even if they have committed such sins; ninnai – you; sindaiyuḷ – in (their) hearts; tigaḻa vaittu – to hold (as upāyam, means); maruviya manattar āgil – if they have full faith (that you are the goal or benefit); aruvinai – deadly sins; payan adu – its result; uyyār – will not enjoy

**Перевод:**

**tirumaṟu mārva – the one who has tiru (śrī mahālakśmi) and maṟu (mole, called as śrīvatsa) on his chest! These two will signify that he is the sarvēśvaran (lord of all). In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 114.15, “śrīvatsavakśā nityaśrī: ajayya: śāśvatō druva:” ((śrī rāma, who has) mole called śrīvatsa on his divine right chest, inseparable periya pirātti forever on his divine chest, one who cannot be won over, one who lives forever and one who has unchanging form,  nārāyaṇan), maṇḍodari describes śrī rāma through the above verse and confirms that these two identities (śri and śrīvatsa) are unique to him. āḻvār, towards the end of this pāsuram says “they will not experience the result of their deadly sins” by referring to the pirātti’s quality of vātsalyam (motherly love) and says here that such pirātti is always present in his divine chest. Other āḻvārs also refer to her presence in his divine chest, such as nammāḻvār’s tiruvāimoḻi 6.10.10 “alarmēl mangai uṟai mārbā” (one who has the divine chest where alarmēl mangai (consort of śrīnivāsa) dwells constantly) and periyāḻvār’s tiruppallāṇḍu 2 “nin valamārvinil vāḻginṟa mangaiyum pallāṇḍu” (mangaḷāśāsanam to pirātti who dwells in your right chest). While āḻvār had hidden the presence of pirātti in mēmporuḷ pāsuram \[hinted her presence through the word un as we had seen in the vyākhyāyam of that pāsuram\], he announces her presence in the beginning itself,in this pāsuram, which speaks about the greatness of those who are fully immersed in that pāsuram \[38th\]. When emperumān grants the means (upāyam) to his āśritars (followers), it is pirātti who functions in the recommendatory role (puruśakāra bhūtai). When he performs the role of master (taking the follower’s service) (upēyam), she joins him in accepting the kainkaryam. Thus her connection is very much required, both in the upāya and in the upēya stitis (states).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about the lowliness due to birth. Since it was not due to them \[the birth was a result of one’s own past karma, yet, after being born, one cannot do anything about it\], this could be excused. In this pāsuram, he says that if they carry out wrong deeds which would not be pardoned, they will not have to endure any punishment because of those wrong deeds, provided they have knowledge of mēmporuḷ pāsuram. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings. திரு மறு மார்வா! நின்னைச் சிந்தையுள் திகழ வைத்து மருவிய மனத்தராகில் மாநிலத்து உயிர்களெல்லாம் வெருவரக் கொன்று சுட்டிட்டு  இட்டிய வினையரேலும் அருவினைப் பயனது உய்யார் அரங்கமா நகருளானே.*

**ninnaic cindaiyuḷ tigaḻa vaittu** – keeping you in their hearts \[mind\] as the only *upāyam* (means) without connection with any other *upāyams* \[such as karma yōga etc\]. During *gāyatri hōmam* (a ritual in which gāyatri mantram is recited) the person will recite gāyatri mantram on the one hand and will feed the *agni* with *samit* (stick), thus carrying out two jobs at a time. If a follower does anything himself to attain emperumān as upāyam, then emperumān’s nature as being the upāyam will dullen. Thus, āḻvār says that apart from (1) pirātti who recommends to emperumān to protect the follower, (2) emperumān himself, who is the protector, (3) the *prapannan* (one who surrenders to emperumān)  and (4) ācāryan, who shows that it is emperumān who is *upāyam and upēyam,* nothing else is required. By saying *vaittu* we can also consider that it shows the *āśrita pāratantriyam* (total dependence of followers) of the emperumān and pirātti since they enter the follower’s mind, taking the follower’s thought of keeping them in his mind as their hold.

**maruviya manattarāgil** – this sequence emphasises the deep faith of the follower in the *upēyam* (goal). The meaning here is – if the follower renounces all the other goals and holds on only to śrīman nārāyaṇan. By using the term *āgil* āḻvār indicates that it is rare for any cētana (sentient entity) to keep firm faith in the upāyam as seen in the sequence *sindaiyuḷ tigaḻa vaittu* and firm faith in the upēyam as seen in this sequence *maruviya manattar.*

**mānilattu uyirgaḷ ellām veruvuṟak konṟu** – Since it is almost impossible to kill all the *jīvarāśis* (creatures), we should not combine the words *mānilattu uyirgaḷellām* with *konṟu* (killing all the creatures in the world); instead, we should combine *mānilattu uyirgaḷellām* with *veruvuṟa* (to be frightened). Thus if even one person is killed, all the creatures get frightened because of the dastardly act. It shows the killer’s proclivity for harming creatures.

**suttittu** – instead of killing them instantly with weapons, torturing them by setting them afire and killing them.

**īttiya vinaiyarēlum** – just as people work hard to obtain an advantage, this person is also working hard to earn sins. *ēlum* – since he couldn’t have done such harmful deeds after obtaining knowledge in *mēmporuḷ* pāsuram, this talks about his behaviour before obtaining that knowledge. laghvatri smruti says “*janmāntara sahasrēśu tapōjñānasamādibi:* I *narāṇām kśīṇapāpānām kriśnē bhakti prajāyatē* īī” (after observing karma jñāna yōgams in tens of thousands of births, for those who get rid of sins (which are the hurdles for the commencement of bhaktiyōgam) , bhakti towards kriṣṇa begins). [śrī bhagavat gītā](http://githa.koyil.org/) 7.19 says “*bahūnām janmanāmantē jñānavān mām prapadyate* I *vāsudēvas sarvamiti sa mahātmā sudurlabha:*  īī (after several virtuous births, *jñāni* (knowledgeable person) worships me, saying that “vāsudēvan is everything (to me)”. Such a mahāpuruśa (great person) is very rare to see). Again, śrī bhagavat gītā 6.54 says “*prayatnāt yatamānastu yōgī samśudda kilpiśa:* I *anēka janma samsiddas tatō yāti parām gatim* īī” (yōgi \[sage like person who meditates deeply\] attains high levels after gaining puṇya (good deeds) in many births, consequently ridding himself of pāpa (sins) and engaging in yōga with his efforts). From all these pramāṇams (authentic sources) it is clear that such a knowledge and bhakti (devotion) will come to a person only after many virtuous births. Thus, such bad behaviour \[as we had seen a little while ago\] will not come to a person even in previous births. Hence by using the term *ēlum* āḻvār says that even if such a rare event occurs . .

**aruvinaip payanaduyyār** – they will not experience the harmful result of such sinful deeds that they had earned. When śrī rāma aimed the arrow at samudra rājan (king of ocean), the samudra rājan fell at śrī rāma’s feet. Immediately śrī rāma shot the arrow against his enemies in \[very far away\] deserts. The opinion here is that emperumān will make the enemies of such persons (\[hat we had seen earlier, indulging in sinful deeds like killing, burning etc\] to suffer the results of their deeds. śātyāya naśākhai says “*tasya putrā: dāyamupayanti: dviśanta: pāpakrutyām suhrudas sādukrutyām* īī” ((when the mumukśu (one interested in mōkśam) goes to paramapadam, his sons will inherit his wealth; his enemies inherit his sins and his friends, his virtues). Thus, this has been mentioned in vēda too. Even if it does not happen that way \[not experiencing result of sinful deeds\], emperumān will save them, just as he saved arjuna from the nāgāśtra of karṇa by making arjuna’s crown as the target. He may even experience the results of such deeds himself to save his followers, just as he took the power of bhagadatta’s weapon on himself and saved arjuna. Since the punishment for their sinful deeds is emperumān’s anger, if emperumān says that he has “tolerated” their deeds, will they \[the harmful deeds\] not disappear? Hence āḻvār says that they will not experience the harmful results of such deeds.

**arangamānagar uḷānē** – the purpose of your coming to tirvarangam (śrīrangam) and lying down here is only to show that you do not do all these things only during the time of *avatāram* (incarnation) but also to help those who come after the period of incarnation. emperumān may desire that he gets the name of having protected such people \[who commit ghastly crimes as mentioned in the earlier part\], but in reality, those who have knowledge of *mēmporuḷ*” will not indulge in such acts. It will be clear, without there being a need to mention it, that these people who get scared that even an act of puṇya (virtue) carried out with the thinking of *upāyam* (means) by oversight, will be counted as pāpa (sin), will \[obviously\] not commit sins. In śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 116.44, pirātti tells hanumān “*pāpānām vā śubhānām vā vadārhāṇām plavangama* I *kāryam karuṇamāryēna na kaścinnāparādyati* īī” (hē monkey! Whether a person has committed sin or a virtuous deed, even if he is to be killed, he should be shown mercy by a person with great qualities. There is no one who has not committed an offence). Sitting on his chest, even if such pirātti who plays recommendatory role to emperumān on behalf of followers, finds faults with his followers, emperumān will say “my follower will not do that. Even if he had done that, it must be an act of good” (periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4.9.2). If we quote this to a person who has only superficial knowledge of emperumān, he may say “in that case they have to go on indulging in sins”. But such thoughts are incongruous, says the vyākhyātā (commentator).

We shall now see pāsuram 41.
