## 41. Vanular ariyalaga

*vānuḷār aṟiyalāgā vānavā enbarāgil tēnulām tuḷaba mālaic cenniyā enbarāgil ūnamāiyanagaḷ  seyyum ūnakārakargaḷēlum pōnagam seyda sēdam taruvarēl punitam anṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ūnam āyinagaḷ seyyum – if they carry out lowly activities \[or\]; ūna kārakargaḷēm – if they carry out lowly activities through others; vān uḷār aṟiyalāga vānavā – Oh one who lives in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) and who cannot be known even by the dwellers of upper worlds such as brahmā et al !; enbar āgil – if they say so; tēn ulām tuḷaba mālai senniyā – Oh one who adorns on his divine crown, the garland of tuḷasi, from which honey is dripping !; enbar āgil – if they say so; pōnagam seyda sēdam – the remnants of bhagavat prasādam (food that had been served to emperumān) eaten (by them); taruvar ēl – if they give (with compassion); anṟē – immediately; punitam – (that ) will be very purifying

**Перевод:**

**vānuḷār aṟiyalāgā vānavā – One who lives forever in paramapadam \[śrīvaikuṇṭam\], not known even to brahmā and other celestial entities. Those sitting on the loft (which is at a higher elevation than the floor) cannot know about starts because they are at a higher level. In the same way, even if they are living in satyalōka (brahmā’s dwelling place) and other such places, these celestial entities cannot know about paramapadanātan. Even though they have some greatness because of  higher level of jñāna (knowledge) and śakti (power), they cannot reach paramapadam with that. The word āgā means “not possible” here. Alternatively, āgā can also mean “should not”. Just as a caṇḍāḷa (person born in a low caste) should not enter a place where rituals are conducted, these celestial entities should not enter paramapadam. But we have seen that jñānis such as āḻvārs know about paramapadanātan. This is not because of their efforts, but because of his \[emperumān’s\] mercy. As said in śrī bhagavat gītā 16.14, those who think that they are “God” cannot see or know about him. Just as people cross oceans with the help of ship, āḻvārs cross the ocean of samsāram with the help of ship called viṣṇu. But these celestial entities are like people who think that they could cross the ocean with their own efforts. Without any distinction between one who knows swimming and one who does not know, it is not possible for a person to cross the ocean by swimming. In the same way, one cannot know about emperumān on his own efforts by anyone irrespective of the fact whether he has or does not have the strength \[to know\]. As per the prapatti lakśaṇa vākhyam of bharata muni “ananyasādyē svābhīśtē mahāviśvāsa pūrvakam I tadē gōpāyatā yāncā prapatti: śaraṇāgati: īī” (when it is not possible through any other means as per one’s desire, beseeching him to be the means through deep faith is prapatti. That itself is śaraṇāgati), the āḻvārs qualified as prapannars (those who surrendered) by taking refuge under him. That he is the only upāyam (means) will be clear when one looks at his greatness of being without any connection with samsāram and having aprākruta vigraham (his divine physical form is composed of sudda satvam, purely good). When one looks at the lowliness of cētana (sentient entity) controlled by the three qualities of satva, rajas and tamas (purely good, passion and ignorance), emperumān’s upāyatvam (being the means) will be evident. When one dvelves into the cētana’s atyanta paratantra svarūpam (having the basic nature of being completely dependent on emperumān), emperumān’s upāytvam will be evident.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous two pāsurams, āḻvār said that there is no shortcoming for these people \[those who have knowledge about the meanings of mēmporuḷ pāsuram\] either because of their birth or because of their behaviour. In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that “it is not only that they do not have any shortcoming. They have the greatness that other samsāris, by having a connection with them, will become purified because of that connection itself”. Let us enjoy the pāsuram and its meanings. வானுளார் அறியலாகா வானவா என்பராகில் தேனுலாம் துளப மாலைச் சென்னியாய் என்பராகில் ஊனமாயினகள் செய்யும் ஊனகாரகர்களேலும் போனகம் செய்த சேடம் தருவரேல் புனிதம் அன்றே.*

**enbarāgil** – just as taittirīya samhitai 7.5.3 says “*mana: pūrvō vāguttara:*” the faculty of  speech says what the faculty of  thought thinks first. In the same way *vānuḷār aṟiayalāgā vānavā* comes out of a person who has taken refuge under emperumān through his mind. Just as *ahirbudya samhitai* 37.30 states, it is evident that these people pray that emperumān should be the upāyam for them. *āgil* – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) indicates that it is very difficult for people to renounce their ability to protect themselves. Thus, it has been shown that these people are those who have taken emperumān as their upāyam.

Next it shows that they have taken [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) himself as upēyam (goal or benefit) too.

**tēnulām tuḷabamālaic cenniyā** – Oh, the one having divine crown decorated with tuḷasi garland, dripping with honey! Since he is adorning with tuḷasi garland, considered to be best among  flowers, it is indicated that he is the greatest among dēvas. Since he is referred to as having *tēnulām tuḷaba mālai*, it indicates that he is very sweet and enjoyable. With these two, it is indicated that he is the one to be attained above all.

**enbarāgil** – just as the word *enbarāgil* \[in the earlier part\] indicates the prārtanā (prayer) for emperumān being the *means*, this word indicates the prayer for attaining him. *āgil* – it is very rare to leave aside all the other goals and consider emperumān as the superior goal. kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) himself had indicated this in his śrī gītā ślōka “*sa mahātmā su durlabha:*”

**ūnamāyinagaḷ seyyum** – people who will torture other creatures for feeding their own stomachs. They are such diśonourable people that they would tie animals such as live goats and cows so that they can give the flesh after cutting them to pieces to those who desire to have these, even in the dead of night. Compassionate persons such as sibi cakravarty will give their flesh for saving other creatures. These people are exact opposite of such compassionate people.

**ūna kārakargaḷ** – Not only will they carry out such heinous acts themselves and become sinners, they will also engage others in such acts and make them sinners. *kārakargaḷ* – engaging others for carrying out tasks. *ūnam* – lowly acts. Since these people would not indulge in such heinous acts after acquiring knowledge about *mēmporuḷ*, we should presume that these acts have been carried out earlier.

**ēlum** – even if they would have done such crimes earlier, they would not do such acts after acquiring knowledge. If they see others suffer {after acquiring this knowledge\], they will suffer the pangs of the others’ pain and swoon. It is pertinent to remember here that [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kuraththazhwan/) once heard the wailing of a frog that had been caught by a snake and thought “Oh, how terrible! Who is it calling out for who could hear it and save it in this state?” He became very agitated and swooned. If such people torture others, it will be only for their benefit, as said by this [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) in the 8<sup>th</sup> pāsuram “*talaiyai āngē aṟuppadē karumam kandāy*” (severing the heads of people who abuse [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the correct action), just as emperumān poses difficulties to jīvātmās \[for their ultimate benefit\].

**pōnagam** – food. Since these jñānis (knowledgeable people) are more supportive than emperumān, just as one says “*amudu seydu*” (offer divine food) in emperumān’s case, āḻvār says *pōnagam seydu* in these jñānis’ case, for want of a better word.

**seyda sēdam** – the food remnants (after partaken by the jñāni). [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/), in his stōtra ratnam 42 says “*tvadīya bhuktōjjitaśēśabhōjinā tvayā nisruśtātmabharēṇa yat yatā*   I *priyēṇa sēnāpatinā nyavēdi tat tatā’nujānandamudāra vīkśaṇai:* īī” (one who eats the food that remains after you have eaten, one who acts as the administrator of your two vibhūtis (realms) having been appointed by you, one who is sweet (to everyone), such [viśvaksēnar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/vishwaksenar/)’s acts were carried out with divine mercy as per the way you ordained . . .). [periyāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/periyazhwar/) in [tiruppallāṇḍu 9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/11/thiruppallandu-9-uduththuk-kalaintha/) says “*kalattaduṇḍu*” (eating the remnants after you had eaten). Thus these terms refer to the food that remains on the (banana) leaf \[which is used as a plate to eat out of, traditionally\] after the food had been partaken.

**taruvarēl** – if one gets such a rare offering. This is not like the bhagavat prasādam ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s mercy) that those, who desire other things also, get. Thus, if such a great rare benefit were to be obtained . . . It is pertinent to recall an incident here – One day, [parāsara bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) ([kūrattāzvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kUraththAzhwAn/)’s son) was discoursing on tiruviruttam (One of the works of [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/)) when he gave the meaning of the word “*maippadi mēni*” as – no one can know that “it is like this” – such is his tirumēni (divine form) that it will darken \[blind\] the eyes of the beholder. His disciple, [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/), who was listening to this, told him that the pāsuram by [tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) in periya tirumoḻi 10.10.9 “*innār enṟaṟiyēn*” has similar meaning to this. bhattar was overjoyed on hearing this and gave the remnants of his food (*pōnagam seyda sēdam*) to nanjīyar as his prasādam (grace).

**punidam anṟē** – if one takes the remaining portion of the food that these knowledgeable people had, it will sanctify that person who takes it, immediately, just as the *kuḷigai* (magic lump, touchstone) called *sparśavēdi* converts iron into gold as soon as it touches it. There is no need to wait for another body, another time, another place, for this effect (to sanctify). *vihakōśam* (a sacred text) says “*nārāyaṇaika niśtasya yā yā vruttis tadarcanam* I *yō yō jalpas sa sa japas tat dyānam yannirīkśaṇam*  īī *tatpādāmbvatulam tīrtam taducciśtam su pāvanam* I *taduktimātram mantrāgryam tatspruśtamakhilam śuci* īī” (whatever activities are performed by a person who is an exclusive devotee of nārāyaṇa, they are all bhagavat ārādanam (worship of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)); whatever words are mumbled by him, they are prayers; whatever he sees, are meditation on viṣṇu; whichever water comes into contact with his feet, becomes incomparable *tīrtam* (sacred water); the food that remains after he has eaten, is sanctified; his every word becomes a *mantra* (vēdic hymn); everything he touches becomes purified). Thus śāstrams also spoke high of *pōnaga sēdam*. If emperumān’s *prasādam* (grace) is *pāvanam* (purifying), the *prasādam* of his followers is *supāvanam* (very purifying).

We shall move on to the 42<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 42. Paludila olugalarru

*paḻudilā oḻugal āṟṟup palasaduppēdimārgaḷ iḻi kulattavargaḷēlum emmadiyārgaḷāgil toḻuminīr kodumin koḷmin enṟu ninnōdum okka vaḻipada aruḷināy pōl madiḷ tiruvarangattānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

madiḷ tiruvarangattānē – Oh one who is residing inside the temple with high walls!; oḻugal āṟu – in the lengthy lineage starting with brahmā, up to themselves; paḻudu ilā – without any blemish; pala saduppēdimārgaḷ – those who are learned in the four vēdas!; em adiyārgaḷ āgil – if they are regarded as “our servitors”; iḻi kulattavargaḷ ēlum – even if they are born in a lowly birth; nīr – you; toḻumin – worship (them); kodumin – teach them (the special knowledge that you have); koḷmin – learn from them (if they have special knowledge); enṟu – thus; ninnōdum okka – as your equal; vaḻipada – to worship; aruḷināy pōl – did you not divine!

**Перевод:**

**paḻudilā oḻugalāṟṟu – Starting from brahmā and reaching up to them, they come from a lineage where no blemish \[transgression\] could be pointed at them. oḻugal – long lineage; āṟu – path. Either in their birth or in their behaviour, they do not slip from their quality of brahminhood (quality of being a brāhmaṇa).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*Those who have this knowledge of mēmporuḷ pāsuram, even if they are born in lowly births and consequently have lowly behaviour, are qualified to be vorśipped by those who are born in higher births (caturvēdis, those who are conversant with all the four vēdas), on a par with sarvēśvaran, and are also qualified for exchanging jñānam (knowledge) with those born in higher births. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings. பழுதிலா ஒழுகல் அற்றுப் பல சதுப்பேதிமார்கள் இழி குலத்தவர்களேலும் எம்மடியார்களாகில் தொழுமினீர் கொடுமின் கொள்மின் என்று நின்னோடும் ஒக்க வழிபட அருளினாய் போல் மதிள் திருவரங்கத்தானே.*

**pala saduppēdimārgaḷ** – in this lineage there were many  who had studied all the four vēdas. Just as it is said *caturvēdā ruśaya:* (riśis are those who recite the four vēdas), the people from this lineage are learned like the riśis. *saduppēdimārgaḷ* – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is calling out to those in this lineage, Oh! caturvēdimārgaḷē (those who have learned the four vēdas)!

**iḻi kulattavargaḷēlum** – even if they are born in a class below which there is no other class. *ēlum* – as said in śrī gītā “*bahunām janmanām antē*” a person gets the knowledge that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is the prāpyam and prāpakam (goal and means) only after many \[hundreds of thousands of\] births. For those with such a high level of knowledge \[of *mēmporuḷ*\], it is unlikely that they would be born in a low birth. If they had been born in a low birth, like a vidura or a darmavyāda . . .

**em adiyārgaḷ āgil** – if they know all the relationships with me \[emperumān\] \[it is said that there are nine types of relationships between a [cētana](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/06/thathva-thrayam-chith-who-am-i/) and [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] and if they act according to that knowledge. The meaning here is that these people think that kainkaryam to emperumān alone is beneficial and emperumān also thinks that for them he is the upāyam and upēyam (means and goal) and all types of relationships for these people.

**toḻumin nīr** – you worship them. “*vidyāmadō danamadas trutiyō’bhijanō mada:* I *ētē madāvaliptānām ēta Eva satām dama:* īī” (for the arrogant, being learned becomes a cause for arrogance; being wealthy is also a cause for arrogance; being born in a high lineage also becomes a cause for arrogance. These (education, wealth and lineage) become the causes for good people to be humble). Thus, you people, who feel that your education, wealth and high lineage should become reasons for humility instead of arrogance, fall at their feet \[prostrate to them\]. *jitantē* ślōkam 1.9 says “*kālēśvapi ca sarvēśu dikśu sarvāsu cācyuta* I *śareerē ca gatau cāpi vartatē mē mahat bhayam* īī” (Oh one who does let his followers down! In all the *puṇya kālam* (auspicious times), in the *puṇya kśētrams* (holy places) in all directions, in the physical body (which is the implement for virtuous deeds) and in the state other than physical body, I have great fear). Thus, as said in sacred texts, only [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s causeless grace gives fearlessness and all the other implements create only fear. You people, who have this belief, worship those who have the same belief. As [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kUraththAzhwAn/), in Śrīvaikuṇṭha stavam 77 says “ *kainkarya nitya niratair bhavatēkabhōgair nityairanukśaṇanavīnarasārdrabhāvai:* I *nityā’pi vāncitaparaspara nīca bhāvair maddaivatai: parijanaistava sangasīya* īī” (make me to be together with those who are your beloved ones and who are fit to be vorśipped by me, who are always engaged in kainkaryams, who consider you as their greatest enjoyment, those whose hearts melt in new experienes \[about you\] every moment, who desire to be servitors to each other), the true nature of ātmā, which is being humble, comes naturally to those born in lower births. It is very rare for you people, born in high lineage and being learned in the four vēdas, due to the arrogance of being born in high birth. The opinion here is that “if you people worship them, your arrogance which makes you suffer in this samsāram, will go”. Have we seen anywhere a person from lower birth protecting one from higher birth? In kaiśika *vruttāntam* (incident), we have seen that nampāduvān, a person from caṇḍāḷa class, was able to get rid of *brahmarakśas* (a demoniacal entity) from the body of a brāhmaṇa who had lost his quality of being a brāhmaṇa.

**kodumin koḷmin** – if they desire to obtain knowledge from you, tell them supportively. If they desire to divine knowledge about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) to you, seek it and enjoy it. Since those exchanges which take place because of class of birth and engaging with each other because of qualities, will not last beyond that birth, they will be like words written on the surface of water and will not last long. Relationship established with these people \[having knowledge of *mēmporul*\] through exchanging of knowledge will continue beyond this birth, into paramapadam and will last as long as ātmā lasts \[forever\].

**enṟu ninnōdum okka vaḻipada aruḷināy pōl** – The opinion here is that “since all the meanings until now in this pāsuram have been divined by you \[emperumān\] , who grants *mōkśapalam* (benefit of liberation), there is no scope for any doubt in this”. *enṟu ninnōdum okka aruḷināy* – you divined “since there is no one superior to me, fit for vorśipping, worship them just as you worship me and proceed in the path of righteousness”. *aruḷināy* – vyākhyātā (commentator) quotes [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who divined in gārudam 219.6…9  “*madbahaktajana vātsalyam pūjāyāncānumōdanam* I *svayamabhyarcanancaiva madartē damba varjanam* īī *matkatāśravṇē bhakti: svaranētrānga vikriyā* I *mamānusmaraṇam nitya yacca mām nōpajīvati* I *bhaktiraśtavidā hyēśā yasmin mlēccē’pivarttatē* īī *sa viprēndrō muni: śrīmān sa yati: sa ca paṇḍita:* I *tasmai dēyam tatō grāhyam sa ca pūjyō yatā hyayan* īī” ((1) motherly love towards my followers, (2) agreeing to my being vorśipped, (3) the person himself vorśipping me, (4) not being ostentatious in my matters, (5) showing affection in hearing my stories, (6) when thinking about me, voice faltering, eyes shedding tears and body developing goose-bumps always, (7) always thinking about me and (8) not asking for any other benefit from me \[except myself\] – if these eight different types of devotion are seen in a person, even if he is a lowly person, he alone is a superior brāhmaṇa; he alone is a sage; he alone is a wealthy person; he alone is a scholar; he can be taught knowledge; knowledge can be obtained from him; he is fit to be vorśipped like me).  *aruḷināy* – isn’t this secret divined by you out of your causeless mercy? [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 7.10.11 “*tīrttangaḷē enṟu pūsittu nalgiyuraippar tam dēviyarkkē* ” (nityasūris also will tell their consorts about the pure nature of those who have connection with emperumān)

**pōl** – like that. We can consider that in the ślōka seen earlier on varāha avatāram (incarnation as varāha), what he divined as varāha, he carried out as periya perumāḷ in the case of [tiruppāṇāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thiruppanazhwar/). First [varāha perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) divined the meanings for this. (toṇḍaradippodi) [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) composed the meanings in this pāsuram in tamiḻ. [periya perumāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), after hearing this pāsuram, carried it out in the case of tiruppāṇāḻvār. Just as śrī rāma heard śrī rāmāyanam from *agastya* (a sage), periya perumāḷ heard these meanings from this āḻvār.

**madiḷ tiruvarangattānē** – instead of merely giving a discourse on this as varāha avatāram, did you not come to tiruvarangam (śrīrangam) and make lōka sārangamuni (a temple priest) who is from a higher birth and status to carry on his head, [tiruppāṇāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thiruppanazhwar/), who is from a lower birth and status? *madiḷ tiruvarangattānē* – only if it is possible to cross the temple compound wall can one cross the limit set by you.

In some texts, it is mentioned that this pāsuram extols the virtues of śrīpāda tīrtam (divine water from the feet of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s followers). The previous pāsuram, *pōnagam seyda sēdam,* mentioned about the greatness of food partaken from a follower of emperumān. It is apt that this pāsuram mentions about the greatness of śrīpāda tīrtam. The meaning for *kodumin koḷmin* should be changed in keeping with this order. If the followers pray for it, provide śrīpāda tīrtam and accept if they offer śrīpāda tīrtam.  *ninnōdum okka vaḻipada aruḷināy* – in śāstram, it is mentioned that ācāryan is to be treated as emperumān’s avatāram (incarnation). Hence, *ninnōdum okka* would mean treating ācāryan like emperumān. Just as it says *svācāryamiva tam druśtvā* (looking  (at a śrīvaiṣṇava) (with dignity) as one’s ācāryan ….), one should worship a śrīvaiṣṇava like one’s ācāryan. Similarly *avidyam vā sa vidyam vā* (irrespective of whether a śrīvaiṣṇava is learned or not, he should be treated with dignity). tiruvāimoḻi 7.10.11 that we had seen earlier *dēvar vaigal tīrttangaḷē enṟu* , nityasūris also lauded because of the connection with śrīpāda tīrtam. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 10.9.10 said *pādangaḷ kaḻuvinar* meaning that nityasūris accepted śrīpāda tīrtam.

We shall next take up the 43<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 43. Amaravor angam arum

*amaravōr angam āṟum vēdam ōr nāngum ōdit tamargaḷil talaivarāya sādi antaṇargaḷēlum numargaḷaip paḻipparāgil nodippadōr aḷavil āngē avargaḷ tām pulaiyar pōlum aranga mānagaruḷānē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aranga mānagar uḷānē – Oh, one who resides inside the temple at śrīrangam; ōr angam āṟum – the unique six parts of vēdam; ōr vēdam nāngum – the incomparable four vēdas; amara – firmly settled in their hearts; ōdi – reciting them; tamargaḷil – among your followers; talaivar āya – being the leader; sādi antaṇargaḷēlum – even if they belong to the class of brāhmaṇa; numargaḷai – your followers; paḻippar āgil – (looking at their birth and behaviour) if they vilify them; nodippadōr aḷavil – in that minute itself; avargaḷ tām – those brāhmaṇas only; āngē – at that same place; pulaiyar pōlum – will become caṇḍāḷas \[people of low births; a wretch\]

**Перевод:**

**amara ōr angam ārum – The six parts of vēda are: (1) śīkśā (articulation or pronunciation of words), (2) vyākaraṇa (grammar), (3) candas (poetic metrics), (4) niruktam (etymology), (5) jyōtiśam (astrology) and (6) kalpam (rituals). These are unique to vēdas. amara – learning the texts and the meanings of these 6 parts and retaining them. ōr angam – the uniqueness to these parts \[of vēdas\] is that if one has knowledge on any of these 6 parts without any deficiency, he is considered by worldly people as having known everything and they celebrate it. It is pertinent to remember that in śrīrangarāja stavam 2.18, bhattar says “śīkśāyām varṇa śikśā pada samagadigamō vyākriyā nirvacōbhyam candaś candaścitau syāt gamayati samayam jyautiśam ranganāta! I kalpē’nuśtānamuktam hyucitagamitayōr nyāya mīmāmsayō: syāt artavyakti: purāṇasmrutiśu tadanugāstvām vicinvanti vēdā: īī” (Oh śrī ranganāta! In the part of vēdas called śīkśa, the method of pronouncing letters (is described); in the parts vyākaraṇam and niruktam, knowledge about words (is created); in the śāstra called candas, candas (such as gāyatri etc) have been told; jyōtiśa śāstram indicates the time for carrying out karmānuśtānam (carrying out deeds ordained in vēdas); in kalpa sūtrams, the procedure for carrying out the karmas is mentioned. In the nyāyamīmāmsa śāstram and purāṇamsrutis which are consistent with the philosophy of vēdas, clarity of the subject will be obtained; vēdas, with sikśa and other parts mentioned above, are searching for you), thus explaining the 6 parts of vēdas.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had spoken about those born in high births and with consequent good behaviour. If added to these greatness, they also have high level of knowledge and if they look at those who do not have any greatness due to their birth and behaviour but have good knowledge \[in mēmporuḷ pāsuram\], and if the former set of people, without recognising that these people have the grace of emperumān, think lowly of the latter set of people only on account of their birth and behaviour, these high ranking people will become caṇḍāḷas (people of very low birth) at that very moment. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings. அமரவோர் அங்கம் ஆரும் வேதம் ஓர் நான்கும் ஓதித் தமர்களில் தலைவராய சாதி அந்தணர்களேலும் நுமர்களைப் பழிப்பராகில் நொடிப்பதோர் அளவில் ஆங்கே அவர்கள் தாம் புலையர் போலும் அரங்கமா நகருளானே.*

**vēdamōr nāngum** – vēdas are incomparable because unlike other texts compiled by a man \[any person\], they do not have faults such as *iruḷ* (delusion or clouded state of mind), *tuyakku* (confusion), *mayakku* (ignorance) and *maṟappu* (forgetfulness). Alternatively, we can consider that they indicate the greatness of sections of vēdas such as *mantram* (hymns), *vidi* (rules) and *artavādam* (eulogy).

**ōdi** – while learning vēdas, the ācāryan (teacher) would first recite the four vēdas and the six parts, and the śiśya (disciple) will then recite the same.  *ōdi* is learning vēdas this way. Since they have learnt this way, we can surmise that they know [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) well, indeed.

**tamargaḷil talaivarāya** – since they know emperumān well in this way, consistent with that knowledge, they will stand at the forefront in any gōśti (assembly) of his followers.

**sādi antaṇargaḷ** – while learning vēdas thoroughly and being in the forefront of their class and such other great qualities are common with kśatriya (warrior) and vaiśya (trader) classes too, apart from these great qualities, they are also of high birth (brāhmaṇa). Thus, in their birth, behaviour and knowledge, they are great.

**ēlum** – the greatness described thus far cannot be found in one person, together. [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) brings in this doubt to show that even if these great qualities happen to be with one person, if that person abuses a śrīvaiṣṇava, these qualities will become useless. In reality, these qualities become reasons for a person with vanity to commit *bhāgavatāpacāram* (showing irreverence to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s follower). Even if the persons are jñānis (knowledgeable) but do not have this greatness, others would carry out inappropriate deeds like stepping on their heads etc. Another interpretation for this is that if they do not have the quality of being vain, they can step even on yama’s head.

**numargaḷai** – those having knowledge of *mēmporuḷ* pāsuram, as a result of which they become leaders of your \[emperumān’s\] followers. Here the word “followers” refers to nityasūris (permanent dwellers of paramapadam). [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/), in his tiruviruttam 79, says “*viṇṇuḷārilum sīriyarē*” (they are greater than nityasūris) and in periyatiruvandādi 79, says “*uṇṇāttut tēsanṟē*” (the radiance of those who live in samsāram is greater than that of those (nityasūris) who live in paramapadam).  *numargaḷai* – those who you deem as your followers and who affirm that you are their means and goal. Those who know “it is he \[[emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] who is happy at attaining us. Hence it is he who gets the benefit out of \[our\] liberation. Even the happiness that we get is not appropriate for us. It is he who is controlling his possession \[us\]. We have no engagement with our protection. Only his causeless mercy is our means”.

**paḻipparāgil** – instead of thinking of them as those who are having the grace of emperumān, forgetting this due to one’s ego, thinking only of their low birth and consequently thinking of them to be below one’s status, speaking abusive words about them and harming them through deeds. The irreverence shown to these parama bhāgavatas (superior followers) is the worst offence that one could do to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). *paḻittal* – committing an offence. In other words, thinking of the low birth of a śrīvaiṣṇava is as terrible a crime as estimating the importance of emperumān based on the material with which his vigraham (divine idol) is made of in a divya dēśam. *paḻipparāgil* – while it is apt to celebrate them, due to ego, one ends up thinking lowly of them due to his ignorance.

The calamity due to such abuse is given now……

**nodippadōr aḷavil āngē** – it is not at some other time, in some other place, with some other body \[form\]. One would get the effect of abusing them at the same time, in the same place and with the same body. Just as we have seen in [pāsuram 41](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/12/thirumalai-41/) *punidam anṟē* (getting the) benefit (of having connection with them) instantaneously, the ill-effect of abusing them would also happen instantaneously.

**avargaḷ tām pulaiyar pōlum** – with the same body, they will become caṇḍāḷas (wretch, born in a very low class). Because of the curse of the sons of rich vaśiśta, the valorous chain that triśanku (a king) wore got turned into leather strap. In the same way, the sacred thread that these high birth brāhmaṇas wear would turn into leather strap. *tām pulaiyar* – despite being great due to their high birth etc as seen earlier, if such greatness becomes cause of their ego and  incites them into carrying out offence against śrīvaiṣṇavas, it will cause irreparable damage to them. If a person were born in a lowly birth, they would still qualify to become a śrīvaiṣṇava. But since these persons fell into a deep pit despite being born in a high birth, and since they had indulged in bhāgavatāpacāram (irreverence to śrīvaiṣṇavas), they go lower down to a level of caṇḍāḷa by deed (instead of caṇḍāḷa by birth). It is pertinent to recall an incident in the life of [kūrattāḻvān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/kUraththAzhwAn/) here. On the day that one piḷḷaippiḷḷai āḻvān became kūrattāḻvān’s disciple, kūrattāḻvān, knowing his disciple’s proclivity for committing bhāgavatāpacāram because of his high birth, decided to rein him in. On an auspicious day, he asked piḷḷaippiḷḷai āḻvān to grant him a gift. piḷḷaippiḷḷai āḻvān was puzzled since everything (that a disciple has) belongs to ācāryan (kūrattāḻvān) and what could he gift him? kūrattāḻvān responded “through all the three karaṇas (organs of thought, word and deed) you should promise me that you will not abuse a bhāgavata”. piḷḷaippiḷḷai āḻvān did the same. After some days, due to force of habit, piḷḷaippiḷḷai āḻvān committed an offence against a bhāgavata through his thought. He felt very ashamed of himself and covering his head with a piece of cloth, he stayed in his residence itself, without going to ācāryan’s tirumāḷigai (residence). kūrattāḻvān, after waiting awhile, came in search of him and found out as to what happened. piḷḷaippiḷḷai āḻvān fell at the feet of kūrattāḻvān, and weeping uncontrollably, told him “it is so very difficult to live with this body without committing an offence against a bhāgavata through the three organs”. kūrattāḻvān felt happy that his disciple felt genuinely sorry for having committed an offence and told him “since you feel so desolate after committing the offence, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will certainly forgive you. Since you know how you feel after committing the offence through thought, you will not commit an offence by deed. Make sure that you do not commit any offence through your words”.

**aranga mānagar uḷānē** – In varāha purāṇam, [śrī varāha mūrty](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) tells [bhūdēvi](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/) “*madbhaktam śvapacam vāpi nindām kurvantiyē narā:* I *padmakōtiśatēnāpi na kśamāmi kadācana* īī” (whosoever abuses my followers, even if my followers are of low birth, eating dog’s meat, I will not forgive them even for crores of years). Thus, emperumān knows clearly the ill-effect of committing an offence against his followers. For those who try to attain emperumān through other upāyams (means) such as karma yōgam etc, by doing appropriate penance, they can get rid of any offence that they may commit in between and continue with their upāyams. But, for one who has to attain mōkśam through connection with emperumān (by surrendering), if he commits a bhāgavata apacāram (offence to his follower), it will sever the connection with emperumān himself and there is no way that he can redeem himself. Just as connection with his followers is enough to attain emperumān, committing offence against his followers is enough to lose emperumān. Hence, a prapannan (one who surrenders to emperumān) should be very careful in ensuring that he does not have any contact with other dēvatas (deities) and does not commit any offence against his followers. Thus, it is clear from this pāsuram that even if a person does not do anything which is considered as good by emperumān, if he stays away from the above-mentioned two offences, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) will become easily approachable to such a person.

We shall take up the 44<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 44. Pennulam sadaiyinanum

*peṇṇulām sadaiyinānum piramanum unnaik kāṇbān eṇṇilā ūḻi ūḻi tavam seydār veḷgi niṟpa viṇṇuḷār viyappa vandu ānaiakkanṟu aruḷai īnda kaṇṇuṟā unnai ennō kaḷaigaṇāk karudumāṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

peṇ ulām – gangā moving about; sadaiyinānum – sivan, having matted hair; piramanum – and brahmā; unnaik kāṇbān – to see you; eṇ ilā ūḻi ūḻi – for innumerable periods \[ūḻi is the time of deluge\]; tavam seydār – those who did penance; veḷgi niṟpa – putting their heads down in shame (since they could not see you); anṟu – during that time; ānaikku – for śrī gajēndrāḻvān (who got trapped in the crocodile’s jaws); vandu – (to liberate him) coming to the shore of the pond; viṇ uḷār viyappa – making even the nityasūris amazed; aruḷai īnda – providing your grace; kaṇṇuṟa – one who is blind \[in not seeing the shortcomings of followers\]; unnai – you; kaḷaigaṇā – being the refuge for all; karudum āṟu ennō – how to think?

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (commentary)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*āḻvār says that all the eminence that he had mentioned for the bhāgavatas (followers) of emperumān would fit them well, considering that an animal, gajēndran, got the rare-to-obtain benefit that even people like brahmā et al could not get. Through this incident, he also indicates the benefit that he had obtained and brings this prabandam (poetic composition) to an end \[the next pāsuram gives the benefit for those who learn these pāsurams\]. Let us enjoy the pāusram and its meanings. பெண்ணுலாம் சடையினானும் பிரமனும் உன்னைக் காண்பான் எண்ணிலா  ஊழி ஊழி தவம் செய்தார் வெள்கி நிற்ப விண்ணுளார் வியப்ப வந்து ஆனைக்கன்று அருளை ஈந்த கண்ணுறா உன்னை என்னோ களைகணாக் கருதுமாறே.*

**peṇṇulām sadaiyinānum** – Siva, who has expansive matted hair so that gangā can move about. The opinion is – is it possible to see sarvēśvaran by having the pride of controlling gangā inside his matted locks? It is pertinent to recall [tirumaḻisai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumazhisai-azhwar/)’s nānmugan tiruvandādi 4 “*āṟu sadaikkarandān aṇḍarkōn tannōdum kūṟudaiyavan enbadum koḷgaittē*” (is it proper to say that rudran, who is holding gangā, hidden in his matted hair, is equal to sarvēśvaran?

**piramanum** – and brahmā, who created that Siva. śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120.3 says “*kartā sarvasya lōkasya brahmā brahmavidām vara:*” (one who creates all the words and who is the greatest among brahmajñānis (those who know brahmam, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/))). Can such brahmā, thinking of the pride of giving knowledge to jīvātmās like a school teacher, attain emperumān? He can at the most count the people that he has created and their pāpa and puṇya (sins and virtues), having created them. Can he measure the immeasurable emperumān? [poygai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/poigai-azhwar/) in his mudal tiruvandādi 56 says “*kadikkamalam tannuḷ irundum kāṇkilān kaṇṇan adikkamalam tannai ayan*” (despite being inside the lotus flower which sprang from emperumān’s navel, brahmā has not seen the divine lotus feet of kriṣṇa).

**unnaik kāṇbān** – to see you. taittirīya upaniśat says “*yatō vācō nivartantē*” (trying to measure the qualities of emeprumān, vēdas returned, unable to measure). Your ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s) greatness is such that vēdas could not measure even one of your auspicious qualities. However much nityasūris enjoy you, they cannot see your limits. To see such an emperumān . śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 5.1.49 says “*niravadya: para: prāptēr niraditō’kśara: karma:* I *sarvēśvaraparādārō dāmnām dāmātmakō’vyaya:* īī” ((you) are faultless; are rare to attain; do not have one to support you; are indestructible; have unlimitedly supreme joy; are the support for all who are engaged with you, the īsvara; are the radiance for all radiant materials; are faultless). It is not that the others do not know your limits. Even you do not know your limits. [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) said the same in his tiruvāimoḻi 8.4.6 “*tanakkum than tanmai aṟivariyān*” (even he does not know his inherent quality). *kāṇbān* – only after seeing him can I get a benefit from him. Unless he makes himself available to be seen, others, however great jñāna (knowledge) or śakti (power) they may have, cannot see him with their capabilities. tattirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 1.10 says “*na sandruśē tiśtati rūpamasya na cakśuśā paśyati kaścanainam*” (his form is such that it cannot be seen by \[our\] eyes; no one sees him with his eyes). rig vēdam says “*na māmsa cakśurabhivīkśatē tam*” (his form cannot be seen by these eyes made of flesh). viṣṇu darmam says “*na māmsa cakśuśā draśtum brahmabhūta: sa śakyatē*” (he, the brahmam, is rare to be seen by these eyes, made of flesh). Thus, the vēda śāstras thundered that he cannot be seen easily.

**eṇṇilā ūḻi ūḻi tavam seydār veḷgi niṟpa** – those who carry out penance for long years such as the period of deluge, step back from their penance with shame, thinking ”the person, to see whom we are carrying out this penance, has not yet come”. dēvatās on whom hiraṇya, rāvaṇa et al carry out penance to grant them benefits, carry out penance on him. mahābhāratam sabhā parvam 14.8 says “*yuga kōti sahasrāṇi viṣṇumārādya padmabhū:* I *punastrailōkyadātrutvam prāptavāniti śuśruma* īī” (we hear that brahmā, born in a lotus flower, did penance for thousands of crores of yugas \[each yuga is 43,72,000 years by our metrics\] and became the head for the three worlds again). *veḷgi niṟpa* – the opinion here is that instead of doing penance for crores of years it would have been better had they fallen into the jaws of a crocodile, and hence they felt ashamed. viṣṇu darmam 69.47 says “*paramāpadamāpannō manasā’cintayaddarim* I *sa tu nāgavara: śrīmān nārāyaṇa parāyaṇa:* īī” (gaJendrāḻvān \[gajēndra, the elephant\], who got into a deep trouble, who had the wealth of kainkaryam \[service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)\] and one who had nārāyaṇa as the supreme refuge, meditated on hari \[nārāyaṇa\]). Thus, these dēvatas felt ashamed that instead of surrendering to emperumān due to the danger of samsāram, their decision to carry out penance with their own efforts has been proved wrong.

**viṇṇuḷār viyappa vandu** *–* nityasūris were surprised that emperumān was ignoring them as well as dēvatās such as brahmā, who were toiling like this, in order to fall at the feet of an animal, with his divine dress and divine hair diśevelled. They marvel at his simplicity. [bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) in his śrīrangarāja stavam 2.57 says ”*atantritaśamupatiprahitahastam svīkruta praṇitamaṇipādukam kimiti cākulānta:puram* I *avāhanapariśkriyam patakarājamārōhata: kripravarabrumhitē bhagavatastvarāyai nama:* īī” (I salute the speed with which you came to climb on *periya tiruvadi* (garuda), who was not in his splendorous decor, as soon as you heard the distressing voice of gajēndrāḻvān, not bothering about the support for your hand that viśvaksēnar extended, not wearing the gem-studded sandals brought for you and with your womenfolk exclaiming in grief “how is this!”). Since those who wonder should be different from those who stand in shame, the term *viṇṇuḷār* refers to nityasūris who are different from brahmā et al \[who were referred to as veḷgi niṟpa\]. *vandu* – coming to the shore of the pond where gajēndra was in distress; since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says ”coming to the shore” instead of saying “going to the shore” it shows that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) considers only that place where  his follower is present as his place and his action to assist his follower is his own.

**ānaikku** – without considering that brahmā et al were born in dēva class and gajēndra was born in animal class, emperumān only looked at the danger that his follower was in and protected him.

**anṟu** – that day when gajēndra realised that his strength would not protect him. viṣṇu darmam 69 says “*gaja ākarśatē dīrē grāha ākarśatē jalē* I *tayōrdvandvasamam yuddam divyam varśasahasrakam* īī” (gajēndra is pulling to the land; crocodile is pulling into the water; between them the battle lasted on equal front for 1000 years by dēva metrics). When gajēndra tried to save himself, there was no difference between him and the penance that brahmā et al had carried out for their benefit. It is pertinent to note that only when gajēndra stepped back from protecting himself did he become greater than those people \[brahmā et al\]. That day you showed mercy on an elephant which was trapped in a crocodile’s jaws; today, when I am trapped in the five senses-like crocodiles of śabdam etc \[sound, sight, taste, smell and touch\], you should show mercy. The elephant suffered for 1000 years; I am suffering since time immemorial. He was trapped in the shores of a small pond; I am caught in the ocean of births. The opinion here is that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) should shower grace on [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) who in caught in a far more serious danger than gajēndra.

**aruḷaiyīnda** – aruḷ is grace. The act of grace \[of emerpumān\] was to free gajēndra from the jaws of the crocodile, gently stroke his injured leg, to give fomentation with his *vastram* (upper cloth) to comfort him, to take the flower from his trunk and place in on his \[emperumān’s\] divine feet. The opinion is that in the same way emeprumān should redeem āḻvār from *śabdādi* (five senses such as sound etc) matters and comfort him by making him a servitor to make up for the lost time also.

**kaṇṇuṟā** – one who is blind. “Since you \[emperumān\] helped me \[not minding my blemishes\]” [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) is happy that [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is without sight – this is a type of nindāstuti ( praising through abuse). This shows emperumān’s sensible quality of not expecting anything in return when he engages in ignoring those who try to protect themselves and blessing those who withdraw from protecting themselves. It shows his great masculinity. āḻvār is surprised that this world is losing out, not knowing that emperumān will even fall at the feet of one who desists from protecting himself and holds on to emperumān as means, bearing in mind his emptiness.

**unnai ennō kaḷaikaṇak karudumāṟē** – those who think that emperumān is the universal protector are ignorant because they do not know that he protects only those who consider him as their means and ignores those who think that emperumān, as well as their own efforts, will protect them. While jitantē ślōkam 1.2 says “*dēvānām dānavānānca sāmānyamadidaivatam* ” (he is equal to both those who follow him and those who do not), since he kills entities such as crocodile to protect entities such as elephant, can we consider him to be protector for all? We could consider this also as *nindāstuti*.

We shall now take up the last pāsuram in this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa ramanuja dāsan

## 45. Valavelum tavala mada

*vaḷaveḻum tavaḷa māda madurai mānagaram tannuḷ kavaḷamāl yānai konṟa kaṇṇanai aranga mālait tuḷavat toṇḍāya tol seert toṇḍaradippodi sol iḷaiya pun kavidaiyēlum empirāṟku iniyavāṟē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaḷam eḻum – being beautiful; tavaḷam – being white coloured; mādam – having storied houses; mā – being great; madurai nagaram tannuḷ – in vada madurai (maturā); kavaḷam – with mouthful of food; māl – huge; yānai – elephant (called kuvalayāpīdam); konṟa – killed; kaṇṇanai – śrī kriṣṇa; aranga mālai – śrī ranganātan; tuḷabam toṇḍu āya – one who is engaged in tuḷasi service; tol seer – one who is fully engaged in bhāgavata śēśatvam (being servitor to śrīvaiṣṇavas); toṇḍaradippodi – toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār; sol – (recited) prabandam called tirumālai; iḷaiya pun kavidai ēlum – even if it has blemishes such as choice of words, poetry metrics etc; em pirāṟku – for my svāmy (master) periya perumāḷ; iniya āṟē – how is it so sweet!

**Перевод:**

**vaḷaveḻum tavaḷamāda – having beautiful, silver-like white coloured storied houses. āḻvār talks about the decorations in vada madurai (maturā) which were missed out when kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) was born there and consoles himself by decorating with words. Since he is going to talk about kuvalayāpīda (elephant) incident, it could be taken that kamsan has got the place decorated for the festival. vaḷam indicates beauty and eḻum indicates excessive. tavaḷa indicates whiteness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*In the  last pāsuram of this prabhandam, āḻvār says that just as emperumān destroyed kuvalayāpīda yānai (the royal elephant of kriṣṇa’s uncle, kamsa), he destroyed those sins which were hurdles for āḻvār to reach him and further says that emperumān’s pleasure is his benefit, bringing this prabandam to an end. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings. வளவெழும் தவள மாட மதுரை மாநகரம் தன்னுள் கவளமால் யானை கொன்ற கண்ணனை அரங்க மாலைத் துவளத் தொண்டாய தொல் சீர்த் தொண்டரடிப்பொடி சொல் இளைய புன் கவிதையேலும் எம்பிறார்க்கு இனியவாறே.*

**madurai mānagaram tannuḷ** – in the great city of madurai. What is this greatness? Initially it was the place which had vāmana āśramam (the hermitage of vāmana) when [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) took the incarnation of vāmana. Later it was the capital of śrī śatrugna. Finally, it was the place where kaṇṇan was born. Thus, since it has connection with emperumān continuously, unlike śrīvaikuṇṭam which appears to be having bright lamps shining during daytime \[there is not much use with the lights in the day time\], this madurai is similar to having bright lamps during night time in this ignorant samsāram and hence is greater than śrīvaikuṇṭam.

**kavaḷamāl yānai konṟa kaṇṇanai** – the elephant kuvalayāpīdam (kamsan’s royal elephant) which had eaten the food called *kavaḷam* to make it mad, and was huge. In the previous pāsuram, [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) said “*ānaikku anṟu aruḷai īnda*” (one who had shown mercy on the elephant) and here he says “*yānai konṟa*” (one who killed the elephant). Through this, exception to the ślōkam “*dēvānām dānavānām ca*” (being equal to those who are his followers and who are not his followers) is being explained. āḻvār indicates here that just as kriṣṇa killed enemies such as kuvalayāpīdam and gave himself to the people of that town to enjoy him, periya perumāḷ removed his sins which were the hurdles in the way of his enjoying emperumān and gave himself to āḻvār for his enjoyment. *kaṇṇanai* – one who came to be seen. On the day that he was born, as [āṇḍāḷ](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/andal/) says in [tiruppāvai 25](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppavai-25-oruththi-maganai/) “*orutti maganāip piṟandu ōr iravil orutti maganāy oḷittu vaḷara*” (he was born to one lady and in the same night he shifted his place to hide himself and to be brought up by another lady), he returned to the place which he left on the night of his birth, to be seen by everyone and to destroy his enemies such as kamsa et al. āḻvār enjoys this greatness.

**arangamālai** – since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) came after that incarnation, kaṇṇan incarnated as periya perumāḷ to remove āḻvār’s enemies. *mālai* – one who has tremendous love towards his followers. One who treats killing enemies of his followers and giving himself to his followers for their enjoyment as being for his own benefit.

**tuḷabat toṇḍāya** – one who carries out service to tuḷasi āḻvār \[here tuḷasi is being elevated to the level of an āḻvār). While carrying out service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is *puruśārtam* (benefit or goal), carrying out service to the follower of emperumān is defined as extreme limit of *puruśārtam* \[*ellai nilam*, as referred to, by our pūrvācāryars\]. Usage of the term tuḷasi here implies all bhāgavatas. Service to tuḷasi is equivalent to service to all bhāgavatars. When service is carried out to all bhāgavatars, sometimes there could be a chance for *apacāram* (offence). But in the case of tuḷasi, there can be no such offence. āḻvār, in his other prabandam [*tiruppaḷḷiyeḻucci*](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2014/12/thiruppalliyezhuchchi/) says in the [last pāsuram](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/thiruppalliyezhuchchi-10-kadimalar/) “*todaiyotta tuḷabamum kūdaiyum polindu tōnṟiya toṇḍaradippodi* “ (toṇḍaradippodi who appeared with flower-like tuḷasai and basket), thus making tuḷasi as his identity.

**tolseer** – one who is fully established in the limit of bhagavat śēśatvam (service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)), which is bhāgavata śēśatvam (service to śrīvaiṣṇavas). By saying *tuḷabat toṇḍāya* he mentions that he is doing the service of stringing tuḷasi for emperumān and by saying *tolseer* he mentions the extreme limit of that kainkaryam, which is śrīvaiṣṇava kainkaryam.

**toṇḍaradippodi sol** – in line with his service orientation towards bhāgavatas, he has *toṇḍaradippodi* as his name. It is clear from this that his identity is not jñānam, ānandam or bhagavat śēśatvam \[our sacred texts say that ātmā is full of jñānam (knowledge), ānandam (happiness) and  is śēśabhūtan (carrying service to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/))\], but bhāgavata śēśatvam (service to followers of emperumān). Just as [nammāḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nammazhwar/) in his tiruvāimoḻi 7.1.11 said “*toṇḍar toṇḍar toṇḍar toṇḍan satakōpan*” (śatakōpan, another name of nammāḻvār, is emperumān’s follower’s follower’s follower’s follower), this āḻvār called himself as *toṇḍaradippodi* (the dust sticking to the feet of a follower of emperumān).

**iḷaiya pun kavidaiyēlum** – [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) says that the words used by him in these pāsurams are very young, \[not choosing apt words due to lack of experience\] and there are faults in his verses. Even though neither of these is true, āḻvār says this as his *naicyānusandānam* (thinking of his lowliness).

**em pirāṟku iniavāṟē** – will these not be sweet to emperumān? As we have seen earlier “*kim mruśtam sutavacanam*” (which is sweet to hear? It is the gibberish words of one’s child), [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) exclaims “is it surprising that periya perumāḷ is happy with my gibberish!” Won’t the gibberish words of a child be sweet to its father? As [āḷavandār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/alavandhar/), in stōtra ratnam 46 says “*bhavantamēvānucaran nirantarm praśānta niśśēśamanōratāntara:* I *katā’hamaikāntika nityakinkara: praharśayiśyāmi sanātajīvita:* īī” (when am I going to make (you) happy, following you constantly, getting rid of all the desires with their scents, having kainkaryam towards you uninterruptedly, having a janma (birth) which is fruitful?), making [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) happy is a person’s *puruśārtam* (goal). Being a cause for emperumān’s happiness is a cētana’s (sentient entity’s) reason for existence. It is pertinent to remember [nanjīyar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nanjiyar/)’s (disciple of [bhattar](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/parasara-bhattar/) and ācārya for [nampiḷḷai](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/nampillai/)) words “\[emperumān’s\] compassion is upāyam (means); happiness is upēyam (goal)”. Unlike other [āḻvārs](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/azhwars/), this āḻvār has not indicated the benefit for those who learn these pāsurams \[in the last pāsuram\] since he considers that emperumān’s happiness is the benefit here. Just as emperumān was happy with this āḻvār for having strung this garland \[called tirumālai\], he will be happy if people learn these pāsurams. śrī rāmāyaṇa yudda kāṇḍam 131.118 says “*rāmāyaṇam idam krutsnam śruṇvata: patatas sadā* I *prīyatē sattam rāma: sa hi viṣṇu: sanātana:* īī” (rāmapirān is always affectionate towards one who hears or reads this entire rāmāyaṇam. īsn’t rāma, the eternal viṣṇu!). The benefit that śrī vālmīki divined for those who learn śrī rāmāyaṇam is also the benefit for those who learn this divya prabhandam.

This brings us to the end of this prabandam. In the next article, we shall see the concluding section.

[toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thondaradippodi-azhwar/) tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam
